2003年导论：你已成为历史了吗？Introduction 2003: Are You History?

让-米歇尔·拉巴泰

Jean-Michel Rabaté

哈贝马斯于1980年9月宣读了他的文章《现代性——一项未完成的事业》的第一稿。这是他在法兰克福领取阿多诺奖时正式发表的答谢辞。二十一年后，确切地说在2001年9月，德里达也获得了同一奖项；授予他的这一荣誉与哈贝马斯对轻重缓急的判断相抵牾，却或许标志着一种终结，即使还谈不上和解。不论人们如何看待这一循环的闭合或这种时空错位——至少在英语国家，各方的党派立场一直十分鲜明——它都明确无误地表明，重新评估结构主义的时候早已到了。在新千年伊始提出的这项简单任务，不仅适逢其时，而且具有历史意义；它应当证实一种猜想：普遍历史或许只是对几个基本隐喻稍作变奏，便不断重复自身，正如博尔赫斯曾经所言。因此，最佳的起点，就是重新发行一本最初出版于1986年的书；当时，结构主义运动本身已开始衰落，并被传言说已成了“历史”，而此书既对与结构主义相关的各种方法和研究路线作出了评估，也提供了一部平易近人的入门读物。我想为这段历史提供一个更宽广的视野，首先提出：尽管表面看来并非如此，这段历史却并未结束。这样一来，我们应该提出的关键问题，与其说是编年史家所问的（它过去是什么？也就是：有哪些人，在何时、何地？），或文化史家所问的（这一切究竟是什么：一道涟漪，一个微不足道的闪现，还是一种更深刻的趋势？），不如说是哲学家所问的：它是什么？

Habermas read the first version of his essay ‘Modernity – an Unfinished Project’ in September 1980. This was his official discourse of thanks upon receiving the Adorno Prize in Frankfurt. Derrida received the same prize twenty-one years later, to be precise in September 2001, when he was granted a distinction that clashes with Habermas’s sense of priorities but marks perhaps a closure, if not a reconciliation. Whatever one thinks of the closure of this loop or time-warp, since partisanship has been strong at least in English-speaking countries, it signals unambiguously that it is high time to reassess Structuralism. This simple task laid out at the beginning of the new millennium is not just timely, it is historical, and should confirm a guess that Universal History may repeat itself by slightly varying on a few basic metaphors, as Borges once remarked. This is why the best starting point is the reissuing of a book first published in 1986, a book that provided as much a user-friendly introduction as an assessment of the varied methods and lines of inquiry associated with Structuralism, at a time when the movement itself had begun to fade and was rumoured to be ‘history’. I would like to offer an even wider scope for this history, and suggest first of all that, contrary to all appearances, this history has not ended. The crucial question one should then pose would be less that of the chronicler (what was it?, meaning: who, when and where was it?) or the cultural historian (what has it all been, a ripple, a mere blip or a deeper trend?), than that of the philosopher: what is it?

在我刚才提到的两次阿多诺奖授奖的背景下，这次再版正当其时：贯穿哈贝马斯1980年演讲以及随后出版的、影响广泛的《现代性的哲学话语》（1985年出版，英译本于1987年问世）的动力，是对法国“新结构主义”的猛烈批判，其中包含了对德里达思想（而非主要对其著述）的系统反驳。值得注意的是，“新结构主义”这个术语从未在英语中流行起来。英语世界更愿意使用的是“后结构主义”，这个术语同样造于20世纪70年代末，追随着当时大量发明的“后”字头术语而来（后现代主义后来显得更为持久地取得成功，尽管它如今也已失去光彩）。让我们设想一下：倘若英国人和美国人使用的是“新结构主义”而非“后结构主义”，事情会怎样？那样的话，人们感受到的便会是折中和跨学科的稀释，而不是把历史强行写入由这个“主义”汇集而成的“学派”，并随之产生一种已经超越了某种较老且精疲力竭的运动的幻觉；人们所想到的便会是更温和的扩散、更广泛的文化传播，而不是克服并猛烈扫除那些建立在令人反感的意识形态之上、因而显得局限的方法。事实上，“新结构主义”与“后结构主义”的概念几乎完全重合；只需点数哈贝马斯批判过的作者，便可验证这一点：在尼采和海德格尔之后，他又列举了德里达、巴塔耶、福柯和卡斯托里亚迪斯。不过，在哈贝马斯的叙述中，德里达是抹平“哲学与文学之间的文类区别”这一危险而广泛的趋向的头号嫌疑人。1然而，同一个德里达也被说成是结构主义的掘墓人；我们还得弄清，在结构主义之前加上“新”与“后”这两个前缀时，它们内在的双重性究竟意味着什么。

This republication is timely in the context of the double Adorno prize I have evoked: the impetus animating Habermas’s 1980 speech as well as the subsequent publication of the influential The Philosophical Discourse of Modernity (published in 1985, with the English translation in 1987) was a violent critique of French ‘neo-Structuralism’ which contained a systematic refutation of Derrida’s thought (more than of his writings). It is important to note that the term ‘neo-Structuralism’ never became current in English. What was preferred was ‘post-Structuralism’, a term also coined in the late 1970s in the wake of the many ‘posts’ invented then (post-modernism was to appear more durably successful, although it too has lost its lustre). Let us imagine what might have happened if the British and Americans had been using ‘neo-Structuralism’ instead of ‘post-Structuralism’: instead of the forced inscription of history into the ‘school’ gathered by the -ism and the concomitant illusion of having superseded an older and exhausted movement, there would have been a sense of compromise and inter-disciplinary dilution; instead of overcoming and violently obliterating methods that appeared limited when based upon unpleasant ideologies, there would have been the notion of gentler diffusion, of broader cultural dissemination. In fact, the concepts of ‘neo-Structuralism’ and ‘post-Structuralism’ overlap almost completely; this can be verified by the roll-call of authors criticized by Habermas: after Nietzsche and Heidegger, he names Derrida, Bataille, Foucault and Castoriadis. In Habermas’s account nevertheless, it is Derrida who figures as the main suspect of a dangerously pervasive levelling of the ‘genre distinction between Philosophy and Literature’.1 However, the same Derrida is also presented as the gravedigger of Structuralism, and we will have to make sense of the inner duplicity of these neo-and post-prefixes when applied to Structuralism.

我的总体论点是：我们可以把哈贝马斯关于“现代性这一未完成的事业”的观念，应用于今天回顾起来似乎是二十世纪最重要、甚至占据主导地位的哲学趋势之一，而不是应用于从启蒙运动产生的全部理性，以及这种理性把“光”投射到过去或现在的黑暗之中的那些夭折了或遭到扭曲的实践。这一点至少单就法国而言应当成立，因为结构主义是法国在国际上输出得最为成功的哲学（这也解释了哈贝马斯忧心忡忡的反应），唯一可能的例外是存在主义；不过我倒愿意论证：与其说存在主义是一种真正的哲学，不如说它更像是“一种时尚，一种道德，一种激情”——这是借用波德莱尔用来界定其现代性概念中“短暂的”、转瞬即逝的部分的话。或者更确切地说，稍稍预告一下下文，我会说，上世纪最具代表性的法国哲学家莫里斯·梅洛-庞蒂，他的重要地位在很大程度上来自这样一个事实：只有他能够以一种“新结构”版本的现象学之名，弥合存在主义与结构主义之间的裂隙。他第一部重要的哲学论著《知觉现象学》（1945）之后，于1953年又出版了《行为的结构》，这并非偶然。2

My general contention is that we can apply Habermas’s idea of an ‘unfinished project of modernity’ not to the entire rationality deriving from the Enlightenment and its aborted or distorted projection of ‘light’ into past or present darkness, but to what appears today retrospectively as one of the most important, if not the dominant, philosophical trends of the twentieth century. This would be true at least of France alone, since Structuralism has been the philosophy that France has exported with most success internationally (which explains Habermas’s worried reaction) with the possible exception of Existentialism, although I would be ready to argue that Existentialism was more ‘a fashion, a morality, a passion’ – to quote Baudelaire, defining the ‘transient’ or ephemeral part of his concept of modernity – than a true philosophy. Or rather, to anticipate slightly what will follow, I would say that the French philosopher who appears as the most emblematic of the last century, Maurice Merleau-Ponty, derives much of his eminence to the fact that he alone was able to bridge the gap between Existentialism and Structuralism in the name of a ‘neo-structural’ version of phenomenology. It is no accident that his first major philosophical treatise, entitled Phenomenology of Perception (1945) was followed in 1953 by The Structure of Behavior.2

的确，如同存在主义，结构主义很快也变成了一种单纯的时尚（那个时候，巴黎依然是专门输出la nouvelle mode——新时尚——的世界之都，服饰如此，思想亦然）；正是这一点，使哈贝马斯等敌对哲学家的拒斥，以及福柯等从这场运动早期的同路人变为揭穿者的人的拒斥，更加尖锐而痛切。哈贝马斯与法兰克福学派的联系，注定使他不喜欢任何一种“非理性的科学主义”——这一奇特的矛盾修辞，概括了那些试图为结构主义分类的德国批评者双重否定的看法。在对一个至少曾被报界认定为他所归属的思想流派所作的、以轻蔑著称的描述中（他与拉康、列维-斯特劳斯和巴特一道，是所谓“结构主义盛宴”的四位参与者之一），福柯把真正思考的耐心与纯粹追逐时尚的热情对立起来。福柯重新接过康德的批判事业，却不像哈贝马斯那样预先假定理性主义的问题已获解决；他把结构主义说成是一代人的Schwärmerei（狂热幻想）：他们迷恋科学模型，过于急切地想躲开萨特及其朋友带给知识生活的政治化或道德化（当然，这些人在这股热潮前后都曾尖声谴责结构主义是一种反对人文主义担当的小资产阶级意识形态）。在一场带着撤回旧说意味的全面修正中，1969年的《知识考古学》承认，它的作者曾过分强调话语或认识层面的共时性，而牺牲了人的能动性。1968年5月那些“事件”的影响，无疑已经被感受到了。1969年，福柯将结构主义贬为媒体炒作捏造的东西，一种年少的诱惑，一宗必须通过与自身不断对话才能驱除的罪过：

Like Existentialism, indeed, Structuralism was soon to turn into a mere fashion (it was a time when Paris was still the world capital specializing in the exportation of la nouvelle mode, both sartorially and intellectually), and this is what gave more edge and poignancy to rejections coming from hostile philosophers like Habermas whose ties with the Frankfurt school would predispose him unfavourably to any kind of ‘irrational scientism’, a strange oxymoron that captures the doubly negative view of German critics who attempted to classify Structuralism, or from early fellow-travellers of the movement turned debunkers, like Foucault. In a notoriously dismissive account of a school of thought he had been associated with in the press at least (he was one of the four participants in what was dubbed ‘‘the Structuralist banquet’’, along with Lacan, Lévi-Strauss, and Barthes), Foucault opposes the patience of true thinking to a purely modish enthusiasm. Taking up again Kant’s critical project, but without begging the issue of rationalism like Habermas, Foucault presents Structuralism as the Schwärmerei of a generation fascinated by scientific models, all too eager to shy away from the politicization or moralization of intellectual life brought about by Sartre and his friends (who, of course, had before and after the craze shrilly denounced Structuralism as a petty bourgeois ideology opposed to humanistic commitment). In a sweeping revision that smacks of recantation, The Archaeology of Knowledge acknowledges in 1969 that its author had unduly stressed discursive or epistemic synchronicity at the expense of human agency. The impact of the ‘events’ of May 1968 has no doubt been felt. In 1969, Foucault reduces Structuralism to a mere fabrication by mediatized hype, a juvenile temptation, a sin that has be exorcized through a relentless dialogue with himself:

……我并不想把结构主义的事业推进到它的正当界限之外。而你必须承认，我在《词与物》中一次也没有用过“结构”这个词。不过，让我们放下那些关于“结构主义”的论争吧；在认真工作的研究者如今已经离开的领域里，它们几乎无法存活；这场本可能成果丰硕的特定争论，如今只有哑剧演员和翻筋斗的艺人在表演了。3

. . . I did not want to carry the structuralist enterprise beyond its legitimate limits. And you must admit that I never once used the word ‘structure’ in The Order of Things. But let us leave our polemics about ‘structuralism’; they hardly survive in areas now deserted by serious workers; this particular controversy, which might have been so fruitful, is now acted out only by mimes and tumblers.3

人们或许不愿扮演哑剧演员和翻筋斗艺人的角色，尤其如今这场马戏表演已经结束了如此之久，即使仅仅是为了弄清福柯那对话式的结论如何不断扭曲转折、翻腾跳跃，以实现一个精心筹划的目标，即“使自己与结构主义脱离关系”。4在这场与一位advocatus diaboli（魔鬼的代言人）的对话中，对方一再回到结构主义这个棘手的问题；福柯承认在方法、策略、描述、分析、形式化、图式、主题、机制等方面有所汇合——简言之，就是与结构主义相关的整套技术程序——但拒绝赋予它哲学的地位。或者说，如果它是哲学，那也是一种肤浅的哲学，过于接近十九世纪末出现的、以对科学进步的天真信仰为特征的实证主义。福柯的考古学将试图迫使这套总体方法论交代其隐蔽的历史维度，使它揭示自身更广泛的关切和隐含的议程：

One may be wary of playing the part of a mime and tumbler, especially now that the circus show has been over for so long, even if it was only to grasp how Foucault’s dialogical conclusion multiplies contortions and somersaults with one calculated aim, that is to ‘dissociate himself from structuralism’.4 In this dialogue with an advocatus diaboli who keeps returning to the vexed issue of Structuralism, Foucault admits to a convergence of methods, strategies, descriptions, analyses, formalizations, schemata, themes, mechanisms – in short the whole array of technical procedures associated with Structuralism – but refuses to grant it the status of a philosophy. Or if philosophy it is, it is a shallow one, too close to a positivism marked by a naïve belief in scientific progress that emerged at the end of the nineteenth century. Foucault’s archaeology will try to force this global methodology to account for its hidden historical dimension, making it reveal its larger concerns and implicit agendas:

当然，我们不得不放弃所有那些曾把我们引向意识主权的话语。但是，过去半个世纪里失去的东西，我们希望在第二层次上重新取得，方式是分析那些分析，或者至少对它们进行根本的质询。我们将追问它们从何而来，它们正不自觉地走向什么历史目的地，是什么样的天真蒙蔽了它们，使它们看不到令它们得以成立的条件，又是什么样的形而上学围栏把它们初步的实证主义圈了起来。5

Of course, we have had to abandon all those discourses that once led us to the sovereignty of consciousness. But what we have lost over the last half-century, we are hoping to recover in the second degree, by means of the analysis of those analyses, or at least by the fundamental questioning that we apply to them. We will ask them where they came from, towards what historical destination they are moving without being aware of it, what naivety blinds them to the conditions that make them possible, and what metaphysical enclosure encloses their rudimentary positivism.5

对于所有把后结构主义与对起源和主体性概念的批判联系起来的人而言，福柯随后揭示的纲领或许会令人吃惊：

The disclosure of Foucault’s subsequent programme may come as a surprise for all those who associate post-Structuralism with a criticism of the concepts of origins and subjectivity:

正因如此，如果不管愿不愿意，我们都必须容忍所有这些结构主义，我们也决不会允许我们自己的思想史受到任何玷污；我们决不会允许那些自十九世纪以来一直把它同起源和主体性问题联系在一起的先验线索被拆散。6

That is why, if we must tolerate all those structuralisms, whether we like it or not, we will not allow any taint to that history of thought that is our own history; we will not allow the unraveling of those transcendental threads that have bound it since the nineteenth century to the problem of origin and subjectivity.6

我还会回到福柯的突然转变：1966年，他还是结构主义方法的拥护者，1969年却已成为高声抨击它的人。他诉诸一段更长的形而上学历史，提醒我们，对于一场如今已无法孤立理解的运动，我们需要一部可靠的历史；这也正是斯特罗克这部优雅而见闻广博的概述无比宝贵的原因：他的叙述的优点之一，就是关注隐蔽的汇合与更深层的演变。正如尼采坚持的那样，历史既需要评判，也需要以事件为取向。弗朗索瓦·多斯那部规模宏大的回顾，确实给我们提供了一部极为精确、详尽的编年史。7但正因为他是专业历史学家，而且还是法国人，他不大愿意像斯特罗克那样作出富于想象力的思想飞跃；后者在描绘一段更长的历史时，以综合性的重新归类代替了线性的历史。既然我不想在这里重复斯特罗克或多斯，我就提出两条轴线：先勾勒一段将整个二十世纪纳入考虑的哲学史，然后近乎短视地聚焦于一个特定年份——1966年。多斯把这一年视为转折点，但对于结构主义在美国和英语国家的接受而言，它的意义还远不止如此，我们需要更仔细地考察这一时刻。

I will return to Foucault’s sudden transformation from an advocate of Structuralist methods in 1966 to a very vocal detractor in 1969. His reliance on a longer metaphysical history reminds us of the need for a reliable history of a movement that we can no longer understand in isolation, which is why Sturrock’s elegant and informed survey proves invaluable: one of the strengths of his account is his attention to hidden convergences and deeper evolutions. History needs to be evaluative as well as event-oriented, as Nietzsche insisted. We do have a very precise and thorough chronicle in François Dosse’s monumental recapitulation.7 But because he is a professional historian, and French moreover, he is reluctant to make the kind of imaginative mental leap performed by Sturrock, who replaces linear history with synthetic regrouping while depicting a longer history. Since I do not wish to repeat either Sturrock or Dosse here, I will suggest two axes: I will sketch a philosophical history that takes the whole twentieth century into account, and then I will become almost myopic and focus on a particular year, 1966. This year is seen as a turning point by Dosse, but it is however more than relevant for the reception of Structuralism in America and the English-speaking countries, and we need to examine this moment with more attention.

本体论与认识论之间的结构主义Structuralism Between Ontology and Epistemology

斯特罗克只能从语言学入手，更确切地说，是从费尔迪南·德·索绪尔对语言学作出的开创性转变入手：他把语言学变成了关于日常使用的语言的系统理论，而不再像以前那样，将它看成纯粹的语文学，或者对单个词的历史变化所作的研究，有时也将词放回各自的词族中考察。不过，“未完成的纲领”这个观念，将我们引向作为可能基础的哲学；在这里，斯特罗克竟然相当详尽地请出了胡塞尔，这或许有些令人意外。大多数关于结构主义的叙述，往往把它描述为一切意识哲学的激烈敌人，因此也是现象学的敌人——现象学研究的是意识构成一个世界的方式。事实上，斯特罗克指出，胡塞尔对意识与世界之间各种交织方式的严密描述，与结构主义之间存在深刻的亲缘性，或者一种客观的联盟；这样做是十分一贯的。例如，在胡塞尔对数学的研究中，数学对象的“观念性”问题，将我们引向的不是一种纯粹的历史传统，而是那些神话般的最初发明者已凭直觉把握的一种“结构”（见第50页）。正如德里达在一篇极其敏锐的评论中将要指出的那样，8数学的客观性，如同一切科学的客观性，不能奠基于主体性；它也不能在纯粹的传统中找到，因为胡塞尔害怕落入历史主义或文化主义的深渊；解决办法可以在语言结构中找到，语言以单义陈述的形式保存这种观念性，以便将其代代传承。

Sturrock could not begin elsewhere than with linguistics, more precisely Ferdinand de Saussure’s groundbreaking transformation of linguistics into a systemic theory of language in its daily use, and not, as it used to be before, straight philolology, or the investigation of historical transformations of single words, at times replaced in their families. However, the idea of an ‘unfinished program’ sends us to philosophy as a possible foundation, and there it may come as a mild surprise that Husserl should be called up at some length by Sturrock. Most accounts of Structuralism tend to portray it as the radical enemy of any philosophy of consciousness, therefore of phenomenology, a study of the way in which consciousness constitutes a world. In fact, Sturrock is quite consistent when he points to a deep affinity or to an objective alliance between Husserl’s rigorous description of the varied inter-meshings of consciousness and the world and Structuralism. In Husserl’s investigation of mathematics, for instance, the issue of the ‘ideality’ of mathematical objects sends us not to a purely historical tradition but to a ‘structure’ already intuited by its mythical first inventors (see p. 50). As Derrida was to note in a wonderfully astute commentary,8 the objectivity of mathematics, like that of all sciences, cannot be grounded in subjectivity; it cannot be found in a pure tradition, since Husserl is afraid of falling into the pit of historicism or culturalism; it is in the structure of language, which preserves this ideality in the form of univocal statements so as to pass them on through generations, that a solution can be found.

必须把歧义“还原”为单义，这带来了认识论上的复杂问题；德里达在对现象学所作的严格而又充满同情理解的修订中考察了这些问题，尽管我在这里无法进入其分析的细节。只需指出：从胡塞尔的第一本书《算术哲学》（1891）到其身后出版的《经验与判断》（1938），在他关于知识与文化相互交织的论述中，“结构”一词不止一次出现在具有战略意义的节点上。胡塞尔试图在人类直观中为逻辑范畴奠基，他拒绝同狄尔泰的历史主义及其Geisteswissenschaften（精神科学）观念作任何妥协，因为他认为后者受到主体主义或社会学主义过多的沾染。因此，当人们努力严密思考事物或事件如何铭刻在意识之中时，“结构”便成为一个不可回避的术语；意识提出网格或模式，没有它们，事物或事件甚至都不会显现。为了摆脱仍黏附于其第一本书中的心理主义，胡塞尔很快就会强调：极其抽象的逻辑共相与语言所提供的语法范畴之间，有一种异乎寻常的结构对应关系。在其最后关于判断的综合论述中，他运用“结构”概念，由个别判断走向普遍者：“从对‘一般而言’的普遍内容的原初给予性所作的这一说明，可以明显看出，因而‘一般而言’的普遍存在，是一种更高的结构形式；它在其意义中包含了‘一般而言’的特殊者的观念，并将它提升为一种更高的形式。”9

The epistemological complications brought about by the necessary ‘reduction’ of ambiguity to univocity are examined in an exacting but empathic revision of phenomenology by Derrida, even if I cannot enter into the detail of his analysis here. Suffice it to note that the term ‘structure’ intervenes at more than one strategic point in Husserl’s account of the interwoven strands of knowledge and culture from his first book, The Philosophy of Arithmetic (1891) to the posthumously published Experience and Judgment (1938). Husserl’s attempt at founding logical categories within human intuition refuses any compromise with Dilthey’s historicism and his idea of Geisteswissenschaften as being too tainted with either subjectivism or sociologism. Structure was therefore a term that imposed itself in the effort to think rigorously about the way things or events get inscribed in a consciousness proposing grids or patterns without which they would not even appear. In order to get rid of the psychologism that still adheres to his first book, Husserl will soon insist on the uncanny structural correspondence between the very abstract logical universals and the grammatical categories provided by language. In his last synthesis on judgement, he uses the concept of ‘structure’ to move beyond particular judgements toward the universal: ‘From this exposition of the original givenness of a universal content ‘‘in general’’, it is evident that the universal being thus ‘‘in general’’ is a higher structural form which includes in its sense the idea of a particular ‘‘in general’’ and raises it to a higher form.’9

那么，对胡塞尔而言，以及“一般而言”，结构究竟是什么？最宽泛的定义是：结构是一种抽象的组织模型，包含一组要素以及它们的组合规则。即使这些要素的性质差异极大，重要的仍是整体的内在一致性。这些要素可以是雪花中的原子簇，可以是支撑美洲印第安部落妇女交换循环的图腾认同，也可以是在一首十四行诗中以对位方式展开的意象网络；在结构中突出的，是要素之间的关系比各要素的内在性质更重要。在胡塞尔的现象学中，如同在索绪尔的语言学中一样，个体的发生被包摄于系统的总体观念之下。发生过程的随机性和经验的混沌，已经被一种秩序的设定所取代，而这种秩序的观念性支撑着分类这一概念本身。

What is a structure, then, for Husserl, and ‘in general’? The broadest definition is that a structure is an abstract model of organization, including a set of elements and the law of their composition. Even when the nature of these elements varies considerably, what matters is the inner coherence of the whole. The elements may be atomic clusters in a snowflake, totemic identifications underpinning circuits of exchange of women in an Amerindian tribe, or a network of images playing in counterpoint through a sonnet; what stands out in a structure is that the relationships between the elements are more important that the intrinsic qualities of each element. In Husserlian phenomenology, as in Saussurean linguistics, the genesis of individualities is subsumed under the global idea of the system. Genetic randomness and empirical chaos have been replaced by the imposition of an order whose ideality underpins the very notion of classification.

跟随索绪尔与胡塞尔，我们可以放心地将这种结构的本体论地位置于括号之中，因为我们已经实行了现象学还原的epoché（悬搁），从而抵达形相本质；或者因为我们已经把实际语言话语的噪声，化约为组合与选择的向量或轴线。既然语言始终是人性的普遍构成部分，结构就可以悬置于客观和主观两极之间；可当我们意识到，迄今谈论的原来是“一般的存在”时，会发生什么？海德格尔对本体论差异的沉思正是从这里出发的，更确切地说，是从《存在与时间》这本仍受惠于现象学、却又对它作出根本颠覆的书出发。即使匆匆阅读，也会注意到海德格尔在短短几页内，便从考察“存在问题的形式结构［Struktur］”（第2节的标题）——其中考虑了那个最为普遍却并非如此“不言自明”的概念（存在）所引发的解释学循环——转向一个更具攻势的纲领：“解构［Destruktion］本体论历史的任务”（第6节的标题）。10这一破除性的纲领表述得十分明确：

With Saussure and Husserl we may safely leave the ontological status of such structures between brackets because we have practised the epoché of phenomenological reduction leading to eidetic essences, or because we have reduced the noise of actual linguistic utterances to vectors or axes of combination and selection. If structures can be left hovering between the objective and the subjective poles since language remains a universal constituent of human nature, what happens when we are made aware that we have been talking so far about ‘being in general’? This is where Heidegger’s meditation on ontological difference takes its point of departure, more precisely with a book that is still in debt to phenomenology but subverts it radically, Being and Time. Even a hurried reader will notice that Heidegger moves in a few pages from an investigation of the ‘Formal Structure [Struktur] of the Question of Being’ (the title of paragraph 2), which takes into account the hermeneutical circle generated by that most general but not so ‘self-evident’ concept (Being), to a more aggressive agenda, ‘The Task of a Destructuring [Destruktion] of the History of Ontology’ (title of paragraph 6).10 The destructive program is stated quite explicitly:

如果存在问题要弄清自身的历史，就必须松动僵化的传统，消解由它造成的遮蔽。我们把这一任务理解为：遵循存在问题的指引，对古代本体论的传统概念进行解构。11

If the question of being is to achieve clarity regarding its own history, a loosening of the sclerotic tradition and a dissolving of the concealments produced by it is necessary. We understand this task as the destructuring of the traditional concepts of ancient ontology which is to be carried out along the guidelines of the question of being.11

这里不是讨论海德格尔这部杰作的地方，我只把意见限于语义层面：每当他写下Destruktion时，译者都舍弃了更直接的对应词“破坏”（destruction），而偏爱“解构”（destructuration）。海德格尔也使用更具德语特色的Abbau（字面意思是“拆除建构”），但Destruktion在《存在与时间》中出现得相当频繁。12避开“破坏”是明智的选择，因为海德格尔坚持这种批判具有积极的一面，不能把它与相对主义式地揭穿陈腐的形而上学传统混为一谈。他的目标既是重新阅读一种至少从柏拉图、亚里士多德以来便陷入希腊“结构”的整个传统，也是通向一种现代主体性的现象学，这种现象学通过现实存在的人或Dasein来接近主体性；Dasein的字面意思是“在那里存在”，其词源本身多少已经包含、或预先假定了存在（Sein）的问题。因此，我们遇到了“结构”在策略上居于核心的用法：海德格尔会谈到“时间化结构”、“终结结构”和“先行结构”，它们都汇聚于更为复杂地交织在一起的“在世界之中存在的结构整体”。所以，如果正如斯特罗克正确推测的那样，真正的前结构主义者或原初结构主义者并不是德·索绪尔，而是胡塞尔，那么海德格尔便显现为第一位后结构主义者；而且，如同所有后结构主义者，他也需要结构概念，才能推进其建设性的破坏或“解构”。

This is not the place for a discussion of Heidegger’s masterpiece, and I will limit my remarks to a semantic level: whenever he writes Destruktion, the translator has rejected the more direct equivalent of ‘destruction’ and preferred ‘destructuration’. Heidegger also used the more German Abbau (literally ‘un-construction’) but Destruktion recurs quite often in Being and Time.12 It is a wise choice to avoid ‘destruction’, in so far as Heidegger insists upon the positive side of a critique which should not be confused with a relativist debunking of worn out metaphysical traditions. He aims both at rereading a whole tradition caught up in Greek ‘structures’ at least since Plato and Aristotle, and opening onto a phenomenology of modern subjectivity approached via the existing person or Dasein, literally a ‘being-there’ whose very etymological roots somehow contain or beg the issue of Being (Sein). Thus we encounter tactically central uses of ‘structure’: Heidegger will speak of a ‘temporalization-structure’, of ‘end-structures’ and ‘fore-structures’ that all converge in the more complexly intertwined ‘structural totality of being-in-the-world’. Thus, if as Sturrock correctly surmises, the real proto- or ur-Structuralist is not de Saussure but Husserl, Heidegger appears as the first post-Structuralist, and like all post-Structuralists, he too needs the concept of structure to proceed with his constructive destruction or ‘de-structuration’.

当德里达追随海德格尔那把此在视为本体论差异之体现的现象学，采取一种略有不同的批判转向时，这一点是否也适用于他？正如海德格尔若不先提出存在问题的Struktur，便无法推进其Destruktion一样，德里达若没有一种可用的语言差异概念，就无法在与形而上学抹除书写语言的做法的对抗中取得进展；这种语言依赖现实的或可能的书写，因此必须被视为揭示了技术的本质。德里达最初的哲学策略，是重新审视海德格尔对现象学的解构，让一种结构主义——胡塞尔现象学及其为科学、历史与文化相互关联的观念性提供严格基础的愿望——与另一种结构主义相对抗，即索绪尔关于不具有实体的关系、代码、系统和二元对立的语言学。他的修正姿态，是借助索绪尔来阅读胡塞尔，因此每当胡塞尔似乎对语言问题置若罔闻，或使用一种形而上学式的透明语言观时，就请索绪尔来提供这样一种语言观：语言是没有本体论分量的系统，完全由相互关系、因而由纯粹差异构成。反过来，每当结构主义似乎有意使自己看不到起源与目的论、事件与生产、构成与目的性意义等问题时，又会请胡塞尔来提供一种严格的哲学反模型。于是，结构主义被看成是在与结构性这一概念本身玩蒙眼捉迷藏。

Can this apply to Derrida when he takes a slightly different critical turn in the wake of Heidegger’s phenomenology of Dasein as embodying ontological difference? Just as Heidegger could not proceed with his Destruktion without having first posited the Struktur of the question of Being, Derrida could not progress in his confrontation with the metaphysical erasure of written language, a language that must be seen as revealing the essence of technology through its dependence on real or possible writing, if he did not have a workable concept of linguistic difference. Derrida’s initial philosophical strategy has consisted in revisiting Heidegger’s destructuration of phenomenology by opposing one kind of Structuralism – Husserlian phenomenology and its wish to provide a rigorous foundation for the linked idealities of science, history and culture – to another structuralism, Saussure’s linguistics of relations, codes, systems and binary oppositions deprived of substance. His revisionist gesture has been to read Husserl with Saussure, so that whenever Husserl appears deaf to the issue of language, or uses a metaphysically transparent view of language, it is Saussure who is called upon to provide a conception of language as a system devoid of ontological weight, as entirely made up of mutual relations, therefore of pure differences. Conversely, Husserl will be called upon to provide a rigorous philosophical countermodel whenever Structuralism seems to blind itself deliberately to issues of origins and teleology, of event and production, of constitution and purposive meaning. Structuralism is then seen as a game of blind man’s buff with the very notion of structurality.

列维-斯特劳斯在20世纪50年代提出的强硬结构主义观，似乎正是这种情形；在这种观点中，德里达辨认出了实证主义或科学主义的回归。这种科学主义的问题不在于它相信科学，而在于：以为结构不过是些拾得的对象，静静等候新的科学研究，因而可以拿它们来操作，这种姿态不可避免地会重复它自称要避免的形而上学迷误的历史。德里达的责难十分有力，而且不应局限于列维-斯特劳斯，而应质疑“结构之结构性”的历史——这是一段与形而上学同样古老的历史。将这一历史展开，本身就意味着重新思考一长串概念，如符号、形式、本质、历史、自然、真理、文化等等。或者更准确地说，德里达指出，结构主义人类学从未在纯粹经验的进路与反复批判经验主义之间作出选择。德里达使用列维-斯特劳斯用来描述其自身活动的术语bricolage（修补式拼装），以此在经验主义与教条主义之间作中介性思考，并严厉地告诫他：

This seemed to be the case with the hard view of Structuralism put forward by Lévi-Strauss in the 1950s, a view in which Derrida identifies a return to positivism or scientism. What is wrong with this scientism is not its faith in science, but that the gesture that believes it can work with structures as if they were just found objects lying in wait for a new scientific investigation will inevitably repeat the history of metaphysical delusions it pretends to avoid: Derrida’s strictures are forceful, and should not be limited to Lévi-Strauss, but put in question the history of the ‘structurality of structure’, a history as ancient as that of metaphysics. Its very deployment entails rethinking a long list of concepts such as sign, form, essence, history, nature, truth, culture and so on. Or to be more precise, Derrida points out that structuralist anthropology never chooses between a purely empirical approach and a recurrent critique of empiricism. Using the term chosen by Lévi-Strauss to describe his own activity, bricolage, as a way of meditating between empiricism and dogmatism, Derrida warns him sternly:

我想强调的只是：超越哲学，并不在于翻过哲学这一页（这通常等于把哲学做得很糟），而在于继续以某种方式阅读哲学家。……我说过，经验主义是威胁一种仍然自认为科学的话语的所有缺陷的母体，尤其在列维-斯特劳斯那里便是如此。如果我们想深入提出经验主义与修补式拼装的问题，我们很可能很快就会得出一些关于结构人类学中话语地位的、绝对相互矛盾的命题。13

What I want to emphasize is simply that the passage beyond philosophy does not consist in turning the page of philosophy (which usually amounts to philosophizing badly), but in continuing to read philosophers in a certain way. . . . I have said that empiricism is the matrix of all faults menacing a discourse which continues, as with Lévi-Strauss in particular, to consider itself scientific. If we wanted to pose the problem of empiricism and bricolage in depth, we would probably end up very quickly with a number of absolutely contradictory propositions concerning the status of discourse in structural anthropology.13

例如，德里达指出，列维-斯特劳斯所提出的亲属关系或创世神话的结构图式，只是作为假说被援引，使人类学家得以在令人困惑的人类实践多样性中引入某种秩序。经验的多样性很快便被包摄于一种由结构提供的认识层面的总体化之下；这些结构属于共相的范畴，或者是人类心灵的基础性构成部分，甚至延伸到无意识思维之中；最后，它们以极不稳固的方式在自然与文化之间稳定下来，成为自然与文化之间那一分界本身的名称。

For instance, Derrida notes that Lévi-Strauss’s structural schemata of kinship relations or creation myths are adduced as mere hypotheses allowing anthropologists to introduce some order into the baffling diversity of human practices. Empirical diversity is soon subsumed under an epistemic totalization provided by structures that fall under the category of universals, or foundational constituents of the human mind reaching even into unconscious thought, and finally stabilizing themselves precariously between nature and culture, as the name of the very divide between nature and culture.

当然，这不过是一场漫长而富有成果的讨论的开端，我无法在这里妥善概括它。讨论始于德里达同时宣告一种“事件”——它采取结构连续网络之内部断裂的形式——以及结构对自身的一种“复叠”。14许多评论者强调断裂这一“事件”，并把1968年5月的那些“事件”看成方便的分水岭，却忘了：即使德里达的姿态开启了后结构主义，那也至少是在1968年5月之前两年。我更倾向于强调第二个因素，即重复或复叠，并把结构性向自身批判性地返回或折叠，视为结构主义必不可少的批判性伴随物。无论如何，这一正面进攻或复叠式评论竟然是在中立地带发动的，或许自有其道理；这块地方也不完全算是“外国”领土，因为德里达和列维-斯特劳斯都已在美国度过一段时间，而一场关于人文科学的重要会议，提供了对结构主义进行总体讨论的机会。现在，我要试着更精确地分析这一历史性“事件”的背景。

This is of course only the beginning of a long and fruitful discussion that I could not sum up adequately here. It starts when Derrida announces both an ‘event’ taking the form of a rupture within the continuous web of structures and a ‘redoubling’ of the structure upon itself.14 Many commentators have stressed the ‘event’ of a rupture, and they have looked to the ‘events’ of May 1968 as a convenient watershed, forgetting that, even if Derrida’s gesture had started post-Structuralism, it would have done so two years at least before May 1968. I am inclined to stress the second element, namely repetition or redoubling, and see in the critical returning or folding of structurality upon itself a necessary critical accompaniment of Structuralism. At any rate, there might be reason for the fact that this frontal attack or redoubling commentary should have been launched on neutral ground, if not exactly ‘foreign’ territory, since both Derrida and Lévi-Strauss had already spent some time in the United States, when a major conference on the human sciences gave the opportunity for a general discussion of Structuralism. I will now try to analyse more precisely the context of this historical ‘event’.

巴尔的摩，1966年Baltimore 1966

正如弗朗索瓦·多斯在法国结构主义编年史中指出的那样，1966年标志着一个通向转折点的高潮；15在法国，两部理论畅销书——拉康的《文集》（Écrits）和福柯的《词与物》——的出版，似乎确证了这一运动在知识生活中的统治地位；在美国，1966年10月的约翰斯·霍普金斯会议则汇聚了巴特、拉康、德里达、戈德曼、韦尔南、托多罗夫和伊波利特，这群杰出的学者应邀向美国听众解释结构主义及其引发的不满。根据这次会议记录先后编辑的两种版本，在书名交叉互换时显露出一种奇特的暧昧性。我们从得意洋洋、甚至以夸张方式叠用复数的《批评的语言与人的科学》（The Languages of Criticism and the Sciences of Man，其最后一个带法语色彩的短语直译了les sciences humaines），转向更受具体语境限制、更为谨慎的单数书名《结构主义之争》（The Structuralist Controversy）。16那个看似无辜的调换正副标题的决定，激发了新的追问，也将场景打开，通向一种更公开的论争。1971年撰写的新序言，明确说出了在1966年尚难以察觉、美国公众势必会忽略的一点：这些法国哲学家或批评家之间，并无意识形态契约，也没有共同的理论立场。一种青春期式的达达主义突然涌现，并铭刻在1968年5月的口号和海报上，标志着“结构主义之死”。结构主义被认为与权威和知识的话语同流合污，而学生和激进分子则主张使全部能量瞬间燃烧起来，无论把这理解为一场喧闹的大狂欢，还是一场总罢工。结构（因此也包括结构主义者）被谴责为拒绝加入民众、在街头起舞。萨特似乎战胜了福柯——直到几年后，两人的道路再度交会，当时抵抗的激进政治选择了更为尖锐、范围更为有限的议题，如囚犯和流离失所的少数群体。在学生“抗争”的高潮中，为全部符号和代码创建一种mathesis universalis（普遍数学）的雄心，仿佛漂浮在极其遥远的柏拉图式理念天空中，由那些迂腐的思想官僚操纵。弗朗索瓦·瓦尔为广受欢迎的文集《什么是结构主义？》所写的“导论”，即使不算哀伤，也极其谨慎，我们可以在其中看到这种情况的痕迹；该文集主要考察语言学、诗学、人类学、精神分析和哲学，而它于1968年冬季出版，也具有症候意义。17相应地，约翰斯·霍普金斯会议论文集1970年的序言指出，一种危险在于“把一种方法或一个‘方法家族’扭曲成一种教条”。18

As François Dosse notes in his chronicle of French Structuralism, the year 1966 marked a climax leading to a turning point;15 in France, the publication of two theoretical best-sellers, Lacan’s Ecrits and Foucault’s The Order of Things, seemed to confirm the dominance of the movement in intellectual life, while in the United States the Johns Hopkins conference of October 1966 gathered Barthes, Lacan, Derrida, Goldmann, Vernant, Todorov and Hyppolite, a group of distinguished scholars who were invited to explain Structuralism and its discontents to an American audience. The two volumes successively edited from these proceedings evince a curious ambivalence in the chiasmic exchange of their titles. We move from the triumphant, even bombastically redoubled plural, The Languages of Criticism and the Sciences of Man whose last gallic phrase literalizes les sciences humaines, to the more context-bound and cautious singular of The Structuralist Controversy.16 The apparently innocent decision to inverse title and subtitle incites fresh questionings while opening the scene onto a more public debate. The new preface written in 1971 spells out what was barely palpable in 1966, and could only be missed by the American public, namely the lack of an ideological pact or a common theoretical position among the French philosophers or critics. The irruption of an adolescent dadaism that found an inscription in the slogans and posters of May 1968 had marked the ‘death of Structuralism’. Structuralism was perceived as compromised with the discourse of authority and knowledge, while the students and radicals were advocating an instantaneous combustion of all energies, whether conceived as a big riotous party or a general strike. Structures (and the Structuralists therefore) had been denounced as refusing to join the people and dance in the streets. Sartre had won against Foucault, it seemed – until their paths crossed again a few years later, when radical politics of resistance opted for more trenchant and limited issues like prisoners and displaced minorities. At the climax of the students’ ‘contestation’, the ambition of creating a mathesis universalis of all signs and codes seemed to float very far in the Platonic sky of ideas manipulated by stuffy mandarins. One can see traces of this in the highly cautious if not mournful ‘Introduction’ written by François Wahl to the very popular collection called What Is Structuralism? – a collection in which the main disciplines examined are linguistics, poetics, anthropology, psychoanalysis and philosophy – and which was symptomatically published in the Winter of 1968.17 According, the 1970 preface to the Johns Hopkins conference points out that one danger has consisted in ‘deforming a method or a ‘‘family of methods’’ into a doctrine’.18

因此，到了1971年，提醒人们德里达、福柯或德勒兹等新崛起的明星已经拒斥结构主义，或已使它遭遇严重危机，似乎成了一件迫切的事。新序言引用了德勒兹的话，他以福柯为出发点，突出了与结构主义相关的共同特征：“冷静而协同地摧毁主体，强烈厌恶起源、失落的起源、重新找回的起源等观念，拆除意识那些统一化的伪综合，揭露所有以意识的进步和理性的展开之名进行的历史神秘化……”19照这种观点，结构主义无非是对萨特式辩证法的否定，是对任何类型黑格尔主义的拒斥。若果真如此，那么列维-斯特劳斯与福柯之间就没有真正的区别，编辑们似乎也正是这样认为的。事实上，福柯没有参加1966年的会议，尽管人们时而提及他。知识考古学的谱系学计划可以被看作结构主义式的，至少直到《词与物》为止：该书勾画了三个主要“知识型”，用来解释近代早期以来知识和心态的演变，却未解释它们之间的转变。在书的结尾，福柯把最后一种知识型描绘为受新的“人文科学”支配，但又提出，对这些科学而言，“人”与其说是主体，不如说是一个正在消逝的对象。然而，到了1971年，已不可能再忽略随《知识考古学》公开发表的、我在前文引述过的那些责难。不过，福柯仍然承认，在其“正当界限”之内，结构主义的事业并非全然徒劳。20

This is why, in 1971, it seemed urgent to recall that Structuralism had been rejected or seriously endangered by emerging stars like Derrida, Foucault or Deleuze. The new preface quotes Deleuze, who takes Foucault as a point of departure to highlight common features associated with Structuralism: ‘A cold and concerted destruction of the subject, a lively distaste for notions of origins, lost origins, recovered origins, a dismantling of unifying pseudo-syntheses of consciousness, a denunciation of all the mystifications of history performed in the name of the progress of consciousness and the unfolding of reason . . . ’.19 On this view, Structuralism would be nothing more than the negation of Sartrian dialectics, a repudiation of any type of Hegelianism. If this is so, there is no real difference between Lévi-Strauss and Foucault, as the editors seem to assume. Indeed, Foucault had been absent from the 1966 conference, even if mentioned once in a while. The genealogical project of an intellectual archaeology could be seen as Structuralist, at least until The Order of Things, which mapped three main ‘epistemes’ explaining the evolution of knowledge and mentalities since the early modern times without accounting for their transfomations. Foucault concluded his book by presenting the last of these epistemes as dominated by the new ‘human sciences’ but suggested that ‘man’ was less a subject than a vanishing object for them. In 1971, however, it was impossible to miss the strictures publicized with The Archaeology of Knowledge that I have quoted earlier. Foucault nevertheless acknowledged that within its ‘legitimate limits’ the Structuralist enterprise had not been totally vain.20

福柯精心措辞的赞许及其保留意见，值得更完整地引述：

Foucault’s carefully articulated praise as well as his reservations are worth quoting more fully:

因为问题并不关乎结构主义。当然，我承认它那些洞见的价值：在分析一种语言、神话体系、民间故事、诗歌、梦、文学作品，甚至或许是电影时，结构描述能揭示若非如此便无法单独辨认的关系；它使我们能够界定反复出现的要素，以及它们的对立形式和个体化标准；它也使我们能够确定构造规律、等价关系和转换规则。尽管起初我有一些保留意见，现在我已经毫无困难地接受：人的语言、他的无意识及其想象，受到结构规律的支配。21

For the problem does not concern structuralism. I recognize the value of its insights of course: when it is a question of analyzing a language, mythologies, folk-tales, poems, dreams, works of literature, even films perhaps, structural description reveals relations that could not otherwise be isolated; it makes it possible to define recurrent elements, with their forms of opposition, and their criteria of individuation; it also makes it possible to lay down the laws of construction, equivalences, and rules of transformation. And despite a number of reservations that I had at the beginning, I now have no difficulty in accepting that man’s languages, his unconscious, and his imagination are governed by laws of structure.21

我认为，这个嵌在更严厉的保留意见中的、相当保守的评价，很好地界定了今天作为结构主义不容置疑的遗产而留存下来的东西。1969年的福柯所不能接受的是：把话语（他偏爱的、更技术性的术语是“话语形成”）当作自在的东西来分析或“解码”，完全不考虑一种具有“计划”和目的论的生产活动；正如我们所见，他既诉诸人类主体性的历史，也诉诸对支撑主体性的各种政治统治形式的重新认识。福柯承认一种科学描述方法的有效性，甚至接受这种方法本身必需的化约（它确实与现象学还原极其不同），但提出，其“考古学”的任务，是对起源、能动性和价值问题开展一种更为根本的研究。

I believe that this rather conservative estimate lodged in the middle of harsher reservations defines quite well what has remained today as the indubitable legacy of Structuralism. What is unacceptable for Foucault in 1969 is that discourses (‘discursive formations’ is the more technical term he prefers) should be analysed or ‘decoded’ as such, without any consideration of a producing activity endowed with a ‘project’ and a teleology: as we have seen, he calls upon as much a history of human subjectivity as a renewed awareness of the types of political domination underpinning it. Foucault accepts the validity of a scientific method of description and he even accepts its own necessary reduction (very different, indeed, from phenomenological reduction), but proposes a more fundamental investigation into issues of origin, agency and value as the task of his ‘archaeology’.

福柯列举的学科很能说明问题，因为它们与约翰斯·霍普金斯会议上代表的学科相对应：人类学、历史学、古典学、比较文学、比较宗教学、语言学、文学批评、哲学、心理学、符号学和社会学都在其中。结构主义者的愿望，是把所有这些话语统一起来，往往是以最简单、最有力的一门作为模型——结构语言学。因此，巴尔的摩会议论文集1971年的序言，将对结构主义日益增长的不满，归因于针对一种此前被认为具有奠基作用的模型的批判，即结构语言学提供的普遍科学范式。麦克西和多纳托将语言学在方法论上的重要性下降，与此前占主导地位的黑格尔主义的急剧退潮联系起来。22在20世纪70年代之交，黑格尔或胡塞尔式的现象学，似乎已让位于一种在福柯、德里达和德勒兹那里相当醒目的尼采主义；不过，这种看法简化了某些问题，因为至少在德里达那里，尼采常常掩盖着、也包含着黑格尔。

The roll-call of disciplines named by Foucault is revealing since it corresponds with the disciplines represented at the Johns Hopkins conference: anthropology, history, classical studies, comparative literature, comparative religions, linguistics, literary criticism, philosophy, psychology, semiology and sociology are all present. The desire of the Structuralists had been to unify all these discourses often by taking the simplest and the strongest as a model – structural linguistics. Thus the 1971 Preface to the Baltimore conference blames the growing dissatisfaction with Structuralism on the critique levelled at a model hitherto considered foundational, the universal scientific paradigm provided by structural linguistics. The declining methodological importance of linguistics is linked by Macksey and Donato to a sharp regression in a hitherto dominant Hegelianism.22 At the turn of the 1970s, Hegelian or Husserlian phenomenology seemed to have given way to a Nietzscheism quite conspicuous in Foucault, Derrida and Deleuze; however, this view simplifies certain issues, since in Derrida’s case at least, Nietzsche often conceals and contains Hegel.

此外，若不带成见地审视1966年巴尔的摩会议记录，就会发现其中显露的某些最具启发性的张力和断裂线，并不遵循“方法论”话语与“形而上学”话语之间那种宽泛的意识形态区分。事实上，从一开始被推到前台的科学是数学，而远非主要是语言学，或被看成普遍符号理论的广义符号学。历史学家夏尔·莫拉泽把发言的重点放在数学发明与文学发明的区别上。23这令人惊讶地预示了20世纪90年代的索卡尔论争，24而拉康正是在这里接过话头：莫拉泽曾把“负一的平方根”25用作非理性符号的例子；它提供了一个能解决特定逻辑问题的恰当公式；这个“算法”究竟是“发明了一个新符号、一个新算法，还是对某些逻辑系统作了不同的组织”？26拉康和巴尔的摩会议其他参与者坚持的，与其说是把符号学当作一切科学之科学、当作提供可重复理性模型的学问所具有的普遍有效性，不如说是构建能指系统：在这些系统的语境中，主体的角色和功能可以被“计算”，也就是可以脱离其人文主义或形而上学意味而予以分析。

Moreover, if one examines the proceedings of the 1966 Baltimore conference without preconception, one can observe that some of the most revealing tensions and fault lines displayed there do not follow the broad ideological division between ‘methodological’ and ‘metaphysical’ discourses. In fact, the science brought to the fore from the start was mathematics, much more than linguistics or a generalized semiology seen as a universal theory of signs. The historian Charles Morazé focuses his presentation on the difference between mathematical and literary invention.23 In a fascinating anticipation of the Sokal debate of the 1990s,24 Lacan takes his cue there: Morazé had used the example of ‘the root of minus one’25 as an irrational symbol; this provides an adequate formula to solve specific logical problems; is this ‘algorithm’ the ‘invention of a new sign or of a new algorithm or a different organization of some logical systems?’26 What Lacan and other participants in the Baltimore conference insist upon is less the universal validity of semiotics taken as the science of all sciences providing a repeatable model of rationality, than the construction of signifying systems in the context of which the role and function of the subject can be ‘calculated’, which means analysed without its humanistic or metaphysical overtones.

因此，主体的性质是可计算的还是不可计算的，在某些讨论中依然是一条关键的分界线，尤其在拉康与德里达之间。27拉康的发言一如既往地炫示“结构”一词，却没有忘记结构必须通向一种新的主体性概念，这一点从那相当难懂的标题就可见一斑：《论结构作为任何主体之先决条件的他异性之混入》。在这篇密集的文本中，拉康多次暗指弗雷格和罗素的逻辑理论，并重新讨论其学说的一些核心主张：他解释说，无意识“像语言一样被结构化”这个观念是同义反复，因为“被结构化”与“像语言一样”完全同义。28更隐晦的是，拉康补充说，能指所能做的一切，就是代表一个主体，但并非如人们预期的那样为另一个主体，而是为另一个能指代表它。29这一格言支撑着许多结构主义文学批评家反复进行的指涉幻觉批判，其中最激进的是迈克尔·里法泰尔。追随俄国形式主义者，里法泰尔及其结构主义同道主张，文学语言通向“文学性”（literality），也就是文学的空间；它依照自身的规律，而非外部现实的规律来组织。拉康“一个能指为另一个能指代表一个主体”的口号还表明，语言在不透明的主体之间作间接的中介。如果我们不必为语言与世界中对象之间那种不大可能的一一对应而烦心，那么从研究受转喻和隐喻支配的诗性语言中获得的教益，就可以应用于弗洛伊德的无意识。每当我做梦时，我就成了一个超现实主义诗人和一个结构主义民族志学者；日常生活中那些意味深长的失误动作，可以提醒我：如果正如维特根斯坦所说，我的语言划定了我的世界，那么，这种语言早已被大他者的话语占据了，而且这占据令人颇不自在。

The calculable or incalculable nature of the subject remains therefore a crucial divide in some discussions, especially between Lacan and Derrida.27 Lacan’s presentation typically parades the term ‘structure’ but without forgetting how it has to reach a new concept of subjectivity right from its rather impenetrable title: ‘Of Structure as an Inmixing of Otherness Prerequisite to Any Subject Whatever’. In this dense text, Lacan makes numerous allusions to Frege’s and Russell’s logical theories and revisits some key tenets of his doctrine: he explains that the idea that the unconscious is ‘structured as a language’ is a tautology because ‘structured’ and ‘as a language’ are entirely synonymous.28 More cryptically, Lacan adds that all a signifier can do is represent a subject, not for another subject as one would expect, but for another signifier.29 This motto underpins much of the recurrent critique of the referential illusion one finds in many Structuralist literary critics, among whom Michael Riffaterre is the most radical. Following the lead of the Russian Formalists, Riffaterre and fellow Structuralists have contend that literary language opens up to ‘literality’, that is, the space of literature that is structured according to its own laws and not those of exterior reality. Lacan’s slogan of ‘A signifier represents a subject for another signifier’ suggests moreover that language mediates obliquely between non-transparent subjects. If one should not bother about an improbable one-to-one correspondence with objects in the world, the lessons derived from a study of poetic language governed by metonymy and metaphor can be applied to the Freudian unconscious. Whenever I dream, I become a Surrealist poet and a Structuralist ethnographer, and the felicitous parapraxes of everyday life can serve to remind me that if my language circumscribes my world, as Wittgenstein would have it, this language is already inhabited, and rather uncomfortably so, by the discourse of the Other.

我们应当在这一广阔背景下，理解拉康派频繁而独特地使用“能指”一词的方式。因此，回到巴尔的摩，重要的是要认识到：罗索拉托在解释和扩展拉康的概念时，强调的并不是索绪尔的语言学理论，而是本韦尼斯特的理论；后者也是一位曾被称为“后结构主义者”的语言理论家。罗索拉托提及雅各布森的“转换语”，并将其与“言说行为的主体”和“所言内容的主体”这一对项联系起来。与罗索拉托一样，托多罗夫和巴特在整个会议中也大量引用本韦尼斯特。他们在一个关键点上意见一致：按照1966年的认识，语言学的新任务是描述言说行为的形式装置，也就是让一个人能够说“我”或写下“我”的那套编码机制；这些术语早已由拉康于1964年在关于《精神分析的四个基本概念》的研讨班中系统引入。的确，拉康、罗索拉托与巴特之间呈现出惊人的趋同，因为他们都希望将主体性纳入考虑，同时又把它限制在一种形式装置之中：这种装置使被化约为语言学之“我”的自我得以表达。他们共同参照的理论，都围绕言说行为问题展开；这一语言学领域最初由某些特定表达构成，在这些表达中，主体性、情态和时态通过语言被生产出来。奇怪的是，英美评论者大体忽略了这种发展，他们很少关注本韦尼斯特，认为他还不够结构主义。他们过于急切地想展示索绪尔的二元定义——如共时／历时、语言系统／言语或能指／所指的二分——所派生的理论模型的局限。然而，1966年向美国公众呈现的结构主义，在认识论上更为复杂、精细而多样。它那仍然占主导的对语言学的诉求，并未被化约为二元模式，而是包含了对言说行为、主体性以及更细微的科学性方式或节点的认识。

It is in this broad context that one should understand the frequent and idiosyncratic use of the term ‘signifier’ among Lacanians. Thus, to return to Baltimore, it is important to realize that when Rosolato explains and expands Lacan’s concepts, he insists on the linguistic theories not of Saussure but of Benveniste, another theoretician of language who has been called a ‘post-Structuralist’. Rosolato alludes to Jakobson’s ‘shifters’ and links them to the dyad of ‘the subject of the enunciation’ and the ‘subject of the enounced’. Like Rosolato, Todorov and Barthes also quote Benveniste liberally throughout the conference. They all agree crucially that the new task of linguistics, as perceived in 1966, is to describe the formal apparatus of enunciation, that is, the set of coded devices allowing a person to say ‘I’ or write ‘I’, terms already introduced systematically by Lacan in 1964 at the occasion of his seminar on the Four Fundamental Concepts of Psycho-Analysis. Indeed, one observes a striking convergence between Lacan, Rosolato and Barthes in so far as they wish to take subjectivity into account while restricting it to a formal apparatus enabling the utterance of the self reduced to the linguistic ‘I’. The theories to which they all refer hinge around the question of enunciation, a linguistic domain originally constituted by the specific expressions in which subjectivity, modality and tenses are produced through language. Curiously, such a development was largely ignored by Anglo-Saxon commentators, who paid little attention to Benveniste who was found to be not Structuralist enough. They were all too eager to show the limitations of the theoretical model derived from Saussure’s binary definitions, like synchrony/diachrony, langue/parole or the dichotomy signifier/signified. However, the Structuralism presented in 1966 to the American public was more complex, sophisticated and diverse in its epistemology. Its still dominant appeal to linguistics was not reduced to binary patterns but entailed an awareness of enunciation, subjectivity and subtler modes or nodes of scientificity.

巴特分析了“我死了”这个悖论式陈述之后，德里达提出了一个类似的问题。德里达先提到爱伦·坡的哥特式故事《瓦尔德马先生案例的真相》，随后质疑巴特符号学的语言学基础。接着，他证明，支撑巴特符号学分析的本韦尼斯特的二元组——discours（话语）与récit（叙事）——会转移，却无法解决索绪尔能指理论遗留的张力或矛盾。“话语”以主观的言说行为为标志，而更具历史性的“叙事”则会抑制主体性标记。德里达遵循自己对胡塞尔的研究，又回到索绪尔，拒绝了这种区分。对他而言，既不存在作为时态的“纯粹现在”，也不存在“纯粹在场”的本体论：事实上，历史时间总已隐含在言说行为的时间之中。30一种与机器或“死的”机制更为接近、而非与自发流淌的生命接近的书写形式，早已在任何第一人称话语中发挥作用。每当我想构成我的主体性，然后从自己的言语中“缺席”，我就需要重复“我”；任何个人真实的经验，都不能免于原初重复。“你不大愿意说‘我死了’。我相信，一个真正的语言行为的条件，就是我能够说‘我死了’。”31

A similar issue was brought up by Derrida after Barthes presented an analysis of the paradoxical statement: ‘I am dead’. Derrida referred to Poe’s gothic tale ‘The Facts in the case of M. Valdemar’ before questioning the linguistic foundation of Barthes’s semiotics. He then demonstrated that what had underpinned Barthes’s semiotical analysis, Benvensite’s binary couple of discours and récit would displace, but not solve, tensions or contradictions left by Saussure’s theory of the signifier. ‘Discourse’ would be marked by subjective enunciation, while a more historical ‘narrative’ would suppress subjective markers. Following his own investigations of Husserl and also going back to Saussure, Derrida refused such a distinction. For him, there is no more a ‘pure present’ as tense than an ontology of ‘pure presence’: in fact, historical time is always implied in the time of enunciation.30 A form of writing closer to machines or ‘dead’ mechanisms than to spontaneous and flowing life is already at play in any first person discourse. I need to repeat ‘I’ whenever I wish to constitute my subjectivity and then ‘absent’ myself from my speech; no experience of the personally authentic is immune to primary repetition. ‘You were reticent about saying ‘‘I am dead.’’ I believe that the condition for a true act of language is my being able to say ‘‘I am dead.’’’31

如同他的年长同事、黑格尔专家让·伊波利特一样，德里达质疑把“言语契约”概念应用于书写的做法。32德里达和伊波利特在一种黑格尔化的弗洛伊德主义中联合起来：在其中，死亡不可挽回，却产生了哀悼的工作；没有这种工作，就不会有伦理学或美学。最后可以说，结构主义的“实定性”往往抹除了这种哀悼的工作；“实定性”是一个黑格尔的术语，并不等同于“实证主义”，而是强调对主观或客观可能性条件的某种忽视。德里达在这一批判或元批判时刻所作的独特转折，是通过增殖不可判定的“准先验者”，使可能性条件与不可能性条件重合。因此，不可判定性依然是结构主义未曾妥善处理的核心哲学问题，是我前面所说的蒙眼捉迷藏中无法根除的盲点。

Like his older colleague, Jean Hyppolite, a specialist on Hegel, Derrida questions the concept of a ‘pact of speech’ applied to writing.32 Derrida and Hyppolite are united in a Hegelianized Freudianism in which death is unredeemable, but generate the work of mourning without which there would be no ethics or aesthetics. One may say in conclusion that it is this work of mourning that tends to be obliterated by the ‘positivity’ of Structuralism, a Hegelian term not equivalent with ‘positivism’ but stressing a certain disregard for subjective or objective conditions of possibility. Derrida’s particular spin on this critical or meta-critical moment has been to collapse the conditions of possibility and the conditions of impossibility by multiplying undecidable ‘quasi-transcendentals’. Thus undecidability remains the central philosophical problem that has not been treated adequately by Structuralism, the ineradicable blind spot in what I called earlier its blind man’s buff.

为了解释1971年之后那看似突兀、几乎追逐时尚的急转弯，我提到了1968年5月引发的冲击波，但提及俄国后形式主义对巴特、甚至德里达等理论家的影响，或许更切题。1965年，朱莉娅·克里斯蒂娃从祖国保加利亚来到巴黎时，用弗朗索瓦·多斯那句贴切的话来说，她“孕育了第二个巴特”。33这就解释了巴特为什么能够写出《S/Z》，以宏大的综合汇集他对巴尔扎克故事《萨拉辛》的阐发：这部书既是从代码角度进行结构主义阅读的汇编，又将所有这些代码一一揭穿，直到它们在无法统摄的多样性中爆炸、粉碎。这将进一步证实，后结构主义往往与结构主义共谋。同时，这也表明，许多领域已经永久吸纳了盛期结构主义提出的术语，却未必接受了它的科学主义意识形态——文学批评就是如此。这一点或许在任何地方都不如在精神分析、特别是拉康派的精神分析中明显，后者在临床中系统地运用结构概念。若埃尔·多尔关于性倒错的优雅著作，提供了一个很好的例子，说明精神分析师必须在呈现出来的症状与底层结构之间作出关键区分：他十分清楚地展示了某位患者的症状如何可能被当成抑郁症症状，但唯有把它们置于严格的概念框架中理解，并视为由被称为癔症的欲望结构所决定，它们才说得通。34在这个意义上，当癔症、偏执狂、性倒错与强迫性神经症仅仅被视为“结构”时，它们便失去了道德训诫式的意味，并可用于重新理解人类主体性。

To account for what looks like a brutal and almost faddish about-turn after 1971, I have mentioned the shock-waves generated by May 1968, but it might be more relevant to mention the impact of Russian post-Formalism on theoretician like Barthes and even Derrida. When Julia Kristeva arrived from her native Bulgaria to Paris in 1965, she ‘gave birth to the second Barthes’, to quote François Dosse’s felicitous phrase.33 This explains why Barthes could write S/Z as a monumental synthesis of his elaborations on Balzac’s tale ‘Sarrazine’ that both plays the role of a compendium of Structuralist readings in terms of codes and performs a debunking of all these codes until they explode and are shattered in their unaccountable multiplicity. This would be another confirmation that post-Structuralism is often complicit with Structuralism. At the same time, this suggests that many domains have permanently incorporated the terminology put forward by high Structuralism without necessarily having accepted its scientist ideology – this would be true of literary criticism. This is perhaps nowhere more visible than in psychoanalysis, especially of the Lacanian tendency, where the concept of structure is used systematically in the clinic. Joel Dor’s elegant book on perversion offers a good example of the crucial distinction a psychoanalyst needs to make between presenting symptoms and underlying structure: he shows very clearly how one patient’s symptoms could be thought to be those of depression but only made sense once they were understood within a rigorous conceptual framework, and seen as determined by the structure of desire known as hysteria.34 In that sense, hysteria, paranoia, perversion and obsessional neurosis lose their moralizing overtones and can be brought to bear on a renewed appreciation of human subjectivity when they are considered as just ‘structures’.

另一个视角，与其说可以由文学批评提供，不如说可以由文学本身提供。一些创造性的作家声称自己拥护某种结构主义观念，其中维托尔德·贡布罗维奇或许表态最为响亮。贡布罗维奇先在波兰，后在阿根廷，再后来在法国，通过揭穿一种陈旧荒谬的文化强加的“形式”，并赞颂一种以不成熟之名炫示其巴洛克式过度的无政府主义自由，展开了一段复杂而辉煌的文学事业。1967年，贡布罗维奇既能宣称“［他］是第一个结构主义者”，也能宣称他是第一个批判结构主义的人。35事实上，可以说，结构主义与后结构主义对立的理论论争，早已在英国、爱尔兰和美国现代主义的文学舞台上上演过。或者更准确地说，结构主义仿佛与所谓“晚期现代主义”是同时代的。乔伊斯的《芬尼根守灵夜》很容易被描述为第一部结构主义小说：它既是一部汇集全部宗教和民族传统的口头史诗，也是一个网格的展开，一种重复的结构模式，以一个原型家庭作为便利的爱尔兰基础，来重述普遍历史。列维-斯特劳斯在20世纪30年代末开始研究美洲印第安人的亲属结构时，是否读过乔伊斯，我们并不清楚；但乔伊斯当然读过列维-布留尔和维科，并设法把他们关于社会生活、制度和语言基础的洞见结合了起来。随后出现了贝克特，以及他对逻辑穷尽的执迷：《瓦特》审视了乔伊斯式的死胡同，并将这种文学结构主义推进得更远，走向对其互文、语言和认识论局限的系统探索。

Another angle may be provided less by literary criticism than by literature itself. A number of creative writers have claimed their allegiance to some conception of Structuralism, among whom Witold Gombrowicz has perhaps been the most vocal. First in Poland, then in Argentina, later in France, Gombrowicz developed a complex and brilliant literary career by debunking the ‘forms’ imposed by an older and absurd culture, and by praising an anarchic freedom that flaunts its baroque excess in the name of immaturity. In 1967, Gombrowicz could assert that ‘[he] was the first structuralist’ but also that he had been the first to criticize Structuralism.35 In fact, one could say that the theoretical debate opposing Structuralism and post-Structuralism had already been played out on the literary scene of British, Irish and American Modernism. Or to be more precise, it looks as if Structuralism had been contemporary with what is called ‘late modernism’. Joyce’s Finnegans Wake could easily be described as the first Structuralist novel: it is both an oral epic summing up all religious and national traditions and the unfolding of a grid, a structural pattern of repetition that takes one archetypal family as a convenient Irish basis for a retelling of universal history. It is not clear whether Lévi-Strauss had read Joyce when he started working on the structure of Amerindian kinship in the late 1930s, but Joyce had read Lévy-Bruhl and Vico of course, and he managed somehow to combine their insights into the foundations of social life, institutions and language. Then came Beckett and his obsessive concern with logical exhaustion: Watt takes stock of the Joycean dead-end and pushes this literary Structuralism further, toward a systematic exploration of its intertextual, linguistic and epistemological limits.

这些匆匆点出的线索，无非旨在表明结构主义尚未被穷尽。这种无处不在的思考风格，过于频繁地被化约为一种由对语言学的误入歧途的迷恋所支撑的官僚式科学主义。我的论点是，这种接近文本与社会（而且往往是作为文本的社会）的方式，并没有在20世纪70年代消亡。结构主义应当被重新评价，并呈现在一个更全面的背景中，将从人类学到符号学、哲学、精神分析、语言学、历史学的各种话语联结起来。它们都归入“人文科学”这个名目之下，实际上涵盖了詹巴蒂斯塔·维科谈论“新科学”时所想到的整个范围：他把这种科学界定为关于“人的技艺”的科学，以区别于笛卡尔式的真理和神一般的确定性。一种扩展并修订了的结构主义，其未完成的事业应当能够给结构、代码和系统引入更多动力与辩证法。它也应当考虑心态的历史，以及现象学与意义相互作用的整套方式，同时承认无意识发挥的根本作用。一旦我们承认结构是无意识的——甚至福柯在宣布放弃早先的归属之后也承认这一点——一旦我们瞥见这些结构如何为家庭、语言、文学、经济或知识系统提供更深的理性，那么，无论愿不愿意，我们就都已经成为结构主义者了。

These rapid pointers aim at nothing more than suggesting that Structuralism has not yet been exhausted. This pervasive style of thinking has too often been reduced to bureaucratic scientism underpinned by a misguided fascination with linguistics. It is my contention that this way of approaching texts and society (and quite often society as text) did not die out in the 1970s. Structuralism should be reappraised and presented in a more global context uniting various discourses, from anthropology to semiotics, philosophy, psychoanalysis, linguistics, history. They all come under the heading of the ‘human sciences’, in fact, the whole spectrum of what Giambattista Vico had in mind when he spoke of a ‘New Science’, a science that he defined as the science of ‘man’s arts’ in contradistinction to Cartesian truths and godlike certainties. The unfinished project of an expanded and revised Structuralism should be able to introduce more dynamics and dialectics to structures, codes and systems. It should also take into account histories of mentalities, the whole array of phenomenological interactions with meaning, while acknowledging the fundamental role played by the unconscious. Once we admit that structures are unconscious, as even Foucault granted it after he had abjured his earlier affiliation, once we glimpse how these structures provide a deeper rationality to familial, linguistic, literary, economic or epistemic systems, then we all have become Structuralists, whether we want it or not.

第二版导论Introduction to the Second Edition

自这部尝试为结构主义作简要界定的书于1986年首次出版以来，结构主义已走到了新的位置。这并不是说，在此后并不很长的几年里，结构主义思想的内容发生了什么变化，而是说，结构主义作为一种思考方式，已在我们的知识空间中占据了一个不同的位置。它曾是时尚，是文化谈论的热点，如今却退潮了。即使在1986年，结构主义也早已不再新鲜，但作为时尚它也尚未过时。20世纪60年代初，结构主义观念开始从法国传入英国和美国，被公开讨论、付诸实践，并受到激烈争论，大部分是在大学内部。最终，它们被从那里带到学院之外那个更浅薄、也更不友善的知识世界；在那里，至少“结构主义”这个词已变得耳熟能详，尽管其内容仍未被很好理解。如同新起的思想时尚总会遭遇的那样，结构主义既找到了偏执的传道者，也遇到了自命的审查者。一方执意将它作为一种无可争辩地客观的、科学主义的思考方式强加于人，另一方则决心抵制它，理由是它枯燥、故弄玄虚且无人情味（而且还是外来的，显然出产于巴黎）。事实上，结构主义又一次引发了知识史中周期性发生的古今之争；危险一如既往：只因一个名字便激起的情绪，可能使它特有的思想体系和研究方法继续受到遮蔽或歪曲。因此需要这样一本书，尝试把结构主义从只会伤害它的论战语境中解救出来。有意思的是，书出版后，一些书评作者似乎对它所采取的调和路线感到不快，仿佛客观评价的时候尚未到来，而作者拒绝为结构主义出征，就危及了结构主义的事业。这些书评作者与本书作者不同，是大学教师；他们的反应表明，即使在那里，结构主义也依然是一个充满争议的话题。

Structuralism has moved on since this brief attempt at a definition of it was first published, in 1986. Not in the sense that the content of Structuralist thought has somehow changed in the quite few years since then, but in the sense that Structuralism as a mode of thought has come to occupy a different point in our intellectual space. From having once been fashionable, and a cultural talking-point, it has receded. Even in 1986, Structuralism wasn’t new any more but neither as a fashion was it passé. Structuralist ideas had started travelling from France into Britain and the United States in the early 1960s, to be aired, practised and heatedly fought over, for the most part inside universities. Eventually they were brought out from there into the shallower, less hospitable intellectual world outside the academy, where the word Structuralism at least became familiar, even if the thing itself was poorly understood. As upstart intellectual fashions always will, Structuralism found both its bigoted evangelists and its would-be censors. One party was bent on imposing it as an unarguably objective, scientistic mode of thought, the other on fighting it off, on the grounds that it was arid, obscurantist and inhumane (and also foreign, having apparently been made in Paris). Structuralism in fact had precipitated another of those periodic quarrels in intellectual history between Ancients and Moderns, and the danger as ever was that the corpus of ideas and investigative methods specific to it would remain obscured or distorted by the emotions aroused by the mere name. Hence the call for a book such as this, to try to rescue Structuralism from a polemical context which could only do it harm. Interestingly, when the book came out, a number of its reviewers seemed offended by the conciliatory line it follows, as if the time for objectivity had not yet come and its author were endangering the Structuralist cause by refusing to go to war for it. These reviewers, unlike the author in question, were teachers in universities and their response proved that even there Structuralism remained a contentious topic.

到了1993年，情况仍然如此吗？一切证据都表明，不是了。在大学内部，而且在大学之外更是如此，“结构主义”这个词已不像以前那么常被听到，如今使用它也不必担心遭到报复。它不再是战斗的呐喊，也不再是吓人的字眼，而是一个中性术语，用来描述一种终于可以纯凭其优点来评价的思考方式，不再根据推销它时表现的劲头或傲慢来评价它。如果能认为结构主义获得这一新的、更健康的位置，是因为如今我们都是结构主义者了，那当然很好：结构主义观念获得了足够的接受，继续维持旧日对抗已无益处。某种程度上，情况确实如此；某种基本的结构主义现已进入知识话语的主流。当我们使用能指与所指、共时与历时、语言系统与言语等关键的结构主义对立项时，已不再需要每次都解释我们的意思。这些术语已在有识见的读者中获得常住资格，我们可以放心使用，相信它们会被理解，并且相信它们所代表的区分的重要性，已得到普遍承认。

Is it still so in 1993? By all the evidence, not. Within universities, and even more so outside them, the word Structuralism is heard less often than it was and may now be used without fear of reprisals. It is no longer either a warcry or a bogey-word but a neutral term, used to describe a mode of thought finally open to assessment purely on its merits rather than on the energy or insolence with which it has been promoted. It would be nice to think that Structuralism has achieved this new, and more wholesome position because we are all Structuralists now, Structuralist ideas having won sufficient acceptance for there to be no profit in perpetuating old confrontations. Up to a point this is the case; a certain basic Structuralism has now entered the mainstream of intellectual discourse. No longer is it necessary always to explain what we mean when we employ key Structuralist oppositions, such as that between signifier and signified, or synchrony and diachrony, or langue and parole. These terms have acquired residential status among intelligent readers and can be used in the confidence that they will be understood, and that the importance of the distinctions they represent has been generally recognized.

另一方面，如果结构主义如今显得比以前更温和、更无害，部分原因在于：在文化沙文主义者那里，取代它成为猜疑和怨恨对象的，是它更具刺激性的后代——后结构主义。自1986年以来，后结构主义在英国取得了很大进展，在美国更为显著。后结构主义这个名称本身就暗示结构主义已被超越，无疑许多后结构主义者也希望如此看待它。他们在这一点上错了，因为任何希望理解后结构主义及其艰深方法的人，都需要结构主义的基础；后结构主义从结构主义派生而来，正如我希望本书最后一章能够显示的，它纠正了结构主义内部的某些倾向，却绝不是全盘否定其洞见。不过，后结构主义是一种远比其母体更难界定或把握的思考方式，而且常被以狂放而失实的方式宣传，以至于它的思想的主要来源、法国哲学家雅克·德里达不得不与它划清界限。许多远不如德里达细腻、审慎的头脑，拿起后结构主义来迷惑外行，而不是启发他们。那些谴责它是思想——甚至道德——恐怖主义和腐化的工具的人，对它提出的指控，让人想起从前对结构主义的指控。我竟然同情那些反对后结构主义的人当中较善于思考的一部分，无疑表明我日益变得持重了；后结构主义的过度和玄虚，远远超过任何曾以结构主义之名做过的事情。

On the other hand, if Structuralism now appears as a milder, more benign mode of thought than it did, that is partly because its place as an object of suspicion and resentment among cultural chauvinists has been taken by its more abrasive progeny, Post-structuralism, which has made great headway since 1986, in Britain and, more dramatically, in the United States. Post-structuralism’s very name implies that Structuralism itself has been superseded, and no doubt many Post-structuralists would want to see it that way. In this they are wrong, because anyone who hopes to understand Post-structuralism and its difficult methods requires a grounding in Structuralism, from which, as I hope the last chapter of the present book may show, Post-structuralism is a derivation, corrective of certain tendencies within Structuralism but certainly not dismissive of its insights as a whole. Post-structuralism is, however, a mode of thought significantly harder to define or to grasp than its parent, and one which has often been publicized in wild and inaccurate ways, to the point where the principal source of its ideas, the French philosopher, Jacques Derrida, has been led to disown it. Minds much less subtle and scrupulous than his own have taken up Post- structuralism and used it to baffle outsiders rather than enlighten them. The charges made against it by those who condemn it as an agent of intellectual – or even moral – terrorism and corruption, are reminiscent of those previously laid against Structuralism, and it is no doubt evidence of a gathering staidness on my part, that I should feel sympathy with the more thoughtful of those who have taken against Post-structuralism, whose excesses and mystifications go well beyond anything ever committed in the name of Structuralism.

因此，结构主义之所以变得体面，部分是由于进驻其文化空间的那场运动的放肆。而一旦变得体面，它也就不可避免地淡出了显要位置，因为最初吸引年轻追随者投向新的思考方式的，正是人们从中感受到的破除偶像的力量。结构主义在它的时代无疑具有破除偶像的力量：它试图削弱我们在思想和文化生活中，通常给予个别思想家贡献的过高评价；这些贡献如此频繁地被高估，而作为原创性得以成立的条件的、同时代思想的匿名合唱，却因此受到损害。纯粹状态的结构主义反对个人主义的意识形态，反对把思想过度归功于个人的做法；这种做法即使不能一直追溯到文艺复兴，至少也可追溯到十九世纪浪漫主义的全盛期。结构主义强调思想体系高于那些迫于事物性质而必须在其中思考的个人；它强调思想中的非个人因素高于个人因素，这种强调有益于人——此事我不在这里详谈，因为本书结论将会讨论。

Structuralism has thus been made respectable in part by the wantonness of the movement which has moved in to occupy its cultural space. And by becoming respectable it has faded inevitably from prominence, since it is the perceived iconoclasm of new ways of thought which first attracts young followers to them. Structuralism was unquestionably iconoclastic in its day, for seeking to undermine the too-high value ordinarily placed in our intellectual and cultural life on the contribution made there by individual thinkers, so frequently overvalued at the expense of that anonymous chorus of contemporary thought that is the enabling condition of originality. Structuralism in its pure state was opposed to ideologies of individualism, to that excessive ad hominem attribution of ideas which dates back to the nineteenth-century heyday of Romanticism, if not all the way to the Renaissance. Structuralism emphasizes systems of thought above the individuals who are forced by the nature of things to think within them, it emphasizes, and salutarily, the impersonal in thought above the personal – a matter I shall not dwell on here because it is taken up in the Conclusion to this book.

然而，一个具有讽刺意味的事实是：尽管结构主义提出各种支持系统性和非个人性的论点，作为一场运动，它在很大程度上却凭借少数法国思想家在国内外获得的个人魅力地位确立了自身；20世纪60年代，大众无论正确与否，都把这些人同结构主义联系在一起。这些居于主导地位的人物共有五位，各自在不同的知识领域活动，这一事实本身就给他们思想之间真实的（尽管也有限的）汇合增添了额外的分量与意义：克洛德·列维-斯特劳斯，法国最重要的人类学家；米歇尔·福柯，哲学家和思想史家；罗兰·巴特，文化批评家和文学理论家；雅克·拉康，弗洛伊德派精神分析师；以及路易·阿尔都塞，政治哲学家和马克思主义理论家。这五个人从未组成一个学派，他们都过于专断，有些还过于自负，不可能忍受这样放弃自己的个性。但他们的思想无疑彼此重叠，并标志了当代思想的一个时代。公众觉得他们共同代表了这个时代，觉得我们正生活在结构主义时代，这又提高了他们作为个人在各门学科中享有的声望。公众这样想是对的，因为结构主义持续具有的力量之一，正是它能从每一个知识探索领域中援引证据，支持其基本信条。尤其在列维-斯特劳斯的版本中，它追求普遍性，因为他相信，人类心灵本身的运作方式，就足以证明结构主义的主张。

It is an ironic fact, however, that for all its arguments in favour of the systematic and impersonal, Structuralism as a movement imposed itself largely by virtue of the charismatic standing accorded both at home and abroad to a handful of French thinkers who rightly or wrongly became associated with it in the popular mind during the 1960s. There were five of these dominant figures, each active in a different intellectual field, a fact which itself lent extra weight and significance to the real (if also limited) convergence of their ideas: Claude Lévi-Strauss, France’s foremost anthropologist; Michel Foucault, a philosopher and historian of ideas; Roland Barthes, culture-critic and theorist of literature; Jacques Lacan, a Freudian psychoanalyst; and Louis Althusser, a political philosopher and theorist of Marxism. These five men at no time constituted a school, being far too imperious and, in some cases, vain to endure any such surrender of their individuality. But their ideas unquestionably overlapped and came to mark an epoch in contemporary thought. The prestige they enjoyed as individuals in a variety of disciplines was increased by the public’s sense that together they were representative of the age, that we were living in Structuralist times. The public was right to think that, because it is one of the continuing strengths of Structuralism that it can call on evidence to support its basic tenets from every field of intellectual endeavour. In the version of Lévi-Strauss particularly, it aspires to universalism, inasmuch as he believes that the human mind itself functions in such a manner as to prove the Structuralist case.

到了1993年，这五位声名卓著的人物的地位发生了变化，受到强调的不再是他们的相似之处，而是他们各自的独特性。甚至到1986年，五人中已有四人去世，留下结构主义最纯粹的纯粹主义者列维-斯特劳斯，成为唯一的幸存者，而他个人又非常寡言。其他人已被那种他们一度似乎正挑战的个人主义风气明确地重新收编。拉康生前自称只是在扩展弗洛伊德，忠实地延续弗洛伊德阐明无意识心灵隐蔽结构的工作——尽管“阐明”从来不大是拉康的长项，因为他的散文风格以优雅而不可理解著称；自1986年以来，他多年来在巴黎所作“研讨班”（实际上是讲座）的更多卷本陆续出版，使他作为思想家显得既更容易接近，也更具戏剧性，并且更不那么弗洛伊德式。福柯于1984年因艾滋病去世后，被大张旗鼓地“揭露”为同性恋者，并被以尼采式的方式呈现为一个执迷的个人主义者，而非从前可以把他看成的那个相当自我隐退的思想史家。1同样是同性恋者的巴特，如今也正在身后被“揭露”，他的著作开始被以远比以前更明显的传记方式来解释，仿佛我们如今了解的他的私人情况，理应在阅读他时发挥决定作用。阿尔都塞在一时精神失常中杀害妻子，此后人生最后十年先住在精神病院，后隐居生活；他的一部自传于1992年身后出版之后，他的思想也正在被个人化解读，在那本书中，他使自己的马克思主义显得基础十分薄弱，而对理论的热爱则根源于他作为一个人的深度不安全感。2唯有列维-斯特劳斯逃过了这套耸人听闻的重新收编程序，继续出版分析北美印第安人神话的、一丝不苟的结构主义研究。

In 1993, the standing of these five prestigious figures has changed in such a way as to stress not their likenesses but their idosyncrasies. Even by 1986, four of the five had died, leaving Lévi-Strauss, the supreme purist of Structuralism, as the lone, and personally very reticent survivor. The others have been decisively recuperated by the very ethos of individualism which they might at one time have seemed to be challenging. Lacan claimed in his lifetime to be merely expanding on Freud, loyally continuing Freud’s work in elucidating the hidden structures of the unconscious mind – although elucidation was never quite Lacan’s thing, since his prose style was a byword for elegant unintelligibility; the publication since 1986 of further volumes of the ‘Seminars’ (in fact, lectures) given by Lacan in Paris over many years has made him seem at once more accessible as a thinker, more theatrical, and less Freudian. Foucault has, since his death as a result of AIDS in 1984, been vigorously ‘outed’ as a homosexual and presented in Nietzschean terms as an obsessive individualist rather than as the quite self-effacing historian of ideas that it was once possible to see him as.1 Barthes, who was also homosexual, is himself now in process of being posthumously ‘outed’ and his writings have begun to be interpreted in far more overtly biographical terms than previously, as if what we now know about him personally were meant to be determinant in our reading of him. The ideas of Althusser, who spent the final ten years of his life first in a psychiatric ward and then in seclusion, having murdered his wife in a moment of insanity, are being personalized after an autobiography published posthumously in 1992, in which he makes his own Marxism seem very flimsily based and his love of theory to have had its roots in his deep insecurity as a man.2 Lévi-Strauss alone has been spared from this lurid recuperative process and has continued to publish analytical and meticulously Structuralist studies of North American Indian myths.

这一切已经改变、并将继续改变人们感知结构主义的方式。它打破了这些思想家在哲学或思想立场上任何尚存的团结表象；他们生前就不喜欢被归为一群，尤其警惕接受“结构主义者”这一标签，因为它似乎限制了他们的思想（列维-斯特劳斯又是例外）。巴特和福柯尤其以思想的流动性，以及追求新思想道路、而非成为自己已发表著作的囚徒的能力为傲。尽管如此，如果这种已经强劲展开的解体过程，使人们以为结构主义从一开始就从未真正存在过，只不过是宣传者投机而仓促的发明，他们无知地把本不该归在一起的思想混成一团，那将是不幸的。他们之间确有共同基础：结构主义无疑在现代思想的演进中代表了一个既连贯又关键的时刻，因此，无论从哲学还是历史的角度看，它都对我们具有持久的重要性。不能仅仅因为结构主义的时刻已经过去，一些明星人物如今显得比以前更为飘忽、更具个人特性，就说它带来的思想已经死去。

All this has already altered and will continue to alter the way in which Structuralism is perceived. It has worked to break up any lingering appearance of solidarity in the philosophical or intellectual positions of the thinkers involved, who even in their lifetimes disliked being grouped together, being especially wary of accepting the label of Structuralist because of the restrictions it seemed to set on their thought (Lévi-Strauss was again the exception). Barthes and Foucault in particular prided themselves on the mobility of their ideas, and on their capacity to pursue new intellectual paths rather than become the prisoners of their published work. It would be unfortunate none the less if the disintegrative process which has set in so strongly were to give people the idea that Structuralism never really existed in the first place, but was the opportunistic and hasty invention of publicists, lumping together in their ignorance ideas which didn’t belong together. There was common ground between them: Structuralism did beyond question represent both a coherent and a cardinal moment in the evolution of modern thought and as such has a lasting importance for us on both philosophical and historical grounds. The ideas that it introduced are not dead simply because the Structuralist moment has passed and some of its stars now seem more erratic and idiosyncratic figures than hitherto.

正如我已暗示的，这本入门书的目标是描述结构主义，而不是为它辩护。凭借这里提供的、有关其思想主张及其理由的证据，我们完全可以放心让结构主义自己为自己辩护。不过，有一点需要先作解释：结构主义为何过了如此漫长的时间才产生？我们生活在一个结构极其丰富的世界里，可是愿意站出来称自己为结构主义者的人，却少得惊人，这是为什么？前者的激增，并未带来后者的大规模增加。结构无处不在：自然和文化中都有，每门艺术、每种科学都能发现它们，从而支持了列维-斯特劳斯的论点，即结构是人类心智活动本身的特征，也是人脑与脑外现实之间关键的同构联系。凡在本质上并非不可分的东西，都可以被证明具有结构，或者是一个能够被分析为构成要素的复杂整体；这些要素自身不能作为独立实体来界定，而应根据它们与同在一个特定系统或结构中的其他要素之间那些可描绘的关系来界定。结构可以是有形的：比如人或其他动物身体的解剖构造；也可以是抽象的：一国的经济，或者更恰当地说，考虑到现代经济之间高度相互依赖，全球经济就是一种结构，尽管它过于复杂，迄今还无法得到完整描述。

The object of this introductory volume is, as I have implied, to descrie Structuralism, not to make a case for it. Structuralism may safely be left to make its own case, on the evidence here provided of its intellectual claims and their rationale. What requires some preliminary explanation, however, is why Structuralism took so long to come about. How is it that, although we inhabit a world super-abundantly provided with structures, remarkably few people have come forward who would call themselves Structuralists? The proliferation of the one has not entailed any large-scale recruitment of the other. Structures are ubiquitous: they are to be found in nature as in culture, in every art and by every science, so supporting Lévi-Strauss’s thesis that they are characteristic of human mental activity as such and the crucial, isomorphic link between the human brain and an extracerebral reality. Everything which is not by its nature indivisible might be shown to possess a structure, or to be a complex whole capable of analysis into its constituent elements, these elements being themselves definable not as independent entities but in terms of the mappable relations in which they stand to the other elements with which they coexist in a particular system or structure. Structures may be tangible: as for example, the anatomy of a human or other animal body; or they may be an abstraction: a nation’s economy, or better, given the high degree of interdependence of modern economies, the global economy, is a structure, if one too complex to yield so far to a full description.

结构主义的独特之处，在于明确地把研究对象作为整体来研究，并把构成那些整体的部分作为部分来研究，也就是说，绝不纯粹根据其内在性质，而是根据它们对所属整体作出的贡献来研究。因此，结构主义按定义就是一种整体论的思考方式，也因此越来越契合我们生活的时代，因为要求我们以整体论而非旧时原子论方式思考的呼声不断响起。举一个显然的例子，在环境及其保护问题上，尤其如此。事实上，没有哪门学科比生态学更必须依照结构主义原则展开；生态学研究植物、动物和种群彼此之间，以及它们与其生存的地理、气候条件之间的相互作用。生态学在揭示构成一个特定生态系统的要素间的相互关系时，或者在例证结构主义的原则——系统以系统性的方式行动和反应，局部事件的影响从不完全局限于局部——时，可以非常引人注目。请想想“混沌”理论家那个著名例子：地球某一地方一只蝴蝶拍动翅膀，可能成为最终促使另一地方生成飓风的那个事件。这就是基本的结构主义，它要求研究天气系统的人，在模型中尽可能纳入广泛的数据，希望有一天能够推导出把每一项数据与其他数据联系起来的全套符号公式或规律。

Structuralism is distinctive for studying its objects explicitly as wholes and the parts which make up those wholes as parts, that is, never purely intrinsically but in terms of the contribution they make to the whole they are part of. Structuralism is thus by definition a holistic mode of thought and as such increasingly in tune with the age we live in, since demands that we should think holistically rather than in the atomistic ways of old are constantly heard. This is especially so, to take an obvious example, when it comes to the environment and how to protect it. No discipline in fact could be conducted on more necessarily Structuralist principles than that of ecology, or the study of plants, animals and populations in their interactions with one another and with the geographical and climatic conditions in which they exist. Ecology may be quite dramatic in exposing the interrelationships between the elements that form a particular eco-system, or in exemplifying the Structuralist principle that systems act and react systematically, that local events are never entirely local in their effects. Consider the famous example of the ‘Chaos’ theorists, which has it that the beating of a butterfly’s wings in one part of the globe may be the event finally to precipitate the generation of a hurricane in another. This is basic Structuralism, asking that students of weather-systems integrate within their models as great a range of data as they are able to, in the hope one day of working out the complete set of symbolic formulae or laws relating one datum to another.

因此，结构主义者的任务，首先是确定构成所研究结构的要素——对于解剖构造，这相当容易；但对于经济或天气系统，正如我们付出代价才认识到的那样，这一点极其困难——然后尝试理解它们往往十分复杂的相互作用方式。所以，结构主义可以简要界定为动态系统的静力学，也就是研究它明知是运动着的系统，却为了研究目的，仿佛它们已在时间中冻结那样来分析它们。

The task of the Structuralist is thus, first to determine the elements out of which the structure in question is formed – easy enough in the case of an anatomy, not easy at all as we know to our cost in the case of an economy or a weather-system; and then to try to understand the often very complex modes of their interaction. Structuralism might then be briefly defined as the Statics of dynamic systems, that is, the study of systems it knows to be mobile but which it analyses as if they were, for the purpose of study, frozen in time.

鉴于结构在从医学到物理学、社会学、心理学、经济学、政治学，一直到艺术的每个知识研究领域都如此普遍、如此重要，结构主义者却这样少而稀落，似乎很奇怪。但许多以研究结构为职业的人，并不觉得需要称自己为结构主义者，也不觉得需要提倡某种称为结构主义的东西，作为开展研究时首选的方法。结构主义与结构不同，是一种局部现象，一场只在有限的几门学科中明确扎根的运动：首要的是语言学，其次是社会人类学、史学、文学研究，以及以这些学科为先导的其他学科。原因在于，在人文学科或人文科学中，对研究对象——无论是一种语言、人类心理，还是文学文本——采取结构性进路，既非显而易见，也非必不可少。这是一种选择，是多种进路中的一种，与其他进路竞争关注和追随者。解剖学家宣称自己是结构主义者，没多大意义，因为理解人体，大概没有结构性理解之外的选择。经济学家也一样，他们只能承认，自己关注的那个极为复杂的对象是结构性的，而且只能得到部分理解，恰恰因为其各部分相互关系的精确性质仍然极难把握。

Given the pervasiveness and importance of structures in every field of intellectual enquiry, from medicine to physics, to sociology, to psychology, to economics, to politics, all the way to the arts, it will seem curious that Structuralists have been so few and far between. But a great many of those who concern themselves professionally with the study of structures have felt no need to call themselves Structuralists nor to advocate something called Structuralism as their preferred method of carrying on their researches. Structuralism, unlike structures, is a localized phenomenon, a movement which only explicitly took root in a limited number of academic disciplines: in linguistics first and foremost, in social anthropology, in historiography, in literary studies, and in other disciplines taking their lead from these. The reason is that here, in the humanities or else human sciences, a structural approach to the subject-matter, be this a language, or the human psyche, or a literary text, is neither obvious nor obligatory. It is an option, one approach among others, competing for attention and for followers with alternative approaches. It would make little sense for an anatomist to proclaim him or herself a Structuralist, because there is presumably no alternative to a structural understanding of the human body. And the same with economists, who can but recognize that the formidably complex object of their attentions is structural, and only partly intelligible for the very reason that the precise nature of the interrelation of its parts remains exceedingly elusive.

因此，重要的是辨认出两种形式的结构主义：一种响应这一名称，另一种认为没必要如此。最广义的结构主义，可以说描述了一种即使不是所有学科、至少也是许多学科所共有的研究方式；狭义的结构主义则是一场相当积极主张自身的思想运动，它在一个特定时刻产生，并试图说服持其他立场的人：在语言学、历史学或文学等学科中，结构主义方法是现有方法中最可靠、最有成果的。这里及本书全文都用首字母大写的Structuralism来标明狭义的结构主义，以区别于首字母小写的structuralism。但小写s的结构主义的存在，显然增强了大写S的结构主义的可信性，因为前者提供了一种稳固确立的方法论背景，后者理应在这一背景下被看待。

It is of importance, therefore, to identify two forms of Structuralism, one which answers to the name, the other which sees no need to do so.
