Structuralism in the broadest sense may be said to describe a mode of enquiry common to very many if not all disciplines; Structuralism in the narrow sense is a fairly assertive intellectual movement which came into existence at a particular moment in time and undertook to persuade those of other allegiances that Structuralist methods are the soundest and most productive on offer in such disciplines as linguistics, or history, or literature. The narrower version of Structuralism is that identified here and throughout this book by a capital letter, in order to distinguish it from structuralism with a lower-cases. But the credentials of capital-S Structuralism are clearly enhanced by the existence of small-s structuralism, as a solidly established methodological background against which it deserves to be seen.

在1986年版《结构主义》的导论中，我写道：“想一想结构主义已经产生的无可置疑的影响，我正着手撰写的这种概览，先例竟然少得令人惊讶。”到了1993年，它依然先例很少，而一本这样的书的作者，对其中缘故再清楚不过了。任何着手撰写这种书的人，面临的困难都十分严重。即使是大写S的结构主义，其研究对象也广泛得令人望而却步；除非是某个异乎寻常的博学通才，否则不可能跟随它进入每个留下过印记的学科。一个算不上博学通才的作者，只能希望写出一部局部的结构主义入门书——这里“局部”的意思是不完整。本研究只涉及那些我觉得多少有能力阐述的结构主义方法的应用。它首先讨论结构主义最初如何在语言学中扎稳根基，接着简要讨论它向社会人类学、史学和精神分析的扩展，再到符号学——又一个自1986年以来流通更广、被理解得更好的术语——把它看作结构主义向整体文化研究的逻辑延伸，然后讨论作为文学（并隐含地包括其他一切）文本研究方法的结构主义，最后讨论后结构主义。

In the introduction to the 1986 edition of Structuralism I wrote: ‘The conspectus I am embarking on has surprisingly few predessors when one reflects on the undoubted impact Structuralism has had.’ It still, in 1993, has few predecessors, and the author of a volume such as this knows only too well why. The difficulties facing whoever sets out to write one are severe. Structuralism, even of the capital-S kind, ranges dauntingly far afield in terms of subject-matter, and to follow it into every discipline where it has made its mark is out of the question, except for some unnatural polymath. A less than polymathic author can only hope to write a partial introduction to Structuralism – partial in the sense of being incomplete. The present study touches only on those applications of Structuralist methods which I feel some competence to expound. It begins with Structuralism as it originally took firm hold in linguistics, goes on briefly to its extensions to social anthropology, historiography and psychoanalysis, thence to semiotics – another term that has gained wider currency and a better understanding since 1986 – seen as the logical extension of Structuralism to the study of culture as a whole, to Structuralism as a method in the study of literary (and by implication all other) texts, and finally to Post-structuralism.

这是我最有资格亲自遵循的路线，因为我接触结构主义，是通过它在文学中的表现，以及较少程度上在语言学中的表现。另一位作者会希望以不同方式描绘结构主义的领域。例如，杰出的瑞士发展心理学家让·皮亚杰于1968年出版的那本小书，3以一章关于数学和逻辑学中的结构主义开篇，然后转向物理学和生物学。它完全不涉及符号学或文学中的结构主义。这样就把这一主题牢固而有说服力地奠基于“硬”科学，从而支持其普遍性主张。而在这里，以不同方式把它奠基于语言研究，仍然可以为它主张普遍性，因为正如数学规律（我们相信）具有普遍适用性，结构主义者认为潜存于所有现存和可能语言之下的那个抽象系统，也可能是普遍的：一种替代系统只能在这个系统内部被设想，因此可以说它在字面意义上是不可设想的。省略对结构主义更严格的科学应用的讨论，并不必然削弱关于其重要性的论证。

This is the itinerary I am myself best qualified to follow, having come to Structuralism by way of its literary and to a lesser extent its linguistic manifestations. Another author would want to map the Structuralist field differently. The little book published by the distinguished Swiss developmental psychologist Jean Piaget in 1968,3 begins, for example, with a chapter on structuralism in mathematics and logic and passes on from there to physics and biology. It does not touch at all on semiotics or Structuralism in literature. That is to ground the subject firmly and persuasively in the ‘hard’ sciences and so back up its claims to universality. By grounding it differently here, in the study of language, it is still possible to claim universality for it since, just as the laws of mathematics are (we trust) universally applicable, so the abstract system that Structuralists argue underlies all existing and possible languages may also be universal, insofar as an alternative would be conceivable only within that system and might therefore be said to be literally inconceivable. The argument in favour of Structuralism’s importance is not weakened necessarily by omitting reference to its more strictly scientific applications.

任何结构主义论述所追求的理想，本身都是结构主义式的：证明在它现存各种形式的表面多样性之下，有一种底层的统一性；譬如，文学结构主义和人类学结构主义具有共同的方法和共同的一套原则，依照它们，一个文本或一批人类学资料可以被以类似的方式“阅读”。需要证明的是，那些远非明显相同的方法之间的形式统一性。既然如此，就很容易看出，结构主义的兴起，十分适合帮助侵蚀相邻却看似独立的学科之间那些日渐陈旧的界限。这种侵蚀早在二十多年前就开始了，但近些年来加速了；结构主义想必对此有所贡献，后来的后结构主义也一样，后者呼吁大学课程改革，尤其响亮且有效。思想狭隘的人，无疑愿意认为结构主义如今正无人惋惜地退入遗忘，认为它是一个制造混乱的闯入者，其思想风头终于谢天谢地地过去了。可喜的是，要这样想已为时过晚；结构主义经过的地方，只要它曾扎根，就留下了持久而有益的影响。另一些气度更宽广、更现实的人，将继续把结构主义视为一种有声望、富有成果的思想运动：它给我们提供了一个令人印象深刻的思想工具，让我们尝试统一自己对周围世界那种严重碎片化的理解。

The ideal at which any account of Structuralism aims is a Structuralist one: to demonstrate that beneath the superficial variety of its existing forms there is an underlying unity, that literary and anthropological Structuralism let’s say have a common method and a common set of principles, by which a text or a body of anthropological data may be similarly ‘read’. What is to be demonstrated is the formal unity between methods far from transparently the same. This being so, it is easy to see that Structuralism was well calculated when it arose to assist in the erosion of obsolescent boundaries between adjacent yet seemingly independent academic disciplines. This erosion began a good twenty years ago, but it has accelerated in more recent years and Structuralism will have had something to do with that, as has Post-structuralism since, which has been particularly loud and effective in calling for curricular reform in universities. Those of a parochial cast of mind would no doubt like to think of Structuralism as now receding unlamented into oblivion, as a disruptive intruder whose intellectual day is mercifully done. Happily, it is too late for that; the passage of Structuralism has had a lasting and beneficial effect in those places where it took root. Others, of a more generous and realistic temper, will continue to value Structuralism as a reputable and productive intellectual movement which has presented us with an impressive intellectual tool by which to try to unify our seriously fragmented understanding of the world around us.

下文的正文与1986年版相比未作改动；若这里那里改几处，却让其余部分保持原样，那就违背了结构主义的一项主要信条：整体的各部分相互依赖，所以零星修改文本，可能会在修改者未曾预料的地方影响文本。不过，我不把结论视为原正文不可分割的一部分，因此对它作了改写；同样，延伸阅读也在某些地方作了更新，以纳入1986年以来出版的著作。

The text which follows has not been altered from that of the 1986 edition; to alter it here and there, leaving the rest as it was, would be to offend against a major tenet of Structuralism, that the parts of a whole are interdependent, so that episodic alterations to a text may affect it elsewhere, in ways unsuspected by the alterer. The Conclusion, however, I do not look on as an integral part of the original text, and that has been recast; likewise the Further Reading has been updated in places to take account of work published since 1986.

1 语言1 Language

在本世纪的进程中，语言已从思想的透明的、被认为无关紧要的媒介，变成了哲学中一个核心而难以解决的问题。我们已经意识到，我们所思考的东西，取决于用来思考的语言的结构，而且没有任何可交流的思想能够脱离语言而存在。关注媒介，原来就是关注思想的实质。我们从前习惯的那些表述暗示思想由语言“传达”；如果它们使我们假定，在语言“之外”存在某种它让我们得以进入的东西，那么它们就是误导的。六十多年前，维特根斯坦写道：“一切哲学都是‘语言批判’……”1这帮助开启了一个至今尚未结束的哲学时代，它将意义问题——一个语言问题——提升为哲学家所能面对的最重要问题。结构主义也与意义的性质密切相关，可以被视为对哲学兴趣这一剧烈转移的一种贡献。

In the course of the present century language has passed from being the transparent, presumedly indifferent medium of thought to being a central and intractable problem of philosophy. We have become aware that what we think is conditional on the structure of the language in which we think, and that no communicable thought is possible independently of language. By giving our attention to the medium it turns out that we are giving our attention to the substance of thought. The formulations we were earlier used to, which implied that thought is ‘conveyed’ by language, are misleading if they cause us to assume the existence of some ‘beyond’ of language to which it gives us access. ‘All philosophy is ‘‘critique of language’’ . . . ’ wrote Wittgenstein sixty or more years ago,1 so helping to inaugurate a philosophical age which has not yet ended and which has elevated the question of meaning – a linguistic question – to be the most important one philosophers can confront. Structuralism, too, is bound up with the nature of meaning and may be seen as a contribution to this scismic shift of philosophical interest.

以前曾有人提出过一种极端主张：结构主义要么是语言学的，要么就什么也不是；它应被认同于某个语言学学派，当它应用于狭义语言学领域之外时，无论其研究对象属于历史学、人类学还是其他学科，它都把对象当作语言来研究。这是一个极其可疑的类比，可以一开始就抛弃。如果我选择以一章语言学结构主义来开始这部结构主义方法的概览，首先是因为语言是任何社会的核心制度，而结构主义是一种具有鲜明社会性的思考方式；其次是因为，毫无疑问，各领域结构主义者所使用的思想，大部分都能在结构语言学中找到最清楚的表述。

The extreme claim has been made before now that Structuralism is linguistic or nothing, that it is to be identified with a certain school of linguistics and that when it is applied outside the narrowly linguistic field it investigates its subject-matter, be it historical, anthropological or of some other discipline, as if it were a language. This is a most doubtful analogy which may be discarded at the outset. If I choose to begin this survey of Structuralist method with a chapter on linguistic Structuralism this is because, first of all, language is the central institution of any society and Structuralism is a peculiarly social mode of thought, and, second, because there is no question but that a preponderance of the ideas with which Structuralists in various fields have worked are to be found most clearly formulated in structural linguistics.

自20世纪20年代初发展以来，结构语言学有两条不同的脉络，一条在欧洲，另一条在北美。欧洲这一脉络之所以被认同于结构主义，是由于导论中简要提到的法国联系；但北美的结构语言学家也形成了某些与欧洲流行观点趋同的语言观，即使他们的观点显得根本分歧，两种结构主义之间的对照也极具启发性。在下文中，只要看起来有帮助，我就会把两者联系起来。这样做的一个特殊理由，是反对有关结构主义的传播主义论点，这一论点把该思想流派看成起源于一个单一中心，随后传播到世界其他愿意接纳它的思想环境。思想传播当然是真实存在的，但除非人们发现这些思想适合它们传入的环境中已盛行的观念，否则它们不大可能被明智地接受。早在欧洲结构主义观念入侵之前，美国已有某种结构主义传统，这一事实有助于解释为什么这些观念会受到欢迎，也有助于证实结构主义者自身的主张：结构主义是一种普遍的，而非局部的思考方式，因为它归根结底植根于人类生物学。

There are two distinct strands in structural linguistics, as it has grown since the early 1920s, one European and the other North American. If the European strand has come to be identified with Structuralism then that is because of the French connection alluded to briefly in the Introduction; but the North American structural linguists also evolved certain views about language which converged with those current in Europe, and even where their views appear radically to diverge, the contrast between the two kinds of Structuralism is most instructive. In the pages which follow I shall, where it seems helpful, relate them one to the other. A particular reason for doing this is to counter the diffusionist argument in respect of Structuralism, whereby this school of thought is regarded as having originated in a single centre and then spread to other, receptive intellectual environments around the world. Of course diffusion of ideas is a reality but it is unlikely that they will be intelligently received unless they are found to fit with ideas already prevalent in the environments they have been carried to. The fact that there was a certain Structuralist tradition in the United States well before any invasion of Structuralist notions from Europe helps to explain why these notions were well received and also helps to bear out the case put by Structuralists themselves that Structuralism is a universal and not a local mode of thought, being anchored in the last resort in human biology.

在北美和欧洲，结构主义都于20世纪20年代在语言研究中扎根。就它关注一般语言而言，它标志着一种在十九世纪已很大程度上衰落的哲学兴趣的复兴。除了德国的威廉·冯·洪堡或美国的W. D. 惠特尼等少数杰出例外，十九世纪的语言学家因专注于各种具体语言，反而看不到语言本身了。他们沉迷于现存或曾经存在的各种语言的事实，牺牲了对普遍语言能力本身的关注；换句话说，他们没有语言理论。即使今天，许多语言研究者也未注意到：研究作为人类普遍能力的语言，与研究各种实际语言，范围上存在巨大差别。当然，前者只能通过后者来研究；十九世纪确实值得赞扬，因为它汇集了实际语言的大量资料，一旦逐种语言进行系统比较，便为更有雄心、更具哲学眼光的研究者制定一套连贯的语言理论开辟了道路。只有当这样的理论得到发展，并以现存语言实践的丰富证据加以检验——本世纪人类学家收集的数据极大地丰富了这些证据——才能说语言学有了科学基础。

Both in North America and in Europe Structuralism took root in the study of language in the 1920s. In so far as it concerned itself with language in general it marked a revival of a philosophical interest that had very much lapsed in the nineteenth century. With a few remarkable exceptions, such as Wilhelm von Humboldt in Germany, or W. D. Whitney in the United States, nineteenth-century linguists had lost sight of language in their preoccupation with languages. The facts of existing or once existing languages absorbed them, at the expense of the universal language faculty itself: they had no theory of language, that is. Even today many students of language fail to remark the vast difference in scope between the study of language as a universal faculty of our species and the study of actual languages. It is true that the first can be studied only by way of the second and the nineteenth century may certainly claim credit for having assembled many data from actual languages which, once they were systematically compared from language to language, opened the way for the more ambitious and philosophical students to work out a coherent theory of language. Only when such a theory was developed and tested against the plentiful evidence of existing linguistic practice – evidence hugely enriched in this century by the data gathered by anthropologists – could linguistics be said to have a scientific basis.

欧洲结构语言学的奠基者，也是常被视为整个结构主义运动之宗师的人，是瑞士语言学家费尔迪南·德·索绪尔。索绪尔1857年生于日内瓦，主要在德国求学，在巴黎任教数年，随后于1891年返回日内瓦大学，并于1913年在那里去世。他受训为印欧语言学家，是梵语及其派生语言的专家，但比其他同类学者更善于思辨；十五岁时，他已懂法语、德语、英语、拉丁语和希腊语，并为一位老师写了一篇《论语言》，试图从他认为在所接触的几种语言中共有的语音模式，推导出某些语言共相。收到文章的老师认为，这种前结构主义的尝试过于自负。但年仅二十一岁时，他就在莱比锡发表了著名的《论印欧语言的原始元音系统》，其方法无疑是结构主义的。本文原本要研究所谓“印欧语a”的各种形式，但随着工作推进，索绪尔开始意识到，问题比他预料的更广，研究一个元音意味着研究全部元音。用这篇论文序言的话说：“显然，实际上落入我们观察范围的，是整个元音系统，它的名称必须写在扉页上。”2

The founding father of structural linguistics in Europe, and the man frequently looked on as the patron of the whole Structuralist movement, was the Swiss linguist Ferdinand de Saussure. Saussure was born in Geneva in 1857, studied mainly in Germany, taught for a number of years in Paris, then returned to the University of Geneva in 1891 and died there in 1913. He was by training an Indo-Europeanist, a specialist in Sanskrit and its derivative languages, but more speculative than others of his kind; at the age of fifteen, when he already knew French, German, English, Latin and Greek, he produced for one of his teachers an ‘Essay on languages’ which attempted to derive certain linguistic universals from phonetic patterns he believed to be common to several of the languages he had met with. This proto-Structuralist exercise was judged presumptuous by its addressee. But at the age of only twenty-one he published in Leipzig a celebrated ‘Dissertation on the primitive vowel-system in Indo-European languages’ whose method is certainly Structuralist. The object of this essay was to study the various forms taken by the so-called ‘Indo-European a’, but as the work proceeded Saussure came to appreciate that the question was a wider one than he had foreseen, and that to study one vowel meant studying them all. In the words of the Dissertation’s preface: ‘it is clear that in fact it is the system of vowels as a whole that falls within the radius of our observation and whose name must be inscribed on the first page.’2

索绪尔生前发表的东西很少，但在任教的晚年，他已从对印欧语或日耳曼语的专门关注，转向在日内瓦讲授普通语言学。1916年，即他去世三年后，两位以前的学生以他的名义出版了一部《普通语言学教程》。这一文本是他们对曾听过索绪尔讲课的若干学生所作的课堂笔记进行比勘后，自行编成的。此后七十年间，《教程》走向世界，在少数国家传播很快，在另一些国家却极其缓慢——第一部英译本直到1959年才问世。3仔细阅读这个就当时而言具有革命性的文本，仍是理解结构主义所依据的原则的最佳入门方式。文本并非索绪尔本人、而是其学生或追随者的作品，这一点并不重要，因为文本阐述的思想是否可靠，并不取决于我们是否知道这些思想被以恰好符合索绪尔本人意愿的形式呈现。即使不能确定作者身份，也能判断它们的价值。我的全文将使用索绪尔的名字，仿佛他明确无疑是《教程》的作者，尽管如我们将会看到的，他与此书之间不确定的关系，同结构主义提出的问题有着饶有意味的关联。

In his lifetime Saussure published very little, but in his later years of teaching he had moved from his special concerns with Indo-European or Germanic languages and given courses of lectures at Geneva in General Linguistics. In 1916, three years after his death, two of his former students published under his name a Course in General Linguistics. This was a text constructed by themselves from their collation of sets of notes taken by a number of students who had attended Saussure’s lectures. In the seventy years since then the Course has made its way around the world, quickly in a few countries but with extreme slowness in others – the first English translation was not made until 1959.3 An attentive reading of this, for its time, revolutionary text remains the best introduction there is to the principles on which Structuralism rests. The fact that the text is not the work of Saussure himself but of his students or followers is unimportant, since the soundness or otherwise of the ideas which the text expounds is not dependent on our knowing whether they are given in precisely the form Saussure himself would have wanted. Their worth may be determined without any certainty as to their authorship. I shall use Saussure’s name throughout my own text as if he were, unequivocally, the author of the Course, although his uncertain relationship to that book bears interestingly, as we shall see, on the issues raised by Structuralism.

像其他任何革新者一样，索绪尔是在反抗一种正统；对他而言，那是十九世纪后期的正统，它规定必须从历史和发生的角度研究语言。语言学家当时选择提出并回答的，主要是特定语言或语群的起源与演变问题。他们执迷于变化，却忽略了究竟是什么在变化。索绪尔引入其语言学的第一个重大转变，意在抵抗这种侧重点，仿佛要把研究对象——语言——稳定地保持足够长的时间，以便考察它持久的结构。洪堡曾宣称：“从恰当的理解来说，语言是一种持久的东西，同时又在每一瞬间转瞬即逝。”4索绪尔要揭示的，正是那持久的方面。

Like any other innovator Saussure was in reaction against an orthodoxy: in his case the orthodoxy of the later nineteenth century which dictated that language be studied historically and genetically. The questions that linguists then chose to ask and to answer were chiefly ones of the origins and evolution of particular languages or groups of languages. They were obsessed with change and ignored what it was exactly that was changing. The first great shift which Saussure introduces into his linguistics is intended to go against this emphasis and, as it were, to hold the object of study – language – steady for long enough to enable its permanent structure to be investigated. ‘Properly conceived of,’ Humboldt had declared, ‘language is something persistent and in every instant transitory.’4 It was the persistent aspect which Saussure meant to uncover.

一种语言可以沿两条轴线研究：一条是时间的，另一条可以说是空间的。索绪尔把这两条轴线称为历时轴与共时轴。历时语言学家研究语言随时间的变化，共时语言学家则静态地研究语言，研究它在某一特定时刻的既定状态。共时论者的前提是：为了研究语言多少具有持久性的构成，可以把它不停变化的能力暂且搁置。研究语言本身——把它视为所有现存语言的假定模板——本质上是一种共时研究，因为它寻找的是语言的恒定因素。结构主义者寻找的结构是持久的，但这并不是说它们永不变化、永远存在。结构也会演变，或许只有在极端抽象的层次上例外：在那个层次上，人们相信自己已辨认出那些若缺少了就不能成为结构的基本构成部分。

A language may be studied along two axes, one temporal and the other, in a manner of speaking, spatial. These two axes Saussure named the diachronic and the synchronic. A diachronic linguist studies a language as it changes through time while a synchronic linguist studies it statically, in its given state at a particular moment of time. The premise of the synchronist is that the capacity of a language to alter unceasingly may be set aside for the sake of studying its more or less permanent constitution. The study of language itself, as the hypothetical template of all existing languages, is essentially a synchronic one because it is searching for the constants of language. The structures which Structuralists look for endure, which is not to say that they never change or last for ever. Structures too evolve, except perhaps at that extreme level of abstraction where one believes one has identified the basic constituents without which it could not be a structure.

索绪尔用以下措辞，划出了这两条对照鲜明的研究轴线之间的关键区别：

Saussure draws the vital distinction between the two contrasted axes of study in these terms:

共时语言学将关注构成一个系统的共存项目之间的逻辑与心理联系，这些联系由同一个集体意识所感知。

Synchronic linguistics will be concerned with logical and psychological connexions between coexisting items constituting a system, as perceived by the same collective consciousness.

另一方面，历时语言学将关注不被同一个集体意识感知的连续项目之间的联系；这些项目彼此替代，自身却不构成一个系统。5

Diachronic linguistics on the other hand will be concerned with connexions between sequences of items not perceived by the same collective consciousness, which replace one another without themselves constituting a system.5

考虑到《普通语言学教程》产生的影响，它的一个奇特之处是，书中从未出现“结构”这个术语。但上面引文中的“系统”完全足以代替它。系统如同结构，由共存的要素形成；它不可能由先后相继的要素形成。如今，要求把语言作为系统来研究，或许显得是个比较平常的规定，但那只是因为我们已如此习惯共时的观点。在索绪尔的时代，这个规定并不平常，因为它违背了语言学家的习惯，他们抵制这种规定所涉及的、将语言从历史中抽离出来的做法。

One of the oddities of the Course in General Linguistics, given the influence it has had, is that it nowhere contains the term ‘structure’. But ‘system’, as it occurs in the quotation above, will do well enough instead. A system, like a structure, is formed of elements which coexist; it cannot be formed of elements which are successive. It may today seem a relatively trivial stipulation that language be studied as a system, but that is only because we have become so accustomed to the synchronic point of view. In Saussure’s time it was not a trivial stipulation because it went against the grain among linguists, who resisted the abstraction which it involved of language from history.

系统性的、共时的视角，为语言研究摆脱缺乏雄心的原子论开辟了道路；那种原子论不过是积累语言事实，这些事实可以彼此关联，却从未得到充分的关系整合。结构主义从超越这类局限的地方开始。正如本研究将反复阐明的，它试图把语言事实理解为单一系统中的要素，而使它能够做到这一点的，正是从历时到共时的深刻视角转变。应当注意，索绪尔把对语言学家真正研究对象的这种整合，置于“集体意识”之中；集体意识是一种抽象，因为它不同于任何特定个人的意识，无论这个人对母语的知识或运用能力多么丰富。在结构主义意义上，语言是一个极其明确的抽象对象。它也属于社会——“集体意识”的另一名称——而绝不属于个人。

The systematic, synchronic perspective opens the way for language-study to be saved from an unambitious atomism, or mere accumulation of linguistic facts, which may be associated with one another but never fully correlated. Structuralism begins on the far side of such limitations as these. As will repeatedly become clear in the present study, it seeks to comprehend linguistic facts as elements in a single system, and it is the profound shift of perspective from the diachronic to the synchronic that enables it to do so. This integration of the linguist’s proper subject-matter is located by Saussure, be it noted, within the ‘collective consciousness’, which is an abstraction, since it is not commensurate with the consciousness of any given individual, however massively knowledgeable or competent in his native language. Language, in the Structuralist sense, is most emphatically an abstract object. It is also the possession of society, which is another name for the ‘collective consciousness’, never the possession of an individual.

在确立了语言学结构主义的第一原则之后——它也完全可以作为结构主义整体的第一原则——现在不妨转而看看美国结构主义，它同样坚持共时观点。但在较早的阶段，它也依附于一种主要是描述性和行为主义的语言解释。最积极的实践者完全不愿接受索绪尔式的思辨，相信语言的真理如果能找到，也只能在识别、分类所有现存语言形式的巨大努力完成之后找到。伦纳德·布龙菲尔德1933年出版的《语言论》，在美国和英国语言学家中的影响，相当于索绪尔《教程》在欧洲的影响；他拒绝把从语言描述进一步走向对语言的概括，视为自己职责的任何一部分：

Having laid down the first principle of linguistic Structuralism, which will serve very well as the first principle of Structuralism tout court, it will be as well now to look across at American Structuralism, which was equally committed to the synchronic point of view. But in its earlier stages it was also bound to a largely descriptive and behaviourist account of language. The most energetic practitioners of it had no truck with speculation of Saussure’s sort, believing that the truth about language would be found, if at all, only at the conclusion of a mighty effort to identify and classify all existing linguistic forms. Leonard Bloomfield, whose 1933 book on Language was as influential among linguists in America and Britain as Saussure’s Course was in Europe, refused to see it as any part of his mandate to move beyond the description of languages to generalization about them:

关于语言，唯一有用的概括是归纳性的概括。我们认为应当普遍存在的特征，可能在下一种进入我们研究范围的语言中就不存在了……无论如何，某些特征确实广泛存在，这一事实值得注意，也需要解释；当我们拥有关于许多语言的充分数据时，就必须重返普通语法的问题，解释这些相似与分歧，但那时展开的研究将不是思辨性的，而是归纳性的。6

The only useful generalisations about language are inductive generalizations. Features which we think ought to be universal may be absent from the very next language that becomes accessible . . . The fact that some features are, at any rate, widespread, is worthy of notice and calls for an explanation; when we have adequate data about many languages, we shall have to return to the problem of general grammar and to explain these similarities and divergences, but this study, when it comes, will be not speculative but inductive.6

这是最严苛的英美经验主义：只有全部事实到手之后，才值得提出理论。（经验主义本身也是一种理论，因此它决定哪些语言事实与研究相关、哪些不相关——这种承认如今已十分常见，但在布龙菲尔德的时代肯定不是如此。）布龙菲尔德的语言学常被称为“分布”语言学，其目标是完整描述各种词类在日常用法中出现的全部语境。因此，它的成果大体具有规范性和统计性。布龙菲尔德所谓“语言学的基本假定”是：“在每一个言语社群中，有些话语在形式和意义上是相同的”（斜体为布龙菲尔德所加）。7这是一个语言学家的最低限度纲领，他相信，对语言的正当研究不应关注“观念”，也不应关注集体或其他任何形式的人的意识。语言是世界中某种物理事件，是对环境刺激的反应；相应地，它的结构都位于表面，是一切已知语法实践的总和。

This is Anglo-Saxon empiricism at its most stringent; not until all the facts are in is it worth advancing a theory. (That empiricism is itself a theory, and as such determines which linguistic facts are relevant to the inquiry and which are not, is an admission commonplace enough today, but certainly not in Bloomfield’s day.) Bloomfieldian linguistics is often referred to as ‘distributional’, its aim being to establish a full description of all the contexts in which the various categories of words can be found in everyday usage. Its achievements are thus broadly normative and statistical. What Bloomfield calls the ‘fundamental assumption of linguistics’ is ‘that in every speech-community some utterances are alike in form and meaning’ (Bloomfield’s italics).7 This is the minimalist programme of a linguist who believed that the proper study of language should be conducted without concern for ‘ideas’ or for human consciousness, collective or otherwise. Language was a certain kind of physical event in the world, a response to stimuli from the environment, and its structures, accordingly, were all on the surface, being the sum total of all known grammatical practices.

布龙菲尔德把语言研究等同于语法研究，这揭示了美国结构主义与索绪尔式结构主义之间一个极其显著的区别。《普通语言学教程》对语法谈得出奇地少；索绪尔在进行那些后来成为此书基础的讲课时，似乎尚未充分思考语法。布龙菲尔德及其他美国结构主义者把语法视为语言的核心特征，索绪尔则选择把意义生成的能力视为核心。在1923年发表的一篇《教程》书评中，布龙菲尔德强调了这种分歧：“具体说来，我与德·索绪尔的主要区别，在于把分析建立在句子而非词的基础上。”8此后一直如此：美国结构主义关注句法，索绪尔式结构主义关注语词符号或词。句法结构的优点，是比索绪尔在语言中发现的高度抽象的结构更为显眼，因为句法就是结构，是一种因语言而异的约定安排，词凭借它进入彼此间各种形式关系。别人对我们说话时，我们听到句法，阅读时我们看到句法。它的结构显眼，是因为它们在语言的表面；转到诺姆·乔姆斯基对结构主义的贡献时，我将再回到这一点。

One very striking difference between American Structuralism and the Saussurean kind is brought out by Bloomfield’s equation of the study of language with the study of grammar. The Course in General Linguistics has remarkably little to say about grammar, to which Saussure seems not fully to have turned his mind at the time when he gave the lectures on which the book is based. Where Bloomfield and the other American Structuralists see grammar as the central feature of language, Saussure chooses to see rather its power of signification. In a review of the Course published in 1923 Bloomfield stresses this divergence: ‘In detail, I should differ from de Saussure chiefly in basing my analysis on the sentence rather than on the word.’8 And so it has remained: American Structuralism is concerned with syntax, Saussurean Structuralism with the verbal sign or word. Syntactic structures have the advantage of being more conspicuous than the highly abstract structures Saussure finds in language, since syntax is structure, being a conventional arrangement, varying from language to language, whereby words enter into various formal relations with one another. We hear syntax when spoken to and see it when we read. Its structures are conspicuous because they are on the surface of language, a point I shall come back to when turning to the contribution made by Noam Chomsky to Structuralism.

不过，在此之前，必须引入索绪尔的另一个关键区分：langue与parole之间的区分，或者如它们通常被译成英语的那样，“language”（语言）与“speech”（言语）之间的区分。9这一区分承接了语言研究的共时轴与历时轴的区分：langue（语言系统）是索绪尔对储存在“集体意识”中的语言“系统”或总体所用的术语。语言的语法显然构成这个系统的很大部分，系统也包括语言的全部词汇或词库。因此，语言系统包含一份语言要素的完整目录，以及它们的组合规则。另一方面，parole（言语）是个人对自己生来置身其中的语言的全部资源的运用。它是实际的话语，可以是口头的，也可以是书面的。如果语言系统是结构，言语就是事件。前者是抽象的，后者是实在的。没有这些事件，我们就不可能了解或研究结构；而没有结构，事件就会没有形式，也没有意义：两者完全相互依赖。

Before that, however, another of Saussure’s cardinal distinctions must be brought in: that between langue and parole or, as they are usually given in English, ‘language’ and ‘speech’.9 This distinction follows on from that between the synchronic and diachronic axes of language-study: langue is the term Saussure gives to the ‘system’ or totality of language stored in the ‘collective consciousness’. The grammar of the language, obviously, makes up a large part of that system, which also includes the full vocabulary or lexicon of the language. The langue thus comprises a full catalogue of the elements of a language together with the rules for their combination. Parole on the other hand is the use which individuals make of the total resources of the language they are born into. It is actual utterance, in speech or in writing. If langue is a structure then parole is an event. The first is an abstraction, the second is real. Without the events there would be no way in which we could know of or investigate the structure, and without the structure the event would be formless and without meaning: the two are wholly interdependent.

语言系统与言语之间的对照贯穿结构主义的各个领域，有时以更现代的方式表述为“代码”与“信息”之间的对照（不过，把语言系统等同于“代码”可能产生误导，因为语言系统的特征是第一性的，向所有人开放，而非像大多数代码那样是第二性的、使用受到限制）。这首先是集体与个人之间的对照。结构主义十分强调这一对照，并必然以牺牲个人为代价支持集体。对索绪尔而言，语言系统是一种社会纽带：

The contrast between langue and parole is one which extends everywhere in Structuralism, sometimes expressed in a more modern way as the contrast between the ‘code’ and the ‘message’ (although it can be misleading to equate the language-system with a ‘code’ when it is characterized by being primary and open to all, rather than secondary, like most codes, and restricted in its use). It is, before all else, a contrast between the collective and the individual. This is a contrast of which Structuralism makes much, championing as it is bound to do the collective at the expense of the individual. For Saussure the langue is a social bond:

它是社群成员通过言语实践积累起来的一笔储备，是潜在地存在于每一个大脑中、或更确切地说存在于一群个体的大脑中的语法系统；因为语言在任何单一个体中都从不完整，只有在集体中才完整地存在。10

It is a fund accumulated by the members of the community through the practice of speech, a grammatical system existing potentially in every brain, or more exactly in the brains of a group of individuals; for the language is never complete in any single individual, but exists perfectly only in the collectivity.10

语言是一种结构，在使用者身上只能得到部分而不完美的实现；这一事实提出了语言系统与言语、或系统与事件如何衔接的关键问题。系统怎么会在我们大脑中获得足够充分的表征，使我们能够在完全没有意识到它的情况下，日常运用语言时利用它呢？索绪尔本人并未正视这个问题，但乔姆斯基却把它放在了当代语言学的核心位置。乔姆斯基肯定不希望被看成结构主义者，因为他作为语言学家的职业生涯，一直在积极而富有成果地反对布龙菲尔德等人的美国结构主义。但我们仍可以把他归入结构主义，因为乔姆斯基语言学从头到尾都是结构性的，而且对索绪尔提出的一些问题具有重要意义。11

The fact that a language is a structure which is realized only partially and imperfectly in those who use it raises the key problem of the articulation of langue with parole or system with event. How is it that the system comes to be sufficiently represented in our brains so that we can, without being at all aware of it, exploit it in our daily use of language? This is not a problem Saussure himself faces up to but it is one which has been placed at the heart of contemporary linguistics by Chomsky. Chomsky would certainly not wish to be thought of as a Structuralist because his career as a linguist has been spent in vigorous and fruitful reaction against the Structuralism of Bloomfield and others in America. But he may be claimed for Structuralism because Chomskyan linguistics is structural through and through and bears importantly on some of the issues raised by Saussure.11

像索绪尔一样，乔姆斯基力图超越从日常语言使用中获得的数据，以构建一种理论，在他的情形中就是语法理论。他发展了一种语言学实践，宣称它与自己年轻时在美国语言学家中盛行的结构主义“在质上”不同；那种结构主义收集并分类事实，却克制自己，不作理论解释。因为它在观点上是行为主义的——布龙菲尔德尤其狂热——它把个人的语言使用视为习惯性的：用布龙菲尔德的话说，是“他所听过的别人说的话的一种复合结果”。12（这里“复合”一词回避了乔姆斯基后来提出的所有有趣问题。）根据这种言语模型，我们知道该说什么，是因为听过别人说话，可以通过重复，或通过“类比”在相似情况下听到的话来表达。布龙菲尔德语言学要求彻底排除“心智”，并以一种范围特别广的“习惯”概念代替它，仿佛我们作为语言使用者，除了对自身所处情境作出反应外，什么也做不了。

Like Saussure, Chomsky strives to go beyond the data available to him from everday language-use, to construct a theory of, in his case, grammar. He has elaborated a practice of linguistics which he declares to be ‘qualitatively’ different from the Structuralism that prevailed among American linguists in his young days – a Structuralism which collected facts and classified them, but forbore to interpret them theoretically. Because it was behaviouristic in its outlook – fanatically so in the case of Bloomfield – it regarded the individual’s use of language as habitual: in Bloomfield’s words, as ‘a composite result of what he has heard other people say’.12 (The word ‘composite’ there begs all the interesting questions which Chomsky was to raise.) According to this model of parole we know what to say because we have listened to other people and can proceed either by repetition or by ‘analogy’ with what we have heard in similar circumstances. Bloomfieldian linguistics calls for the complete elimination of ‘mind’ and its replacement by a peculiarly extensive notion of ‘habit’, as if we were, as language-users, incapable of doing more than respond to the situations we find ourselves in.

乔姆斯基努力把“心智”恢复到语言学中，方法是展示布龙菲尔德那样的模型，如何完全不能解释我们运用语言时无限的创造力。他论证说，我们不断用自己的语言生成此前从未听过的句子，这些句子未必紧密取决于说出它们的情境，也无法用“类比”这种薄弱的概念解释。也就是说，语言远不止是习惯，按照行为主义的刺激与习得反应模型，永远无法令人满意地解释儿童期的语言习得。

Chomsky has worked to restore ‘mind’ to linguistics by showing how starkly a model such as Bloomfield’s fails to account for the infinite creativity of our language-use. He argues that we are constantly producing sentences in our language which we have never heard before, which may not be closely conditional on the circumstances in which they are uttered, and which cannot be accounted for by any weak concept like ‘analogy’. Language, that is, is far more than habit and could never be satisfactorily acquired in childhood on the behaviourist model of stimulus and learned response.

对乔姆斯基来说，解释语言习得是结构语言学家所面对的最大挑战。没有人怀疑语言中存在结构——我们的句法中显然有表层结构；也没有人怀疑，成功使用语言表明掌握了这些结构，尽管我们可能完全无法描述每次说话或写作时所遵守的句法规则。但乔姆斯基认为，这些表层结构还不够，因为它们有时有歧义，能够得到不止一种有意义的解释。这提供了一条线索，指向语言结构的另一层次，即乔姆斯基多年来使之颇为知名的“深层结构”；不过由于“深层”一词附带了他不愿意要的“深奥”意味，他如今已不喜欢这个术语。“深层结构”没有歧义，只能接受一种语义解释，并通过乔姆斯基所谓“转换”或“生成”语法的规则，被转化为表层结构。用他的话说，这些转换“依赖于结构……意思是，它们凭借词被组织为短语的方式，应用于词串”。13也就是说，转换取决于句法组织。句子“Mary has lived in Princeton”（玛丽在普林斯顿住过）转化为疑问句“Has Mary lived in Princeton?”（玛丽在普林斯顿住过吗？），用乔姆斯基的术语来说，是通过把一个名词短语（“Mary”）与助动词的第一个要素（“has”）互换来完成的。依照乔姆斯基，任何人类语言都不允许以这种“结构依赖”方式之外的方式进行此类转换。

To account for the acquisition of language is for Chomsky the greatest challenge open to the structural linguist. No one questions that there are structures in language – surface structures are apparent in our syntax; or that to use language successfully demonstrates a command of these, though we might be quite unable to offer any description of the syntactical rules we are observing every time we speak or write. But these surface structures are not enough, Chomsky suggests, because sometimes they are ambiguous or capable of more than one meaningful interpretation. This is the clue to another level of linguistic structure, the ‘deep structures’ to which Chomsky has over the years given a certain celebrity, though he no longer likes the term ‘deep’ in view of its unwanted connotations of profundity. The ‘deep structures’ are not ambiguous, they are open to only one semantic interpretation, and they are turned into surface structures by the rules of what Chomsky calls a ‘transformational’ or ‘generative’ grammar. These transformations are, in his phrase, ‘structure-dependent . . . in the sense that they apply to a string of words by virtue of the organisation of these words into phrases.’13 That is to say that the transformation depends on the syntactical organization. The sentence ‘Mary has lived in Princeton’ is transformed into the interrogative ‘Has Mary lived in Princeton?’ by, in Chomskyan terms, interchanging a noun phrase (‘Mary’) with the first element of the auxiliary (‘has’). According to Chomsky no human language allows of any such transformations except in this ‘structure-dependent’ fashion.

这里无须深入探讨这些转换规则的形式或运作方式：乔姆斯基语法不是适合外行追求的学问，因为它很快就变得代数化，而且极其复杂。在这部语言学结构主义思想的概览中，我们关注的是乔姆斯基提出的语言习得模型，与索绪尔在语言系统和日常语言事件之间作出的区分，二者间的密切联系。

There is no need here to go into the form that these transformational rules take, or the manner in which they operate: Chomskyan grammar is not a pursuit for the lay person because it soon grows algebraic and extremely complex. What concerns us, in this conspectus of Structuralist ideas in linguistics, is the close connection between Chomsky’s proposed model of language acquisition and Saussure’s distinction between the language-system and everyday language events.

我们很容易把索绪尔的两个术语——语言系统与言语——等同于乔姆斯基为了标明其系统与事件之区分而提出的两个术语：“语言能力”与“语言运用”。不过，一眼就能看出，索绪尔的语言系统与乔姆斯基的“语言能力”之间有一个重要区别：前者被明确规定为属于社会或语言社群的东西，后者则仍属于个人。索绪尔多少忽略了个人如何掌握这个本质上属于集体的系统的问题，乔姆斯基却非常直接地处理它。为了说明个人的语言创造力这一事实，他假定存在关于整个语言系统的“默会知识”，一种“内化”的语法，使我们每个人都能生成潜在无限多的新句子。在旧行为主义模型的刺激与反应之间，或在后来受计算机影响的模型的“输入”与“输出”之间，乔姆斯基插入了自己的中介，即一种人类特有的先天心智结构。唯有这种结构，才能回答那个否则无法解决的问题：我们每个人如何都能相当充分地、尽管从不完全地掌握语言系统：

It is tempting to equate Saussure’s pair of terms, langue and parole, with the pair which Chomsky introduced to mark his own distinction between system and event: ‘competence’ and ‘performance’. One can see at a glance, however, that there is a significant difference between Saussure’s langue and Chomsky’s ‘competence’, in that where the first is specified as something belonging to society or the language-community, the second remains the possession of the individual. Saussure more or less ignores the question of how the individual acquires a mastery of the essentially collective system, whereas Chomsky tackles it very directly. In order to explain the fact of the individual’s linguistic creativity he posits a ‘tacit knowledge’ of the language-system as a whole, an ‘internalized’ grammar which enables each of us to generate a potentially infinite number of new sentences. Between the stimulus and the response of the old behaviourist model, or the ‘input’ and ‘output’ of subsequent, computer-influenced models, Chomsky inserts his own intermediary in the form of an innate mental structure specific to humankind. This structure alone can answer the otherwise insoluble question of how we can each have a considerable, if never a complete mastery of the langue:

具体说来，我们必须问：儿童如何能够根据有限的现有数据，构建一种我们不得不认为他拥有的语法，具有特定的规则选择和安排，以及运用这些规则的限制性原则？换句话说，一个能够复制这种成就的学习模型，其内部结构必须是什么样的？显然，我们必须尝试以满足两类经验条件的方式，来刻画先天结构。第一，我们必须把一种足够丰富的结构，作为先天属性归于有机体，以解释这样一个事实：所假定的语法，是在既定的数据接触条件下习得的；第二，我们不能把一种丰富得与已知语言多样性不相容的结构归于有机体。我们不能把英语知识作为先天属性归于儿童，因为我们知道，他既能学英语，也能学日语。我们也不能仅仅赋予他形成联想、或应用结构语言学分析程序的能力，因为……这些程序得出的结构，并不是我们必须假定的生成语法结构。14

Specifically, we must ask how, on the basis of the limited data available to him, the child is able to construct a grammar of the sort that we are led to ascribe to him, with its particular choice and arrangement of rules and with the restrictive principles of application of such rules. What, in other words, must be the internal structure of a learning model that can duplicate this achievement? Evidently, we must try to characterise innate structure in such a way as to meet two kinds of empirical conditions. First, we must attribute to the organism, as an innate property, a structure rich enough to account for the fact that the postulated grammar is acquired on the basis of the given conditions of access to data; second, we must not attribute to the organism a structure so rich as to be incompatible with the known diversity of languages. We cannot attribute knowledge of English to the child as an innate property, because we know that he can learn Japanese as well as English. We cannot attribute to him merely the ability to form associations, or to apply the analytical procedures of structural linguistics, because . . . the structures they yield are not those that we must postulate as generative grammars.14

乔姆斯基的先天心智结构曾遭遇强烈反对，尤其当它被重新表述为语言使用者关于其语言的“默会知识”时；因为一种默会的、而且我们大多数人在任何条件下都无法使之明确的知识，似乎像一种多余的奢侈品，即使我们承认乔姆斯基所采用的、哲学家认可的“知道如此”与“知道如何”之间的区分。（作为假说，这种语言“知识”会受到维特根斯坦那个令人困惑的问题的挑战：“假如有人问：‘你什么时候知道怎样下棋？一直都知道吗？还是只在走一步棋的时候？而且每走一步都知道全部棋艺吗？知道怎样下棋竟只需如此短的时间，而下一盘棋却长得多，这多么奇怪！’”15）不过，通过坚持必须以结构性的“语言能力”来理解语言使用事实，乔姆斯基给语言研究带来了它本来不会拥有的活力和普及度。他的结构主义版本，对我们如何看待人类能力有重大意义，也增强了各种结构主义共同的信念：人类心智是结构主义的，因为它不可能是别的；它在分析对象中找到的结构，在某种意义上对应于先天的心智结构，其模式令人想起莱布尼茨的“预定和谐”。的确，乔姆斯基曾用“预设”一词，描述婴儿首次接触自身社群语言时的心智；意思是，心智在生物学上适于习得任何语言，因此习得一种特定语言，代表的不是能力增长，而是能力受到限制。

Chomsky’s innate mental structure has met with strong objections in its time, especially when it has been reformulated as the language-user’s ‘tacit knowledge’ of his language, since knowledge that is tacit, and which most of us would be incapable of making explicit under any conditions, seems like a luxury, even granting Chomsky the distinction philosophers recognize between ‘knowing-that’ and ‘knowing-how’. (As a hypothesis, such a ‘knowledge’ of language is susceptible of the perplexing question put by Wittgenstein: ‘Suppose it were asked: ‘‘When do you know how to play chess? All the time? or just while you are making a move? And the whole of chess during each move? How queer that knowing how to play chess should take such a short time, and a game so much longer!’’’15) However, by insisting on the need for a structural ‘competence’ to make sense of the facts of language-use, Chomsky brought to the study of language a dynamism and a popularity it would otherwise have lacked. His version of Structuralism has considerable implications for the view that we take of human capacities, as well as strengthening the belief common to all versions of Structuralism that the human mind is Structuralist because it cannot be anything else, that the structures it finds in what it analyses match in some sense innate mental structures, in a pattern reminiscent of Leibniz’s ‘pre-established harmony’. Chomsky, indeed, has used the term ‘pre-set’ to describe the mind of the human infant in its first exposure to the language of its community, meaning that it is biologically fitted to acquire any language at all and that the acquisition of a particular language thus represents a restriction of its capacity, not a growth.

乔姆斯基沿袭美国结构主义的传统，把句子视为语言的基本单位，对词则表现出相对较少的兴趣。索绪尔式结构主义恰好相反。它追随索绪尔本人的榜样，把注意力固定在作为语言符号发挥作用的词上。索绪尔式结构主义者给语言下的定义，是由语言符号构成的系统；为了便于论证，我们可以说，这里“词”与“符号”两个术语可以互换。16

Chomsky has followed in the tradition of American Structuralism in taking the sentence to be the basic unit of language and in showing relatively little interest in the word. With Saussurean Structuralism the reverse is the case. Following the example of Saussure himself, it has fixed its attention on the word in its function as linguistic sign. The definition of language offered by a Saussurean Structuralist would be that it is a system formed of linguistic signs, and for the sake of argument we can say that the terms ‘word’ and ‘sign’ are here interchangeable.16

然而，如果符号是语言系统的基本要素，它们却不是简单要素。无论采用什么定义，语言都是声音与意义的关联，而在索绪尔对语言符号的分析中，这种关联具有首要地位。他把符号分析为两个方面或两面：语音或声学的方面，以及语义的方面。符号既是音，又是义——不过使用“语音”、“声学”或“声音”等术语必须小心，因为它们只适用于口头形式的符号；书面形式的符号既不必被读出，也不必被听见。在作出这一保留说明之后——它将在本书关于后结构主义的最后一章中，重新显示全部力量——我仍将追随索绪尔，把符号的双重方面描述为声学的与概念的。应当注意，这两方面都是心智性的，理由很充分：它们不可分割；符号是一个统一体，其两个方面完全相互依赖而存在。没有意义的声音不属于语言，它用现代传播理论家找到的那个贴切说法来说，不过是“噪声”；而没有声音（或没有作为书写的物质表现）的意义，也是不可能的。

If signs, however, are the basic elements of the language system, they are not simple elements. Language, by any definition, is a correlation of sounds with meanings and in Saussure’s analysis of the linguistic sign this correlation is paramount. He analyses the sign into two aspects or faces: a phonetic or acoustic aspect and a semantic one. The sign is both sound and sense – though one needs to be careful in using terms such as ‘phonetic’, ‘acoustic’ or ‘sound’ since these apply to signs only in their spoken form; in their written form they need be neither pronounced nor heard. Given which caveat – it will return in full force in the last chapter of this book, on post-Structuralism – I shall continue to follow Saussure in characterizing the twin aspects of the sign as acoustic and conceptual. Both these aspects, be it noted, are mental, for the good reason that they are indissoluble; the sign is a unity and its two aspects exist in total dependence one on the other. Sound without sense is not a part of language, it is, in the telling description found by modern theorists of communication, mere ‘noise’; and sense without sound (or without its material manifestation as writing) is impossible.

索绪尔为符号的两个方面找到了signifiant和signifié两个术语，或如它们通常被译为英语的那样，“signifier”（能指）与“signified”（所指）。17符号、能指、所指这一套术语，或许是索绪尔给予结构主义最有影响的礼物，因为它提供了分析意义生成过程的手段，使我们能够方便地区分符号显现的一面与抽象的一面。一旦成功理解，这一区分就在语言分析、文学分析及其他各种文本分析中，提供了一个无与伦比的工具。的确，罗兰·巴特曾努力判断，那些在大众心目中被贴上“结构主义者”标签的思想家，究竟可能有什么共同之处；他得出的结论是，“大概是严肃地诉诸意义生成的词汇……看看谁在使用能指与所指、共时与历时，你就知道结构主义的事物观是否已经成形”。18这里“严肃”一词值得强调，因为并非所有对索绪尔词汇的诉诸，都配得上这个形容。

For the two aspects of the sign Saussure found the terms signifiant and signifié or, as they are usually translated into English, ‘signifier’ and ‘signified’.17 This terminological complex of Sign, Signifier and Signified is perhaps Saussure’s most influential gift to Structuralism, providing as it does the wherewithal to analyse the process of signification and to distinguish readily between the manifest and the abstract faces of the sign. Once it has been successfully understood, this distinction provides an unrivalled tool in linguistic as well as literary and other kinds of textual analysis. Struggling, indeed, to decide what it was that all those thinkers labelled in the popular mind as Structuralists might have in common, Roland Barthes concluded that it was ‘probably the serious resort to the lexicon of signification . . . look to see who uses signifier and signified, synchrony and diachrony, and you will know whether the structuralist view of things has taken shape.’18 The word ‘serious’ is worth stressing here, since not all resorts to Saussure’s lexicon qualify as that.

因此，索绪尔式的符号是一个抽象对象，不应把它同它所代表的东西、同世界中的某个东西混淆。这个东西就是哲学家所谓的指涉对象。结构主义者并不总能如他们应当做的那样，坚持区分符号的概念方面——即所指——与指涉对象；令人惊讶的是，索绪尔本人在《教程》文本中似乎也混淆了它们。他在书中确立的有关语言符号的第一原则，是它具有“任意性”；他的意思是，符号的形式并非由它所代表的东西、或指涉对象所决定，或由它提供“理据”。证据是，不同语言对同一个指涉对象有极为多样的符号；由此可以得出，这些符号本来可以采取不同于实际形式的另一形式。不过必须认识到，我们语言中符号的任意性，并不使我们有改变它们的自由：它们是任意的，却也由一种共识固定下来，这种共识在语言数百年的历史中积累形成，我们作为个人无力改变。同一种动物，在英吉利海峡一侧被称为horse，另一侧被称为cheval，这一事实展示了任意性。这是具有共同指涉对象的两个符号，但我们不能、也绝不应由此断言，它们是具有共同所指的两个能指，因为horse的所指存在于英语社群的“集体意识”中，cheval的所指存在于法语社群的“集体意识”中；两个所指都不会站在一片田野里。

The Saussurean sign, then, is an abstract object, it is not to be confused with whatever it is the sign of, with something in the world. This something is what philosophers know as the referent. Structuralists do not always keep hold as they should of the distinction between the conceptual aspect of the sign, or signified, and the referent, and remarkably, Saussure himself appears to confuse them in the text of the Course. The first principle which he lays down there concerning the linguistic sign is that it is ‘arbitrary’, by which he means that its form is not determined or ‘motivated’ by the thing it is the sign of, or referent. The proof of this is the enormous variety of signs to be found in different languages for the same referent, from which one can conclude that these signs could have taken a different form from that which they in fact have; though it has to be realized that the arbitrariness of the signs of our language does not set us free to change them: they are arbitrary but also fixed, by a consensus which has accrued over the centuries of a language’s history and which we are powerless as individuals to change. A demonstration of arbitrariness is the fact that the animal which on one side of the English Channel is referred to as horse is referred to as cheval on the other side. These are two signs with a common referent, but we cannot and must not conclude from that that they are two signifiers with a common signified, because the signified of horse is to be found in the ‘collective consciousness’ of the English language-community and the signified of cheval in that of the French language-community; neither signified is to be found standing in a field.

如果把所指与指涉对象当成同义词，就会严重误解结构主义的性质；《普通语言学教程》文本中有一处正是如此，其中写道：“所指‘牛’在边界一侧以boeuf为能指，在另一侧则以Ochs为能指。”19这种错误表述，同索绪尔自己引入的术语相矛盾，是后来其他人所犯许多类似错误的典型。索绪尔的这种混淆，肯定可以归因于《教程》编辑者编成文本的方式：他们比勘汇集了出自其思想演变不同阶段的材料；20但在那些重复同样错误的人那里，我们看到的是别的东西，即我们所有人在维持符号的概念方面作为一种抽象、并使之不与其互补的声学或书写方面分离时，都面临相当大的困难。一种观念论把我们引入错误：它让我们拆开符号的两个方面，并认为概念方面先于声学或书写方面。许多人假定所指先于能指而存在，或意义正在“等待”表达。这种假定的结果，就是把必然储存在某种前语言库藏中的所指，同世界中无限多的潜在指涉对象等同起来；后者无疑在人类以语言赋予它们意义之前就已存在。这是在后结构主义和雅克·德里达的著作中十分突出的一个问题，我将在适当之处再谈它。

Grave misunderstandings of the nature of Structuralism follow if signifieds and referents are taken to be synonymous, as they are at one point in the text of the Course in General Linguistics, where one can read that ‘the signified ‘‘ox’’ has as its signifier boeuf on one side of the frontier, but Ochs on the other side’.19 This mis-statement of the case, which contradicts the very terminology Saussure has himself introduced, is typical of many similar ones made since by others. In Saussure’s case, the confusion can certainly be put down to the way in which the text of the Course was constructed by its editors, collating material dating from different stages in the evolution of his ideas,20 but in the case of others who have repeated the same error, we have evidence of something else, which is the considerable difficulty we all face in preserving the conceptual aspect of the sign as an abstraction indissociable from its complementary acoustic or graphic aspect. We are led into error by a certain idealism, whereby we dissociate the two aspects of the sign and take the conceptual aspect to have precedence over the acoustic or graphic aspect. Many people assume that signifieds pre-exist signifiers, or that meanings ‘await’ expression. The effect of that assumption is to assimilate the signifieds, stored up as they must be in some pre-verbal repository, to the infinite number of potential referents in the world, which undeniably do pre-exist their human investment by language. This is a question that comes very much to the fore in post-Structuralism and in the work of Jacques Derrida, and I shall return to it at the appropriate point.

语言符号的“任意性”比人们有时意识到的更为根本，因为它确立了语言相对于现实的自主性。自然语言之间差别极大，不仅表现在词汇上，如boeuf与Ochs的例子，而且也表现在语法结构上。结构主义的语言观，从根本上反对那种古老却仍颇流行的语言观：它把语言看成一张名称表，或对周围世界内容逐项进行的约定命名。只有把等待命名的事物清单，限制在简单明了的实物或动作类别之内，才能有说服力地主张语言是一张名称表。如果语言全是名词和动词，这一论点或许成立。但因为语言包含其他类别的符号，而且这些符号在使用时彼此进入复杂的逻辑关系，名称表的论点就变得完全不充分了。

The ‘arbitrariness’ of the linguistic sign is a more radical matter than is sometimes realized, because it establishes the autonomy of language in respect of reality. Natural languages differ very markedly from each other not only in their vocabulary, as in the case of boeuf and Ochs, but also in their grammatical structure. The Structuralist point of view in language is fundamentally opposed to that old but still quite popular conception of language which sees it as a nomenclature or a conventional item-by-item naming of the contents of the world around us. It is possible to argue plausibly that a language is a nomenclature only for as long as one restricts the inventory of things waiting to be named to straightforward categories of physical objects or actions. If a language were all nouns and verbs, the argument might hold. But because languages contain other categories of sign, and because the signs enter into complex logical relations with one another when they are used, the nomenclature argument becomes wholly inadequate.

名称表论者赋予语言之外的世界优先地位，而结构主义倾向于颠倒这种优先性，提出：远不是世界决定语言的秩序，而是语言决定世界的秩序。因为没有两种语言以完全相同的方式组织世界——任何尝试把一种语言译为另一种语言的人，很快就会认识到这种差异——也就没有两个语言社群能以完全相同的方式理解世界。约翰·帕斯莫尔用一句格言精巧地概括了“任意性”的这一深刻后果：“语言因以不同方式作区分而有所不同。”21这是从结构主义之前的时期所做的全部语言比较工作，以及人类学家在与自身文化截然不同的文化中所做的语言田野工作中，应当学到的首要教训。美国结构语言学大量依靠人们从美洲印第安语言的语法结构中获得的知识；这些结构在英语使用者看来常常极其古怪，因为它们选择表达的形式关系，不是我们所习惯的。这种比较性的结构主义不预先假定任何一种语言结构优于另一种，只认为所有结构彼此存在或大或小的差别。不存在一种“主导”结构，可以用来评价其他“次等”或更“原始”的结构。用索绪尔曾予以应有敬意的、美国第一位伟大语言学家的话说：

Structuralism tends to reverse the precedence which a nomenclaturist accords to the world outside language, by proposing that far from the world determining the order of our language, our language determines the order of the world. Because no two languages order the world in quite the same way – a disparity soon appreciated by anyone who tries to translate from one language into another – no two language-communities can understand the world in quite the same way. This profound consequence of ‘arbitrariness’ is neatly summed up by John Passmore in the dictum, ‘Languages differ by differentiating differently’.21 This is the prime lesson to be learned from all the comparative work done on languages in the period which preceded Structuralism and of the linguistic field- work done by anthropologists in cultures strikingly different from their own. American structural linguistics relied heavily on the great deal that had been learned of the grammatical structure of American-Indian languages, which often strikes an English mind as most eccentric, because the formal relations which it chooses to express are not those we are used to. Structuralism of this comparative kind does not presuppose that any one linguistic structure is superior to any other, only that all are greatly or slightly different from one another. There is no ‘master’ structure by which to evaluate other, ‘lesser’ or more ‘primitive’ structures. In the words of the first great American linguist, to whom Saussure paid due tribute:

没有任何关系是语言必须表达的；只有某些关系比其他关系更自然地被提示出来，表达它们具有更大的实用价值；至于这些关系是什么，我们只能从对语言的一般研究中获知；我们自身受教育而形成的偏好，并不是通向它们的可靠指引。22

There are no relations to which a language must necessarily give expression; there are only certain ones which are more naturally suggested, of which the expression is more practically valuable than others: and what these are, we can learn only from the general study of languages; our own educated preferences are no trustworthy guide to them.22

惠特尼在这里坚持的相对主义，比其他结构语言学家更为极端，后者不会承认“没有任何关系是语言必须表达的”。例如，在较早一派的美国结构主义者中，爱德华·萨丕尔的观点总体上最接近索绪尔；他认为，语法结构所表达的某些关系是“必不可少的”，因而大概也是普遍的：

Whitney here maintains a relativism more extreme than that of other structural linguists, who would not concede that ‘there are no relations to which a language must give expression’. Edward Sapir, for example, whose views generally come closest among the earlier school of American Structuralists to those of Saussure, holds that certain of the relations expressed in grammatical structure are ‘essential’ and therefore presumably universal:

如果我想就一个农夫、一只小鸭和杀的动作，传达一个可理解的意思……我就找不到办法回避究竟是谁在杀这个问题。没有任何已知语言能够或确实会回避它，正如任何语言都不可能在不使用具体概念符号的情况下说出东西。23

If I wish to communicate an intelligible idea about a farmer, a duckling, and the act of killing . . . I can find no way of dodging the issue as to who is doing the killing. There is no known language that can or does dodge it, any more than it succeeds in saying something without the use of symbols for the concrete concepts.23

无论施动者与动作这两个关系范畴，是否如萨丕尔主张的那样具有普遍性，我们都可以说，普遍的是对关系、或对结构的需要。不可能存在非结构性的语言或思想。

Whether or not the relational categories of agent and action are universal, as Sapir claims them to be, one can say that what is universal is the need for relations, or for a structure. There can be no language or thought that is not structural.

人们可以、也很容易在任何结构之外，假定人类心智中有一个不可接近、未分化的过程，语言是它的表现，并且实际上给予它结构。索绪尔作出了这一假定：

It is possible and tempting to posit, beyond any structure, an inaccessible and undifferentiated process in the human mind of which language is the manifestation and to which it actually gives a structure. Saussure makes this assumption:

从心理上说，撇开用词语表达这一点，我们的思想不过是一团模糊、没有形状的东西。哲学家和语言学家向来一致认为，如果没有符号，我们就无法以清楚而恒定的方式区分任何两个观念。思想本身就像一团旋动的云，其中没有任何形状在本质上是确定的。在引入语言结构之前，没有预先确立的观念，也没有任何明确区分的东西。24

Psychologically, setting aside its expression in words, our thought is simply a vague, shapeless mass. Philosophers and linguists have always agreed that were it not for signs, we should be incapable of differentiating any two ideas in a clear and constant way. In itself thought is like a swirling cloud, where no shape is intrinsically determinate. No ideas are established in advance, and nothing is distinct, before the introduction of linguistic structure.24

像这样试图借助隐喻抵达语言之外，是轻率的，因为用语言来做这件事，只能产生一个悖论：把据称无形式的东西，作出系统性或结构性的表述。不过，假定有一个无形式的底层，假定思想多少与语言、或与那些代表思想被分节表达的可交流观念相对立，至少能让人以动态眼光看待语言结构。在这一模型中，语言有某种东西可加以结构化；“结构”一词本身开始作为动词使用，并引导我们走向某些结构主义者钟爱的“结构化”（structuration）观念（按照皮亚杰，它是人类智力的基本能力）。

It is rash to strive to reach, by means of metaphors, beyond language in this way, when by doing so in language one can produce only a paradox: a systematic or structural formulation of the supposedly formless. However, the hypothesis of a formless substratum, of thought as somehow opposed to language, or to the communicable ideas which represent the articulation of thought, enables one at least to take a dynamic view of linguistic structure. In this model, language has something to structure, and the term ‘structure’ itself enters service as a verb and leads us on to the notion, dear to some Structuralists, of structuration (the basic faculty of the human intelligence according to Piaget).

语言相对于现实享有的显著自主性，来自其系统性质。我们应当把语言系统看成作为整体、而非以可分离的要素适用于现实。若把它理解为词与世界之间大量一一对应关系的总和，就完全误解了它。正如我们所见，系统的构成要素所采取的形式，并非由其指涉对象决定，而是由它们在系统中占据的位置决定。每个要素，正如索绪尔强调的，都是“一种形式，而不是一种实体”。25这或许难以理解，但它是符号及整个语言的“任意性”的根本后果。符号是形式，因为作为同一个完整系统的成员，它们彼此决定。它们在系统中的位置决定了索绪尔所谓的“价值”，他谨慎地把价值同“指称意义”区分开来。后一个术语被他保留用来指符号在指涉行为中对世界的实际应用；它是语言与现实之间的关系。另一方面，符号的“价值”是一种内部关系，取决于该符号同语言中其他符号的多重关系。

The remarkable degree of autonomy which language enjoys in respect of reality follows from its systematic nature. We should think of the language-system as being applicable to reality as a whole instead of in its separable elements. To conceive of it as the sum of very many one-to-one correspondences of words and world is to misconceive it utterly. The form taken by the constituent elements of the system is determined not by their referents, as we have seen, but by the place they occupy within the system. Each element is, as Saussure insists, ‘a form, not a substance’.25 This is perhaps hard to grasp, but it is the radical consequence of the ‘arbitrariness’ of signs and of language as a whole. Signs are forms because they determine one another, as fellow members of the one integral system. It is their place within the system which decides what Saussure calls their ‘value’, which he is careful to distinguish from their ‘signification’. This latter term he reserves for the actual application of a sign to the world, in an act of reference; it is a relation between language and reality. The ‘value’ of a sign, on the other hand, is an internal relation, depending as it does on that sign’s multiple relations with other signs of the language.

索绪尔在这里显然实践的是词的语言学，而不是句子的语言学；但他关于语言符号本质上是形式而非实体的断言，可以从萨丕尔那里获得佐证，后者无疑是句子和语法的语言学家。对萨丕尔而言，同样“词只是一种形式，一个被明确塑造的单位，它从整个思想的概念材料中纳入多少，取决于语言的特性允许多少”。26在这里，“整个思想”——萨丕尔并未追问，究竟如何能把它从实际的表达形式中抽离——扮演的角色，同索绪尔那里一般“思想”的未分化流动相同：它多少是某种现成的东西，等待按照一种特定语言所允许的任何形式来分节表达。萨丕尔采取的是比较研究者的态度，对他而言，不同语言之间的比较，就是考察其不同形式结构；索绪尔则超越现存语言结构的特征，着眼于结构本身的结构，着眼于使语言得以可能的东西。

Saussure is here practising very obviously a linguistics of the word, and not the sentence, but his assertion that the linguistic sign is essentially a form rather than a substance can be corroborated from Sapir, who was certainly a linguist of the sentence and of grammar. For Sapir too, ‘The word is merely a form, a definitely molded entity that takes in as much or as little of the conceptual material of the whole thought as the genius of the language cares to allow.’26 In this instance the ‘whole thought’ – and Sapir does not ask how that can ever be abstracted from the actual forms of its expression – plays the same role as the undifferentiated flux of ‘thought’ in general plays for Saussure, as what is somehow there to be articulated in whatever form a particular language permits. Sapir’s is the attitude of a comparatist, for whom the comparison between different languages is a matter of considering their different formal structures; Saussure is looking beyond the characteristics of existing linguistic structures to the structure of structure itself, to what makes language possible.

符号“价值”的观念，引向一个更具涵盖性的观念：语言是差异的系统。没有一个符号能够自给自足：从语言学角度说，它之所以是它，凭借的是它所不是的东西。如果它算得上一个“实体”，那也是一个否定性的实体，在性质和功能上都具有差异性：

The notion of a sign’s ‘value’ leads up to the more embracing notion of a language as a system of differences. No sign is sufficient unto itself: it is what it is, linguistically, by virtue of what it isn’t. If it is an ‘entity’ at all, then it is a negative entity, differential in its nature as well as in its function:

在语言本身中，只有差异。比这一点更重要的是：尽管一般而言，差异预先假定了差异所存在于其间的肯定项，但在一种语言中，只有差异，没有肯定项。不论看能指还是所指，语言都不包含先于语言系统而存在的观念或声音，而只包含从该系统产生的概念差异与语音差异。在一个符号中，比任何与它关联的观念或声音更重要的，是周围有哪些其他符号。27

In the language itself, there are only differences. Even more important than that is the fact that, although in general a difference presupposes positive terms between which the difference holds, in a language there are only differences, and no positive terms. Whether we take the signifier or the signified, the language includes neither ideas nor sounds existing prior to the linguistic system, but only conceptual and phonetic differences arising out of that system. In a sign, what matters more than any idea or sound associated with it is what other signs surround it.27

符号在声音与意义两个方面的差异性质，并不妨碍符号的肯定性使用；在使用时，它的差异性质被遮蔽了，因为我们没有必要意识到它。我们仍可以说，系统具有否定性的、差异性的性质（尽管有人反对索绪尔，尤其是罗曼·雅各布森，认为语法范畴不能以这种差异方式理解），而语言事件则是肯定性的。索绪尔采用了国际象棋的类比（措辞与维特根斯坦后来使用的惊人相似），因为游戏是受规则约束的结构的理想例子。国际象棋中的一枚棋子归入兵或象的类别，不是凭借它脱离游戏时的本来面目——一块雕刻过的木头或象牙——而是凭借国际象棋规则赋予它的价值，以及兵与马、后等等之间的区分。每次移动棋子时，我们未必意识到这种差异性，但很明显，这样的“事件”完全由它发生于其中的游戏结构决定。这一类比尤其有助于说明形式与实体之间的关键区别，因为对实际下棋的人而言，棋子的实体性质无关紧要。同样，对实际使用语言的人而言，语言的实体也无关紧要——视其为口头还是书面的实体，它可能引起生理学家或笔迹学家的兴趣——因为语言使用者运用的是自己语言的形式。

The differential nature of the sign in both its aspects, as sound and as sense, is no impediment to the positive use of signs; their differential nature is then eclipsed inasmuch as there is no need for us to be aware of it. Again, one might say that where the system is of a negative, differential nature (though it has been argued against Saussure, notably by Roman Jakobson, that grammatical categories cannot be understood in this differential way), linguistic events are positive. Saussure adopts the analogy (in strikingly similar terms to those later found by Wittgenstein) with the game of chess, games being ideal examples of rule-bound structures. A piece in chess falls under the category of pawn or bishop not by virtue of what it is independently of the game, which is a carved bit of wood or ivory, but by virtue of the value invested in it by the rules of chess and by the differentiation of pawns from knights from queens and so on. We may not be conscious of this differentiality every time we move a chess piece, but it is very clear that such an ‘event’ is wholly determined by the structure of the game in which it occurs. The analogy is especially helpful in demonstrating the crucial difference between form and substance, given the insignificance, for someone actually playing chess, of the substantiality of the pieces. In the same way, for someone actually using language, the linguistic substance, which may be of interest to a physiologist or a graphologist, according to whether it is the spoken or the written substance, is of no account, since what the language-user is using is the forms of his or her language.

因此，语言的符号受到所谓“差异的游戏”的支配，这是后结构主义者喜爱的一个概念。因为符号只是形式，它们既不具有完全的稳定性，也不具有完全的同一性。一个符号发生变化，就会使它在系统中与之建立关系的其他符号也发生变化；无论改变的是符号的声音还是意义，都是如此。语言变化当然是历时因素，而非共时因素，但它们并不影响语言本身的系统性质；结构主义的共时观点完全不否认外部因素促使语言的语法、语音或语义结构发生变化的影响，只坚持这些变化本身应被看成“结构依赖”的。正如萨丕尔所说，一种特定语言在随时间演变时显现的“漂移”，以语言结构为条件，并“由使用者无意识地选择那些朝某一特定方向累积的个人变异而构成”。28另一个事实是，虽然新形式进入语言，属于该语言的历时或历史研究，但这发生时，新形式并不马上取代旧形式；必定会有一段新旧形式共存、竞争的时期，直到一方退让，或双方都被保留，并获得不同“价值”，以便被以不同方式使用。在这一共存时期，语言变化成为共时研究；的确，它特别能说明语言共存所包含的意义，因为当一种新形式与一种旧形式竞争时，它们之间的“价值”差异可能尤为微妙。

The signs of a language thus become subject to the so-called ‘play of differences’, a concept popular among post-Structuralists. Because they are mere forms, signs have neither complete stability nor complete identity. Change in one sign will entail changes in those other signs with which it enters into relations in the system, and this is true whether the changes are to the sound of the sign or to its meaning. Changes in a language are of course a diachronic factor, not a synchronic one, but they do not affect the systematic nature of language itself; the synchronic point of view of Structuralism does not at all deny the influence of outside factors working to bring about changes in the grammatical, phonetic or semantic structure of a language, it only insists that these changes themselves be seen as ‘structure-dependent’, that, as Sapir has it, the ‘drift’ which a particular language displays in its evolution through time is conditioned by the structure of the language and ‘constituted by the unconscious selection on the part of its speakers of those individual variations that are cumulative in some special direction’.28 It is a fact, also, that although the entry into a language of new forms belongs to the diachronic or historical study of that language, when this happens the new forms do not replace old ones straight away; there is sure to be a period when new and old forms coexist and compete until one or other gives way, or until both are preserved and accorded different ‘values’ in order that they may be differently used. During this period of coexistence linguistic change becomes a synchronic study: indeed, an especially telling example of what linguistic coexistence implies since where a new form and an old compete the differences in ‘value’ between them may be particularly subtle.

索绪尔认识到，语言的符号既有“价值”，也有“指称意义”，这就首次引入了语言的“符号学”功能（这一术语源自希腊词semeion，意思是“符号”）。符号学是本书第三章的主题，我在这里不多停留，只说明：它研究的是诸如构成语言的那些符号，只要这些符号属于一个系统；它研究它们如何具有意义，而不是它们意味什么。因此，符号学可以同语义学对照，后者关注符号的“指称意义”，或我们如何使用它们来指涉。符号学关乎作为单位的词，语义学则关乎组合成句子的词：“在语义学中，‘意义’产生于不同符号之间的顺序、恰当配合和彼此调适。这是完全不可预测的。它向世界敞开。而在符号学中，意义向自身回转，仿佛被包含在自身之中。”29作为符号学项目，语言符号可以在声音和意义两方面与其他同类符号区分开来。例如，英语符号horse（马）的语音界定，来自它类似却不等同于hearse（灵车）、coarse（粗糙的）、whores（妓女）、house（房屋）等一长串近同音词的事实（其中包括真正的同音词hoarse〔嘶哑的〕；它与horse在语音上完全没有区别，但通常会由所在句子的意义作出语义区分）。如果符号的发音或书写偏离规范太远，使它在语音或字形上无法与声音或外观相近的其他符号区分，那么显然，只有语境才能消除歧义。但作为语言中的抽象项目，某个符号所具有的同一性，是由它与相邻项目之间细小的语音或字形差异确保的。

Saussure’s recognition that the signs of a language have a ‘value’ as well as a ‘signification’ brings in for the first time the ‘semiotic’ function of language (the term derives from the Greek word semeion meaning ‘sign’). Semiotics is the subject of the third chapter of this book and I will not linger over it here except to say that it concerns itself with signs such as those of which a language consists in so far as these belong to a system: with how they mean rather than with what they mean. Semiotics may thus be contrasted with semantics, which concerns itself with the ‘signification’ of signs or the use we make of them to refer. Semiotics has to do with the word as a unit, semantics with words combined in sentences: ‘In semantics the ‘‘meaning’’ results from the sequence, appropriateness and adaptation of the different signs among themselves. That is absolutely unpredictable. It opens out on to the world. Whereas in semiotics meaning is turned in on itself and as it were contained within itself.’29 As a semiotic item a linguistic sign may be differentiated from others of its kind both by sound and by sense. The English sign horse, for example, is defined phonetically by the fact that it resembles but is not identical with hearse, coarse, whores, house, and so on through a long list of such near homophones (including the actual homophone hoarse, from which it is not differentiated phonetically at all but will usually be so semantically by the meaning of the sentence in which it is used). Should the pronunciation or inscription of a sign depart far enough from the norm to render it phonetically or graphically indistinguishable from those adjacent to it in sound or appearance, then, obviously, only the context can disambiguate it. But as an abstract item of the language such identity as a particular sign commands is secured by the small phonetic or graphic differences between it and neighbouring items.

符号的概念“价值”较难确定，因为面对符号常见的“指称意义”，我们很快就会看不到它的“价值”；它在指涉行为中的肯定性使用，使我们无法注意到构成它的差异性。不同语言之间的比较，往往是展示语义“价值”如何运作的最简单办法。索绪尔自己提供的例子涉及英语词sheep和法语词mouton，两个符号都用来指同一种动物。但即使它们的指称意义相同，“价值”却不同，因为英语中还有另一个符号mutton，与lamb并列，用来指作为食物的羊肉。法语没有对应词，而用mouton一个词同时指活着的动物及其肉。因此，两种语言中这两个词的“价值”并不相容，因为英语在这一例子中提供了一种法语所没有的对照或关系。关于结构语义学这一问题，下文还会多谈；目前只需强调，任何符号系统中的任何符号，都因属于该系统而具有“价值”。

Its conceptual ‘value’ is rather harder to determine because here we are quick to lose sight of a sign’s ‘value’ in the face of its common ‘signification’; its positive use in an act of reference prevents us from attending to its constituent differentiality. A comparison between different languages is often the simplest way in which to demonstrate how semantic ‘values’ function. The example which Saussure himself provides involves the English word sheep and the French word mouton, which are two signs used alike to refer to the one animal. But if they are identical in signification they are not so in ‘value’, because the English language contains another sign mutton to go alongside lamb, to refer to sheep when they are eaten. The French language has no equivalent, but uses the one term mouton to refer to both the living animal and its meat. Hence the incompatibility between the ‘values’ of the two terms in the two languages, since English in this instance offers a contrast or relation absent from French. There will be more to be said on this matter of structural semantics at a later point; suffice it for now to emphasize that any sign in any sign-system has a ‘value’ by virtue of belonging to that system.

当一个符号是在指涉行为中可以彼此替代的几个选项之一时，它的“价值”就尤其明显；因此，英语中nag（驽马）、mount（坐骑）、pony（小马）、steed（骏马）等其他符号的存在，使horse的“价值”更加明显；这些符号都可以被选来指一匹实际的马，而且显然都具有超出所指动物单纯“马性”的附加意义。这些附加意义就是符号的“内涵”，转向符号学问题时，我们将对此多作讨论。符号的语义“价值”问题，使我们面对语言语义结构的百科全书性质：它是一个词项间关系如此丰富的网络，或许将永远使分析无法穷尽它。

A sign’s ‘value’ becomes especially apparent when it is one of several alternatives which might be substituted for one another in an act of reference; thus the ‘value’ of horse is made more apparent by the existence in English of such other signs as nag, mount, pony, steed and the like, all of which might be chosen to refer to an actual horse and all of which clearly possess meanings additional to the mere ‘horse-ness’ of the creature in question. These additional meanings are the sign’s ‘connotations’, of which there will be more to be said when we turn to the matter of semiotics. The question of a sign’s semantic ‘value’ brings us up against the encyclopedic nature of the semantic structure of a language, which is a network so rich in relations between terms that it will perhaps always defeat analysis.

语言的系统性质，给语言使用引入了一种可以称为“纵向”的维度，因为每个进入口头或书面实际话语的符号，都潜在地唤起与它存在关系的所有其他符号。索绪尔把语言的这一维度称为“联想”维度，并将它与语言的另一个“横向”维度对照：后者是线性的，或“组合”的维度。自索绪尔以来，联想维度越来越多地被称为“聚合”维度，这使它听起来比索绪尔心目中的东西更具形式性，因为特定符号对其使用者唤起的许多联想，可能是私人的，因而不能完全算作聚合的——如果这一术语指的是整个语言社群共有的一组可相互替换的符号。不过，索绪尔坚持语言同时在两个维度上运作，这再次使他有别于美国结构主义的语法传统，后者多少只关注语言的组合维度。

The systematic nature of language introduces into its use what might be called a ‘vertical’ dimension, since each sign which finds its way into an actual utterance, in speech or in writing, invokes, potentially, all the other signs to which it bears a relation. This dimension of language Saussure calls the ‘associative’ and he contrasts it to the other, ‘horizontal’ dimension of language: the linear or ‘syntagmatic’ one. Since Saussure’s own time, the associative dimension has come more and more to be known as the ‘paradigmatic’, which makes it sound more formal than what Saussure appears to have had in mind, since many of the associations which particular signs carry for those who use them may be private and thus less than paradigmatic, if by this term we mean sets of substitutable signs common to the whole language- community. However, Saussure’s insistence that language works in both dimensions simultaneously marks him off once again from the grammatical tradition of American Structuralism, which is concerned more or less exclusively with the syntagmatic dimension of language.

索绪尔所作的对比，是实际说出的东西与未说出的东西之间的对比，或者说，又是语言事件与语言系统之间的对比。符号只是多重联想的节点，不能把这些联想等同于整个语言系统；然而，它们仿佛是活生生的证据，表明这样一个系统确实存在，而且一个符号不可避免地会引出另一个符号。这种观念把缺席的概念引入了对语言的说明：“组合关系是在场的关系。它们存在于一个序列中同时在场的两个或多个项之间。联想关系则是缺席的关系。它们存在于构成一个记忆群的各项之间。”30 我们使用的语言符号，即使不是全部，至少大多数都带有这些“缺席的”联想；当然，可以使这些联想在场，并把它们插入一个组合之中。雅各布森认为，正是这一过程构成了语言的“诗性”使用区别于日常使用的特征（见文学结构主义一章）。语言研究者或许有理由不把它纳入考察，而且他们大多也确实如此；理由是，“缺席”的东西只会使人徒劳地分心，无法专注于在场之物提出的问题。但忽视语言使用的联想维度还有一个不那么光彩的原因，那就是我们在发现它的影响力之大时可能感到的恐惧。我们许多人愿意相信，在使用语言时，我们或多或少完全控制着它；每次写作或说话时，词语或思想的次序都是由我们决定的。我们不愿承认，语言本身可能比我们更强大，而我们已经使用的符号所带来的联想，可能正在决定接下来选择哪些符号。“说话主体”或语言使用者的这种权威丧失，是结构主义一个极为重要且充满争议的方面；正如我将努力说明的，后结构主义充分发挥了这一点。它可以追溯到索绪尔的这一坚持：语言系统在每一时刻都作用于语言事件。

The contrast which Saussure makes is between the actually uttered and the unuttered, or, once again, the language-event and the language-system. The multiple associations of which signs are simply the node are not to be identified with the language-system in its entirety, but they are as it were the living proof that such a system exists, and that one sign leads inescapably to another. This conception introduces the notion of absence into the account of language: ‘Syntagmatic relations hold in praesentia. They hold between two or more terms co-present in a sequence. Associative relations, on the other hand, hold in absentia. They hold between terms constituting a mnemonic group.’30 These ‘absent’ associations with which most if not all of the linguistic signs we use are invested may of course be brought to presence and inserted into a syntagma – this process is the one which, Jakobson argues, characterizes the ‘poetic’ use of language as opposed to the everyday use (see the chapter on Literary Structuralism). Students of language are perhaps entitled to leave it out of account, as they mostly have done, on the grounds that what is ‘absent’ is a wasteful distraction from the problems posed by what is present. But there is a less creditable reason also for this neglect of the associative dimension of language-use, which is the fear we may feel at discovering how influential it is. Many of us like to think that when we use language we control it more or less totally and that it is we who determine the sequence of words or thoughts each time we write or speak; we are not happy to allow that language itself can prove more powerful than we are and that the associations of the signs we have already used may be determining the choice of the signs to come. This loss of authority in the ‘speaking subject’ or language-user is a most important and contentious aspect of Structuralism, exploited to the full in post-Structuralism as I shall hope to show, and it can be traced back to this insistence of Saussure that the language-system impinges at every moment on language-events.

如果说符号的所有组合联结都可以编成规则体系——美国的“分布主义”语法学家就试图这样做——那么，符号的聚合联想却并非如此。索绪尔宣称：“一个联想群所包含的项目没有固定数目；它们出现的次序也不固定。”31 人们至多只能把它们分为语音联想和语义联想这两个必需的类别，同时希望不存在第三类，即语言学家特有的语法联想。对于 horse（马）这样的一个符号，其语音联想或许最终可以列举出来，从我已经提出的那些开始，继续编目，逐步远离最初的符号。对于同一符号的语义联想，则不可能编出类似的目录，理由如我所说：它们没有尽头，也不存在一种确定的或公认的排列次序。语音联想是非个人性的，由语言的语音结构决定（自然，有些人对它们比另一些人敏感得多，能够列出更长的有效联想清单）；但语义联想允许无比更大的自由，尽管最终它们也必然受到语言语义结构的限制，因为结构之外的东西，按字面意义说，是不可想象的。关于一个符号的“联想关系”，索绪尔本人只肯说，它们形成一个“星座”。32

If it is possible to codify all the syntagmatic associations of signs – this is what American ‘distributionalist’ grammarians sought to do – the same is not true for their paradigmatic associations. ‘[An] associate group has no particular number of items in it; nor do they occur in any particular order,’ declares Saussure.31 The most one can do is to divide them into the two necessary classes of phonetic and semantic associations, while hoping that there is not a third class, peculiar to linguists, of grammatical associations. The phonetic associations of a given sign such as horse might eventually be listed, starting with those I have already suggested and pursuing the catalogue ever further from the sign with which one began. A similar catalogue for the semantic associations of the same sign is out of the question, for the reasons I have indicated, that there is no end to them and no definitive or commonly agreed order in which they can be placed. Phonetic associations are impersonal, being determined by the phonetic structure of the language (some people are much more alert to them than others, naturally, and could produce longer lists of valid associations) but semantic ones allow for an infinitely greater freedom, even if in the end they too must be bounded by the semantic structure of the language because what lies outside that is literally inconceivable. All that Saussure himself will say of the ‘associative relations’ of a sign is that they form a ‘constellation’.32

我已经指出，《教程》中没有任何地方出现“结构”这个词。事实上，直到该书首次出版约十二年后，欧洲结构主义才可以说开始成为语言思想中一个独特的学派。“结构”一词第一次真正以我们今天赋予它的意义公开出现，是在1928年海牙举行的一次语言学家会议上；当时它所用于的语言学分支是语音学。革新者是三位在俄罗斯以外工作的俄罗斯语言学家：罗曼·雅各布森、S.卡尔采夫斯基和N.特鲁别茨科伊，他们的活动基地在布拉格。布拉格结构主义在语言和文学领域、乃至整个艺术领域都富于创新；即使早期领袖或已去世、或已离开该国，它在捷克斯洛伐克仍延续下来。对于后来构成结构主义的那一整套学说而言，它是一个极为重要、却颇受忽视的源流。

I have already pointed to the fact that the word ‘structure’ occurs nowhere in the Course. It was indeed not until some twelve years after that book was first published that European Structuralism may be said to have begun as a distinctive school of linguistic thought. The occasion for the first true airing of the term ‘structure’ in the sense we now give it was a conference of linguists held at The Hague in 1928, and the branch of linguistics it was then applied to was phonetics. The innovators were three Russian linguists working outside Russia: Roman Jakobson, S. Karčevsky and N. Troubetskoy, whose base was Prague. Prague Structuralism, which was innovative in the fields both of language and literature, and of art in general, and which survived in Czechoslovakia even after its early leaders had either died or left the country, is a most important if rather neglected tributary to the stock of doctrines which have come to constitute Structuralism.

1929年，也就是他们在海牙发言的次年，这三位语言学家用法语匿名发表了所谓的《论纲》，亦即宣言，其总标题宣示了他们与索绪尔的联系：“从把语言视为系统的观念中产生的方法问题”。他们所关注的系统，索绪尔谈得相对较少，不过他所说的确实是无可挑剔的结构主义论述：语言的语音或音位系统。布拉格语言学家准备采用的方法，是“通过必不可少地明确音位之间存在的关系，也就是说，通过勾画所考察语言的结构图式，来刻画音位系统”33；他们宣称，用这种方法研究语言的其他系统，也能取得同样丰硕的成果。从此，语言研究就可以明确地成为结构主义的研究。

In 1929, the year after their intervention at The Hague, these three linguists published, in French and anonymously, their so-called Theses, or manifesto, whose overall title proclaimed their affiliation with Saussure: ‘Problems of method stemming from the conception of language as a system’. The system they were concerned with was one on which Saussure had had relatively little to say, though what he did say was impeccably Structuralist: the phonetic or phonological system of language. The method which the Prague linguists intended to follow was to ‘characterize the phonological system by necessarily specifying the relations existing between the phonemes, that is, by tracing the plan of the structure of the language under consideration’33 – a method which they declared could be followed equally fruitfully in studying other systems of the language. Hence-forward the study of language could be explicitly Structuralist.

索绪尔已经预见到，研究语言的声音方面，需要仔细区分声音产生的研究和声音接受的研究。仅仅作为声音，它们是生理学的研究对象，生理学可以处理言语器官如何运作等问题。但当它们作为一种语言的声音，进入该语言使用者的耳中时，就成为语言学家的研究对象；语言学家可以考察它们作为语言声音系统的参与者所具有的性质。声音产生的生理方面可以留给语音学研究，音位学则转向声音系统的分析。结构主义关心的是音位学。

Saussure had foreseen that the study of the sound aspect of language needed to be carefully divided into the study of the production of sounds and the study of their reception. Merely as sounds they are a subject for physiology, which can take up questions of how the speech organs function. But as the sounds of a language, in the ear of a user of that language, they become a subject for the linguist, who can investigate their nature as participants in a language’s sound-system. Phonetics may be left to study the physiological side of sound-production, phonology turns instead to the analysis of the sound-system. It is with phonology that Structuralism is concerned.
