一种语言的声音系统由哪些单位构成，在布拉格结构主义者发表宣言许多年前就已被发现：它们是该语言的“音位”，可以定义为一种语言所具有的最小的区别性声音单位。它们是区分不同词语的声音。因此，在英语中，p和b是不同的音位，因为 pill（药丸）和 bill（账单）是不同的词；同样，i和u是不同的音位，因为 pill 和 pull（拉）是不同的词；l和t也是如此，因为 pill 和 pit（坑）是不同的词。音位必须通过替换检验来发现，这通常称为“换位”：改变词语的语音构成，看看它在什么地方变成了另一个词。因此，音位学必须从符号的语义方面，也就是所指出发；所指决定了一个符号在变成另一个符号之前，语音变化所容许的范围。这个检验考察的是意义，而不是声音。

The units out of which the sound-system of a language is formed had been discovered many years before the Prague Structuralists produced their manifesto: they are the ‘phonemes’ of the language, which might be defined as the smallest distinctive units of sound that a language possesses. They are the sounds which tell different words apart. Thus, in English p and b are separate phonemes because pill and bill are separate words; similarly, i and u are separate phonemes because pill and pull are separate words, and l and t because pill and pit are separate words. Phonemes must be found by a test of substitution, usually known as ‘commutation’, whereby changes are induced in the phonetic make-up of the word in order to see at what point it becomes a different word. Phonology thus has to start from the semantic aspect of a sign, or the signified, which determines the permissible extent of phonetic variation in a sign before it turns into a different sign. The test is one of sense, not sound.

每一种语言都有自己的音位系统，承认其他语言可能并不承认的区别。雅各布森给出了一个很有说服力的例子：在英语中，r和l是不同的音位——rash（皮疹）和 lash（鞭打）是不同的英语词——但在韩语中却不是，因此韩语使用者可以用一个声音替换另一个声音，而不损失任何意义。这也解释了为什么韩语使用者在学习英语时，会经常用l代替r，而没有意识到自己现在面对的已不是单纯的声音，而是音位。34

Each language has its own phonological system, recognizing differences which other languages may not recognize. Jakobson gives as a telling example of this the fact that whereas in English r and l are distinct phonemes – rash and lash are distinct English words – in Korean they are not, so that a speaker of Korean can substitute one sound for the other without any loss of sense, hence the regularity with which, when learning English, a Korean speaker may substitute l for r unaware that he has now to deal not with mere sounds but with phonemes.34

任何一种语言的音位数目都出乎意料地少，这使音位系统成为整个语言系统性的一个特别好的模型，因为它只需有限数目的要素，就能够生成数量巨大的不同符号。在这一点上，音位可以与词语相比；词语可能形成的有意义的组合，肯定是数不清的。这证实了索绪尔式结构主义和乔姆斯基式结构主义共有的首要原则：语言是一种以“无限的建构能力”为特征的结构。35

The number of phonemes in any given language is unexpectedly small, a fact which makes the phonological system a particularly good model for the systematicity of language as a whole since with a restricted number of elements it is able to generate an enormous number of different signs. In this respect phonemes may be compared with words, whose possible meaningful combinations are certainly innumerable, bearing out the cardinal principle common to Saussurean and Chomskyan Structuralism that language is a structure characterized by a ‘constructivity without limit’.35

在布拉格发展起来的音位学结构主义，其主要目的之一，是把历年来收集并分类的语音资料整合起来；以往这些资料从未被理解为一个适用于任何语言的单一抽象图式的表现。新结构主义者有更高的目标，这建立在他们的一个信念之上，用特鲁别茨科伊的话说就是：“音位系统不是孤立音位的机械总和，而是一个有机整体，其成员是音位，其结构受规律支配。”36

The phonological Structuralism developed in Prague had as one of its main purposes to integrate the phonetic data which had been collected over the years and classified but never grasped as the manifestation of a single abstract scheme, applicable to any language whatsoever. The new Structuralists had higher aims, founded on their conviction that, in Troubetskoy’s words: ‘A phonological system is not the mechanical sum of isolated phonemes, but an organic whole whose members are phonemes and whose structure is subject to laws.’36

不过，布拉格结构主义者起初还没有一种系统而有意义的方式，能够充分地把音位彼此联系起来。索绪尔曾谈到音位的“对立”性质，从而把它们与语言的其他要素归为一类，但音位究竟如何能够被认为彼此“对立”，却并不清楚；它们彼此不同，可是差异并不必然构成对立关系。音位学尚待作出的发现，是音位可以被分析；它们并不是早期语言学家所想象的不可分割的原子（萨丕尔称之为“符号原子”）。在20世纪30年代，主要是由雅各布森，将音位——先是元音，后是辅音——进一步分解为“区别特征”。这种更深一层的分析，根据音位是“浊音”还是“清音”、“鼻化”还是“非鼻化”等等，在它们之间建立了新的关系。通过这种方式，可以说明英语的b与英语的p之间是一种浊音对清音的关系，英语的d与英语的t之间也是如此。如果接着问，这是否意味着这一对或那一对辅音是“同一个”音位的浊音形式和清音形式，答案是“不”；它们不是任何某一东西的浊音形式和清音形式。它们仅仅是一种关系的两项，仅此而已：对立的音位是结构主义否认自足实体的一个理想例证。把音位分析为区别特征，也意味着语言学家的工作进一步简化，因为在一种特定语言中起作用的区别特征，比音位还少——雅各布森认为，法语的声音系统只有五种，而土耳其语的八个元音音位中只有三种。

But what the Prague Structuralists did not at first have was a systematic and meaningful way of relating phonemes adequately one to the other. Saussure had written of the ‘oppositive’ nature of phonemes, thereby assimilating them to the other elements of language, but it is not clear how phonemes can be held to be ‘opposed’ one to another; they differ from each other but difference is not necessarily a relation of opposition. The discovery in phonology which remained to be made was that phonemes could be analysed, they were not the indivisible atoms (‘symbolic atoms’ Sapir called them) earlier linguists had imagined them to be. During the 1930s, and chiefly by Jakobson, phonemes, first vowels and then consonants, were further broken down into ‘distinctive features’. This deeper analysis established new relations between phonemes according to whether, for example, they were ‘voiced’ or ‘unvoiced’, ‘nasalized’ or ‘not nasalized’, etc. By such means English b can be shown to stand to English p in a relation of voiced to unvoiced, as does English d to English t. If one then asks whether this means that one or other pair of consonants are the voiced and unvoiced versions of the ‘same’ phoneme, the answer is ‘no’, they are not the voiced and unvoiced versions of anything. They are the two terms of a relation and that is all: opposed phonemes are an ideal example of Structuralism’s denial of self-sufficient entities. The analysis of phonemes into distinctive features also represents a further economy for the linguist inasmuch as there are even fewer distinctive features at work in a particular language than there are phonemes – Jakobson allows five only for the sound-system of French, and three among the eight vocalic phonemes of Turkish.

确立这个由“区别特征”构成的深层音位系统，就完成了对语言声音系统的最终切分；结构主义对成对的对立项的依赖，也由此获得新的严密性和分量，而这一依赖在索绪尔那里已初见端倪。正如雅各布森所说：“这些区别性性质的对立，是真正的、逻辑学所定义的二元对立；也就是说，对立的每一项都必然蕴含其相反项。”37 因此，“浊音”与“清音”，或者“鼻化”与“非鼻化”，都是一对其间不容许有中间项的术语。第二个例子非常有助于说明，在这样的系统中，一种特征的缺席与它的在场同样有意义。

The establishment of this underlying phonological system of ‘distinctive features’ achieves an ultimate segmentation of a language’s sound-system, and lends a new rigour and weight to Structuralism’s dependence on pairs of opposed terms, already adumbrated by Saussure. As Jakobson claims: ‘The oppositions of such differential qualities are real binary oppositions as defined in logic, i.e., they are such that each of the terms of the opposition necessarily implies its opposite.’37 Thus ‘voiced’ and ‘unvoiced’, or ‘nasalized’ and ‘not nasalized’ are pairs of terms allowing of no middle term between them, the second example showing very usefully that in such a system the absence of a feature is equally as significant as its presence.

“区别特征”是语言分析所能达到的最远处，再往前就会离开语言系统，进入人体解剖系统。“浊音”和“清音”这类术语，看上去像是取自声音产生的机制，但两者的区别却是一种听觉上的区别，我们对所听到的东西的理解正依赖于它。（对书面语言的要素，即“字素”的分析，想必可以按照类似于音位分析所使用的术语来进行，并把书写符号的“区别特征”编成规则体系。不过，似乎还没有关于沿着这些思路开展研究的充分报告。）

‘Distinctive features’ are the furthest point it is possible to reach in the analysis of language without leaving the language-system for that of the human anatomy. Such terms as ‘voiced’ and ‘unvoiced’ look to be drawn from the mechanics of sound production, except that the distinction between them is an acoustic one on which depends our understanding of what we hear. (The analysis of the elements of the written language, or ‘graphemes’, could presumably be conducted in similar terms to those used in the analysis of phonemes, and the ‘distinctive features’ of written signs be codified. However, no adequate report seems to exist of research conducted along such lines.)

最后，需要明确，音位学研究的是语言系统，而不是言语。音位是抽象物，而不是一个实际发出的声音：“音位不能等同于声音，但它也不在声音之外；它必然存在于声音之中，既内在于声音，又叠加在声音之上：它是在种种变化背后保持不变的东西。”38 用索绪尔的术语说，我们语言的音位系统在我们头脑中被“内化”了；用乔姆斯基的术语说，它被“习得”了，尽管我们在说话时很少、甚至从不意识到它。“区别特征”是语言学家分析中的事实，而不是说话者意识中的事实，不过，每当听或说发生错误、一个音位被错误地换成另一个音位时，我们都会得到语言声音系统存在的提示。一种语言的音位系统是文化现象，而不是自然现象：它实际上代表着自然变为文化的那个转折点，或者说，代表着自然产生的“噪声”连续体被分节为有意义的言语中不连续、彼此区别的声音的那个转折点。结构主义音位学是语言学与结构主义的社会学用途之间的桥梁，后者是下一章的主题。

One needs to be clear, finally, that phonology occupies itself with the study of langue and not of parole. The phoneme is an abstraction, not an actually uttered sound: ‘The phoneme is not to be identified with the sound, yet nor is it external to the sound; it is necessarily present in the sound, being both inherent in it and superposed upon it: it is what remains invariant behind the variations.’38 In Saussurean terms we can say that the phonological system of our language is ‘internalized’ in our brains, or in Chomskyan terms that it is ‘acquired’, even though we are seldom or never conscious of it when speaking. ‘Distinctive features’ are a fact of the linguist’s analysis and not of the consciousness of speakers, though we receive indications of the sound-system of language whenever we meet with error in hearing or speaking and one phoneme is mistakenly exchanged for another. The phonological system of a language is a cultural phenomenon, not a natural one: it represents indeed the point where nature becomes culture, or where the continuum of naturally produced ‘noise’ is articulated into the discontinuous and distinct sounds of significant speech. Structuralist phonology is the bridge between linguistics and the sociological uses of Structuralism, which are the subject of the next chapter.

2 社会科学2 Social Sciences

从20世纪20年代末、30年代初结构主义首次以一种整合并解释音位学资料的方法而显出独特性时起，它的创始者就相信，他们作为结构主义者，正在与那个时代特有的一种思维方式相契合。结构主义是一个应运而生的观念，也是对先前思维方式的一场广泛反动的一部分；这些先前的方式，可以分别称为原子论的、经验主义的或行为主义的。与结构主义者分歧最尖锐的学者和思想家，往往把他们的资料当作自成一体的事实，把搜集这些事实当作目的本身。他们对事实与事实之间的关系注意得太少，而这些事实本可以恰当地被看作某些系统的成员。如果经验主义者提出深层理论来解释资料，这些理论也往往缺乏雄心；有些人甚至完全拒绝被视为理论家。泽利格·S.哈里斯是美国结构语言学的核心人物，也是乔姆斯基的老师；他尤其以否认自己的语言研究具有任何区别于描述性意图的理论性意图而闻名。

From the time in the late 1920s and early 1930s when Structuralism first distinguished itself as a method of integrating and explaining the data of phonology, its originators believed that, as Structuralists, they were falling into line with a mode of thought characteristic of the age. Structuralism was an idea whose time had come and a part of a widespread reaction against the modes of thought that had preceded it, which might variously be called atomistic, empiricist or behaviouristic. Those scholars and thinkers with whom Structuralists were in the sharpest disagreement had tended to treat their data as self-contained facts, and the gathering of those facts as an end in itself. They had paid too little attention to the relation of fact to fact within the systems of which the facts could pertinently be taken to be members. If the empiricists advanced underlying theories to account for their data, they were unambitious ones, and some refused to be thought of as theorists at all: Zellig S. Harris, a central figure in American structural linguistics, and a teacher of Chomsky, was especially notorious for disclaiming any theoretical as opposed to a descriptive intention in his linguistic researches.

早期结构主义者觉察到，这种不敢冒险的经验主义正在退却，通向一种更富思辨性的心智取向的道路已经敞开。音位学家特鲁别茨科伊与同样流亡国外的俄罗斯同胞雅各布森一样，是一位在文化和科学上兴趣广泛而多样的人。1933年，他在写作中，把自己正在帮助促成的语言研究革命与当代西方思想的总体方向联系起来：

The early Structuralists sensed that empiricism of this unadventurous stamp was on the retreat, and that the way was open for a more speculative cast of mind to replace it. Writing in 1933 the phonologist Troubetskoy who, like his fellow exile from Russia, Jakobson, was a man of wide and various cultural and scientific interests, associated the revolution he was helping to bring about in the study of language with the general direction of contemporary Western thought:

当今音位学的首要特征，是它的结构主义和系统性普遍主义……我们所处时代的特征，是所有科学学科都趋向于以结构主义取代原子论，以普遍主义取代个体主义（当然，这些术语是在哲学意义上使用的）。这一趋势可以在物理学、化学、生物学、心理学、经济学等领域观察到。因此，当今的音位学并不是孤立的。它是一场更广泛的科学运动的一部分。1

Present-day phonology is characterised above all by its structuralism and its systematic universalism . . . the age in which we live is characterised by the tendency of all scientific disciplines to replace atomism by structuralism and individualism by universalism (in the philosophical sense of those terms, of course). This tendency may be observed in physics, chemistry, biology, psychology, economics etc. Present-day phonology is not isolated therefore. It forms part of a wider scientific movement.1

如同其他任何新的思维方式，结构主义并不是一种彻底的、毫无先兆的创新，而是西欧知识界已经盛行的各种观念的汇流。在这里追索哪一种结构主义论点可能来自何处，将是悖谬的；之所以悖谬，是因为那会重新回到结构主义之前那种对演变、对观念起源而非其形式的执迷。相反，结构主义教导我们，要欣然废黜法国政治哲学家路易·阿尔都塞讥讽地称为“起源与终点之神”的那些东西。2 这一警告并不像结构主义的反对者乐于解释的那样，是反对一切形式的历时探究，而是反对一种常见谬误：认为任何系统最终都可以根据其起源、或者其起源、或者其最终归宿来解释。

Like any other new mode of thought Structuralism was not a total and unheralded innovation but a confluence of ideas prevalent already in the intellectual world of Western Europe. It would be perverse to set out here to trace which Structuralist thesis might have come from where; perverse because that would be to revert to the pre-Structuralist obsession with evolution and with the genesis instead of the form of ideas. Structuralism teaches, on the contrary, the joyful dethronement of what the French political philosopher Louis Althusser sardonically calls ‘the Gods of Origins and Ends’.2 This is a warning not, as Structuralism’s opponents like to interpret it, against pursuing any form of diachronic inquiry whatsoever, but against the common fallacy that any system can finally be explained in terms either of its origins or of its origins or of its ultimate destination.

不过，为了更好地描述结构主义思想，既然我们现在正从它在语言学中的表现，转向它在其他受到它格外深刻影响的学科中的表现，简要提及两股思想潮流会有所帮助；它们对雅各布森和特鲁别茨科伊等语言学家的心智形成具有很强的塑造作用。这两股潮流就是现象学和格式塔心理学。

However, the better to describe Structuralist thought, now that we are passing from its linguistic manifestations to those in other disciplines where it has been especially influential, it will help to refer briefly to two currents of ideas which were strongly formative on the minds of linguists such as Jakobson and Troubetskoy. These two currents are those of phenomenology and of Gestalt psychology.

现象学是一个在德国兴盛的哲学思想流派，主要体现在胡塞尔及其学生海德格尔的著作中，后来通过萨特的大量著作和梅洛-庞蒂的著作，在法国获得了一定程度的普及。盎格鲁-撒克逊哲学家对它研究甚少，赞赏更少；这种忽视很可能也是他们后来抗拒结构主义本身的一个因素，因为结构主义在若干重要方面依赖于现象学的某些洞见。

Phenomenology is a school of philosophical thought which flourished in Germany, chiefly in the work of Husserl and of his pupil Heidegger, and was later popularized up to a point in France, in the abundant writings of Sartre and those of Merleau-Ponty. It has remained little studied and even less admired by Anglo-Saxon philosophers, a neglect that may well be a contributory factor to the resistance subsequently shown to Structuralism itself, which is dependent in important ways on certain of phenomenology’s insights.

哲学家不时试图在绝对可靠的基础上重新确立自己的学科，现象学就是其中一次；换句话说，它试图使哲学成为一门所有理性之人都必须认同的科学。现象学的抱负并未实现，这些抱负是要使哲学思想摆脱各种先入之见；即使哲学家在考察知觉、感觉、知识等根本问题时，也会被这些成见引入歧途。它试图准确描述意识，并一劳永逸地消除许多哲学把人的思想主体与客体分开的古老做法。现象学把主体与客体视为构成一个整体，并把两者之间的关系视为“意向性的”。我们的思想对象，由我们自己以知觉、想象、回忆等不同方式加以“意指”。正如现象学家喜欢反复说的：意识总是对某物的意识；它是一个整体，只有以错误的方式，才能把它分割为相互独立的主体和客体。因此，意识中的一切都是有意义的，因为意识的功能就是把人类经验的流动综合成可理解的结构。

Phenomenology was one of the periodic attempts made by philosophers to re-establish their discipline on absolutely certain foundations, to make it, that is to say, a science to which all rational persons must accede. Phenomenology’s ambitions, which have not been realized, were to free philosophical thought from the preconceptions which lead even philosophers astray when they consider ultimate questions of perception, sensation, knowledge and so on. It was an attempt at the accurate description of consciousness, and at removing once and for all the ancient division practised by so much philosophy of the subject from the object of human thought. Phenomenology takes subject and object to constitute a whole, and the relation between one and the other to be ‘intentional’. The objects of our thought are ‘intended’ by ourselves in different ways, of perceiving, imagining, remembering, etc. As phenomenologists like to repeat: consciousness is always consciousness of something, it is a whole divisible only falsely into an independent subject and object. In consequence everything in consciousness is meaningful, it being the function of consciousness to synthesize the flow of human experience into intelligible structures.

现象学是一种哲学，而不是心理学；它力求描述的是意识的本质，而不是意识在这个或那个个人身上如何运作。困扰胡塞尔的问题是，逻辑和数学的真理并不是心理学真理，个体心灵如何能够认识它们：“我们现在的问题，恰恰涉及那些构成几何学主题的理想对象：几何学的理想性（如同一切科学的理想性），如何从其最初的个人内部起源——在那里，它是最初发明者灵魂的意识空间中的一种结构——走向其理想的客观性？”3

Phenomenology is a form of philosophy and not of psychology, it seeks to describe the essence of consciousness and not consciousness as it functions in this or that individual. The question which exercised Husserl was how truths such as those of logic and mathematics, which are not psychological truths, can be known by individual minds: ‘Our problem now concerns precisely the ideal objects which are thematic in geometry: how does geometrical ideality (just like that of all sciences) proceed from its primary intrapersonal origin, where it is a structure within the conscious space of the first inventor’s soul, to its ideal objectivity?’3

用结构主义术语说，这个问题就是系统与事件如何接合的问题。胡塞尔对上述问题的回答是：几何学的理想性借助语言，从个体内部的“源头”出发；语言使它客观化，使它成为人际之间的真理，而不再是个人内部的真理。现象学把“世界”等同于所有能够被说出或写出的东西，在这一点上，它是结构主义真正的盟友。

In Structuralist terms the issue is that of the articulation of system with event. Husserl’s answer to the question posed above is that geometrical ideality proceeds from its ‘source’ within the individual by means of language, which objectifies it and makes it an inter-instead of an intra-personal truth. Phenomenology is a true ally of Structuralism in equating the ‘world’ with everything that can be said or written.

它也是真正的盟友，因为它在意识的可变因素与不变的或结构性的特征之间划出如此绝对的界限：前者无关紧要，而辨明后者则是哲学的任务。胡塞尔借助我们对外部世界的知觉究竟具有什么性质这一古老问题，来阐明现象学方法：

It is a true ally, too, in drawing so absolute a distinction between the variables of consciousness, which are of no account, and the invariant or structural features of it, which it is philosophy’s task to identify. Husserl takes the age-old problem of the nature of our perception of the external world, in order to clarify the phenomenological method:

例如，物体知觉的现象学不会（仅仅）报告实际发生的或可以预期的知觉；相反，它将呈现不变的结构系统，没有这些系统，对一个物体的知觉，以及对同一个物体本身的在综合中相互协调的多重知觉，都是不可想象的。4

For instance, the phenomenology of perception of bodies will not be (simply) a report on the factually occurring perceptions or those to be expected; rather it will be the presentation of invariant structural systems without which perception of a body and a synthetically concordant multiplicity of perceptions of one and the same body as such would be unthinkable.4

即使在这个简短的例子中，也能看出，现象学如何以一种能动的眼光看待人的意识：在知觉中，我们组织自己的环境；凭借保持或记忆的能力，我们综合现在与过去。说意识是人类——想必也是动物——的一种“结构化”能力，并不算过分。

There is evidence, even in this brief example, of how phenomenology takes an active view of human consciousness: in perceiving we organize our environment, and in our capacity for retention, or memory, we synthesize present and past. It would not be going too far to say that consciousness is a ‘structuring’ faculty of human-and, presumably, animalkind.

如同后来出现的结构主义，现象学宣称自己是一种方法，而不是一种教义。它的方法中一个关键环节，是通常所谓把世界的实际独特性“加括号”；我们在有意识生活的每一时刻都体验到这种独特性。必须把这种独特性放到一边，因为它不能成为哲学的一部分，它完全是主观的、心理性的。因此，现象学家所处理的经验，是这些其他的、独特的经验的实例；它们是现实经验中人际共有或可以交流的那个方面。现象学与结构主义的接近，在这里十分明显。词语和句子作为客观语言系统中的项目，也都是本质性的东西；它们无法表现我们用它们来描述的经验的独特性。此外，语言具有自主性，因此也可以说，它把事物鲜活的现实“加括号”，因为我们可以自由使用语言，而不必关心它是否真实。正如现象学家对想象的结构和对知觉的结构同样感兴趣，结构语言学家也可以继续研究，而从不问自己，哪些语言结构被用来讲述关于世界的真实之事，哪些被用来讲述不真实或错误之事。

Like Structuralism after it, phenomenology declared itself to be a method and not a doctrine. A crucial part of its method involved what is generally called a ‘bracketing’ of the world in its actual uniqueness, which we experience in every moment of our conscious lives. That uniqueness has to be set on one side because it cannot be part of philosophy, it is entirely subjective and psychological. The experiences in which the phenomenologist deals are thus examples of these other, unique experiences; they are real experiences in their inter-personal or communicable aspect. Here the closeness of phenomenology to Structuralism is very apparent. Words and sentences too, as items in the objective language-system, are essences, they cannot represent the uniqueness of the experiences we use them to describe. Moreover, in its autonomy, language also may be said to ‘bracket’ the living reality of things, inasmuch as we can use it freely without concern for its truthfulness. Just as the phenomenologist is as interested in the structure of imagining as in that of perceiving, so the structural linguist can proceed without ever asking himself which linguistic structures are used to say true things about the world and which to say untrue or wrong things.

为结构主义增添分量的当代思想中第二场大规模、影响广泛的运动，是格式塔心理学。它与现象学一样起源于德国，兴盛时期也大致相同，在1910年至20世纪30年代之间。Gestalt 是德语词，意为“模式”，格式塔心理学的核心主张是，所有有意识的经验都是具有模式的。格式塔心理学同样与现象学一样，研究整体；它与心理学中行为主义的信念相反，后者用刺激和反应来研究人类行为的孤立片段——一个刺激产生一个反应。相反，格式塔心理学家认为，这种简单化歪曲了人类如何知觉环境并对其作出反应的真实情形：

The second large-scale and widely influential movement in contemporary thought which lent weight to Structuralism was Gestalt psychology which, like phenomenology, was German in origin and flourished over much the same period, between 1910 and the 1930s. Gestalt is the German word meaning ‘pattern’ and the essence of Gestalt psychology is the claim that all conscious experience is patterned. Gestalt psychology, again like phenomenology, deals in wholes; it runs counter to the beliefs of behaviourism in psychology, which studied isolated episodes of human behaviour in terms of stimulus and response – one stimulus producing one response. The Gestalt psychologists argued, on the contrary, that such simplicity was a misrepresentation of how human beings perceive and respond to their environment:

我们的观点将是：有机体并不是以局部的、彼此独立的事件来回应局部刺激，而是对它所面对的刺激模式作出反应；这种回应是一个统一的过程，一个功能整体，它在经验中呈现的是一个感官场景，而不是局部感觉的拼镶。5

Our view will be that, instead of reacting to local stimuli by local and mutually independent events, the organism responds to the pattern of stimuli to which it is exposed; and that this answer is a unitary process, a functional whole, which gives, in experience, a sensory scene rather than a mosaic of local sensations.5

这段话的作者沃尔夫冈·苛勒本人最初接受的是物理学家的训练，他把物理学家的物理“场”概念带入了心理学：场是一个可以划定边界的整体，在其中可以观察到各种力量沿着特定路径、遵循特定规律发生动态的相互作用。来自“场”外部的干预会扰乱这一系统的平衡，而系统的重新调整不会只是局部的，而是全面的、系统性的。人的意识之“场”也是如此；面对外部环境的刺激，它不断被要求重新调整自身。格式塔思想也以系统为界，研究的不是独立实体，而是各项之间的关系。（在我引述的苛勒那段话中，把“感官场景”与局部感觉的“拼镶”对比，是不恰当的，因为拼镶本身就是艺术作品，因此也是一个有组织的整体或结构的例子。）格式塔心理学的发现是，我们通过把环境的各个要素相互区别开来，在语境中理解环境。回到索绪尔引入的术语，一个特定要素的“价值”——比如我们知觉到的一种颜色——由其他不同颜色的在场来决定；它没有独立于我们知觉它时所在语境的“绝对”价值。

Wolfgang Köhler, the author of these words, had himself been trained first as a physicist and brought with him into psychology the physicist’s notion of the physical ‘field’ or delimitable whole within which may be observed a dynamic interplay of forces following certain paths and obeying certain laws. An intervention from outside the ‘field’ disturbs the equilibrium of this system, whose readjustment will be not purely local but general and systematic. Similarly with the ‘field’ of human consciousness, which is constantly being called upon to readjust itself faced with stimuli from the external environment. Gestalt thought is system-bound once again and studies not independent entities but the relations between terms. (In the quotation from Köhler which I have given, his contrast between a ‘sensory scene’ and a ‘mosaic’ of local sensations is an unhappy one, since a mosaic is itself a work of art and as such an example of an organized whole or structure.) The finding of Gestalt psychology is that we make sense of our environment contextually, by differentiating its elements one from the other. To revert to the term Saussure introduced, the ‘value’ of a particular element, let us say a colour that we perceive, is determined by the presence of other, different colours; it has no ‘absolute’ value independent of the context in which we perceive it.

因此，格式塔心理学与现象学一起，促使人们认为，人类心灵的功能，要么是在它所关注的任何东西中识别结构，要么就是把结构投射到它所关注的任何东西之中。不可能有不具组织形式的心理经验，也不可能有一个心理实体能够独立于与它发生关系的其他实体而被设想。我们像以结构方式知觉那样，也以结构方式记忆。苛勒和其他人提出，由于中枢神经系统的运作方式，结构主义铭刻在我们的身体之中。经验的结构反映着身体结构，或者用苛勒简短的说法：“经验中的单位对应于其深层生理过程中的功能单位。”6 自然就这样使我们所有人都成为结构主义者。像苛勒所提出的那样，两个平行运作的结构可以被描述为“同构的”，也就是具有相似形式的；当结构主义要把一个结构与另一个结构联系起来，以确定两者的关系时，同构概念显然有很大作用。结构主义的终极梦想（或幻想），也许是发现一个结构之结构，使我们能够声称，一切结构都彼此同构。

Along with phenomenology, then, Gestalt psychology encourages the view that the function of the human mind is either to recognize structures in whatever it turns its attention to or else to project structures into whatever it attends to. There can be no mental experience which is not organized and no mental entity conceivable independently of the others with which it enters into relations. We remember structurally just as we perceive structurally. The suggestion made by Köhler and others is that Structuralism is inscribed in our bodies because of the manner in which the central nervous system works. The structures of experience reflect bodily structures or, as Köhler briefly puts it, ‘units in experience go with functional units in the underlying physiological processes’.6 Thus does Nature make Structuralists of us all. Two structures functioning in parallel, as proposed by Köhler, can be described as ‘isomorphic’ or of like form, and when it comes in Structuralism to relating one structure to another, so as to determine their relations, the concept of isomorphism clearly has a large part to play. The ultimate Structuralist dream (or fantasy) might be of the discovery of a Structure of Structures which would enable us to claim that all structures were isomorphic of one another.

最为明确地承认自己的观念与格式塔心理学观念之间亲缘关系的结构主义思想家，是人类学家克洛德·列维-斯特劳斯。他也理所当然地认为，结构主义是适合当今时代的唯一思维方式或智识方法。他是所有思想家中最纯粹的结构主义者：他不仅把结构主义方法运用于自己的专业领域社会人类学，还从他认为无可置疑的成功中，得出了有关人类心灵性质的广泛哲学结论。他认为自己的成就与物理科学或人文科学中其他研究者的成就趋于一致，是对组织的一般研究的一项贡献。

The Structuralist thinker who has acknowledged most roundly the affiliation between his own ideas and those of Gestalt psychology is the anthropologist Claude Lévi-Strauss, who also takes it for granted that Structuralism is the one appropriate mode of thought or intellectual method for the present age. He is the most purely Structuralist thinker of all, having not only put Structuralist methods to work in his professional field of social anthropology but also drawn sweeping philosophical conclusions from what he regards as their unquestioned success about the nature of the human mind. He looks on his achievement as convergent with those of others, working in the physical or the human sciences, and as a contribution to the general study of organization.

社会人类学家从事的是对（通常）异己社会的研究，这为结构主义提供了特殊的优势。首先，异己社会既不是完全熟悉的，也不是完全陌生的；它是一种人类组织，必然与其他社会共享某些规则、习俗和信仰，也必然具有某些它自身独有的规则、习俗和信仰。与其他社会比较时，它同时显示出相同与差异。其次，一个社会本质上是一个共时系统，应当研究它当下的配置；在这一点上，它与索绪尔所定义的语言完全相同。社会人类学家没有必要用发生学或历史的方式解释自己的发现，而且这样做确实可能造成极大的误导；对于他在田野工作中观察到的东西，恰当的解释显然是共时的解释，即根据某一特定实践在此时此地意味着什么来解释它，而不是根据它如何起源来解释它。人类学家只须在一个限度内考虑他所研究民族的历史——这里始终假定它拥有以经证实的文献记录为形式的历史——那就是，这个民族对自身历史的理解，在其今日生活中发挥了作用。共时观点在人类学中必然占据主导地位。

The study of (normally) an alien society, which is what social anthropologists engage in, offers particular advantagesto Structuralism. In the first place, an alien society is neither altogether familiar nor altogether unfamiliar; it is a human organization certain to share some of its rules, customs and beliefs with other societies and certain also to possess some rules, customs and beliefs peculiar to itself. It shows both sameness and difference when it is compared with other societies. Second, a society is essentially a synchronic system, to be studied in its present configuration: in this it is exactly the same as a language as Saussure defines it. It is unnecessary, and may indeed be extremely misleading, for a social anthropologist to attempt to explain his findings in genetic or historical terms, when the appropriate explanation of what he observes in the course of his work in the field is clearly the synchronic one, which explains any particular practice in terms of what it means here and now and not in terms of how it originated. The anthropologist needs to take account of the history of the people he studies – always supposing it has a history, in the form of authenticated documentary records – only to the extent that the people’s understanding of its own history plays a part in its life today. The synchronic point of view necessarily presides in anthropology.

结构主义之所以如此适合社会人类学家的需要，第三个原因是，对他们来说，它实际上并非新事物；因为他们历来不仅要描述社会结构，还要解释社会结构，建立人类共同体的可用模型。一个社会如果没有观察者可以感知的结构——而观察者本人被认为因未亲自卷入该结构而享有优势——就会完全无法理解，只是一堆无法证明任何事情的随机资料。无疑存在一些自称纯粹经验主义的社会描述，不过，如果能告诉我们，对于一个给定社会，经验主义观察者选择记录哪些事实，随后又按何种次序把它们组装起来，那就好了。一个人类学事实，只有被记录下来之后才成为人类学事实：它是观察者对所观察事物的叙述，通常用一种不同于所研究社会语言的语言传达出来。

A third reason why Structuralism lends itself so well to the requirements of social anthropologists is that it is in fact nothing new for them, since they have traditionally set out not simply to describe social structures but also to explain them, to produce working models of human collectivities. A society without a structure that is perceptible to the observer, who is himself presumed to enjoy the benefit of being uninvolved personally in that structure, would be quite unintelligible, a random collection of data proving nothing. There are no doubt accounts of societies which purport to be purely empiricist, but it would be good to be told which facts about a given society the empiricist observer chooses to record and what order he then assembles them in. An anthropological fact only becomes so once it has been recorded: it is an account by an observer of something observed and conveyed, usually, in a language other than that of the society under study.

我希望，把列维-斯特劳斯在20世纪40年代登场之前完成的许多社会人类学研究称为原结构主义，并非不合理。列维-斯特劳斯此后倡导并践行的结构主义，遭到怀疑，有时甚至敌意，尤其来自在英美人类学经验主义传统中受训练的人类学家。这主要是因为，列维-斯特劳斯的结构主义声称能解释的东西太多，解释方式又如此合乎逻辑，以至于他把它作为一种文化理论提出来；在许多人类学家看来，这远远超出了已获得的证据。经验主义传统积累的资料，似乎证实了不同社会之间社会实践的无穷多样性；而列维-斯特劳斯则声称，同样的资料能够以越来越抽象的方式被用来揭示所有文化的根本统一性。他代表着结构主义最极端的普遍主义意志。

It would not be unreasonable, I hope, to describe much social anthropology done before Lévi-Strauss came on to the scene in the 1940s as proto-Structuralist. Structuralism as advocated and practised by Lévi-Strauss since that time has met with suspicion and sometimes hostility, particularly from anthropologists trained in the empiricist tradition of Anglo-American anthropology. This is mainly because Lévi-Strauss’s Structuralism claims to explain so much and to explain it so logically, to the point where he proffers it as a theory of culture, which to many anthropologists is to go far beyond the evidence acquired. Where the empiricist tradition accumulates data which seem to confirm the endless diversity of social practices, between one society and another, Lévi-Strauss claims that the same data can be used, in ever more abstract ways, to reveal the fundamental unity of all cultures. He represents Structuralism in its most extreme will to universalism.

然而，列维-斯特劳斯式结构主义与它之前的理论人类学之间，存在许多共同之处，尤其是其中被称为“功能主义”的分支。功能主义也受共时观点约束，因为它力求根据遇到的各种实践的日常社会功能来解释它们。每一种实践都是一个部分，其“价值”在于它能够被证明在整体中占据的位置，而这就是简单的结构主义。最重要的是，人类学不能让自己为个体行为的反复无常或怪异而烦扰，因为它必须严谨地把自己限制在集体层面。它寻求的是一个社会的集体行为最终赖以建立的系统性规则组合，可以忽略那些纯属个体的背离或越轨。因此，像A.R.拉德克利夫-布朗这样一位经验主义人类学家，在写下如下论述时，与列维-斯特劳斯一样，是一位严格的结构主义者：“同样，我们在任何人类社会中观察到的社会现象，并不是个别人类成员的性质所产生的直接结果，而是把他们结合起来的社会结构的结果。”7

Yet there is much common ground between Lévi-Straussian Structuralism and the theoretical anthropology which preceded it, especially that branch of it known as ‘functionalism’. Functionalism, too, is tied to the synchronic point of view, seeking as it does to explain the practices it comes across in terms of their everyday social function. Each practice is a part whose ‘value’ lies in the place it can be shown to occupy within the whole, and that is simple Structuralism. Anthropology above all cannot afford to worry itself about the vagaries or eccentricities of individual behaviour because it must remain scrupulously restricted to the collective. It seeks the systematic sets of rules on which a society’s collective behaviour ultimately rests, and may ignore those defections or transgressions which are merely individual. Thus an empiricist anthropologist like A. R. Radcliffe-Brown is every bit as severe a Structuralist as Lévi-Strauss in writing that: ‘So also the social phenomena which we observe in any human society are not the immediate result of the nature of individual human beings, but are the results of the social structure by which they are united.’7

如此直截了当地否定个体是人类学研究的适当对象，非常清楚地显示出，结构主义反对一切试图从心理学上解释它所搜集资料的做法。列维-斯特劳斯本人把这种反个体主义推到了过分的程度（我还会回到这一点），但并非所有结构主义者都有义务如此。结构主义无法记录不可分类的现象，但仍可以珍视它们；在这一点上，它与其他任何科学方法并无不同。

Such a forthright dismissal of the individual as a proper subject of anthropological study brings out very well the opposition of Structuralism to all attempts at a psychological explanation of the data which it collects. Lévi-Strauss himself carries this anti-individualism to excessive lengths (I will come back to this), but this is not incumbent on all Structuralists. Structuralism cannot register phenomena that are unclassifiable but it may still esteem them; in this it is no different from any other scientific method.

列维-斯特劳斯的结构主义与人类学中先于它出现的结构主义原型，最大的区别在于各自的范围。像拉德克利夫-布朗这样的结构主义者，所研究结构的抽象程度，明显低于列维-斯特劳斯所追求的结构。家庭、学校、教会都是社会结构，都可以在一定程度的抽象层次上加以描述，以揭示它们如何运作、成员之间具有何种关系。这样的描述具有结构主义性质，却还很难称为作为学派的结构主义，因为后者具有更广泛的抱负，不把一整系列看似混乱又令人困惑的此类证据整合为一个单一模型，就不会满足。列维-斯特劳斯大大扩大了社会人类学的范围，也因此使这门学科对外行更有吸引力。他寻求把一个民族的文化实践与其社会、经济和政治实践整合起来，从而打开了这门学科。过去，普通读者可能会对诸如澳大利亚原住民亲属结构的技术性描述感到厌倦和困惑；但只要提出这些结构可以与原住民岩画中可观察到的形式联系起来，他立刻就会被吸引。而列维-斯特劳斯正是通过论证此类联系而成名的。厄内斯特·盖尔纳用一个对比，机智地概括了人类学适宜研究领域的这种巨大扩展：“例如，结构是一个人可以和谁结婚；文化是新娘穿什么。”8 得益于列维-斯特劳斯及他所广泛推行的结构主义，文化开始被视为完全值得进行理智而系统的研究的事情。

The great difference between Lévi-Strauss’s Structuralism and the prototypes of Structuralism that preceded it in anthropology lies in their respective scopes. A structuralist such as Radcliffe-Brown studies structures which are markedly less abstract than those pursued by Lévi-Strauss. Families, schools, churches are all social structures and may all be described with a certain degree of abstraction in order to bring out how they function and what the relations are between their members. Such a description will be structuralist but hardly Structuralist, because Structuralism has wider ambitions and cannot rest until it has integrated a whole range of seemingly confused and confusing evidence of this kind into a single model. Lévi-Strauss has greatly enlarged the scope of social anthropology and made it a much more intriguing discipline for outsiders in doing so. By seeking to integrate the cultural practices of a people with their social, economic and political practices he has opened the subject up. Where once the lay reader might have been bored and bemused by technical accounts of, let us say, Australian aboriginal kinship structures, he is at once attracted by the suggestion that those structures can be assimilated to the forms observable in aboriginal rock-art; and it is in arguing for assimilations of this kind that Lévi-Strauss has made his name. The dramatic enlargement of the proper field for anthropological study is wittily summarized in Ernest Gellner’s contrast: ‘Structure was, for instance, whom one could marry; culture was what the bride wore.’8 Thanks to Lévi-Strauss and the Structuralism he has so widely imposed, culture has come to seem a matter fully deserving of intelligent and systematic investigation.

盖尔纳的评论，我想并不是为了恭维列维-斯特劳斯，却揭示了结构主义中一种特别可贵的东西：它对资料极大的开放性，不论这些资料以前被认为多么琐碎。对传统人类学家来说，新娘的服装很可能显得完全无足轻重，而她结婚时遵守的婚姻规则则具有持久的重要性。但忠实地践行结构主义，就会反对那种居高临下地认定社会行为的某些方面无关紧要的假设；因为它的原则迫使它对一切都感兴趣，或者，如果这是一种超人的抱负，至少也要把它遇到的一切都视为有趣，因为它们都有意义。新娘服装这个问题，放在符号学名下讨论更合适（见第3章）；眼下只须指出，它们从来不只是功能性的。在任何社会中，新娘服装的意义都不仅在于让她保暖或仅仅蔽体；其颜色、形状、装饰和材料，无不遵守某些规范，或者在某些情况下打破规范。人类学家完全可以建立一个模型，用以解释一个给定社会中可观察到的各种着装实践。

Gellner’s remark, which is not I think meant to be flattering to Lévi-Strauss, brings out something especially valuable in Structuralism, which is its great opennes to data, however trivial they may previously have been thought to be. To a traditional anthropologist a bride’s costume may well have seemed an entirely negligible matter, whereas the marriage-rules observed in her union were of lasting importance. But when it is faithfully practised, Structuralism works against any condescending assumption that some aspects of social behaviour are insignificant, because its principles force it to take an interest in everything or, if that is a superhuman ambition, at least to treat everything it meets with as interesting because significant. Brides’ costumes are something better discussed under the heading of Semiotics (see Chapter 3), but suffice it for now to point out that they are never merely functional. There is more to a bride’s costume in any society than keeping her warm or simply covered; its colour, shape, decoration and material all either follow or in some cases break certain norms, and it is perfectly feasible for an anthropologist to construct a model whereby to explain the various clothing practices observable in a given society.

列维-斯特劳斯的结构主义，旨在使文化的一切此类表现都变得可理解。他相信，它们证明了一种无意识逻辑的存在；这种逻辑的实际形式在不同社会之间必然有所变化，但其根本结构为全人类所共有。因此，作为人类学家，他采取的方式恰好与比较语言学家必须采取的方式一样，依据这样的假设：不论一种语言与另一种语言有多大差别，所有语言都共有一种根本结构，一种没有它任何语言都不可能成为语言的本质。

The aim of Lévi-Strauss’s Structuralism is to make all such manifestations of culture intelligible. It is his belief that they are evidence of an unconscious logic whose actual forms will necessarily vary between one society and another but which is, in its fundamental structure, common to all humanity. As an anthropologist he has proceeded, therefore, in just the same way as a comparative linguist must, on the assumption that however much one language may vary from another there is a fundamental structure common to them all, an essence without which no language could be a language.

结构语言学对列维-斯特劳斯的影响，确实异常直接。1942年冬，他作为被占领的法国的流亡者，来到纽约，与已从被占领的布拉格流亡而来的雅各布森在同一机构授课。聆听雅各布森关于音位学、尤其是关于把音位分析为成对对立的“区别特征”的讲授，极大地帮助列维-斯特劳斯走上了通往成熟结构主义的道路。在巴西印第安人中进行田野工作时，他已经发现，人类学在向当地知情人搜集信息时，对语言有多么依赖，而人类学家要准确记录一种完全陌生的语言，又有多么困难。他从新音位学中学到的，是如何把混乱与繁多化为秩序：“我将从结构语言学中学到的是……重要的不是迷失在众多不同的项之间，而是考察把它们相互连接起来的、更简单、更可理解的关系。”9 对这番自述还可以补充一点：列维-斯特劳斯结构主义中如此鲜明的二元论特征，也见于雅各布森的音位学图式。正如我所指出的，这个图式比早先的表述前进了一步，因为它使构成音位的区别特征彼此处于对立关系之中。列维-斯特劳斯在其精彩的社会和文化阐释实践中所使用的图式，完全依赖于这种成对的二元术语或概念，尽管他愿意承认，他认为内在于社会和文化组织的某些对立，比另一些更弱。

The influence of structural linguistics on Lévi-Strauss has indeed been unusually direct. In the winter of 1942 he, an exile from occupied France, found himself in New York and lecturing at the same institution as Jakobson, an exile now from occupied Prague. Listening to what Jakobson had to say about phonology, and in particular about the analysis of phonemes into opposed pairs of ‘distinctive features’, helped greatly to set Lévi-Strauss on the path towards his mature Structuralism. He had already found, during his field-work among Brazilian Indians, how reliant anthropology is on language as it sets about gathering information from native informants and how hard it is for the anthropologist accurately to notate a wholly alien language. The lesson he learned from the new phonology was one of reducing confusion and multiplicity to order: ‘What I was to learn from structural linguistics was . . . that instead of losing one’s way among the multitude of different terms the important thing is to consider the simpler and more intelligible relations by which they are interconnected.’9 To which acknowledgement one might add that the binarism which is so marked a feature of Lévi-Strauss’s Structuralism is also to be found in Jakobson’s phonological scheme, which, as I have indicated, represented an advance on earlier formulations because it sets the distinctive features of which phonemes are composed into relations of opposition one with another. The schemata which Lévi-Strauss employs in his brilliant exercises in social and cultural interpretation rely exclusively on such binary pairs of terms or concepts, even though he is prepared to allow that some of the oppositions he believes to be endemic in social and cultural organization are weaker than others.

列维-斯特劳斯对作为语言基本要素的音位概念格外感兴趣，因为他把它看作自然与文化相接的那个点；而自然与文化的对立，早已使他觉得是关键的对立：如果我们要理解人类生活中特属于人的东西，就必须识别并描绘这一对立。列维-斯特劳斯深受马克思影响，具有这位思想家那种近乎执迷的意识：一切社会或文化的东西都是人造的，因此也可以重新制造。真正自然的东西，按定义是不可理解的；但文化的东西偏离了自然，是可理解的，因此能够被整合进某种系统性的解释。在语言学中，音位是我们在仍处于文化领域的同时，所能达到的最接近自然的地方，因为音位是一种社会制度中的要素，因此已不再是自然声音。最重要的是，它是一种有意义的声音。

Lévi-Strauss was especially interested in the notion of the phoneme as the basic element of language because he saw it as the point where nature joined with culture, and the opposition between nature and culture had already struck him as the crucial one which we must recognize and chart if we are to understand what is specifically human in human life. Greatly influenced by Marx, Lévi-Strauss has that thinker’s obsessive awareness that everything that is social or cultural is man-made and might therefore be remade. The truly natural is by definition unintelligible, but the cultural, which is a departure from the natural, is intelligible and thus capable of being integrated within some systematic explanation. In linguistics the phoneme is as near as we can come to nature while remaining cultural, because a phoneme is an element in a social institution and therefore no longer a natural sound. It is, above all, a meaningful sound.

列维-斯特劳斯相信，他能够从人类学世界中为音位提供一个巧妙的对应物，那就是“乱伦禁忌”，即禁止与近亲发生性关系的规定；他假定这种禁忌为一切社会所共有。对列维-斯特劳斯而言，乱伦禁忌是文化取代自然、或取代单纯生物性的那个转折点，因为它促成了在直系家庭群体之外结婚，并建立起一种“交换网络”，其中供交换的项目是女性。这样建立的网络，成为人类群体之间“交流”的手段；正是通过交流，当一个群体把自己的神话、仪式、习俗和物品“传达”给另一个群体时，列维-斯特劳斯社会模型所极度依赖的那些转换才可能发生。在从一个群体传到另一个群体、或从一个人群传到另一个人群的过程中，这些神话等会发生变化，因为接受它们的群体必然试图把它们纳入自己已有的文化储备之中，必要时也使它们适合自身的自然环境。

Lévi-Strauss believed that he could provide an ingenious parallel for the phoneme from the anthropological world, and that was the ‘incest taboo’ or proscription of sexual relations with near kinsfolk which he assumes to be common to all societies. The incest taboo is for Lévi-Strauss the point at which culture takes over from nature, or from mere biology, leading as it does to marriage outside the immediate family group and to the institution of a ‘network of exchange’ in which the items offered for exchange are women. The network so founded becomes a means of ‘communication’ between human groups and it is through communication that the transformations on which Lévi-Strauss’s model of society relies so heavily can take place, when one group ‘communicates’ its myths, rituals, customs and goods to another. In the process of passing from group to group, or population to population, these myths etc. undergo changes, since the group receiving them will inevitably seek to assimilate them to the existing cultural stock and to its own natural environment if need be.

把语言学家的音位与乱伦禁忌并列，并不总能使列维-斯特劳斯的人类学同行信服，因为这一类比依赖于把性关系与婚姻视为同义；不过，报道社会人类学内部的争论，并不属于我在这里的任务。重要的是，列维-斯特劳斯后来从事人类学研究时，坚信结构音位学是一个值得遵循的模型，可以调整后用于研究亲属关系这类传统人类学问题。亲属关系十分适合他的方法，因为它与术语体系紧密相连，也就是说，与各个社会选择用来指称不同亲属关系程度和血统传承的名称紧密相连。因此，可以把一个亲属系统看作一种“语言”：

The parallel between the linguist’s phoneme and the incest taboo is not one which has always impressed Lévi-Strauss’s fellow anthropologists, depending as it does on treating sexual relations and marriage as if they were synonymous; but it is no part of my commission here to report on quarrels internal to social anthropology. What is important is that Lévi-Strauss went on to practise anthropology in the strong conviction that structural phonology is a model fit to be followed and to be adapted for use in the study of such traditional anthropological questions as kinship. Kinship lends itself well to his methods because it is bound up with terminology, or with the names which various societies choose to give to various degrees of family relationship and descent. A kinship system may thus be looked on as a ‘language’:

如同音位，亲属称谓是意义的要素；如同音位，只有被整合进系统中，它们才获得意义。“亲属系统”与“语音系统”一样，是心灵在无意识思维层面建构的。最后，亲属关系模式、婚姻规则、某些类型亲属之间相似的规定态度等等，在世界上彼此分散的地区和根本不同的社会中反复出现，使我们相信：在亲属关系领域和语言学领域一样，可观察的现象是普遍而隐含的规律发生作用的结果。10

Like phonemes, kinship terms are elements of meaning; like phonemes, they acquire meaning only if they are integrated into systems. ‘Kinship systems’, like ‘phonetic systems’, are built by the mind on the level of unconscious thought. Finally, the recurrence of kinship patterns, marriage rules, similar prescribed attitudes between certain types of relatives, and so forth, in scattered regions of the globe and in fundamentally different societies, leads us to believe that, in the case of kinship as well as linguistics, the observable phenomena result from the action of laws which are general but implicit.10

因此，无论在人类学还是其他领域，结构主义者的任务，都是使经验性的东西同时成为逻辑性的东西，实现列维-斯特劳斯所谓的“具体逻辑”。人类学所研究社会中的个体，对于调节他们相互之间某些交往的亲属系统，很少或完全没有明确的认识。这个系统是“无意识的”，直到人类学家把它带入意识之中。因此，结构主义者从外部、而非内部把握系统。社会人类学方法与语言学方法之间还有一个常见的比较：揭示特定社会实践逻辑的外部视角，被称为“主位”（emic）视角；它与内部的、经验性的或“客位”（etic）视角相对照。这两个术语借自语言声音研究中同样相互对照的“语音的”（phonetic）和“音位的”（phonemic）研究方式，前者是描述性的，后者是分析性的。结构主义者非“主位”即无所作为；事实上，列维-斯特劳斯甚至声称，在语言学中，“客位”或纯经验的层面并不存在。他借助俄罗斯心理学家A.R.卢里亚的发现论证说，我们的大脑在听到音位的那一刻，就将音位分析成了区别特征。对他而言，这进一步证明，我们不可能不成为结构主义者。11

It is thus the task of the Structuralist in anthropology, as in other fields, to make the empirical be also the logical, to achieve what Lévi-Strauss calls a ‘concrete logic’. The individuals within the societies which anthropology studies have little or no explicit knowledge of the kinship system which regulates certain of their dealings with one another. That system is ‘unconscious’ until it is brought into consciousness by the anthropologist. The Structuralist thus grasps the system from outside and not from within. In a further comparison commonly made between the methods of social anthropology and of linguistics, the outside view which reveals the logic of particular social practices is known as the ‘emic’ view, and this is contrasted with the inside, empirical or ‘etic’ view – these two terms having been borrowed from the similarly contrasted ‘phonetic’ and ‘phonemic’ approaches to the sounds of language, where the first is descriptive and the second analytical. The Structuralist is ‘emic’ or nothing; indeed, Lévi-Strauss has gone so far as to claim that in linguistics the ‘etic’ or purely empirical level does not exist; he uses the findings of the Russian psychologist A. R. Luria to argue that our brains analyse phonemes into their distinctive features in the very act of hearing them. This is further proof for him that we cannot but be Structuralists.11

对亲属关系的结构主义研究，趋向于形成一种代数：它所处理的是术语和态度构成的多样、却在结构上连贯的系统。因此，它对外行人几乎没有吸引力。不过，对于列维-斯特劳斯运用其方法研究的社会人类学其他关键主题——图腾主义、仪式、神话和艺术——情况并非如此。有一点值得认识到：列维-斯特劳斯试图整合对这些不同社会实践的研究，以证明它们具有共同逻辑，这是一种论战性的行动。他尤其是在挑战西方人类学家及一般民众中一种古老却仍然盛行的假设：认为人类学所研究的民族头脑简单，甚至似乎与我们在种类上都有所不同。列维-斯特劳斯毕生努力，通过揭示原住民社会所依赖的思想系统具有出人意料的复杂性，来提高原住民在西方的地位；这其中有一种慷慨精神。但必须说，这种慷慨也伴随着相应的冲动：猛烈抨击西方思想的科学主义傲慢和个人崇拜。他把结构主义运用于图腾主义等现象，无疑部分是为了责备我们：数百年来，我们错误地相信自己的经济和技术优越性，这导致了全世界原住民社会的毁灭和自然环境遭到掠夺。结构主义所依据的思想原则与生态学原则之间存在密切联系，这是人人都看得见的；生态学把环境视为一个系统，其中没有任何要素可以脱离其他相关要素而被孤立地考察。在列维-斯特劳斯的著作中，这一生态学的对应关系非常明显。

The Structuralist study of kinship tends towards an algebra of diverse though structurally coherent systems of terms and attitudes; as such it is of scant interest for laypeople. The same does not hold, however, for those other key topics of social anthropology to which Lévi-Strauss has applied his method: to totemism, ritual, myth and art. It helps to realize that in seeking to integrate the study of all these various social practices so as to prove their common logic, Lévi-Strauss is being polemical. In particular, he is challenging the old but still prevalent supposition among Western anthropologists and citizens generally that the peoples whom anthropology studies are simple-minded, to the point even where they may come to seem different in kind from ourselves. There is a generosity in Lévi-Strauss’s lifelong efforts to raise the standing of indigenous peoples in the West by revealing the unsuspected complexity of the systems of thought on which their societies rest; a generosity which goes, it has to be said, with a corresponding urge to belabour Western thought for its scientistic arrogance and cult of personality. Lévi-Strauss’s applications of Structuralism to such phenomena as totemism are certainly meant partly to reprove us for the centuries of mistaken belief in our own economic and technological superiority, which have led to the destruction all over the world of indigenous societies and to the plundering of the natural environment. A close connection is there for all to see between the intellectual principles which inform Structuralism and the principles of ecology, which regards the environment as a system no element of which can be considered in isolation from other, related elements. In the work of Lévi-Strauss this ecological parallel is very apparent.

他把自己的结构主义提出为一种方式，用来调和“客位”与“主位”这两个据说相对立的秩序。这两个秩序也就是经验与逻辑、或现实与理想的秩序。如果列维-斯特劳斯是对的，那么它们在结构主义中融为一体，因为他的结构真正居于事实与理论之间；无论事实还是理论，都不能被承认为独立于结构而存在。列维-斯特劳斯认为，在所谓“原始”社会中，最容易观察到现实与逻辑的这种融合。因此，《野性的思维》12这样一本考察图腾主义性质的书，也应当被看作对图腾主义的辩护。图腾主义是一种在我们许多人看来很奇特的社会制度，但列维-斯特劳斯把它呈现为对自然环境一种极富才智、理性而细致的组织，其目的在于把动植物物种之间观察到的区别，与人类群体之间的区别对应起来。因此，列维-斯特劳斯声称在图腾主义中起作用的原则，是“同构”：周围人类世界的结构，被认为可以与周围自然世界的结构相互对应。列维-斯特劳斯坚持，图腾主义是一个理智的、甚至审美的系统，而不是我们可能傲慢地想象的一个实用系统。我们惯常的看法是，如果原住民在辨别那些在我们看来一样的动植物物种方面明显出色——他们常常确实如此——那是因为他们能由此获得实际的、大概是经济上的好处。但他与此相反，主张图腾主义的辨别系统是理论性的，因为它们发挥着如何理解世界的模型的作用。事实上，图腾主义者远比表面看来更精深：他们是实践中的结构主义者，对他们而言，感性的与可理解的东西是一体的，因为他们生活于其中的动植物构成了一个有差异的意义系统。

He proposes his Structuralism as a way of reconciling the supposedly opposed orders of the ‘etic’ and the ‘emic’. These two orders are also those of the empirical and the logical, or the real and the ideal. In Structuralism, if Lévi-Strauss is right, they merge, because his structures are truly intermediary between facts and theory, neither of which can be recognized as existing independently of the structure. It is Lévi-Strauss’s view that this merging of the real with the logical is to be observed most readily in so-called ‘primitive’ societies. Thus a book like The Savage Mind,12 which investigates the nature of totemism, should be read also as a defence of totemism, a social institution quaint to many of us but presented by Lévi-Strauss as a most resourceful, intelligent and observant ordering of the natural environment with a view to correlating the distinctions observed between species of fauna and flora with distinctions between human groups. The principle which Lévi-Strauss claims is operative in totemism is thus one of ‘isomorphism’, in that the structure of the surrounding human world is seen as open to correlation with that of the surrounding natural world. Lévi-Strauss is insistent that totemism is an intellectual and even an aesthetic system, not, as we might superciliously imagine, a practical one. Contrary to our received view that if indigenous peoples are conspicuously good, as they frequently are, at discriminating between plant and animal species which to us look the same, then this is because they derive practical, probably economic advantages from being so, he maintains that totemistic systems of discrimination are theoretical, because they function as models of how to make sense of the world. Totemists, in fact, are a great deal more sophisticated than they appear to be: they are practising Structuralists for whom the sensuous and intelligible are one, since the plants and animals among which they live form a differential system of meanings.

从这类论证中不难看出，为什么列维-斯特劳斯被指责为“心智主义”。他否认这一指责，理由是，他的目的在于超越西方心灵坚持在心智与现实之间作出的区分；在他看来，这种区分肤浅而过时。在这里，可以认为他遵循了胡塞尔的现象学要求：同时把主体与客体保持在视野之中，作为同一条探究轴线的两极。图腾主义者仿佛能够同时看见并理解的能力，在研究图腾主义的列维-斯特劳斯身上也得到体现。然而，区别在于，图腾主义者本身是一个理想化形象，而列维-斯特劳斯不是。图腾主义者是一个理想化形象，因为并非所有图腾主义者都很擅长图腾主义；有些人可能完全无法识别并运用其文化所遵循的差异系统。尽管列维-斯特劳斯表示，希望人们只把他当作结构主义的代言人来读，而不是当作独特的个人克洛德·列维-斯特劳斯来读，但这种理想化似乎并不适用于他本人。如果他被接受为结构主义的纯粹声音，那么，对我们这些读者而言，他就达到了与他笔下图腾主义者相同的理想化程度；但如果他未能被如此接受，他的学说被看作个人的学说，那么，他表面上的谦逊和自我隐退便毫无用处。在这里，用他的结构主义反过来对付他，是正当的；可以指出这样一个真理：在我们生活的这种高度个人化的文化中，努力实现匿名，就是有意识地使自己与众不同，从而使自己个体化。在这一点上，列维-斯特劳斯的结构主义是矛盾的。

It is easy to see from such arguments as these why Lévi-Strauss has been accused of ‘mentalism’, a charge which he rejects on the grounds that his purpose is to move beyond the, as he sees it, facile and outmoded distinction which the Western mind insists on making between the mental and the real. Here he might be seen as following Husserl’s phenomenological prescription, of keeping both subject and object in view at once, as the two poles of one axis of inquiry. The ability of the totemist to see and understand, as it were, simultaneously, is reflected by the same ability in Lévi-Strauss as a student of totemism. The difference, however, is that where the totemist is himself an idealization, Lévi-Strauss is not. The totemist is an idealization because not all totemists may be very good at totemism; some may be quite unable to recognize and apply the system of differentiation followed by their culture. This does not seem to apply to Lévi-Strauss himself, despite his expressed wish to be read only as the spokesperson for Structuralism and not as the distinctive individual Claude Lévi-Strauss. If he is accepted as the mere voice of Structuralism, then he achieves the same degree of idealization for us, his readers, as the totemists whom he is writing about; but if he fails to be so accepted, and his doctrine is taken to be a personal one, his apparent modesty and self-effacement are of no avail. It is legitimate here to turn his Structuralism against him and point to the truth that to strive to achieve anonymity within a highly personalized culture such as we live in is knowingly to distinguish and thus to individualize oneself. In this respect, Lévi-Strauss’s Structuralism is ambivalent.

它的效果，是赋予它声称揭示的思想系统以非凡的自主性和广阔范围。列维-斯特劳斯提供给我们的结构模型，具有我们在语言结构中已经见过的那种可扩展性。它们不仅涵盖已知资料，而且潜在地涵盖尚待发现的资料。实际上，它们是真正理论性的，因为它们不仅为已实现的东西、也为可能的东西立法；正如乔姆斯基假定我们每个人天生具有的语言习得心理结构，会受到我们实际习得语言的结构的极大限制，结构主义人类学家也把实际社会结构看作对系统生产能力的特定限制。列维-斯特劳斯的思想系统，建立在一组组二元对立项以及转换规则之上，系统通过这些规则能够产生越来越多实际的“事件”；它们是未来可能性的储备库，就我们所能看见的而言，没有给绝对的创新留下余地。无论人类学家未来可能记录到什么社会组织或集体行为形式，它们的可能性都已为人所知；根据现有证据绘制的图式中，已有一个空格在等待它们。这些图式是一切结构主义思想的特征，而不仅属于列维-斯特劳斯式人类学；它们指向一种对系统思想至上地位几乎神秘的信仰，认为系统思想有自己的规则，可以从一个或多或少稳定的结构中生成新的事件。

Its effect is to endow the systems of thought which it claims to reveal with a remarkable autonomy and range. The structural models which Lévi-Strauss offers us have the same extensibility which we have already met with in the case of linguistic structures. They take account not only of the known data but also, potentially, of data yet to be discovered. They are truly theoretical in fact, in legislating for the possible as well as the actualized; and just as Chomsky’s posited mental structure which we are each of us born with for language acquisition is greatly constrained by the structure of the language we actually acquire, so the Structuralist anthropologist looks on actual social structures as particular limitations of the productivity of the system. Lévi-Strauss’s systems of thought, which are based on sets of binarily opposed terms and on rules of transformation by which the system is able to yield more and more actual ‘events’, are a reservoir of future possibilities, leaving, so far as one can see, no room for absolute innovation. Whatever forms of social organization or collective behaviour may be registered by anthropologists in the future, their possibility is already known; there is an empty pigeon-hole waiting for them in the schema drawn up on the existing evidence. These schemata are a feature of all Structuralist thought and not just of Lévi-Straussian anthropology; they point to an almost mystical belief in the sovereignty of systematic thought, which has its own rules for generating new events from a more or less stable structure.

因此，列维-斯特劳斯开始了一场宏大的探索，寻找一种无处不在、本质上不受时间限制的文化与社会逻辑：

Lévi-Strauss has thus embarked on a mighty quest, for an all-pervading and essentially timeless logic of culture and society:

一个民族的习俗，作为整体，总是带有一种风格：它们构成系统。我确信，这些系统的数目不是无限的；人类社会如同个体——在其游戏、梦或幻想中——从不在绝对意义上创造，而只是从一个可以重建的理想组合库中选择某些组合。只要编制一份清单，列出所有已观察到的习俗、所有在神话中想象的习俗、儿童和成人游戏中唤起的习俗，以及健康和患病个体的梦和精神病理行为，就能编制一张类似化学元素周期表的表；其中，所有现实的或仅仅可能的习俗，都按家族归类呈现，我们只需识别出社会事实上采用了哪些。13

The customs of a people as a whole are always marked by a style: they form systems. I am convinced that these systems do not exist in unlimited numbers, and that human societies, like individuals – in their games, their dreams or their fantasies – never create in an absolute sense, but limit themselves to choosing certain combinations from an ideal repertoire which it would be possible to reconstitute. By drawing up an inventory of all the customs that had been observed, all those imagined in myths, those also evoked in the games of children and adults, and the dreams of healthy and sick individuals and psychopathological behaviours, one would be able to draw up a periodic table like that of the chemical elements, in which all real or merely possible customs would appear grouped in families, and in which we would simply need to recognize those which societies have in fact adopted.13

这一结构主义纲领具有令人屏息的包罗万象性，它承诺能够把人类行为中即使最私密、最独特的片段也纳入其模型；除了列维-斯特劳斯本人，也许没有多少人会赞同它。正是在这种规模的主张中，他的结构主义表明自己是一种意识形态，而不只是探究方法。但他的极端主义引起的疑虑和怀疑，不应妨碍我们赞赏他在着手分析一套特定社会实践或神话材料时，实际展示出的逻辑。我刚才给出的引文，是他自传性著作《忧郁的热带》中一章的开头；该章分析了一个名叫卡杜韦奥的美洲印第安民族的习俗、制度、态度和艺术。要同时体验列维-斯特劳斯结构主义的非凡抱负及其极大的优雅，没有比这篇错综复杂却引人入胜的论述更好的地方了。其中，对卡杜韦奥人体彩绘的分析，也许是最令人瞩目的成就：首先，我们可能以为，这种艺术形式可以描述，却不能分析；其次，它所涉及的线条似乎复杂得令人费解。这些彩绘是展示结构主义分析能力的理想对象，因为它们看似既无意义，又杂乱无章。一旦列维-斯特劳斯对它们加以研究，它们就两者都不是了。

The breathtaking inclusiveness of this Structuralist programme, which promises to be able to incorporate within its model even the most intimate and idiosyncratic episodes of human behaviour, would not perhaps be subscribed to by many besides Lévi-Strauss himself. It is in claims of this magnitude that his Structuralism reveals itself as an ideology rather than simply a method of inquiry. But the doubts and suspicions which his extremism arouses in us should not prevent us from admiring the logic of his practical demonstrations when he settles to analyse a particular corpus of social practices or myths. The quotation I have just given opens a chapter in his autobiographical volume, Trisles tropiques, in which he analyses the customs, institutions, attitudes and arts of an American-Indian people called the Caduveo. There could be no better place to experience both the extraordinary ambitions of Lévi-Strauss’s Structuralism and its great elegance than in this intricate but absorbing account. The analysis of Caduveo body-painting is the most arresting achievement of all perhaps, because, first, we may suppose that such an art-form can be described but not analysed and, second, because of the baffling complexity of line it seems to involve. These paintings are ideal as an object on which to demonstrate Structuralism’s powers of analysis because they are seemingly both insignificant and random. Once Lévi-Strauss has attended to them they seem neither.

他的分析建立在所需的成对对立项之上——男人／女人、雕刻／绘画、具象／抽象、角／曲线、对称／不对称，等等。（把其中某些对子称为“对立”，有些牵强；而具象图案与抽象图案之间这种对立，肯定是由刚从巴黎来的西方人类学家引入的。）他成功地把秩序引入卡杜韦奥人体彩绘令人眼花缭乱的众多形式之中，从而赋予它以往未曾得到的尊严和严肃性。随后，他通过一系列过于冗长详细、无法在此复述的论证，把这种艺术实践中揭示的结构，与卡杜韦奥社会的结构联系起来。不过，列维-斯特劳斯就其艺术的社会功能所作的结论很重要，它显示出，他的结构分析最终如何能够超越自身赖以建立的二元划分。卡杜韦奥社会似乎与任何其他社会一样，被马克思主义者喜欢称为“内部矛盾”的东西所撕裂。这些矛盾是其社会组织的结果，事实上无法解决：

His analysis is built on the necessary pairs of opposed terms – men/ women, carving/painting, representationalism/abstraction, angles/curves, symmetry/asymmetry, and so on. (It is stretching things to call some of these pairs ‘opposed’, and an opposition such as that between representational and abstract motifs is surely one imported by the Western anthropologist, fresh from Paris.) He manages to introduce order into the bewildering multiplicity of forms in Caduveo body-painting and in so doing to accord it a dignity and seriousness not accorded to it before. He then goes on to assimilate the structure revealed in this artistic practice to the structures of Caduveo society, in a series of arguments too long and detailed to retail here. The conclusion which Lévi-Strauss comes to, however, concerning the social function of their art is important for showing how his structural analysis can ultimately transcend the binary divisions on which it depends. Caduveo society, it seems, like any other society, is riven by what Marxists like to refer to as ‘internal contradictions’. These contradictions are the consequence of their social organization and are in fact insoluble:

但是，社会层面所欠缺的、或者禁止他们设想的补救办法，不可能完全不为他们所感知。它在暗中助长了他们的不安。由于他们无法认识它并把它付诸生活，他们便开始梦见它。不是以一种会与他们的偏见发生冲突的直接形式，而是以一种经过转置、看似无害的形式：在他们的艺术之中。14

But the remedy that was lacking on the social plane, or which they were forbidden to envisage, could not escape them altogether. Insidiously, it helped to disturb them. And since they were unable to take cognisance of it and live it, they began to dream it. Not in a direct form which would have come up against their prejudices; in a transposed and apparently inoffensive form: in their art.14

一个社会的神话，与卡杜韦奥艺术的情形相同；在阐释神话方面，列维-斯特劳斯展示了非凡的耐心和技巧。神话如同艺术，具有在想象层面调和那些在现实层面无法解决的社会矛盾的功能。如果我们承认，在本世纪，最重要的是弗洛伊德引导我们把摆脱现实约束的心理图式，解释为由愿望满足所塑造的东西，那么，弗洛伊德对列维-斯特劳斯思想的影响，在这里就十分明显。对列维-斯特劳斯而言，艺术和神话都处理社会可能原本会压抑的材料；因此，它们具有治疗作用，也可以被看作与这些艺术和神话所属人群的社会、经济及其他难以改变的现实处于辩证关系之中。“这样的思辨……并不寻求描绘现实之物，而是要为现实的缺陷提供辩解。”15

And as with Caduveo art so it is with a society’s myths, in the interpretation of which Lévi-Strauss has displayed an extraordinary patience and virtuosity. Myths, like art, have a function of reconciling on the imaginary plane those social contradictions which cannot be resolved on the real plane. The influence of Freud on Lévi-Strauss’s thought is very apparent here, if we accept that it is Freud above all in this century who has led us to interpret mental schemata, once freed from the constraints of reality, as informed by wish-fulfilment. Both art and myth for Lévi-Strauss deal with material which societies might otherwise keep repressed; they are thus therapeutic, and may be seen as standing in a dialectical relationship to the social, economic and other intractable realities of the populations whose art and myths they are. ‘Such speculations . . . do not seek to depict what is real but to justify the shortcomings of reality.’15

列维-斯特劳斯并非第一位认识到神话的重要意义的人类学家，即神话可以作为一个社会如何理解周围世界的证据；不过，可以公允地说，由于他关于主要是美洲印第安民族神话的大量出版物，我们如今对神话总体的看法，已少了许多居高临下的态度。列维-斯特劳斯用神话为他的结构主义提供大量进一步的证据。它们是他的理想研究对象：

Lévi-Strauss was not the first anthropologist to grasp the high significance of myths as evidence of a society’s way of understanding the world around it, but it is fair to claim that as a result of his extensive publications on the myths, chiefly, of American-Indian peoples, we now take a much less patronizing view of myth in general. Lévi-Strauss uses them to provide copious further evidence for his Structuralism. They are an ideal subject for him:

神话研究使研究者面对一种乍看矛盾的情形。一方面，似乎在一个神话的进程中，任何事情都可能发生。没有逻辑，没有连续性。任何特征都可以归给任何主体；一切可以设想的关系都能找到。在神话中，一切都成为可能。但另一方面，在相距极远的地区收集到的神话，竟然惊人地相似，这否定了这种表面上的任意性。因此，问题是：如果神话的内容是偶然的，我们又如何解释世界各地的神话如此相似这一事实？16

Mythology confronts the student with a situation which at first sight appears contradictory. On the one hand it would seem that in the course of a myth anything is likely to happen. There is no logic, no continuity. Any characteristic can be attributed to any subject; every conceivable relation can be found. With myth, everything becomes possible. But on the other hand, this apparent arbitrariness is belied by the astounding similarity between myths collected in widely different regions. Therefore the problem: if the content of a myth is contingent, how are we going to explain the fact that myths throughout the world are so similar?16

这种多样性与相同性之间的表面矛盾，对结构主义者来说，是一条极为鼓舞人心的线索，因为它指向世界各地神话共有的深层结构；而这正是列维-斯特劳斯相信自己在神话分析中揭示出的结构。他采用的技术相对简单：把每个神话划分为构成它的要素，即“神话素”，根据这些“神话素”在神话中的“功能”将其分类，最后在对神话结构的整体解释中，把不同功能类别彼此联系起来。列维-斯特劳斯非常喜欢在音乐作曲过程中寻找自己研究程序的类似物；他把神话分析的效果，比作以乐谱形式呈现神话。在乐谱中，我们不仅沿着各行从左到右按次序阅读，也在各谱表之间垂直阅读，以把握音乐中同时发生的和声（音乐和声与神话所追求的想象中的社会或宇宙“和谐”之间的类比，确实是列维-斯特劳斯论证的重要部分）。我不打算详述他的实际分析方法，因为在第4章讨论结构主义的文学应用时这样做，会更有用；神话既然是叙事，分析它们的方法便很容易扩展到我们自身文化所习惯的虚构叙事或其他叙事。列维-斯特劳斯提供的是一种极为系统的阅读，所读的是那些以前似乎或多或少任意、甚至混乱的故事。他又一次赋予逻辑，而当代西方心灵看来，这些东西几乎以一种令人反感的方式缺乏逻辑。这种分析自有其美，是简约之美，因为列维-斯特劳斯所努力达到的逻辑解释，能够解释并统一大量资料。这些资料所属的不同层次，最终也被证明具有共同的逻辑；例如他对“阿斯迪瓦尔的故事”所作的极其精细复杂的分析，就使一个美洲西北部印第安神话的地理、经济、社会学和宇宙论方面，以非常惊人的方式形成了连贯整体。17

This apparent contradiction between variety and sameness is to the Structuralist a most encouraging clue because it points to an underlying structure common to myths everywhere: and that is the structure Lévi-Strauss believes he has revealed in his analyses of myths. The technique he adopts is relatively simple, being to divide each myth up into its constituent elements or ‘mythemes’, to classify these ‘mythemes’ in terms of their ‘function’ within the myth and finally to relate the various classes of function to one another in an overall explanation of the structure of the myth. Lévi-Strauss is very fond of finding analogies for his procedures in those of musical composition, and he likens the effect of his analysis of myths to presenting them in the form of a musical score, where we not only read sequentially along the lines, from left to right, but also vertically from staff to staff in order to grasp the simultaneous harmonies of the music (the analogy between musical harmony and the imaginary social or cosmological ‘harmony’ aspired to in myth is very much part of Lévi-Strauss’s argument). I shall not dwell on his actual method of analysis, because it will be more useful to do that in Chapter 4, in connection with the literary applications of Structuralism; myths being narratives the method used to analyse them may readily be extended to the fictional or other narratives our own culture is accustomed to. What Lévi-Strauss supplies is an extremely systematic reading of what had before seemed a more or less arbitrary, even chaotic story. Once again, he endows with logic what to a contemporary Western mind appears almost offensively to lack it. The analysis has its own beauty, which is the beauty of economy, since the logical explanation which Lévi-Strauss works towards serves to explain and to unify a great many data. The various levels on which those data belong are themselves ultimately shown to share a logic, as in his hugely elaborate analysis of the ‘Story of Asdiwal’, in which the geographical, economic, sociological and cosmological aspects of a North-west American-Indian myth are made to cohere quite remarkably.17

分析者之所以能够从神话的一个方面或层次转向其他方面或层次，是因为他预先假定，自己在不同领域发现的模式，是彼此的转换。没有哪一个模式是原型，而其余模式只是派生物；分析者可以从任何地方开始，并希望得出同样的结果。列维-斯特劳斯强调，在他那四大卷详尽分析美洲印第安神话的著作中，用作“参照”或起点的神话，是任意选择的。对他而言，他正在分析和整理的庞大故事群，没有“中心”。

The analyst is enabled to pass from one aspect or level of the myth to others by his presumption that the patterns he discovers in the different spheres are transformations of each other. No one pattern is the master from which the others are taken as derivative; the analyst can start anywhere and hope to produce the same result. Lévi-Strauss stresses that the myth which serves as his ‘reference’ or starting-point in his four large and exhaustive volumes of analysis of American-Indian myths was arbitrarily chosen. There is for him no ‘centre’ to the great corpus of stories he is engaged on analysing and ordering.

这种转换原则对于任何结构主义都极为重要，因为它规定，一个表层结构应被理解为另一个表层结构的变体。在列维-斯特劳斯那里，它不仅在特定神话内部起作用，揭示一组关系是另一组关系的逻辑转换；它也在神话之间起作用，揭示一个神话是另一个神话的逻辑转换，而后者本身又可能借自邻近民族。这样，神话思想系统的自主性就得到了保证，因为是神话自身生成其他神话，而人类社会只能声称自己是这些转换发生的场所。对列维-斯特劳斯而言，人类文化史就是一个逐步实现“大系统”内在的越来越多逻辑可能性的历史。

This transformational principle is one of great importance for any Structuralism because it lays down that one surface structure be understood as a version of another. With Lévi-Strauss it operates not only within particular myths, revealing one set of relations to be a logical transformation of another set, but also between myths, revealing one myth to be the logical transformation of another, itself perhaps borrowed from a neighbouring people. In this way the autonomy of the system of mythical thought is guaranteed, since the myths themselves generate other myths, and all that human societies can claim to be is the locus of the transformations. For Lévi-Strauss the history of human culture is one of the gradual realization of more and more of the logical possibilities inherent in the Great System.

虽然神话是故事，但在结构上，它们并不像我们自身社会中所习惯的故事那样明显具有历时性；阅读时，它们不给我们同样的时间顺序感，也不给我们一个情节从另一个情节逻辑地推演出来的感觉。即使这种较弱的时间顺序感，在列维-斯特劳斯那样的分析中也受到冲击，因为这种分析把取自故事不同部分的相关“神话素”汇集在一起。神话中仍存在一种推进，从识别出冲突或矛盾，到在想象中解决它；但在这一转换的两项之间，时间顺序几乎或完全不起作用。列维-斯特劳斯宣称，音乐和神话是“消除时间的机器”18，尽管两者当然都必须在真实时间中展开，这就是其表演的时间。因此，神话是社会人类学与相关学科历史编纂之间的一座极好的桥梁；结构主义也进入了后者，并产生了强大的影响。

Although myths are stories they are not so obviously diachronic in their structure as the stories we are accustomed to in our own society; they do not give us when we read them the same sense of chronology or logical consequence of one episode from another. And even this weakened sense of chronology comes under attack in an analysis such as that conducted by Lévi-Strauss, which groups together related ‘mythemes’ taken from different parts of the story. There is still a progression in the myth, from the recognition of a conflict or contradiction to its imaginary resolution, but between these two terms of the transformation chronology plays little or no part. Music and myths, Lévi-Strauss declares, are ‘machines for suppressing time’,18 even though both, of course, have to unfold in real time, which is the time of their performance. Myths are thus an excellent bridge between social anthropology and the related discipline of historiography, into which Structuralism has also moved, and to powerful effect.

这似乎不对，因为如果有哪门研究致力于拒绝共时观点，那肯定就是历史学。社会人类学关心静态与恒常，几乎不可能不屈从于结构主义；而历史学，人们可能认为，几乎不可能不抵制结构主义，因为历史学家据说关心的是连续发生的事物和变化。事实上，人类学与历史学长期以来被视为对立的；例如，拉德克利夫-布朗就敦促真正的人类学家，不要依赖那些被轻蔑地称为“推测的历史”19的东西，即当地知情人对其社会当下状况如何形成所提供的叙述。神话也是“原来如此”的故事，自称用发生学的方式解释现存事态；因此，列维-斯特劳斯完全从共时角度处理神话，也就更加颠覆了它们表面上的功能。

This should seem wrong, since if ever there was a study committed to the rejection of the synchronic viewpoint it is surely history. Where social anthropology could hardly not have succumbed to Structuralism, given its concern for the static and the constant, history, one might think, could hardly not have stood out against it, since historians are supposedly concerned with the sequential and the changing. Anthropology and history indeed were long looked on as opposites, and the true anthropologist was urged, for example, by Radcliffe-Brown to do without what was slightingly described as ‘conjectural history’,19 or those accounts offered by native informants of how the present state of things in their society had come about. Myths, too, are ‘just-so’ stories, purporting to explain existing states of affairs in genetic terms, which makes Lévi-Strauss’s treatment of them in exclusively synchronic terms the more subversive of their apparent function.

适用于“推测的历史”的说法，似乎也可以适用于那些拥有自身过去的文献证据的社会中，经证实的历史。一种极端的结构主义可能声称，在解释当下的事态时，历史、或者我们社会存在的历时轴线，是无关紧要的；这也确实是人们有时对结构主义作出的假设，即它对过去视而不见。但事实并非如此。共时观点与历时观点的对立被夸大了。对于语言问题中发生学或历史解释的可能性，索绪尔本人从未持封闭态度，因为任何语言学家都不可能如此。语言随着时间而变化，它们具有历史，而这种历史至少可以部分重建：一部分根据书面记录，一部分通过推演，以回溯的方式应用共时原则。索绪尔只是坚持，不应混淆共时与历时观点，而语言爱好者却经常混淆它们。最常见的例子，是试图参照词源来确定语言中某个现行词语的“价值”，仿佛它今天的“价值”应当由遥远过去的价值决定，而不是由它与其他现行词语的关系决定。结构主义与历史编纂并非不可调和；相反，它们能够进行极其富有成效的合作。

What goes for ‘conjectural history’ might also seem to go for authenticated history, in those societies possessing documentary evidence of their own past. An extreme Structuralism might claim that history, or the diachronic axis of our existence in society, is irrelevant when it comes to accounting for the current state of things; and this indeed is the supposition sometimes made about Structuralism, that it is blind to the past. But this is not so. The opposition between the synchronic and diachronic points of view has been overdone. Saussure himself was never closed to the possibilities of genetic or historical explanation in questions of language, for no linguist can be. Languages change through time and they have a history which it is possible, at least in part, to re-create, partly from written records and partly by deduction, by retrospective application of synchronic principles. Saussure was merely insistent that the synchronic and diachronic viewpoints should not be confused, as they frequently are by amateurs of language – the commonest example of such confusion is the attempt to define the ‘value’ of a current term in the language by reference to its etymology, as if its ‘value’ today were to be determined by its value far in the past rather than by the relations it has with other current terms. Structuralism and historiography are not irreconcilable, but, indeed, capable of the most fruitful collaboration.

历史学家清楚地认识到共时轴线与历时轴线之间的区别，会给他们从事历史研究的方式带来某些根本性的后果。首先，对于他们自己作为此时此地从事实践的历史学家的位置，他们可能会认同列维-斯特劳斯的认识：“历史从不是历史……而是为……而写的历史。”20 他的意思是，不可能存在独立于撰写它的历史学家的自在历史，只能有为这位或那位历史学家而写的历史；历史学家会把大量完全属于当下的兴趣、偏见、修辞手法等等带入写史工作。关于极权社会“重写”过去的奥威尔式噩梦，不应阻止我们认识到，一切社会都会随着自身需要和知识的变化而重写过去。历史从来不是超越时间的，而必然是片面的，是一种文本；因为它在某个时代被写成，便总可以被另一个时代重写。超越自身时代而存留下来的伟大历史编纂作品，本身就成为历史文献，为我们丰富地呈现其写作时代及其主导意识形态。比如，吉本的著作中既有大量关于罗马帝国的内容，也有大量18世纪英国的痕迹，因为那部宏伟的历史也是为吉本心目中的读者而写的历史，他与这些读者共享一种文化。

The clear recognition by historians of the distinction between the synchronic and diachronic axes has certain radical consequences for the way they do history. In relation, first, to their own position as practising historians here and now, they may come to share Lévi-Strauss’s realization that ‘History is never history. . . but history-for. . . ’,20 by which he means that there can be no such thing as history-in-itself, independent of the historians who write it, but only history-for this historian or that, who will bring to the business of writing history a great many interests, biases, rhetorical devices and so on that belong absolutely to the present. Orwellian nightmares about the ‘rewriting’ of the past practised by totalitarian societies should not stop us recognizing that all societies rewrite the past as their own needs and knowledge change. History is never timeless but necessarily partial, a text which, because it has been written in one age, may always be rewritten by another. The great works of historiography which survive their own age themselves become historical documents, informing us richly about the age in which they were written and its prevailing ideologies – there is a great deal of the English eighteenth century in Gibbon, let us say, as well as a great deal about the Roman empire, because that mighty history was also a history-for the audience which Gibbon had in view and with which he shared a culture.

作为英语使用者，我们可能忽略两者的区别：一是作为对过去的记录或书写的历史，二是作为实际发生之事的过去本身，因为“history”这个词通常兼指两者。它是一个具有两个截然不同的所指的能指。在这里，我宁愿用“历史编纂”而不用“历史”，以便记住，我们所关心的是历史的书写。既然历史编纂显然是由某人产生的，就更容易接受它必然是为某人而作的。历史编纂是把过去带入现在，或者至少把过去的某些要素和方面带入现在；当下对这些要素和方面有所兴趣，也很可能认为它们曾受到严重忽视。

As English-speakers, we may lose sight of the distinction between history as what has been recorded of or written about the past and the past itself, taken as what actually occurred, because the word ‘history’ generally covers both. It is a signifier with two crucially distinct signifieds. For present purposes, I shall prefer the term ‘historiography’ to history, in order to keep in mind that it is the writing of history that we are concerned with. It is easier to accept that historiography must be for someone because it has clearly been produced by someone. Historiography is a bringing of the past into the present, or at least those elements and aspects of the past in which the present takes some interest and which it may well believe have been seriously neglected.

列维-斯特劳斯所挑战的，是他认为偏袒历时性、反对共时性的一种偏见：实际上是一种历史主义，与“进步”的观念，以及西方当下优越于任何地方任何过去的观念相联系。因此，他的结构主义可以被提出为一种矫正：“民族学家尊重历史，但不给它以特权价值。他把它视为一种与自己研究互补的研究形式：一种在时间中展开人类社会的扇面，另一种在空间中展开。”21 但正如我们所见，在结构主义中，在结构主义者自身文本的均质时空里，时间可以成为空间，空间也可以成为时间。可以说，传统历史学家对共时性的偏见，主要来自历史编纂长期以来在因果关系上的既得利益。如果历史学家以序列呈现过去，这些序列就不是随机而不连续的，而是把某些特定要素呈现为后来其他要素的原因的序列。许多传统历史写作遵循的格言，是 Post hoc ergo propler hoc，亦即“在此之后，故而由此所致”。因果关系让我们非常满意地理解过去，而且不能与时间顺序分开。结构主义怀疑因果关系，并不是因为它愚蠢到否认因果作用，而是因为它把过去的各种要素分为原因与结果的方式容易误导。结构主义试图辨明的关系是双向的；真正结构主义地看待原因与结果，就是把它们看作一种关系的两项。我们都同意，没有先前的原因就不可能有结果；但我们较难理解，没有后来的结果也不可能有原因，理由很充分：在结果出现之前，原因尚未获得其原因的地位。因此，原因不发生在历史中，而发生在历史编纂中，是历史学家文本内部的一种结构关系。历史学家在分配原因和结果时，也在历史中分配意义；历史意义是一种抽象物，归根结底，只能定位于历史编纂的研究者或读者的心灵之中。我们应当与A.J.格雷马斯一起接受：

What Lévi-Strauss challenges is what he believes to be a prejudice working in favour of diachrony and against synchrony: a form of historicism in fact, bound up with the notion of Progress and the superiority of the present in the West over any past anywhere. His Structuralism may thus be put forward as a corrective: ‘The ethnologist respects history, but does not accord it a privileged value. He conceives it as a form of research complementary to his own: one opens the fan of human societies in time, the other in space.’21 But in Structuralism, as we have seen, time can become space and space time, in the homogeneous space-time of the Structuralist’s own text. The prejudice among traditional historians against synchronism stems mostly, one might argue, from the vested interest historiography has long had in causation. If historians present the past in terms of sequences, these are not random and discontinuous sequences but ones in which particular elements are shown as the causes of others coming after them. The motto under which much traditional history-writing operates is that of Post hoc ergo propler hoc, or ‘After which therefore because of which’. Causation makes sense of the past very satisfactorily for us, and is not to be dissociated from chronology. Structuralism is suspicious of causation not because it is foolish enough to deny its efficacity but because of the misleading way in which it categorizes various elements of the past into causes and effects. The relations which Structuralism seeks to identify are two-way and a properly Structuralist way of seeing causes and effects is as the two terms of a relation. We all agree that there can be no effect without a previous cause, but find it harder to grasp that there can be no cause without a subsequent effect, for the good reason that until the effect is present the cause has not acquired its causal status. Thus causes occur not in history but in historiography, as a structural relation within the historian’s text. By distributing causes and effects the historian also distributes meaning in history and historical meaning is an abstraction locatable in the last resort only in the mind of the student or reader of historiography. We should accept with A. J. Greimas that:

因此，对意义的任何把握，都会产生把历史转变为恒定之物的效果：不论我们面对的是对一个生命的意义，还是对一个故事（或历史）的意义的探究，这种探究——也就是说，我们以信息接受者的态度，置身于一种语言表现面前这一事实——所产生的后果，都是历史的算法呈现为状态，换言之，呈现为静态结构。22

Thus, any grasping of meaning has the effect of turning histories into permanences: whether we have to deal with an investigation into the meaning of a life or the meaning of a story (or of history), the investigation – the fact, that is, that we place ourselves before a linguistic manifestation in the attitude of the addressee of the message – has as its consequence that the algorithms of history present themselves as states, or in other words as static structures.22

因果关系是历史事后认识的一个突出例子，而事后认识，就是从过去某一时刻的未来出发来解释那一时刻；即使传统历史学家，也不断彼此警告不要如此，却仍然继续如此，因为如果没有事后认识，根本就不可能有历史编纂。事后认识意味着历史学家侵入自己的研究领域，带着他所关注的当时行动者不可能拥有的知识。结构主义的益处之一，是使我们更加敏锐地意识到，事后认识如何渗入历史编纂，并提出某些办法，使它得到部分抵消。

Causation is a prime example of historical hindsight, and hindsight is that future-oriented interpretation of a moment in the past against which even traditional historians constantly warn one another but which they continue to practise because if they lacked hindsight there could be no historiography at all. Hindsight represents the intrusion of the historian into the field of his study bearing knowledge denied to the contemporary actors in whom he is interested.
