It is one of the benefits of Structuralism to have made us more acutely aware of how hindsight infects historiography and to suggest ways in which it may be partially offset.

总的来说，做到这一点的方法，是少关注时间顺序，少关注个体独一无二、异乎寻常的行为。结构主义所反对的那种历史编纂风格，广泛以其法语名称“l’histoire événementielle”或“事件史”为人所知。“事件史”本质上就是把过去原子化为伟人和伟大机构响亮的功业：军队及其将领、国家及其君王和政治显要人物的功业。它仿佛是一种完全用大标题写成的历史，极为关注日历或事件发生的日期。在“事件史”中，那些被个别化的时刻和人物，与其说是在历史语境的衬托下显得突出，不如说是把语境吞并了，因为它们自身就成了一个社会许多年间的代表。

In general, this can be done by paying less attention to chronology and less attention to the singular and exceptional deeds of individuals. The style of historiography to which Structuralism is opposed is widely known by its French name of ‘l’histoire événementielle’ or ‘event-history’. ‘Event-history’ is essentially the past atomized into the resounding exploits of Great Men and Great Institutions; of armies and their generals, and nations and their kings and political eminences. It is history written, as it were, exclusively in headlines and much taken up with the calendar or with the dating of its events. In ‘event-history’ the singularized moments and persons do not so much stand out against their historical context as subsume it, by coming to represent in themselves an entire society over many years.

结构主义历史编纂正好与此相反。针对“人创造历史”这一古老且仍然盛行的观点，它提出相反的观点：“历史创造人。”历史学家过去可能主要寻找“事件”，结构主义者则寻找那些事件发生于其中的系统，并参照这个系统来评估事件的历史价值。

Structuralist historiography is the exact antithesis of this. Against the old and still prevalent opinion that ‘men make history’ it sets the contrary opinion, that ‘history makes men’. Where historians may have looked in the past mainly for ‘events’, the Structuralist looks for the system within which those events happened and by reference to which their historical value may be assessed.

结构主义历史编纂反对个别化的冲动，这种冲动是“事件史”的标志，结构主义者拒绝它，认为它是对过去的可悲歪曲，因为它贬低了所有复杂的知识形式、习俗、信仰和制度；这些东西安排着即使最杰出的个体的生活，也在很大程度上决定着社会的演变。结构主义历史学家将研究费尔南·布罗代尔所谓的“社会现实”：

Structuralist historiography counters the urge to singularize which is the mark of ‘event-history’ and which a Structuralist rejects as a sorry falsification of the past, downgrading as it does all the complex forms of knowledge, customs, beliefs and institutions which order the lives of even the most pre-eminent individuals and which also largely determine the evolution of societies. The Structuralist historian will investigate what Fernand Braudel calls ‘social realities’:

我所指的是集体生活的一切宏观形式、经济、制度、社会建筑，实际上是文明，尤其是文明。诚然，过去的历史学家并非不了解这些现实；但除少数令人惊叹的先驱之外，他们太常把这些现实看作背景，只为解释，或者仿佛要解释那些非凡人物的行动而加以布置，历史学家如此津津乐道地详述的，正是这些行动。23

By this I mean all the broad forms of collective life, economies, institutions, social architecture, civilisations in fact, above all civilisations – all of them realities of which the historians of yesterday were not ignorant, it is true, but which, except for a few astonishing precursors, they too often saw as a backcloth, arranged solely in order to explain, or as if they wanted to explain the actions of exceptional individuals on which the historian dwells so pleasurably.23

布罗代尔本人，也许是近来愿意自称结构主义者的最杰出历史学家，但他并未发明结构主义历史编纂；两次世界大战之间，法国所谓年鉴学派的研究，已经为它作了准备，该学派以同名历史期刊为中心。多年来，年鉴派历史学家及其继承者和模仿者，改变了西方历史编纂的方向：从关注著名人物——国王、皇帝、统帅、首相、科学或艺术天才之类——以及较富戏剧性的历史事件，转向匿名的、不变的、平凡的东西。在这一过程中，历史编纂更接近了社会人类学，历史学家也开始认识到，可以把过去某一特定时刻的某个社会或人类群体，作为一个由同时存在之物组成的共同体来研究；简言之，历史编纂不必害怕采取共时观点。它无法停止时间，却可以更多地考虑时间内部的“水平”维度。结构主义促成了一场广泛的转向，摆脱“事件史”特有的躁动节奏；在那种历史中，戏剧性事件和鲜明生动的个人在舞台上接踵而过，声势过于喧嚣，那些过于平静或卑微、无法符合它关于何谓真正“历史性”的标准的岁月和社会阶层，被排除在外。结构主义历史编纂试图重建所有这些事件发生于其中的永久性或或多或少永久性的制约，使事物放慢下来；它还通过关心当时社会的整体，而不只关心其中幸运地脱颖而出的要素，使视野拓宽。它有意关注平静无事，因为无论过去还是现在，一切生命中的极大部分都是平静无事的。只有知道杰出人物和非凡事件是相对于什么而言的例外，才能充分理解其非凡之处。结构主义可以合理地声称，它把历史编纂推向更大的现实主义。它偏爱研究同质的人群，而不是伟人，因此倾向于对过去作统计性而非传记性的评估。一种致力于追踪少数人生涯的传记式历史编纂，会使过去显得缩短，因为叙事不断催促我们向前，走向传记主人公的黯然退场，或者更可能是死亡。这样的方式不可避免地与变化的、“短时段”的意识形态相连。相反，结构主义历史编纂以执着于“长时段”著称。布罗代尔评论说，这一视野“不论好坏”，都由“结构”一词主导：

Braudel himself was perhaps the most distinguished recent historian willing to declare himself a Structuralist, but he did not invent Structuralist historiography, which had already been prepared for in France between the two world wars by the work of the so-called Annales school, centred round the historical journal of that name. Over the years the historians of Annales, together with their successors and imitators, have changed the course of Western historiography, away from a preoccupation with the famous – kings, emperors, commanders, prime ministers, scientific or artistic geniuses and the like – and with the more melodramatic historical events, and towards the anonymous, the unchanging, the humdrum. In the process historiography has moved closer to social anthropology, and historians have come to see that it is possible to study a given society or human group at a given moment in the past as a community of the coexistent, that historiography in short need not be afraid of taking the synchronic view. It cannot arrest time but it can take much greater account of the ‘horizontal’ dimension within time. Structuralism has inspired a widespread move away from the agitated tempo characteristic of ‘event-history’, in which dramatic events and picturesque individuals follow one another all too loudly across the stage, to the exclusion of those years and classes of society too uneventful or humble to meet its criteria of what is actually ‘historical’. Structuralist historiography slows things down, by attempting to reconstruct the permanent or more or less permanent constraints within which all these events occurred, and also broadens things out, by concerning itself with the whole of contemporary societies instead of just with those elements of it fortunate enough to have singled themselves out. It takes a deliberate interest in the uneventful, because so very large a part of all lives, past or present, is uneventful. Exceptional persons and events can only be fully understood as such once we know what they were the exceptions from. Structuralism may reasonably claim to shift historiography in the direction of a greater realism. It prefers the study of homogeneous groups of people to that of Great Men, being drawn thereby to the statistical assessment of the past rather than the biographical. A biographical style of historiography, devoted to tracing the careers of the few, has a foreshortening effect on the past because of the way in which narrative urges us ever onwards towards, in the case of biography, the eclipse or more likely the death of its subject. Such an approach is inescapably tied to an ideology of change, of the ‘short run’. Structuralist historiography, on the other hand, is famous for its obsession with the ‘long run’, a perspective which is dominated, ‘for good or ill’, remarks Braudel, by the term ‘structure’:

社会观察者所理解的结构，是一种组织、一种连贯性，是现实事物与社会群体之间相当固定的关系。对我们历史学家来说，结构无疑是一种集合、一种建筑，但更是一种现实：时间只能缓慢地磨损它，并把它传递到漫长的时期中。某些长寿的结构成为无数代人的稳定要素：它们拖累其历史，阻碍——也就是说，支配——历史的发展。另一些结构则更快地崩解。但所有结构既是支撑，也是障碍。24

By structure, observers of the social understand an organisation, a coherence, fairly fixed relations between realities and social masses. For we historians a structure is no doubt an assemblage, an architecture, but even more a reality which time wears away only slowly and transmits over long periods. Certain long-lived structures become stable elements for infinite generations: they encumber their history and hinder – that is, govern – its development. Others are quicker to crumble away. But all are both supports and obstacles.24

布罗代尔本人的主要历史编纂著作，题名《腓力二世统治时期的地中海》，极好地表明了他的结构意识所导致的深刻转变。在一部研究欧洲历史某个时期的著作中，以地中海这样的地理单位为焦点，是具有挑衅性的；因为传统上，那个时期充满了帝国和民族国家统治者的功业。但说到 longue durée，即长时段，地理结构显然是所有结构中持续最久的，也很可能对一个人群的历史施加最严厉、也最具塑造性的制约。布罗代尔这样的视野，更优先考虑气候与地形所规定的生活连续性，以及那些最直接依赖它们的人类活动——农业、工业、商业。通过这样把历史编纂与地理学、地质学和经济学整合起来，结构主义的历史编纂方式兑现了结构主义致力于统一知识的承诺。在极端形式中，它甚至可能声称能够提供一种“总体史”：在某个特定群体或社会历史的某个特定时刻，为其建立一种可运作的模型，其密度和复杂性，与我们作为此时此地自身群体或社会成员所意识到的相同。很少有人会接受，即使在假设上，这样的完整性也是可以达到的；仅仅因为历史学家自己的“心态”，就不可避免地会混入他试图从资料中重建的心态之中。

The title of Braudel’s own major work of historiography, The Mediterranean in the Reign of Philip II, is an excellent indication of the profound shift which his structure-mindedness leads to. It is provocative to take a geographical unit such as the Mediterranean as the focus of a work dealing with a period of European history full traditionally of the exploits of the rulers of empires and nation-states. But when it comes to the longue durée, or long run, geographical structures are clearly those which last longest of all and which may well exert the severest as well as the most formative constraints on the history of a population. A perspective such as Braudel’s gives a higher priority to the continuities of life imposed by climate and topography, as well as to those human activities – agriculture, industry, commerce – most immediately dependent on them. In thus integrating historiography with geography, geology and economics, the Structuralist mode of historiography bears out the promise that Structuralism works to unify knowledge. In an extreme form it may even claim to be able to deliver a ‘total history’, which would be to construct a sort of working model of some particular group or society at a particular moment of its history, of the same density and complexity as we are aware of as members of our own group or society here and now. Few would accept that such completeness is even hypothetically attainable, if only because of the inevitable admixture of the historian’s own ‘mentality’ with that which he is seeking from his data to reconstruct.

原则上，遵循结构主义方法的历史学家，应该不大可能把自己的先入之见强加给证据，因为他把自己的任务看作使证据形成连贯整体，而不发生时代错置。历史学家如今试图描绘过去某个特定群体或阶级“心态”的许多尝试，就属于这一类。这种“心态”不是心理学意义上的，因为不应假定，它曾存在于该群体或阶级中这个或那个个体的头脑内。它是历史学家的建构，因此是一种理想化，是为了解释特定时期的各种社会或文化“事件”而重建的一种假设性的“群体心灵”。

In principle, a historian who pursues a Structuralist method should be less likely to impose his own preconceptions on his evidence, taking it as his task to render that evidence coherent without anachronism. The many attempts now being made by historians to depict the ‘mentality’ of a particular group or class of people in the past are of this kind. Such a ‘mentality’ is not psychological, in the sense that it is not to be supposed to have been found within the mind of this or that individual within the group or class in question. It is the historian’s construct and thus an idealization, a hypothetical ‘group-mind’ recreated in order to explain various social or cultural ‘events’ in a particular period.

此类历史编纂既富有人情，也趋向客观性，尽管从未完全达到。历史学家自己的作用，是搜集和排列资料，而不是明确解释它们。一位法国思想史家经常被人们与结构主义联系起来，却不断力求与这样的标签保持距离；用他的话说，历史学家是一位“考古学家”，其工作是对“遗迹作内在的描述”。25 然而，遗迹并不是过去本身，而是过去以各种档案材料的形式留存在当下的东西。福柯所关注的，是这些材料的不连续性；我们关于过去的记录，不仅仅是不完整，它们是复杂性无限的文化、文明和历史时代留下的一点可怜残余。我们大多数人从小熟悉的那种叙事性历史编纂，特别善于向我们掩盖其所依据证据中可悲的空白和断裂；它有意或无意地借助自身散文的连续性及想象的运用来掩盖它们。因此，当代历史学家若需要为某个历史行动提供动机，就会从自己的心理学知识中提供它，大概依据这样的假设：心理学知识是科学的，因此适用于一切时期和地点。结构主义者会认为，这一假设是错误的；如果可能，一个时代的心理必须从该时代存留下来的文献中了解：人的动机不是超越时间的，而受文化限制。例如，用“野心”来解释一位伊丽莎白时代冒险家的行动，并不等同于用类似特质解释一位维多利亚时代企业家的行动；“野心”在两个时代的作用和性质可能很不相同。野心也有历史。

Historiography of this sort is both humane and tending towards if never quite attaining objectivity. The historian’s own role is to assemble and arrange data rather than explicitly to interpret them. In the words of a French historian of ideas popularly associated with Structuralism, and constantly anxious to keep his distance from any such label, the historian is an ‘archaeologist’ and his job is to give an ‘intrinsic description of the monument’.25 The monument, however, is not the past as such but what remains of the past in the present by way of archival materials of every kind. The characteristic of these materials on which Foucault concentrates is their discontinuity; our records of the past are worse than incomplete, they are a paltry survival of cultures, civilizations and historical epochs of infinite complexity. Narrative historiography, of the kind most of us grew up with, is particularly clever at hiding from us the sorry gaps and discontinuities in the evidence on which it is based; it hides them, wittingly or not, by the continuities of its own prose and the use of imagination. Thus a contemporary historian who is faced with supplying the motive for some historical action will supply it from his own psychological knowledge, probably on the assumption that psychological knowledge is scientific and so valid for all periods and places. A Structuralist would argue that this assumption is wrong, and that the psychology of an age must be learned, if possible, from the surviving documents of that age: human motives are not timeless but culture-bound. To explain the actions of an Elizabethan adventurer by ‘ambition’, let us suppose, is not the same as explaining the actions of a Victorian entrepreneur by a similar trait; the role and nature of ‘ambition’ in the two ages may be quite different. Ambition, too, has a history.

在这里，结构主义具有一种优势：与生活本身的连续性相反，它的解释模型总会显示其本质上的不连续性。时间顺序的一个悖论是，它使我们觉得体现了经验的连续性，实际上却表明恰恰相反的东西。按定义，编年序列是一份断裂的记录，却毫不费力地说服我们忽略那些断裂。传统历史编纂诱使我们接受，它用来排列历史事件的时间序列，属于事件本身，而不是属于历史学家自身的文化。历史学家所用的时间尺度或时间“代码”，彼此甚至并不一致；列维-斯特劳斯乐于指出这一点。例如，史前史家使用的时间尺度，在“事件”之间可能相隔数百万年；若把它用于现代社会，效果就是抹去这个社会中几乎曾经发生的一切。他的结论是：“如果总的代码不是由可以排成线性序列的日期构成，而是由不同的日期类别构成，每一类都提供自己独立的参照系统，那么，历史知识的不连续性和分类性质就清楚地显现出来。”26

Structuralism has the advantage here that its explanatory models always display their essential discontinuity, in contrast to the continuities of life itself. It is one of the paradoxes of chronology that it should impress us as embodying the continuity of experience when in fact it signals the very opposite. A chronology is by definition a broken record which persuades us without much difficulty to overlook the breaks. Traditional historiography tricks us into accepting that the temporal series in which it arranges historical events are those pertaining to the events themselves rather than to the historian’s own culture. The timescales or temporal ‘codes’ used by historians are not even congruent with one another, as Lévi-Strauss takes pleasure in observing, pointing out that the time-scale used, say, by a prehistorian, in which there may be millions of years between ‘events’, would, if applied to a modern society, have the effect of abolishing almost everything that had ever happened in it. His conclusion is that: ‘If the general code does not consist of dates which can be arranged in linear series, but in classes of dates each of which provides its own autonomous system of reference, the discontinuous and classificatory nature of historical knowledge appears clearly.’26

列维-斯特劳斯乐于这样提醒历史学界注意一个许多人可能不愿承认的事实：他们的时间序列其实是一个个“代码”。结构主义者可以声称的优势，不是他不必在一种特定、任意的时间“代码”中操作，而是他意识到自己正在这样做。他接受自己所做之事的相对性。传统历史编纂试图隐藏其所呈现的过去叙述中的空白，结构主义历史学家则可以按福柯的建议，把它们放到前台：“不连续性曾是时间离散的污点，历史学家的任务就是从历史中消除它。如今，它已经成为历史分析的基本要素之一。”27

Lévi-Strauss delights in thus drawing the attention of the historical profession to the fact many of them may be reluctant to admit, that their temporal series are so many ‘codes’. The advantage which the Structuralist may claim is not that he is exempt from operating within a particular, arbitrary temporal ‘code’ but that he is conscious of doing so. He accepts the relativism of what he is doing. Where traditional historiography sought to hide the gaps in the accounts it presented of the past, the Structuralist historian may, as Foucault recommends, bring them to the fore: ‘Discontinuity was the stigma of temporal dispersal which it was the historian’s job to suppress from history. It has now become one of the fundamental elements of historical analysis.’27

福柯反对被称为结构主义历史学家，主要似乎源于他的愿望：把历史编纂呈现为一门自主的学科，它已经采用了结构主义方法，并不需要外部的推动。之所以如此，是因为历史学家赋予历史中经济因素的重要性不断增加；部分受到马克思主义的影响，马克思主义坚持，在任何社会中，经济关系或生产关系是首要的，而其他一切——观念、习俗、信仰的“上层建筑”——最终都可以解释为由它们派生。应当注意，经济学是最典型的结构研究，索绪尔本人就认识到了这一点，引用它作为自己的共时语言学的模型。经济是一个运作整体的理想例子，其中所有部分相互作用，完全彼此依赖。它有历史，其当前状况的某些特征可以得到历史解释；但是，对该状况恰当的经济解释是共时的，必须通过观察构成经济的各种极为复杂的关系来确定。如今，这种观察越来越通过建立经济的计算机“模型”来完成，这就是纯粹的结构主义。人们注意到，这种模型建构未能产生有用的实际结果，而非理论洞见，但不必因此责怪结构主义方法。不过，我已经如此强调的索绪尔语言“价值”概念，基本上是一个经济概念；我们这一代比大多数世代更敏锐地意识到经济价值如何因结构性原因而波动，这有助于凸显索绪尔这个隐喻的重大意义。

Foucault’s objections to being called a Structuralist historian seem to follow largely from his wish to present historiography as an autonomous discipline, which had already adopted Structuralist methods without being prompted from outside. It had done so because of the ever-increasing importance which historians accorded to economic factors in history, partly under the influence of Marxism, with its insistence that in any society economic relations, or the relations of production, are primary and that everything else – the ‘superstructure’ of ideas, customs, beliefs – can eventually be explained as deriving from them. Economics, be it noted, is the structural study par excellence, as Saussure himself recognized by citing it as a model for his own synchronic linguistics. An economy is the ideal example of a functioning whole all of whose parts interact and depend utterly on one another. It has a history and historical explanations may be offered for particular features of its current condition; but the proper economic explanation of that condition is synchronic, to be determined by observation of the various very complex relations which constitute the economy. Increasingly, such observation is achieved nowadays by constructing computer ‘models’ of the economy, which is unadulterated Structuralism. The noted failure of such model-building to produce useful practical consequences, as opposed to theoretical insights, need not be held against the Structuralist method. But the Saussurean concept of linguistic ‘value’, of which I have already made so much, is basically an economic one; and the fact that our present generation is more keenly aware than most of how economic values fluctuate for structural reasons helps to bring out the great significance of Saussure’s metaphor.

去世时，福柯在巴黎法兰西学院担任一个听起来也许奇怪的职位：思想系统史教授。这个头衔是为他量身设立的，以认可他卓有成效地践行的那种历史编纂。在他最著名的著作《词与物》中，福柯引入了“épistème”（知识型）的概念，作为一种根据特定历史时期理解周围世界的方式来刻画这些时期的办法。（Epistème 是意为“知识”的希腊词，我们熟悉它，是因为它构成了较长的术语“epistemology”，即认识论的一部分；认识论是关注知识理论的哲学分支。）福柯提出的主张是，每个时代组织知识的方式，都不同于前后时代；但在一个特定知识型的范围内，知识的不同分支会根据同样的原则组织资料。福柯把来自三门人们可能认为互不相关的科学——语言、经济学和博物学——的资料联系起来，试图证明它们都共享一种编排系统。共时性无所不能：

At the time of his death Foucault held the perhaps strange-sounding appointment of Professor of the History of Systems of Thought at the Collège de France in Paris. The title was created to suit the man, in recognition of the kind of historiography he had so conspicuously practised. In his best-known book, The Order of Things, Foucault had introduced the notion of the ‘épistème’ as a way of characterizing particular historical periods according to the manner in which they made sense of the world around them. (Epistème is the Greek word for ‘knowledge’ and familiar to us as a part of the longer term ‘epistemology’, which is that branch of philosophy that concerns itself with the theory of knowledge.) The claim which Foucault advances is that each age organizes its knowledge differently from preceding and succeeding ages, but that within the circumference of a particular épistème different branches of knowledge will organize their data according to the same principles. Foucault assimilates data from three, as one might think, disparate sciences – language, economics and natural history – to try and prove that all share a common system of ordering. Synchronicity is all-powerful:

如果图尔内福、林奈和布丰的博物学与自身之外的任何东西相关，那么，它所关联的并不是生物学，不是居维叶的比较解剖学或达尔文的进化论，而是博泽的一般语法，是劳、韦龙·德·福尔邦内或杜尔哥著作中那种对货币与财富的分析。28

If the natural history of Tournefort, Linnaeus and Buffon relates to anything other than itself, it is not to biology, to the comparative anatomy of Cuvier or the evolutionism of Darwin, it is to the general grammar of Bauzée, to the analysis of money and wealth such as one finds in Law, Veron de Fortbonnais or Turgot.28

也就是说，你们应当通过他们的同时代人来认识他们，而不是通过其前辈或后继者。如果普遍遵循福柯的方法，效果就是把各种不连续性引入历史编纂平滑的流动之中。一位传统的生物学思想史家，不会乐于把18世纪生物学与18世纪语言学或政治经济学相联系，以评估前者；对于后两者，他很可能一无所知。他宁愿把它与之前和之后的生物学相联系。这样做，就又落回结构主义所反对的目的论历史编纂中：一个时期的观念，要么被理解为对更早时期观念的改进，要么被理解为仅仅给后来更好的观念作准备，仿佛生物学家及其他人的心灵总是指向未来，而不是当下。福柯的知识型具有相当大的力量，它导致一个历史时期与另一个时期看似断开；而福柯似乎一直谨慎地避免提出，历史编纂怎样才能最好地从一个时期走向另一个时期，而不重新恢复连续性的旧幻觉。

That is, by their contemporaries shall ye know them, and not by their predecessors or their successors. If Foucault’s method were generally followed it would have the effect of introducing all manner of discontinuities into the smooth flow of historiography. A conventional historian of biological ideas would not be happy to evaluate eighteenth-century biology by relating it to eighteenth-century linguistics or political economy, of which he will most likely know nothing; he would rather relate it to the biology that came before and after. In so doing he falls back into the teleological historiography to which Structuralism is opposed, whereby the ideas of one period are understood either as an improvement on those of earlier periods or as a mere preparation for the better ideas that succeeded them, as if the minds of biologists and others were bent always on the future and not on the present. The force of Foucault’s épistème, which results in the apparent severance of one historical period from another, is considerable, and Foucault seems carefully to have avoided ever suggesting how historiography might best pass from period to period without reinstating the old illusion of continuity.

福柯是一位极端的怀疑论者：他并不提出，自己的历史编纂会更接近过去的实际。恰好相反：他始终断然坚持，自己作为历史学家所能处理的，是存留下来的文献；由于这些文献是“文本性的”，它们永远不能成为过去事态的直接或即时证据，只能成为间接的、因而也具有“修辞性”的证据，表明当时的人如何记录和理解自己的时代。因此，这种具有超强自觉意识的历史编纂所必须处理的材料，就是福柯所称的“话语构成”，它能够从可用资料中辨认出这些构成。它们形成一种范畴网格，每个时代都把它覆盖在现实之上，以便理解现实：“对可见之物的那种指称，它通过一种前语言的分类，使可见之物能够被转写为语言。”29 这些“话语构成”并不具有其他结构主义历史学家声称在其“心态”中找到了的统一性。福柯批评把一种可发现或可重建的“心态”当作特定时代或社会群体标志的观念，恰恰就是因为，它强加了一种中心化的历史时期观念；这随后成为解释各种同时代现象的动因，尽管这些现象原本正是据以推断“心态”特征的资料。福柯不愿陷入这一循环程序，而是坚持资料的局限，既重视资料之间可以观察到的汇合，也重视其不相容之处。他坚持离散原则，反对统一原则，在这一点上，他是一位后结构主义者。此外，作为历史学家，他热切地强调所有历史时代中都曾存在的控制话语的机制：也就是说，允许某些事实、意见和观念被说出，同时禁止另一些被说出的机制。按照福柯的看法，一切话语都受权威者的权力支配，因此，他作为历史学家所揭示的话语结构，需要被看作专制的、压制性的。作为自由至上主义者，他把历史编纂及其所依据的文献，看作强者和正统者针对弱者和异端者的一场合谋。

Foucault is an extreme sceptic: he does not propose that his kind of historiography will reach closer to the actuality of the past. Rather the reverse: he is at all times categorical in his insistance that what he has to work with as a historian are the surviving documents which, because they are ‘textual’, can never be direct or immediate evidence of how things were in the past but only mediate and therefore also ‘rhetorical’ evidence of how contemporaries recorded and understood their own times. The material with which this ultra-self-conscious historiography has to work is thus what Foucault terms the ‘discursive formations’ it is able to identify in the data available to it. These form a kind of categorical grid which each age places over reality in order to comprehend it: ‘that designation of the visible which, by a sort of prelinguistic sorting, enables it to be transcribed in language.’29 These ‘discursive formations’ do not have the unity which other Structuralist historians would claim to have located in their ‘mentalities’. Foucault criticizes the idea of a discoverable or re-creatable ‘mentality’ as the mark of a given age or social group for this very reason, that it imposes a centralized conception of a historical period which then becomes an agency by which to explain a whole variety of contemporary phenomena, even though those phenomena were originally the data from which the characteristics of the ‘mentality’ were deduced. Rather than succumb to that circular procedure, Foucault insists on the limitations of his data and on the incompatibilities that may be observed between them equally with the convergences. He is a post-Structuralist in his adherence to the principle of dispersion as against the principle of unification. Moreover, as a historian he emphasizes passionately the mechanisms that have existed in all historical ages for controlling discourse: that is, for permitting certain facts, opinions and ideas to be uttered while forbidding others. All discourse, according to Foucault, is subject to the power of those in authority, so that the discursive structures that he may uncover as a historian need to be seen as authoritarian and repressive. As a libertarian he looks on historiography, together with the documents it rests on, as a conspiracy of the powerful and orthodox against the powerless and eccentric.

这种怀疑与结构主义无关；即使福柯的历史编纂观念在许多地方与结构主义观点相汇合，结构主义也远不足以涵盖他。布罗代尔所践行的结构主义相信，历史编纂能够达到有关过去的某些真理，而且结构主义历史编纂能够比其他类型更接近它们。福柯太具有尼采主义倾向，不可能容许任何这样的简单信念。对他而言，话语就是话语，“真实”话语与其他任何话语没有区别，都是某种修辞策略，旨在扩大一个社会群体对另一个群体的权力或权威。真理作为一个概念，也不能免于权力游戏，或福柯认为与权力游戏同时进行的“欲望”游戏。“真实话语”受到损害，因为“它无法认识到贯穿自身的求真意志”。30 这样的宣言使他的抱怨更有力度：给他贴上结构主义者标签的人，只是暴露了他们自身词汇的空缺。他不会受他们的话语胁迫，也不会成为他们对他施加权力而未加承认的意志的受害者！

This suspicion has nothing to do with Structuralism, which is far from containing Foucault even if his ideas about historiography converge at many points with Structuralist views. Structuralism, as practised by Braudel, believes that historiography can accede to certain truths about the past, and that a Structuralist historiography can accede to them more closely than other kinds. Foucault is too much of a Nietzschean to allow any such simple belief. Discourse is discourse, so far as he is concerned, and the ‘true’ discourse no different from any other, being a certain rhetorical strategy that aims to extend the power or authority of one social group over another. Truth as a concept is not exempt from the power-game or the game of ‘desire’ that Foucault sees as being played simultaneously with it. ‘True discourse’ is vitiated because ‘it cannot recognise the will to truth that traverses it.’30 Such declarations lend force to his complaint that those who label him as a Structuralist only draw attention to a gap in their own vocabulary. He will not be coerced by their discourse or become the victim of their unacknowledged will to power over him!

3 符号学3 Semiotics

列维-斯特劳斯及其他人在人类学中运用的结构主义，直接源自语言学中践行的结构主义；列维-斯特劳斯宣示了自己对索绪尔和雅各布森洞见的忠诚，即使他有时把自己的方法与他们的方法之间的类比推得过远。例如，他提出，应当把婚姻规则和亲属系统视为一种语言，它允许女性流通，正如口头或书面语言允许词语流通一样。这一类比因具有误导性而受到严厉批评；人们可能得出结论，列维-斯特劳斯式结构主义，充其量是索绪尔模型的一种松散衍生物，最坏则是一种隐喻性的衍生物，尽管它本身是一种富于激励、引人入胜的研究方法。1

The Structuralism employed in anthropology by Lévi-Strauss and others derives directly from the Structuralism practised in linguistics; Lévi-Strauss has declared his allegiance to the insights of Saussure and of Jakobson, even if he has at times pressed the analogy between his methods and theirs too hard, in proposing, for example, that marriage-rules and kinship systems be seen as a language which allows of the circulation of women just as spoken or written language allows of the circulation of words. This analogy has been keenly criticized as misleading, and one might conclude that Lévi-Straussian Structuralism is at best a loose and at worst a metaphorical derivative of the Saussurean model, though an invigorating and absorbing method of research in its own right.1

当代历史学家的结构主义，很少或完全不承认欠语言学结构主义的债。正如我们从福柯的例子中所见，历史学家更愿意把结构方法在历史编纂中无可怀疑的成功，归因于学科内部自身的发展，这主要建立在它对经济、人口及其他“结构性”资料的新依赖之上。因此，历史学家自称结构主义者，是极不常见的，因为他们用某种其他称谓来表明自己的归属，就已经足够；如今任何自称“社会史家”的人，很可能都遵循一种大致符合结构主义的研究方法。

The Structuralism of contemporary historians acknowledges little or no debt to linguistic Structuralism. As we have seen in the case of Foucault, historians prefer to attribute the undoubted success of structural methods in historiography to an indigenous development within the discipline, founded largely on its new reliance on economic, demographic and other ‘structural’ data. As a consequence, it is most uncommon for historians to declare themselves to be Structuralists, when it is enough for them to reveal their affiliations by some other description; anyone these days calling him- or herself a ‘social historian’ is likely to pursue a method of research broadly conforming with Structuralism.

不过，在本书的其余部分，我将讨论结构主义观念和方法直接而明确地向狭义语言学领域之外的投射。其中第一个投射领域，就是分别被称为 Semiotics 或 Semiology 的符号学；正如我前面所指出的，这两个术语都源自意为“符号”的希腊词 semeion。Semiotics 或 Semiology 简单说就是对符号或符号系统的研究，代表着结构主义观念向人类文化研究所能作出的最大扩展。原则上，它比社会人类学包容性更强；后者传统上限于特定的社会和文化制度，而不是试图把文化作为整体来研究。符号学（Semiotics／Semiology）没有这样的限制。

In the rest of this volume, however, I shall be dealing with direct and explicit projections of Structuralist ideas and methods outside the narrowly linguistic sphere. The first of these projections is into what is called, variously, Semiotics or Semiology, terms deriving, as I have earlier indicated, from the Greek word for ‘sign’: semeion. Semiotics or semiology is quite simply the study of signs or of systems of signs and represents the largest possible extension of Structuralist ideas into the investigation of human culture. In principle, it is more embracing than social anthropology, which has traditionally restricted itself to particular social and cultural institutions, rather than attempt to study culture as a whole. Semiotics/ semiology knows no such limitation.

不过，首先要谈两个术语本身，即 Semiotics 和 Semiology。假定它们指称的是完全相同的科学或研究——在论证的这一阶段，我将这样假定——那么，可以说它们是两个具有共同指涉对象的术语。用索绪尔的话说，它们的“指称意义”是相同的。但这并不使它们同一，因为它们以不同方式指向共同的指涉对象。它们具有不同的“价值”。事实上，它们组成的一对术语，恰好类似于德国逻辑学家弗雷格为区分一个术语的“指涉对象”与其“涵义”而举出的那一对；这一划分影响深远。弗雷格的例子是“晨星”和“昏星”两个术语，两者都可以用来指称同一个天体。这两个可替代使用的术语，构成两种不同的“涵义”或指涉形式。不难看出，我们对某颗星的思考，可能因听到它被称作“晨星”还是“昏星”而有所不同；两种表达的含义或内涵并不相同。选择一个而不用另一个，很可能是由一天中的时间所决定，而不是出于使用者的某种情感意图。但这仍不能使两个表达等同。事实上，鉴于两个表达中变化部分“晨”与“昏”之间的鲜明对比，结构主义者可能会把它们视为一对二元对立项，因此一项蕴含另一项（不过，“晨”与“昏”之间的对立，因而其蕴含关系，比真正对立的“夜”与“昼”之间要弱）。

First, though, there is the matter of the two terms themselves, Semiotics and Semiology. Assuming, as I shall do at this point of the argument, that they both name precisely the same science or study, they can be said to be two terms with a common referent. As Saussure would put it, their ‘signification’ is the same. This does not make them identical, however, because they refer to their common referent in distinct ways. They have different ‘values’. They form, in fact, a pair of terms just like the one adduced by the German logician Frege to draw his influential distinction between the ‘referent’ of a term and its ‘sense’; Frege’s example being the terms ‘morning star’ and ‘evening star’, both of which may be used to refer to the same astral body. The two alternative terms constitute two distinct ‘senses’ or forms of reference. It is not hard to see that we may think about a particular star differently according to whether we hear it named ‘morning star’ or ‘evening star’; the implications or connotations of the two expressions will not be the same. The choice of one over the other may well be determined by the time of day rather than by some emotive purpose on the part of the user. But this still does not make the two expressions equivalent. Indeed, given the strong contrast between the variable elements of the two expressions, ‘morning’ and ‘evening’, a Structuralist might claim them as a pair in binary opposition one with the other, so that the one implies the other (though the opposition and hence the implication is weaker between ‘morning’ and ‘evening’ than it is between the true opposition, ‘night’ and ‘day’).

一些术语或表达并不相同，却能用来指称同一个对象；区分它们的“涵义”，是我在讨论结构语言学时已经涉及的一点。这对符号研究极为重要，因此，这种研究作为一个指涉对象，竟然可以由两个不同表达来指称，实在是恰到好处。作为替代称谓的 Semiotics 与 Semiology 之间的区别，需要用符号学本身解释一切符号——无论语言符号还是其他符号——的方法来解释。

This distinction between the ‘senses’ of terms or expressions which are not the same but which can be used to refer to the same object is one I have touched on in the discussion of structural linguistics. It is of great importance for the study of signs, which makes it happily appropriate that this study, as a referent, should be referrable to by two different expressions. The distinction between Semiotics and Semiology, as alternatives, requires interpretation by the very methods which Semiotics and Semiology themselves bring to the interpretation of all signs, linguistic or otherwise.

简言之，两个术语因历史和地理原因而有区别。Semiotics 的历史比 Semiology 悠久而显赫得多。它的使用可以追溯到古希腊人；17世纪末，它又出现在英国哲学家约翰·洛克的著作中，随后在本世纪于北美获得了相当大的复兴。Semiology 更像一个新贵；它原本是个冷僻的术语，由索绪尔本人接过来用以指称符号系统研究，而且，与其竞争者不同，它没有传播到西欧之外。因此，这两个术语的内涵差异很大。一个自称 semiotician 的人，是在宣示自己隶属于北美符号研究传统；而一个把自己称作 semiologist 的人，则是在宣示自己隶属于从索绪尔传下来的传统。因此，这两个术语本身也形成了一种内涵不同的对立。北美发展起来的 Semiotics 与欧洲发展起来的 Semiology，在侧重点、甚至方法上都存在区别；熟悉情况的读者可以从这两个描述性标签本身的差别中“读出”这些区别。这样的解释过程，本身可以称为 semiosis（符号作用）；而如何进行这一过程的方法，构成了符号学家的核心兴趣。

Briefly, the two terms are distinct for historical and geographical reasons. Semiotics is a term with a much longer and nobler history than Semiology. Its use can be traced back to the ancient Greeks, and it recurs in the work of the English philosopher John Locke in the late seventeenth century before having a considerable revival in this century in North America. Semiology is more of a parvenu; it was the obscure term taken over and used by Saussure himself to refer to the study of sign-systems, and, unlike its rival, it has not travelled outside Western Europe. Thus, the connotations of the two terms vary considerably. Someone declaring themselves to be a semiotician is declaring an affiliation with the North American tradition of sign-study, whereas the person who presents him- or herself as a semiologist is declaring an affiliation with the tradition descending from Saussure. The two terms thus themselves form an opposition, of different connotations. There are differences of emphasis and even of method between Semiotics as it has developed in North America, and Semiology as it has developed in Europe, and these differences may be ‘read’ by the informed reader into the difference between the descriptive labels themselves. Such a process of interpretation, the methods for conducting which form the essential interest of the semiotician or semiologist, may itself be called semiosis.

从现在起，我将固定使用 Semiotics 来指称符号研究，不过，后面还会有机会回到这个术语及其竞争者 Semiology 之间“价值”的区别。显然，符号学与哲学的某些分支相近，尤其是研究推理的分支。事实上，一些符号学家并不愿区分符号学与最广义上理解的逻辑学，因为两者都把自己的任务视为编制思想规则。因此，符号学史与哲学史紧密相连。但它不仅仅与哲学史相连，因为符号学的含义，肯定远比逻辑学所声称的范围更广。与逻辑学相比，它高度关注实际运用，因为它所关心的推理遍布我们的日常生活。正是通过这些推理，我们理解，或未能理解，自己的环境。符号学具有深刻的社会性，因为符号在人类生活的每一时刻，以及我们在社会中的相互交往中，都发挥着根本作用。

From now on I shall stick to the term Semiotics to refer to the study of signs, though there will be occasion to revert later on to the distinctions in ‘value’ between this term and its rival, Semiology. Semiotics, clearly, is akin to certain branches of philosophy, notably those which study inference. Some semioticians indeed have not wished to distinguish between Semiotics and logic, interpreted in the widest sense, since both take it as their task to codify the rules of thought. Thus the history of Semiotics is intimately bound up with the history of philosophy. But it is not only bound up with the history of philosophy, because Semiotics has far wider implications, certainly, than logic lays claim to. Compared with logic it is highly pragmatic, because the inferences with which it is concerned are ones which pervade our everyday lives. They are the inferences by which we make sense, or fail to make sense, of our environment. Semiotics is profoundly social, because of the fundamental role which signs play in every moment of human life and of our interaction in society.

为了初步说明符号在我们生活中的普遍存在，因而也说明符号学潜在的无所不在，我将以医生和病人更熟悉地称为症状的那些身体符号为例。一个有趣的事实是，在 Semiology 因索绪尔的使用而重新流行以前的数百年里，几乎相同的术语“semeiology”，一直在英国医学界用来指称研究症状的医学分支。（“semeiology”中第二个看似多余的 e，我们可以有把握地说，带有“希腊性”的内涵，因为它可以直接追溯到希腊词 semeion；就我们自身的医学传统而言，症状研究也是从希腊人开始的。）症状是某种身体机能失常的符号。它们引导懂得正确解释方式的人，找到它们所表征的疾病的名称，因而也找到该疾病的性质。因此，医生的解释是一种推理，而症状或符号本身并不是疾病，却能引导受过适当训练的医生认识疾病。在医学语境中，符号是那些否则会不被察觉的东西的可见、或能以其他方式感知的代表；典型的医学症状，是患病身体表面上的某种印记，必须把它解读为身体内部某种问题的证据，或者是比完全无知的外行从局部症状所能看出的更为普遍的问题的证据。2 正如我所说，医生对症状的解释是一种推理，但也必须认识到，它是一种约定性的推理，是依据那个社会、那个时刻，该医生实际或潜在可以获得的医学知识作出的。也就是说，医生必须知道如何解释症状；而把医学资料纳入当时通行的文化知识系统，也标志着它被纳入符号学领域：“第一位发现脸上一系列红点与麻疹之间存在恒定关系的医生，作出了一项推理；但一旦这种关系成为约定，并被记录在医学论著中，我们就得到了一项符号学约定。”3（埃科把原文的“符号学约定”大写。）

As a preliminary example of the pervasiveness of signs in our lives, and hence of the potential ubiquity of Semiotics, I will take those bodily signs which doctors, and patients, know more familiarly by the name of symptoms. it is an interesting fact that for centuries before the term Semiology came back into currency as a consequence of its use by Saussure, the nearly identical term ‘semeiology’ had been in service in the medical profession in England to refer to that branch of medicine which studies symptoms. (The redundant-seeming second e in ‘semeiology’ we can safely say connotes ‘greekness’, being directly traceable to the Greek term semeion; and the study of symptoms began with the Greeks, so far as our own medical tradition is concerned.) Symptoms are signs of some bodily malfunction. They lead whoever knows the correct way to interpret them to the name and hence the nature of the disorder they are the symptoms of. The interpretation of the doctor is thus an inference, and the symptom or sign something which is not itself the illness but which leads a properly trained physician to the illness. In the medical context signs are the visible or otherwise sensible representatives of what would otherwise remain undetected, the archetypal medical symptom being a mark of some sort on the surface of the afflicted body which must be read as evidence of some trouble within the body, or of trouble of a kind more generalized than would be apparent to a wholly ignorant layman from the localized symptom.2 The interpretation of the symptom by the doctor is, as I have said, an inference, but it is also, one must appreciate, a conventional inference, made in terms of the medical knowledge actually or potentially available to that doctor in that society at that time. That is, the doctor must know how to interpret the symptom, and it is this insertion of the medical datum into a prevailing system of cultural knowledge which also marks its insertion into the field of semiotics: ‘The first doctor who discovered a constant relation between a series of red spots on the face and measles, drew an inference; but once this relation had become a conventional one, and been recorded in medical treatises, then we got a SEMIOTIC CONVENTION’3 (Eco’s capitals).

因此，作为符号，症状具有引导其医学解释者的心灵继续前进的性质，使它从眼前之物转向不在眼前之物。没有什么东西本身就能成为符号或症状。一个身体不适的人脸上的红点，只对那些具备少量医学知识或解释能力、能把它们如此解读的人，才是症状。可以说，它们只有在被解释时才成为症状，正如一个结果的原因，只有在结果已经出现之后才成为原因一样。按照另一位17世纪英国哲学家托马斯·霍布斯的定义，符号是（鉴于霍布斯和洛克之后，符号学在这个国家陷入的被忽视状态，而且即使近年它在别处复兴，这种状态仍然持续，17世纪必须被记为它在英国的鼎盛时期）：

As signs, then, symptoms have the property of leading the mind of their medical interpreter on, causing it to pass from what is before it to what is not before it. Nothing can be a sign or a symptom in itself. Red spots on the face of someone feeling unwell are a symptom only for those with the small amount of medical knowledge or powers of interpretation required to read them as such. One might say that they become a symptom only with their interpretation, in just the same way as the cause of an effect becomes so only once the effect is in place. A sign, in the definition of another seventeenth-century English philosopher, Thomas Hobbes (given the neglect into which Semiotics fell in this country after Hobbes and Locke, and in which it has remained even in the recent years of its revival elsewhere, the seventeenth century has to go down as its English heyday):

……是某一后件的明显前件；反过来，也是某一前件的后件，条件是此前已经观察到类似的后果；观察到的次数越多，符号的不确定性就越小。因此，在某一类事务中经验最多的人，拥有用来猜测未来的符号也最多，因而最为审慎；他比刚接触这类事务的人审慎得多，这种差距是天生才智和临机应变的任何优势都无法弥补的，尽管许多年轻人也许认为恰好相反。4

. . . is the evident antecedent of the consequent; and contrarily, the consequent of the antecedent, when the like consequences have been observed before: and the oftener they have been observed, the less uncertain is the sign. And therefore he that has most experience in any kind of business, has most signs, whereby to guess at the future time; and consequently is the most prudent: and so much more prudent than he that is new in that kind of business, as not to be equalled by any advantage of natural and extemporary wit: though perhaps many young men think the contrary.4

审慎是一种美德，霍布斯赋予它出乎意料的高度价值，把它视为获得长久而安稳生活的手段；但并非所有具有符号学倾向的人都有义务如此。不过，在这段文字中，霍布斯引入了符号理解能力与良好公民品格之间的联系；这种联系可以在一些现代符号学家的著作中见到，它在那里得到了大力发展——美国人查尔斯·莫里斯和法国人罗兰·巴特的方式不同，却也相互趋近。符号学的一项主张是，我们越能胜任对日常生活中周围无数符号的阅读，生活就会越警觉、越明智。我还会回到这一主张。

Prudence is a virtue on which Hobbes sets an unexpectedly high value, as the means to a long and secure life, but it is not incumbent on all those semiotically inclined. In this passage, however, Hobbes introduces a connection between semiotic capacity and sound citizenship which can be found strenuously developed in the work of some modern semioticians – differently yet also convergently, in the American Charles Morris and the Frenchman Roland Barthes. It is part of the claim of Semiotics that the more competent we are at the reading of the infinitely many signs around us in daily life, the more alertly and intelligently we will live. I will come back to this claim.

医学症状作为符号这一类别的例子，具有一个优点：它们本身不是语言性的。它们有助于拓宽思考符号的语境；而从语言转向一般交流时，正需要这样拓宽。这并不是要掩盖这样一个事实：医学症状几乎在每次出现时都会被用词语解释；病人的红点会引导医生想到“麻疹”这个词，也就是一个语言符号。也可能发生这样的情形：医生认为没有必要说出病人疾病的名称，而是直接把手伸进药包，拿出一些药片。即使不是所有符号学家，至少一些人会把这种医疗行为视为解释行为，因为它依赖于识别特定症状。我们无法知道，“麻疹”这个词是否曾在中间进入医生的意识，或者他是否能够完全不借助这种语言符号，就开出治疗方案。如果他的反应“纯粹”出于习惯，或者用行为主义术语说是“不经思考的”，这也不妨碍把它理解为解释性的。从符号学角度，不必在借助其他符号或词语来解释某个符号，与诉诸直接行动来解释它之间，作出绝对的区分。就符号学而言，词语就是行动，行动就是词语；而在语境中，两者都是符号。

Medical symptoms have the advantage, as examples of the class of signs, of not being in themselves linguistic. They serve to widen the context of thought about signs as it needs to be widened when one passes from language to communication in general. This is not to hide that medical symptoms will in nearly every case of their occurrence be interpreted in words: the patient’s red spots will lead the doctor to the word ‘measles’ – that is, to a linguistic sign. It may also happen that the doctor will not think it worth naming the patient’s complaint but will reach straight into his bag and produce some tablets. This piece of medical behaviour would be treated by some if not all semioticians as an act of interpretation, depending as it does on recognition of the particular symptoms. There is no knowing whether the word ‘measles’ has entered the doctor’s consciousness in between whiles, or whether he is able to prescribe treatment without any such resort to the signs of language. If his response is ‘purely’ habitual, or ‘mindless’ in terms of behaviourism, that does not prevent it being construed as interpretive. No hard and fast distinction need be made, from the semiotic point of view, between the interpretation of a sign by the use of other signs, or words, and its interpretation by resort to direct action. Semiotically speaking, words are actions and actions words: and both, in context, are signs.

在社会中我们可以使用的众多符号系统之中，语言的首要地位是不容怀疑的；因为归根结底，很难想象，一个非言语符号系统的符号，可以不断被有用地交换，却不在某个时刻被翻译为语言。作为熟练的驾驶者，当交通信号灯由红变绿时，我们无需有意识地思考就会把车向前开；但几乎可以肯定，在我们成为驾驶者之前，曾有一个时候，必须用词语向我们解释这种颜色变化的意义。没有《公路法规》的文本，交通信号对于不熟悉它们的人就会毫无意义。事实上，洛克著名地宣称，“分节的声音”是人们交流思想时“最方便”、因此也最普遍使用的符号，从而凸显了语言在符号系统中的首要地位。5 但这一首要地位并不意味着符号学的领域与索绪尔语言学的领域重合。它既更宽，也更窄：更宽，因为它包含言语符号之外的其他符号；更窄，因为语言的符号功能只是语言的一个方面。

The pre-eminence of language among the many systems of signs available to us in society is not to be doubted, for ultimately it is hard to think of a system of non-verbal signs whose signs can go on being usefully exchanged without at some point being translated into language. As practised drivers we move our cars forward without conscious thought when a traffic signal changes from red to green; but there was almost certainly a time, before we were drivers, when the significance of this change of colour had to be explained to us in words. Without the text of the ‘Highway Code’ traffic signals would be meaningless for anyone strange to them. The pre-eminence of language among sign-systems was famously brought out indeed by Locke when he declared ‘articulate sounds’ to be the ‘most convenient’ and therefore the most generally used signs whereby people communicate their thoughts.5 But this pre-eminence does not mean that the field of Semiotics is coextensive with that of Saussurean linguistics. It is both wider and narrower: wider because it takes in other than verbal signs, and narrower because the semiotic function of language is only one aspect of it.

索绪尔本人在《普通语言学教程》中，对符号学（Semiology）作了简短而具有经典地位的表述，既承认语言的首要地位，也承认研究符号，就是研究比语言更多的东西：

Saussure himself, in his brief but canonical formulation of Semiology in the Course in General Linguistics, recognizes the pre-eminence of language but also that to study signs is to study something more than language:

语言是一个表达观念的符号系统，因此可以与文字、聋哑字母、象征性仪式、礼貌形式、军事信号等等相比。它只不过是这些系统中最重要的一个。

A language is a system of signs expressing ideas, and hence comparable to writing, the deaf-and-dumb alphabet, symbolic rites, forms of politeness, military signals, and so on. It is simply the most important of such systems.

因此，可以设想一门科学，研究符号作为社会生活一部分所发挥的作用。它将构成社会心理学的一部分，因而也构成一般心理学的一部分。我们将把它称为符号学（semiology）……它将研究符号的性质及支配符号的规律。既然它尚不存在，就不能断言它将会存在。但它有权存在，已经预先为它准备好了一个位置。语言学只是这门一般科学的一个分支。6（原文斜体为索绪尔所加。）

It is therefore possible to conceive of a science which studies the role of signs as part of social life. It would form part of social psychology, and hence of general psychology. We shall call it semiology. . . It would investigate the nature of signs and the laws governing them. Since it does not yet exist, one cannot say for certain that it will exist. But it has a right to exist, a place ready for it in advance. Linguistics is only one branch of this general science.6 (Saussure’s italics)

Semiology 这个术语第一次出现，是在索绪尔一份标注为1894年的手稿中；在此后过去的九十年里，不能说这门已为其备好位置的科学，真正适当地诞生了。从制度层面说，无论 Semiology，还是它的美国对应物 Semiotics，都没有取得本应取得的进展。至今，教授它或专业从事它的大学及其他机构仍相对很少。考虑到这门学科的范围和吸引力，这很遗憾；但它很可能曾经、并仍然因自身纯粹的无所不在而受到阻碍。没有哪一门研究或知识分支，是符号学不能有益地探究的，因为没有哪一门研究或知识分支不以符号为基础：要么像数学或形式逻辑那样，发展了自身的符号系统；要么依赖自然语言。因此，如同结构主义本身，符号学也可能被无意中践行；践行者把注意力转向其特定学科或职业所使用的符号系统，或者转向我们共有的语言符号系统在其中得到的特定使用。缺少专门被称为符号学家的人，并不意味着符号学的理解或关切不广泛。例如，社会语言学和社会心理学其他分支中开展的许多研究，很可以归在符号学名下。

The first mention of the term Semiology occurs in a manuscript of Saussure’s dated 1894; in the ninety years which have passed since then it cannot be said that the science whose place was ready for it has come properly into being. Neither Semiology nor its American equivalent Semiotics has made the headway it should have done, in institutional terms. There are still relatively few universities or other institutions where it is taught or pursued professionally. Given the scope and attractions of the subject this is a pity, but it may well have been held back, and continue to be so, by its sheer pervasiveness. There is no branch of study or knowledge into which semiotics cannot usefully probe, because there is no branch of study or knowledge which is not sign-based, either because it has evolved sign-systems of its own, like mathematics or formal logic, or because it is reliant upon natural language. Like Structuralism itself therefore, Semiotics may be practised unwittingly, by those who turn their attention to the systems of signs which their particular discipline or profession employs, or to the particular uses made there of our common linguistic system of signs. The lack of semioticians specifically so called does not mean that semiotic understanding or concern is not widespread. A good deal of the research done, for example, in socio-linguistics and other branches of social psychology might well come under the heading of Semiotics.

北美符号学派的主要理论家C.S.皮尔士认为：“整个宇宙……即使并非完全由符号组成，也充满了符号。”7 如果这一观点正确，那么符号学家潜在的研究对象就没有边界，即使他所关心的是符号的方法或理论，而不是其范围。皮尔士这番令人生畏的断言，似乎意味着：我们所认识的宇宙，完全由符号组成。他是在区分独立于心灵的、实际存在或可能实际存在的宇宙，与人类认知所认识的宇宙。认知的宇宙由符号构成，因为它是已经转化为词语的宇宙。皮尔士是在呼应哲学传统对感性宇宙与可理解宇宙的区分（须记住，这一区分本身属于可理解一侧，“感觉材料”这个概念也是如此：我们无法在语言中接触不可理解之物）。因此，无论我们知道什么，我们都是把它作为符号来知道，知道它具有意义，因而可以交流。符号学的领域是意指的各种形式。

It was the view of the leading theorist of the North American semiotic school, C. S. Peirce, that ‘the entire universe . . . is perfused with signs, if it is not composed exclusively of signs’.7 If this view is correct, then the semiotician’s potential subject-matter knows no bounds, even if his concern is with the method or theory of signs rather than with their extent. What Peirce seems to have meant by this daunting assertion was that the universe as we know it is composed exclusively of signs: he is making a distinction between the universe as it really is, or may really be, independently of the mind, and the universe as known in human cognition. The cognitive universe is formed of signs because it is the universe as already turned into words. Peirce is echoing the traditional philosophical distinction between the sensible and intelligible universes (bearing in mind that the distinction itself belongs on the intelligible side of the divide, as does the notion of ‘sense-data’: we cannot, in language, have access to the non-intelligible). Whatever we know, then, we know as a sign, we know it to be significant and thus communicable. Semiotics has as its realm the forms of signification.

符号功能本身，也许最容易通过丹麦语言学家路易·叶尔姆斯列夫引入的术语来理解（他不知疲倦地向语言学和符号学引入新的复杂术语）：内容与表达。这是叶尔姆斯列夫对索绪尔的所指与能指作出的扩展版本。任何符号系统，无论是语言系统还是其他系统，其功能都是使内容与表达这两个平面相关联；两者每一次这样的关联，都构成一个符号。叶尔姆斯列夫为这一契约的双方使用的术语，也许优于索绪尔的术语，因为它们更熟悉，也更容易把握。在本章中，我将固定使用它们。

The semiotic function itself is most easily grasped perhaps by way of the terms introduced by the Danish linguist Louis Hjelmslev (a tireless introducer of new and complex terms into both linguistics and semiotics): those of content and expression, which are Hjelmslev’s extended versions of Saussure’s signified and signifier. The function of any sign-system, linguistic or otherwise, is to correlate the two planes of content and expression and each such correlation of the two constitutes a sign. Hjelmslev’s terms for the two parties to this contract are perhaps preferable to Saussure’s in that they are more familiar and more readily graspable. I shall stick to them in this chapter.

用“契约”来描述符号功能，是合理的，而且确实有帮助，因为内容与表达平面之间的关联具有约定性质。在每一个个别情形中，这些关联都不可避免地是文化性的，而不是“自然的”。就语言本身而言，这一点很容易说明；在考察索绪尔关于言语符号“任意性”的原则时，我们已简要见过。一种语言在表达与内容之间建立的关联，可能与另一种语言建立的关联一致，也可能不一致。我们可以假定，没有任何两种语言，在表达的整个范围内建立完全相同的关联。叶尔姆斯列夫断言“……不存在普遍的形成方式，只存在普遍的形成原则”8时，所指的就是这一点。他重新表述了索绪尔的奠基原则：语言作为符号系统，相对于现实具有自主性。举一个明显的例子，我们既可以用它说世界中存在的东西，也可以说世界中不存在的东西。而既然我们是通过自己出生时置身其中的语言来认识世界的，就可以合理地论证：是我们的语言决定现实，而不是现实决定我们的语言。

It is legitimate, and indeed helpful, to use the term ‘contract’ to describe the semiotic function because of the conventional nature of the correlation between the planes of content and expression. These correlations are, inescapably and in every single case, cultural and not ‘natural’ ones. This point is quite easily made in respect of language itself, as we briefly saw when considering Saussure’s principle of the ‘arbitrariness’ of the verbal sign. The correlations made by one language between expression and content may or may not coincide with those made by another. We may assume that no two languages make exactly the same correlations throughout the whole range of expression: this is what Hjelmslev is getting at when he asserts that ‘ . . . no universal formation exists, only a universal principle of formation.’8 He is restating the founding principle of Saussure, that as a system of signs language is autonomous in respect of reality. We can use it, to take an obvious example, to say what isn’t in the world, as well as what is. And since we come to know the world through whatever language we have been born into the midst of, it is legitimate to argue that our language determines reality, rather than reality our language.

冒着使问题更混乱、而不是更清楚的风险，我将在这里引入叶尔姆斯列夫作出的另一个区分，即形式与实质之间的区分；在表达与内容两个平面上，都必须认识到这一区分。在人类语言的表达平面上，这一区分相当容易把握，我们已经在区分语言的声音即音位，与人类发音器官所能产生的非语言声音连续体时见过它。音位是形式，未分化的声音连续体是实质。在语言内容平面上，这一区分稍难捉摸：它区分的是一种语言或其他符号系统实际分化出来的内容，与内容之外的东西，也就是我们称为世界的连续体；那是内容本身由之而来的、取之不尽且超越内容的源泉。在这个平面上，由任何一种特定语言划分出来的内容——须记住，所有语言都会以不同方式划分它——是形式，而语言“之外”的世界是实质。这个“之外”，相当于索绪尔假设为我们实际观念基底的那种模糊而未分化的“思想”。例如，它就是那个实际天体，根据偏好，既可以是“晨星”，也可以是“昏星”。但它也是一个问题，因为用叶尔姆斯列夫的术语说，我们只经验到形式，而不是实质：为了在弗雷格那颗星的例子中指出这个“之外”，我所能做的全部，只是产生第三个“形式”，即“实际天体”这个形式。逃离语言的尝试，结果却是在语言中进行的。

At the risk of confusing matters rather than clarifying them, I shall bring in here a further distinction which Hjelmslev makes. This is between form and substance, a distinction which has to be recognized on the planes of both expression and content. On the plane of expression in human language, it is fairly simple to grasp the distinction, which is the one we have already met with in distinguishing the sounds of a language, or phonemes, from the continuum of non-linguistic sound capable of being produced by the human speech apparatus. Phonemes are forms, the continuum of undifferentiated sound is the substance. On the plane of linguistic content the distinction is a little more elusive, being between the content as actually differentiated by a language or other system of signs, and what lies beyond the content: i.e., the continuum we call the world, that inexhaustible and transcendent source from which the content itself derives. On this plane, the content as divided up by any particular language – and all, remember, will divide it up differently – is the forms, and the world ‘beyond’ language is the substance. This ‘beyond’ is the equivalent of the cloudy, undifferentiated ‘thought’ which Saussure posited as the substratum of our actual ideas. It is the actual astral body, for instance, which is both the ‘morning star’ and the ‘evening star’, according to taste. But it is also a problem, since we have experience only of the forms and not of the substance, in Hjelmslev’s terms: in order to indicate the ‘beyond’ in the case of Frege’s star, all I have been able to do is to produce a third ‘form’: the form ‘actual astral body’. The attempt to escape from language turns out to have been made in language.

叶尔姆斯列夫这一进一步区分的重要性，在于它使他能够明确整体图式中作为符号学家所关心的有限部分：即形式之间的关联，而且仅此而已。无论表达的实质还是内容的实质，都不是他直接关心的。因此，语音学和语义学可以留给其他人。

The importance of this further distinction of Hjelmslev’s is that it enables him to specify that restricted part of the overall scheme which is of concern to him as a semiotician: which is the correlation of forms and this alone. The substance, be it of expression or of content, is of no immediate concern. Phonetics and semantics may be left, therefore, to others.

从某种角度说，这是一种严苛的限制，不过，对于叶尔姆斯列夫所自认为的科学家而言，它是恰当的；因为若严格遵守它，就意味着符号学家可以关注自己符号系统的句法，以及其理论上的优点和弱点，却不愿由此进一步走向对符号在社会使用中的实际解释。这就是研究符号的语法，却忘掉人类学；如果符号学内部目前有两个思想流派——看起来确实如此——这可能源于一些符号学家的感觉：文化符号的实际解释不是他们的事，他们的工作是坚守这门学科的形式方面。但对于不是专业符号学家的人，符号学的实践方面更有收获，因为只要对它所涉及的相对少量而简单的规则有适当的基础认识，就可以把任何人变成周围无数文化符号的更敏锐、更有知识的“读者”。

This is an austere limitation in one way, though proper for the scientist that Hjelmslev saw himself to be, because if strictly observed it means that semioticians may involve themselves with the syntax of their systems of signs, and their theoretical strengths and weaknesses, but be unwilling to go on from there to the actual interpretation of signs in their social uses. This is to study the grammar of signs and forget the anthropology; and if currently there are two schools of thought within Semiotics, as there seem to be, this may stem from the sense some semioticians have that the actual interpretation of cultural signs is none of their business, that their job is to stick to the formal aspects of the subject. But for those who are not professional semioticians, it is the practical side of Semiotics that is the more rewarding, since a proper grounding in the relatively few and simple rules it involves can turn anybody into a more acute and informed ‘reader’ of the vastly many cultural signs by which we are surrounded.

如果“通信理论”如今没有被工程师相当狭窄地定义为只关心信息的成功传输、而不问其内容，那么，人们也可以说符号学是一种交流理论，因为一切符号，无论语言符号还是其他符号，都是凭借被不止一个人认识而具有意义的。它们是约定性的、制度化的；在提出它们时，我们知道这一点，这就保证了我们所面对的人能够理解我们，条件是他们并非完全陌生于我们的文化惯例。符号学的一项规定是，任何不在交流系统中发挥作用的东西，都不可能是符号；也就是说，不可能存在真正“私人的”符号。对于维特根斯坦提出的那个著名问题，即能否存在“私人语言”——“而那些别人都不理解、但‘我似乎理解’的声音，可以被称为一种‘私人语言’”9——符号学的回答是，任何可以被称为“语言”的系统，至少潜在地必须是公共的：我自己理解的声音的意义，可以教给别人，因为声音与意义、表达与内容之间必要的关联已经存在。无论是否私人，它们都是约定性的。

If the ‘theory of communication’ had not now become rather narrowly defined by engineers, as being concerned with the successful transmission of messages irrespective of their content, then one might say that Semiotics too was a theory of communication, because all signs, linguistic or other, signify by virtue of being known to more than one person. They are conventional and institutionalized, and our knowing this, in proffering them, is our guarantee that the person we proffer them to, provided they are not wholly alien to our own cultural conventions, will understand us. It is a stipulation of Semiotics that whatever does not function within a communicative system cannot be a sign: that there can be no truly ‘private’ signs. The semiotic answer to the celebrated question raised by Wittgenstein, of whether there can be a ‘private language’ – ‘And sounds which no one else understands but which ‘‘I appear to understand’’ might be called a ‘‘private language’’’9 – is that any system that may be called a ‘language’ must be at least potentially a public one: others may be taught the meaning of sounds I understand myself, since the necessary correlations of sound and sense, expression and content are in place. Private or not, they are conventional.

一个符号要成为符号，必须是约定性的；但这并不意味着，我们使用符号所面对的每个人，都必须参与相关的约定。正如我们可能向其他母语使用者使用母语符号，而他们不知道那些特定符号，因此不理解它们，我们也可以创制只在自己与另一个人之间、甚至只与自己之间成立的约定。“秘密”语言与一般语言在种类上没有区别；它们只是作为有时被称为“受限代码”的东西发挥作用。

The fact that a sign must be conventional in order to qualify as a sign does not mean that everyone we use signs to has to be party to the convention in question. Just as we may use the signs of our native language to other natives who do not know those particular signs and so do not understand them, so we may elaborate conventions which hold between ourselves and only one other person or even with ourselves alone. ‘Secret’ languages are no different in kind from language in general; they merely function as what are sometimes called ‘restricted codes’.

通过这一规定，即符号必须是公共的，或者能够被公开，符号学避免了自己变成个体心理学的一个分支。一个约定性的符号，不论使用者的私人意图如何，仍然是一个约定性的符号。当代符号学的一种传统，把对符号使用者动机的排除推到了极限，例如它宣称：“符号学可以定义为对交流过程的研究，也就是对用来影响他人、并被所要影响的人识别为这种手段的研究。”10 这一传统在美国符号学家查尔斯·莫里斯的追随者中尤为强大；它如此把自己限制于明显而成功的交流行为，因而是行为主义的。上文所引作者埃里克·比桑斯喜欢把这些行为称为“符号行为”（semic acts）。比桑斯等人的首要主张是，必须有两方才能产生一个“符号行为”；这一点当然成立，即使一个成功的“符号行为”也可以发生在单一个体的想象中，由他或她与自己交流。但是，不那么确定的是：是否像比桑斯及其他人坚持的那样，行为的双方必须同样意识到彼此之间正在传递什么。在许多交流行为中，他们会如此，但并非全部。这样一个基础狭窄的模型，没有充分考虑失败的“符号行为”，即符号机制失灵、没有信息传递或传递了错误信息的情形；更重要的是，就符号学而言，它也没有充分考虑那些根本没有明确交流企图的“符号行为”。生活中有许多时候，我们可能无意间“交流”，或者以无法控制的方式“交流”；这给符号学提出了极为重要的问题。另一种符号学传统，以罗兰·巴特为最有力的代表，一直比比桑斯所代表的传统更宽广地看待这一问题，并假定交流可能是无意的。也就是说，一个“符号行为”并不必然要求参与双方默契配合。一位法国语言学家，同时也是巴特的强烈反对者，把这一区别很好地表述为“真正的交流与单纯的表现之间，或交流与意指之间”的区别。11 问题似乎取决于穆南所使用的“真正”一词：“真正的交流”要求“符号行为”的一方具有交流意图，另一方则相应地确信，他或她按这一意图理解了该行为。巴特非常反对这样的确信。他主张，“符号行为”中的不确定性、甚至彻底的歧义性，要高得多。唯一可以确定的是，行为的构成要素是符号，因此它们具有意指作用；它们意指什么，并不总是确定，可能需要符号学家的专业知识来尝试判定这个问题。符号学这两种传统的区别，具有重大含义：一方要把它限制于“真正”交流的行为，另一方更愿意扩展它，把一切意指行为都包含在内。在本章的其余部分中，须记住这一区别。

By this stipulation, that signs be either public or able to be made public, Semiotics guards itself against being turned into a branch of individual psychology. A conventional sign remains a conventional sign irrespective of the private intentions of the person using it. One tradition in contemporary Semiotics carries this exclusion of the sign-user’s motives to the limit, declaring, for example, that ‘Semiotics may be defined as the study of processes of communication, that is, the means used to influence others and recognised as such by those one seeks to influence.’10 This tradition, especially strong among the followers of the American semiotician Charles Morris, is behaviouristic in thus limiting itself to patent and successful acts of communication, or ‘semic acts’ as the writer quoted above, Eric Buyssens, likes to call them. It is the prime contention of such as Buyssens that it takes two to create a ‘semic act’, which is surely the case, even though a successful ‘semic act’ may also take place in the imagination of a single individual communicating with him- or herself. But what is not so surely the case is whether it is necessary, as Buyssens and others maintain, for both parties to the act to be similarly aware of what is passing between them. In a great many acts of communication, they will be, but not in all. Such a narrowly based model does not take adequate account of failed ‘semic acts’, where the sign apparatus malfunctions and no message or a wrong message passes; nor, more crucially for Semiotics, does it take adequate account of those ‘semic acts’ where no explicit attempt is being made at communication at all. There are many occasions in life when we may ‘communicate’ without meaning to, or in ways we cannot control, and this raises most important questions for Semiotics. Another semiotic tradition, exemplified most powerfully by Roland Barthes, has always taken a wider view of the matter than the tradition represented by Buyssens, and supposed that communication may be inadvertent. A ‘semic act’, that is, does not necessarily entail the connivance of the two parties to it. The distinction is well brought out by a French linguist and strong opponent of Barthes, as being one between ‘true communication and mere manifestation, or between communication and signification’.11 The question hinges seemingly on the word ‘true’ as Mounin uses it: ‘true communication’ requiring an intention to communicate in one party to the ‘semic act’ and a corresponding presumption in the other party that he or she has understood the act as intended. Barthes was quite opposed to such presumptions. He argues for a much higher level of uncertainty or even downright ambiguity in the ‘semic act’. The only certainty is that the constituents of the act are signs and therefore that they signify; what they signify is not always sure and it may require the expertise of the semiotician to try and decide the question. The distinction between the two traditions in Semiotics, of those who would limit it to acts of ‘true’ communication, and those who prefer to widen it to take in all acts of signification, has large implications, to be kept in mind for the rest of this chapter.

一切符号都在表达与内容两个平面之间构成关系，但并非所有符号都以相同方式构成关系。符号学需要根据符号如何使表达与内容关联来对符号分类。它们可能全都是约定性的，但绝非全都像语言符号那样“任意”。事实上，某些语言符号在我们看来，比其他符号较少任意性，甚至完全不是任意的，而是“自然的”：所谓拟声词，被认为建立在表达或声音平面与内容或意义平面之间的一种“自然”关系之上。我们认为，像英语 whoosh（嗖）这样的词，模仿了它在语言上所表达的那种声响。有时，对于感叹词也作同样的主张——ouch（哎哟）、wow（哇）、tut（啧）等等——它们同样被认为是受其意义制约的形式。但正如索绪尔及时提醒我们的，所有这样的形式都是约定性的，与语言中其他任何符号一样，受到完全相同的变化力量的作用。拟声词因语言而异，表达疼痛、惊讶等的感叹词也是如此。如果它们的形式完全由意义决定，它们的变化就会小得多，甚至根本没有变化。例如，在发出独特叫声的鸟类的名称中，明显存在某种程度的模仿，这无疑是事实；但它并不使相关的语言形式免于语言规律，也丝毫不减少它们的约定性。

All signs form a relation between the two planes of expression and content, but not all signs form it alike. There is a need in Semiotics to classify signs according to how they relate expression to content. They may all be conventional, but they are not by any means all as ‘arbitrary’ as the linguistic sign. Some linguistic signs, indeed, strike us as less arbitrary than others, or even as not arbitrary at all but ‘natural’: so-called onomatopoeic words are assumed to be founded on a ‘natural’ relation between the plane of expression, or sound, and the plane of content, or sense. We assume that an English word like whoosh mimics the noise of which it is the linguistic expression. And the same claim is also sometimes made for exclamations – ouch, wow, tut, and so on – which are likewise held to be forms conditioned by their sense. But all such forms are, as Saussure was prompt to warn us, conventional and subject to exactly the same forces of change as any other sign of the language. Onomatopoeic words vary from language to language and so do exclamations of pain, surprise and so on. They would vary much less, if at all, if their form was determined exclusively by their sense. The undoubted fact that a certain degree of mimicry is apparent in the names of, for example, birds producing a distinctive cry, does not exempt the linguistic forms in question from the laws of language, nor does it make them any the less conventional.

不过，它确实提醒我们：在任何符号类型学中，都需要依据表达与内容之间关系的性质，引入几项大致的区别。最受符号学家青睐的类型学，是C. S. 皮尔士在上个世纪晚期发展起来的。皮尔士一再回到区分和归类符号的问题上；他最终建立的体系列出了不少于六十六种不同的符号，是其勤勉钻研的一座丰碑，尽管其复杂性使它作为供他人使用的工作模型几乎毫无用处。符号学家仍在研究这个问题，不断在各种符号之间引入更精细的区别。这是他们的乐趣。其他人不必辨认这些细微差别；对于专家以外的人，作出几项粗略区别就足够了。

It does alert us, however, to the need to introduce a few broad distinctions into any typology of signs according to the nature of the relation between expression and content. The typology which has found greatest favour among semioticians is that which was developed by C. S. Peirce in the later years of the last century. Peirce returned again and again to the problem of distinguishing and classifying signs and the system with which he ended up, listing as it does no fewer than sixty-six distinct kinds of sign, is a monument to his assiduity even if its complexity has rendered it nearly useless as a working model for others. Semioticians continue to work at this question and to introduce ever subtler distinctions between kinds of signs. Such is their pleasure. It is not necessary for others to recognize such subtleties; broad distinctions may be made which are sufficient for all but an expert.

皮尔士把符号分为三类：“象征符号”“指示符号”和“像似符号”。象征符号我们已经遇到过，因为在皮尔士的分类中，语言的符号属于象征符号。12在皮尔士看来，象征符号是“依据某种法则而指向其所表示对象的符号，这种法则通常是一般观念之间的联想，它的作用在于使人把该象征符号解释为指向那个对象”。13

Peirce divides signs into three classes: ‘symbols’, ‘indices’ and ‘icons’. Symbols we have already met with because in Peirce’s classification the signs of a language are symbolic.12 For Peirce a symbol is ‘a sign which refers to the object that it denotes by virtue of a law, usually an association of general ideas, which operates to cause the symbol to be interpreted as referring to that object.’13

因此，在象征符号中，表达与内容的关系是“任意的”：两者之间没有“自然的”联系。使用象征符号的人必须学会它们的意义——对于一种我们全然不懂的语言，没有帮助或指导，我们就无法推知其表达的内容。关于皮尔士对象征符号的定义，还有一点需要说明：他所说的“对象”不能等同于世界中的一个物体。这正是叶尔姆斯列夫的术语发挥其独特作用之处；皮尔士的对象不是语言之外、处在超越符号学的形而上学“实体”中的某种东西，而是内容平面上与某一特定表达相关联的那一部分。因此，皮尔士的定义并没有把我们带到词典之外：词典列举了我们作为某一语言共同体或文化的成员所能使用的表达与内容的对应关系。值得再说一次，符号学的范围与文化领域完全重合；用艾柯的话说，“表达并不指称一个对象，而是承载一种文化内容”。14

Thus, with symbols the relation of expression to content is ‘arbitrary’: there is no ‘natural’ link between one and the other. The meanings of symbols have to be learned by those who employ them – there is no deducing without help or instruction the content of the expressions of a language utterly unknown to us. A point also needing to be made about Peirce’s definition of a symbol is that the ‘object’ he speaks of must not be identified with an object in the world. This is where Hjelmslev’s terminology comes into its own; Peirce’s object is not something outside language, in the metaphysical ‘substance’that transcends Semiotics, but rather that segment of the content plane which is correlated with a particular expression. Peirce’s definition, therefore, does not take us outside the dictionary, which lists the correlations of expression and content available to us as members of a particular language-community or culture. Semiotics is coextensive, it bears repeating, with the field of culture, and in Eco’s words, ‘an expression does not designate an object but is the bearer of a cultural content’.14

除了象征符号，皮尔士还承认另外两大类符号，其中表达与内容的关系不再是“无理据的”。第一类是指示符号：“由于确实受到它所表示的对象的作用，而指向该对象的符号。”15在指示符号中，表达与内容之间存在自然联系。许多指示符号涉及纯粹自然的因果序列：例如说“太阳周围的一圈光环”是下雨的“征兆”，或者烟是火的征兆。同样，路面上的碎玻璃可能是刚刚发生车祸的征兆。按皮尔士的术语，这三种符号都具有指示性；表达通过各种物理法则，也就是自然法则，与内容相联系。不过，这种自然的决定关系并不妨碍这些符号同时也是文化的，并依有关文化的规范而获得不同的价值。“太阳周围的一圈光环”只是预示下雨的许多气象征兆之一，在特定文化中，它可能流行，也可能不流行。不管是否流行，在符号行为中使用它的人，若是农民，可能出于实际的理由；也可能出于较宽泛的交际理由，例如展示自己熟悉天气谚语。指示符号尽管包含自然理据，却像其他任何符号一样可以被解释。它的作用远不止于单纯作出指示。

In addition to symbols, Peirce recognizes two other broad classes of signs in which the relation between expression and content is no longer ‘unmotivated’. The first of these classes is the index: a ‘sign which refers to the object that it denotes by virtue of having been really affected by that object’.15 With an index there is a natural connection between expression and content. Many indices involve purely natural sequences of cause and effect: as when a ‘ring round the sun’ is said to be a ‘sign’ of rain, or smoke to be a sign of fire. Similarly, broken glass on a roadway may be the sign of a recent accident. These three signs are all, in Peircean terms, indexical; expression is linked to content by various physical, i.e. natural laws. But this natural determination does not stop such signs being also cultural and acquiring a different value according to the norms of the culture concerned. A ‘ring round the sun’ is only one of a number of meteorological signs portending rain, and it may or may not prevail in a given culture. Whether or not it does, whoever uses it in a semic act may so so for practical reasons, if he is a farmer, or for more loosely communicative ones, to display perhaps an acquaintance with weather-lore. An index, for all the natural motivation it incorporates, is open to interpretation like any other sign. It is far from exhausted by serving merely as an index.

皮尔士的第三类、也是最后一类主要符号，是像似符号。它是

The third and last of Peirce’s main classes of signs is the icon. This is

仅仅凭借自身的特征而指向其所表示对象的符号；无论是否存在这样的对象，它都同样具有这些特征……任何东西，无论是性质、存在的个体还是法则，只要它与某物相像，并被用作该物的符号，就是该物的像似符号。16

a sign which refers to the object that it denotes merely by virtue of characters of its own, and which it possesses just the same, whether any such object exists or not . . . Anything whatever, be it quality, existent individual or law, is an icon of anything in so far as it is like that thing and used as a sign of it.16

因此，像似符号中有一种图像成分，这正是皮尔士选择这个术语的原因。表达与内容的关系是物理上的相似关系。作为像似符号的例子，可以举出温斯顿·丘吉尔在第二次世界大战中极为成功地使用的手指V形手势。17这个手势是把食指与中指举到面部高度，指尖朝上、朝向身体外侧，它像似于Victory（胜利）一词的首字母。它是一个符号的符号。然而，尽管它有像似性，它仍具有高度的约定性；证据是，没有解释，谁也无法正确理解它。它必须经由制度化，才能作为符号发挥作用。

There is thus a picture-element in an icon, which is why Peirce chose the term. The relation of expression to content is one of physical similarity. As an iconic sign one might instance the digital V-sign used so successfully by Winston Churchill during World War II.17 This sign, made by holding the first and second fingers of the hand pointing upwards and away from the body at face height, was iconic of the initial letter of the word Victory. It was a sign of a sign. But for all its iconicity it was highly conventional, the proof of which is that no one could have interpreted it successfully without explanation. It required institutionalization to function as a sign.

皮尔士这种相对简单的符号三分法还可以进一步细化——有些符号完全可能是“混合的”，而非纯粹属于某一类的例子——但这里不予讨论。业余符号学研究者要理解符号学的原则，甚至其实践，只需掌握象征符号、指示符号与像似符号之间的区别。不过，我还要从皮尔士那里引入一个术语，因为它很有用，那就是解释项。它的用处在于强调：任何符号，无论形式如何，都必须能够按照通行的约定得到解释，这是符号的本质。解释项是皮尔士对某一特定符号所引起的反应的称呼（注意，它并不是指解释符号的人：解释项不是人）。比如说，丘吉尔的V形手势的解释项，是“我们最终将赢得这场战争”的确信。目睹V形手势的人或许并没有明确说出这一串话，但倘若一定要他们实际表明对这个符号的解释，他们可能会说出这样的话。当然，符号学家只能处理理想化的情况，无法处理不同个体对同一个符号的反应中无穷无尽、各不相同的细微差异。

Refinements of Peirce’s relatively simple tripartite division of signs – and some signs may well be ‘mixed’ rather than pure examples of one class or another – are possible, but they will be ignored here. The distinction between symbols, indices and icons is all that the amateur semiotician needs to understand the principles and even the practice of Semiotics. There is one more item of terminology which I shall bring in from Peirce, however, because it is useful, and that is the term interpretant. This is useful for emphasizing that it is of the essence of any sign, of whatever form, that it be interpretable in accordance with prevailing conventions. The interpretant is the name Peirce gives to the response to a particular sign (and not, note, to the interpreter of the sign: the interpretant is not a person). The interpretant of the Churchillian V-sign is, let us say, an assurance that ‘We shall win the war in the end’. Such a string of words may not have been explicitly formulated by those witnessing the V-sign but it is one they might have produced if pressed actually to declare their interpretation of the sign. The semiotician, of course, can deal only in idealizations, and not with the infinitely many unique shades of difference in response to the one sign among individuals.

重要的是，一个符号的解释项，结果又是更多符号。即使对丘吉尔V形手势的反应是在遭轰炸街道的废墟中勇敢露出的微笑，这一点依然成立，因为这个被引发的微笑是一种约定的指示符号（还是象征符号？我们确实知道微笑是那种被我们称为“愉快”的情感的自然表现吗？），并会被他人如此识别。其他人对这个勇敢微笑的反应，又成为它的解释项；如此无限继续。符号与解释项的序列没有终点，使我们置身于艾柯欣然称作“无限符号衍义的过程”18之中（原文用大写字母）。这个过程有点像被困在一本词典里，或者，按艾柯更愿意采用、而且更恰当的说法，被困在一部百科全书中：原则上，为一个符号下定义，可以把人类的全部知识都牵连进来。

The important thing is that the interpretant of one sign turns out to be more signs. This remains true even if the response to Churchill’s V-sign is a smile, bravely worn amidst the wreckage of a bombed street, because this induced smile is a conventional index (or symbol? for do we know for certain that a smile is a natural manifestation of the feeling we call ‘cheerfulness’?) and will be recognized as such by others. The response of these others to the brave smile then becomes its interpretant; and so on ad infinitum. The sequence of signs and interpretants is unending, involving us in what Eco delightedly calls ‘a process of UNLIMITED SEMIOSIS’18 (his capitals). The process is a little like being trapped inside a dictionary or, as Eco would rather and rightly have it, an encyclopedia, where it is feasible in principle to implicate the whole of human knowledge in the definition of a single sign.

如果把皮尔士与叶尔姆斯列夫使用的两套术语结合起来，就可以把“符号行为”分为三个组成部分：表达、内容和解释项。必须特别强调，这三个组成部分都属于公共领域，而不属于相关个体的心理领域。它们是语言系统的抽象对象，在具体的言语行为中得到实现；换句话说——这在本书中并非首次提及——我们借以表达意义的手段，是为我们提供的，而不是由我们创造的。我们赋予这些手段的，是运用它们进行交流的意愿。用现象学哲学家梅洛-庞蒂的话说：“组织起来的符号有其内在的意义，这种意义依赖的不是‘我思’，而是‘我能’。”19个体意愿这样进入先于自我而存在的非个人系统，而人类又只能通过这个系统交流，这既是符号学中、也是结构主义中最深刻、最令人困扰的问题。本研究的结论将更直接地讨论这个问题。

If one blends the sets of terms used by Peirce and by Hjelmslev, one can divide the ‘semic act’ into its three constituents: expression, content and interpretant. These three constituents are all, very emphatically, in the public domain and not in that of the psychology of the individuals concerned. They are the abstract objects of a langue realized in particular acts of parole, which is to say, not for the first time in this book, that the means by which we signify are provided for us and not created by us. What we bring to them is the will to employ them in communication. In the words of the phenomenological philosopher Merleau-Ponty: ‘Organised signs have their immanent meaning, which depends not on ‘‘I think’’ but on ‘‘I can’’.’19 This insertion of the individual will into the impersonal system which pre-exists the self, but through which alone human beings can communicate, is the deepest and most vexed problem in Semiotics as in Structuralism, and it is one which will be discussed more directly in the Conclusion to the present study.

符号学家常把所研究的符号系统称作“代码”，尽管这个术语含义并不明确。所有代码的共同点是其人为性；只有已经熟悉代码规则的人，才能成功接收以该代码传递的信息。社会本身可以被描述为一个庞大而极其复杂的代码网络，使社会内或大或小的人群能够交流各自的意义。即使在最大、最普遍的代码——一个人群的母语——内部，也存在许多次级代码，或者说向一部分而非全部受话者传递信息的约定手段。我们应当记住，我们交谈时，除了语言符号本身，还运用其他符号来强化或扩展话语的意义：重音、语调与节奏都是交谈中熟悉的手段，只有遵守支配其使用的规则，我们才能成功地使用它们。只要如此，这些手段就是约定的，属于一种得到承认的符号系统。假如我们用英语对某人说“You didn’t fool me”（你没骗过我），重读句子的最后一个词，我们就确信别人会按我们的意图来理解。我们遵守的是一种约定，一些语言学家会承认它完全属于语言范畴，即使受话者是尚不了解这项约定的小孩或外国人，也适合使用。（在书面英语中，同一内容可以通过另一种方式表达，即像上面那样使用斜体字；这提醒我们，在结构主义思想中，语言是抽象对象，不能与它可能采取的实际物理形式混淆——我们没有人把书面英语和口头英语看成两种不同语言，而是看成同一种语言的两种形式。）我们所使用的完全因人而异的词语重音，就像人们说话时运用的某些——但并非全部——身体姿势一样，处在任何代码之外，除了偶然碰巧，不可能得到正确解释。所有代码都像其他符号系统一样，不断变动；起初未编码的姿势或语言用法，最终可能被接纳进入代码。因此，符号学家处理的系统是动态的，同所有结构一样，既可能不断丰富，也可能不断退化。有趣的是，手势或语调等符号系统使我们的交际能力大为扩展，而我们的词典对此却全无记载。对于哪些姿势或语调是约定的、哪些不是，我们没有可供参照、据以裁决的权威。作为这些语言辅助手段的使用者，我们假定它们全都表达意义；但应记住，如果它们不属于得到承认的代码，其意义充其量也是含混的。

Semioticians write frequently of the systems of signs they study as ‘codes’, even though the term has its ambiguities. What is common to all codes is their artificiality; only those already acquainted with the rules of the code can receive messages in it successfully. Society itself may be presented as a vast and extremely intricate network of codes which enable small or large groups of people within it to communicate their meanings. Even within the largest and most pervasive of all codes, the native language of a population, there are numerous sub-codes, or conventional means of conveying information to some rather than all one’s addressees. We should remember that when we speak with each other we employ signs additional to the linguistic signs themselves in order to intensify or expand the meaning of our words: stress, intonation and cadence are all familiar devices in conversation and we can use them successfully only if we observe the rules governing their use. So long as we do that, these devices are conventional and belong to a recognized semiotic system. If we say to someone in English ‘You didn’t fool me’, laying stress on the last word of the phrase, we are in no doubt of being understood as we want to be. We are observing a convention which some linguists would allow was a properly linguistic one, fit to be employed even if our addressee is a young child or a foreigner as yet ignorant of it. (In writing English, the same content can be expressed differently, by the use of italic letters, as above; this is a reminder that a language, in Structuralist thought, is an abstract object, not to be confused with the actual physical forms it may take – we none of us think of written and spoken English as two different languages, but as two forms of the one language.) Word stresses we may use which are wholly idiosyncratic, like some – though not all – of the physical gestures people employ in speaking, fall outside any code and are not susceptible of a correct interpretation except fortuitously. All codes are in constant flux, like any other system of signs, and gestures or linguistic usages which begin by being uncoded may eventually be accepted into the code; the systems which semioticians deal with are thus dynamic, being open like all structures to constant elaboration as well as degradation. The considerable extension to our powers of communication which semiotic systems such as those of gesture or intonation represent is one, interestingly, quite absent from our dictionaries. We have no authority to which we can refer to settle questions about which gestures or intonations are conventional and which are not. As users of these linguistic aids we presume that all of them signify, but we should remember that if they are not part of a recognized code their meaning is at best ambiguous.

这样大幅扩展符号学的领域，使它包含整个文化，在对此持怀疑态度的人看来，是一种让我们的生活充斥过多意义的思想恐怖主义。有些较为谨慎的符号学家，只把那些明确表明自己是符号的文化现象视为符号：交通信号、印刷标记、船舶旗号，以及大量其他类似系统。纯粹从句法的角度研究这些系统是可能的，也可能有益：许多这样的代码，其系统性毫无疑问可以通过符号学的“工程设计”来改进；一旦从符号学的角度考虑道路标志，就会很快明白这一点——它们是一团象征符号、指示符号和像似符号的混合物。（一个艰难却富于启示的试验，是重新设计我们现行的道路标志系统，或者说非系统，使所有标志都具有像似性，从而可能获得普遍适用性。）

This dramatic extension of the semiotic field, to include the whole of culture, is looked on by those suspicious of it as a kind of intellectual terrorism, overfilling our lives with meanings. There are more cautious semioticians who take to be signs only those cultural phenomena which declare themselves to be signs: traffic signals, printers’ marks, ships’ flags, and a vast many other such systems. It is possible and may be profitable to study these systems purely from the syntactical point of view: the systematicity of many such codes could quite certainly be improved by semiotic ‘engineering’, as one soon realizes when considering road signs from the semiotic point of view – they are a muddle of symbols, indices and icons. (An arduous but revealing test would be to redesign our present system or non-system of road signs so that all of them were iconic and thus potentially universal.)

然而，毫无疑问，把整个文化当作自身领域的广义符号学，才是更丰富、更重要的研究。它像列维-斯特劳斯的社会人类学一样，在文化与自然之间划出鲜明的界线，并相信落在界线文化一侧的任何东西都可以被解释，以评估其社会“价值”。即使像呼吸这样“纯粹”属于生物学的现象，也可能如此；这是语言学家萨丕尔选中的一个巧妙例子。

However, there is no doubt but that it is Semiotics in its wider sense, which takes the whole of culture as its domain, that is the richer, more important study.
