Like the social anthropology of Lévi-Strauss it observes a sharp dividing-line between the cultural and the natural and believes that everything falling on the cultural side of the divide is open to interpretation, in order to assess its social ‘value’. This may be the case even with a phenomenon as ‘purely’ biological as breathing, an ingenious example picked on by the linguist Sapir.

通常，一个特定个体所特有的呼吸节律，被看作严格属于个体自身规定的事情。但是，如果重点转向把某种呼吸方式看作合乎礼仪或社会传统的结果……那么，关于呼吸的整个问题，就立刻不再只是个体的事务，而呈现出一种社会模式的面貌。20

Ordinarily the characteristic rhythm of breathing of a given individual is looked upon as a matter for strictly individual definition. But if the emphasis shifts to the consideration of a certain manner of breathing as due to good form or social tradition . . . then the whole subject of breathing at once ceases to be a merely individual concern and takes on the appearance of a social pattern.20

这里的术语可能并非符号学术语，因为萨丕尔只会自称人类学家，但他对文化出人意料地侵入自然的洞察，却是纯粹的符号学。一旦我们的呼吸方式可以发生某种变化，呼吸就成为不只是功能性的过程。呼吸符号学大概永远不会成为很引人入胜的研究，即使对热衷于养生时尚的人也是如此；但由于呼吸这种现象的必要性与普遍性，它是一个极好的例子，说明文化如何出人意料地深入我们的生活。

The terminology here may not be semiotic, because Sapir would have claimed only to be an anthropologist, but his insight into the unsuspected intrusions of culture on nature is pure Semiotics. As soon as some variation becomes possible in the manner in which we breathe, breathing becomes a more than merely functional process. The semiotics of breathing is never likely to be a very compelling study, even among health faddists, but because of the necessity and universality of the phenomenon it makes an excellent example of the unsuspected intrusiveness of culture in our lives.

这种涉猎广泛的符号学，研究意识形态及其在整个社会中的传播方式。符号学家必须集中注意这种传播的手段和过程，因而应当格外敏锐地意识到，那些往往十分熟悉、不引人注目的表达具有何等丰富的内容。20世纪20年代，在革命后的俄国，杰出的语言学家、文学“人类学家”米哈伊尔·巴赫金就有了这种认识；在他的引领下，后来形成了一个完整的俄国文化符号学学派。巴赫金主要研究文学，但绝不局限于文学；他希望在每一种文化表现中，发现任何社会中相互竞争的各种意识形态的物质证据。事实上，对他而言，这也是意识形态存在的唯一可发现的证据，因为意识形态不是社会生活中私人而非物质的力量，而是公共、具体的力量：“我们最容易把意识形态的创造想象成理解、领会和感知的某种内部过程，却没有注意到，它实际上是在外部、向眼睛、耳朵、手展开的。它不在我们之内，而在我们之间。”21这是一个社会研究者的观点，他能够告诉我们关于表意与交际性质的真相，否则我们会对此全然不知。巴赫金不是正统马克思主义者，因为他乐于看到不同意识形态在同一社会中共存，更不用说相互竞争；但是，他的马克思主义立场足以使他希望为我们给社会“祛魅”，他的唯物主义立场也足以使他拒绝承认观念以任何其他形式存在，除了以在社会成员之间传递的符号的形式。

Semiotics of this wide-ranging kind has to do with ideologies and how they are transmitted throughout societies. By concentrating as they must on the means and the process of such transmission, semioticians should become unusually alert to the richness of content of often quite familiar and unobtrusive expressions. This was a realization reached in the 1920s in post- Revolutionary Russia by the remarkable linguist and literary ‘anthropologist’ Mikhail Bakhtin, from whose lead there has descended a whole Russian school of cultural semioticians. Bakhtin, who was primarily but far from exclusively a student of literature, expected to find material evidence of the various ideologies competing in any society in every kind of cultural manifestation. Indeed, this was for him the only evidence discoverable for the existence of ideologies, which are not private and immaterial forces in social life but public, concrete ones: ‘We are most inclined to imagine ideological creation as some inner process of understanding, comprehension, and perception, and do not notice that it in fact unfolds externally, for the eye, the ear, the hand. It is not within us, but between us.’21 This is the point of view of a student of society who has truths to tell us about the nature of signification and communication of which we should otherwise be altogether unaware. Bakhtin was not an orthodox Marxist because he revelled in the coexistence, not to say the competition of different ideologies within the same society, but he was Marxist enough to want to ‘demystify’society for us and materialist enough to refuse to recognize the existence of ideas except in the form of the signs passing between the members of the society.

由此，巴赫金建立起一种有力的传统：把符号学看作“揭示”，把符号学家看作具有人类学意识、最能代表我们“解读”社会的观察者。这种符号学家不一定非要有自己强烈的政治或社会信念，尽管他很可能会有，而且这些信念可能是激进的。为这种“揭示”目的而开展符号学的危险，在于它会试图把社会描述成一种阴谋：有知识的观察者在文化表现中发现的隐藏意义，被解释为某一阶级企图强制其他阶级，或者维持自身霸权的行为。不过，符号学这样被赋予“倾向性”的风险，十分值得承担，因为显然，符号学家也可以像任何其他社会人类学家一样，以或多或少中立的精神施展其技艺。

Bakhtin thus installs a potent tradition of Semiotics as ‘revelation’ and of the semiotician as the anthropologically minded observer best capable of ‘reading’ society on our behalf. It is not essential that a semiotician of this kind should have strong political or social beliefs of his own, though it is likely that he will and that these beliefs will be radical. The danger of Semiotics practised for such ‘revelatory’ ends is that it will seek to present society as a conspiracy, whereby the hidden meanings found in cultural manifestations by the informed observer are interpreted as the attempts by one class to coerce others or to preserve their hegemony. However, the risk of Semiotics being thus ‘loaded’ are well worth taking, for it is clear that semioticians may also practise their art in a more or less neutral spirit just like any other social anthropologist.

把符号学作为对社会的政治性“解读”来实践的人中，最引人注目的无疑是罗兰·巴特；与巴赫金一样，他所受的训练主要在文学方面。巴特1957年的《神话学》，以近乎马克思主义的观点解释了一系列法国文化现象，是一部极为敏锐而尖刻的著作。在他人生的那个阶段，巴特倾向于用马克思主义分析中弱肉强食的词汇看待社会：资产阶级恶意地掠夺受压迫的无产阶级（后来，他又像巴赫金一样认识到多元主义以及意识形态间公开竞争的优点，便大大缓和了这一观点）。对写作《神话学》时的巴特而言，符号衍义是卫生保健，它揭露某个强大的社会群体如何滥用符号，损害其他群体。他故意以带有倾向性的方式使用“神话”一词，指称对表意过程的这种系统性滥用。按照巴特的说法，哪些对象可以遭到这样的滥用，并没有限制：

Unquestionably the most spectacular practitioner of Semiotics as a political ‘reading’ of society was Roland Barthes, whose training, like Bakhtin’s, was mainly in literature. Barthes’s book of 1957, Mythologies, is a wonderfully acute and sardonic essay in the interpretation of a number of French cultural phenomena from a near-Marxist point of view. At that stage in his life Barthes was inclined to see society in the predatory terms of Marxist analysis, with the bourgeoisie preying malevolently on a repressed proletariat (a view he greatly softened later when he came to appreciate, again like Bakhtin, the virtues of pluralism and of open competition between ideologies). For the Barthes of Mythologies semiosis is hygiene, it brings to light the abusive way in which signs can be used by one powerful social group at the expense of others. He uses the term ‘myth’ in a deliberately tendentious way to refer to such systematic abuse of the process of signification. According to Barthes there are no limits as to which objects may be so abused:

那么，任何东西都能成为神话吗？我相信都能，因为宇宙具有无限的暗示性。世界上的每个对象，都可能从封闭、沉默的存在转变为被言说的状态，向社会的占用开放，因为没有任何法则，无论自然的还是非自然的，禁止我们谈论事物。一棵树就是一棵树。但一棵经米努·德鲁埃［当时一位著名的法国儿童诗人］言说的树，就不再完完全全是一棵树，而是一棵经过装饰的树，被调整得适合某种消费，披上文学的花边、意象、反抗，总之，披上附加于纯粹实体之上的一种社会用途。22

Anything, therefore, can be a myth? I believe it can, for the universe is infinitely suggestive. Every object in the world may pass from an enclosed, mute existence to a spoken state, open to appropriation by society, for no law, natural or not, forbids us to speak of things. A tree is a tree. But a tree spoken by Minou Drouet [a celebrated French child poet of the time] is no longer altogether a tree, but a decorated tree, adapted for a certain consumption, invested with literary frills, images, revolts, in short with a social usage which is added to the pure substance.22

当巴特开始阐述其符号学理论，以证明自己出色的实践有其依据时，他大量倚重叶尔姆斯列夫，尤其是叶尔姆斯列夫关于区分外延符号系统与兼有内涵的符号系统的简短论述。巴特本人研究的是内涵，而非外延。按叶尔姆斯列夫的说法，区别在于：在一个内涵符号系统中，表达平面本身已经是一个符号系统。以语言为例，最容易理解这一点：作为语言学家研究的符号系统，语言具有外延作用，它使表达与内容相对应；但一旦这一基本的外延功能被取代，正如语言被用于文学时那样，外延系统就成为某种内容的表达，而该内容有待读者确定。米努·德鲁埃，或者任何以文学方式使用语言的人，之所以“言说”树木，并不是为了外延性的目的，告诉我们某个特定对象的符号是如此，而是为了某种文学目的，也就是文化目的。诗人的树，不管愿不愿意，都被转变成巴特所说的“意识形态的片段”23，因为它代表了社会中某一特定群体共有的对树木的评价。至少自浪漫主义以来，树木就是“诗意的”；它们是“自然的”——因而巴特选择它们作为文化的例证，就带有反讽意味。在儿童诗的语境中，与人为性相对的“自然性”、与城市性相对的“乡村性”，都是“树”这一符号有效的内涵，还有“诗意性”的内涵，因为儿童诗的特点，就是非常忠实地反映普遍公认的观点，即什么对象或主题具有“诗意”，什么没有。对于这种符号的意识形态内涵，通常至少有一部分可以达成共识，尽管不同的语言或符号学研究者可能给出不同解读。内涵必须始终允许争论。在实践中，符号学应当鼓励解释文化的自由，同时承认：一种没有任何其他人赞同的解释，必须视为失败。

When he came to propound the theory of his Semiotics, in order to justify his brilliant practice of it, Barthes relied heavily on Hjelmslev, and in particular on Hjelmslev’s brief remarks distinguishing semiotic systems which denote from those which also connote. Barthes himself was a student of connotations, not of denotations. The difference, as Hjelmslev puts it, is that a connotative sign-system is one in which the expression plane is already a sign-system. This is most easily understood by taking language as the example: as a sign-system studied by a linguist, language denotes, it correlates expressions and contents; but once this basic denotative function is supplanted, as it is in the case of a literary use of language, the denotative system becomes the expression of a content to be determined by the reader. Minou Drouet, or any other literary user of language, does not ‘speak’ trees for the denotative purpose of telling us that such is the sign for that particular object, but for some literary, i.e., cultural purpose. The poet’s tree is turned, willy-nilly, into what Barthes declares to be a ‘fragment of ideology’23 because it represents a certain evaluation of trees common to a particular grouping in society. Trees, since Romanticism at least, are ‘poetic’; they are ‘natural’ – hence the irony of their choice by Barthes as instances of the cultural. ‘Naturalness’, as opposed to artificiality, or ‘rurality’ as opposed to urbanity, these are valid connotations of the sign ‘tree’ in the context of a child’s poem, along with the connotation of ‘poeticalness’, since it is the characteristic of children’s poetry to reflect very faithfully the commonly accepted view of what objects or themes are and are not ‘poetic’. It is usually possible to reach a consensus on some at least of the ideological connotations of such signs, though different students of language or semiotics may give different readings of them. Connotations must always be open to argument. In practice Semiotics should encourage freedom in the interpretation of culture, while recognizing that interpretations with which no one else concurs must be deemed to have failed.

巴特属于那些认为符号学必须以语言学为先导的人。这个观点不受某些符号学家欢迎，例如专门研究动物交际的人；因为显然，一些动物物种传递信息的手段尽管精巧得惊人——例如蜜蜂，其“舞蹈”动作传达花粉来源的距离和方向——却远不能算拥有真正的语言。但巴特进一步预言：

Barthes was one of those who believe that Semiotics has to take its lead from linguistics, a view unpopular among those semioticians who, for example, have specialized in the study of animal communication, since it is clear that for all the startling elaborateness of the means some animal species have for passing messages – bees, for example, whose ‘dance’ movements communicate the distance and direction of pollen sources – they fall very far short of having a true language. But Barthes went further in prophesying that:

未来无疑在于一种内涵语言学，因为社会以自然语言所提供的第一系统为起点，不断发展第二意义的系统；这种发展有时公开承认，有时加以掩饰或合理化，几乎已经成为一种真正的历史人类学。24

the future no doubt lies in a linguistics of connotation, because, starting with the primary system provided for it by natural language, society is constantly developing systems of second meanings, and this elaboration, which is sometimes openly avowed and at others masked or rationalised, comes close to being a true historical anthropology.24

巴特式符号学的效果，是赋予一种文化中的每个时刻、每个事件以超出单纯功能的意义。只要我们有时间、有意愿去寻找，意识形态的证据就无处不在，尽管身处该文化的绝大多数人，一辈子都不知道自己的行为可以怎样被“解读”。巴特坚持认为，功能性的东西本身也必然表意，因为它同时也是社会性的：“一旦有社会，每一种用途就都转变为该用途的符号。”25因此，我们吃的食物或穿的衣服具有功能，因为它们滋养我们或保护我们免受风雨寒暑之害；但若认为其功能穷尽了它们的社会“价值”，就太天真了。食物和服饰都被赋予丰富的内涵；事实上，考虑到它们所显示的多样性，可以认为它们正是巴特所从事的那种人类学的核心研究对象。如果我们的呼吸方式存在变化，那么我们的饮食和穿着方式的变化，就要大得多。

The effect of Barthesian Semiotics is to invest every moment and event in a culture with a significance beyond the simply functional. If we have but the time and the will to seek, the evidence of ideology is pervasive, even if the vast majority of those who are members of the culture live their lives unaware of how their behaviour may be ‘read’. Barthes insists that the functional itself inescapably signifies because it is also social: ‘as soon as there is society, every usage is converted into a sign of that usage’.25 Thus the food we eat or the clothes we wear are functional inasmuch as they nourish us or protect us from the elements, but it would be naïve to think that their function exhausts their social ‘value’. Both food and clothing are richly invested with connotations; indeed, they might be thought of as being of central interest to the kind of anthropology on which Barthes was engaged, given the variety they display. If there is variation in the way that we breathe, there is a very much greater variation in the way that we eat or dress.

在评价符号学本身在社会中的作用时，以巴特为代表的扩展性传统，与源自皮尔士的更谨慎、更讲求方法的传统之间，出现了一个意味深长的汇合。巴特把自己呈现为明察秋毫的观察者，能够教导那些不如自己自觉的公民，如何解读身边的符号，进而明白一个社会的意义如何被“决策群体”操纵。因此，对符号学的正确理解将促成更健康的社会，因为这个社会受到更好的教育，并得以“祛魅”。在美国传统中，皮尔士最有影响的追随者查尔斯·莫里斯，同样把符号学看作青年教育的一个至关重要的部分：

When it comes to evaluating the role of Semiotics itself within society there is a telling convergence between the expansive tradition represented by Barthes and the more cautious, methodical tradition stemming from Peirce. Barthes presents himself as the lucid observer able to instruct citizens less aware than himself in the ways of reading the signs around them and coming to see how a society’s meanings may be manipulated by ‘decision groups’. Thus a proper understanding of Semiotics will make for a healthier because better educated and ‘demystified’ society. In the American tradition, Peirce’s most influential follower, Charles Morris, looks on Semiotics similarly as a crucial part of the education of the young:

……一个真正民主的社会，会把扩大其成员的符号能力与资源、使之多样化，作为原则性的目标……只有在这样的社会中，符号学才会在教育过程中获得基础地位，使个体准备好抵抗其他符号使用者对自己的利用，避免在自身行为中出现病态符号，并为不断修正和创造符号作出自己的贡献，因为健康的社会有赖于此。26

. . . a truly democratic society would aim, as a matter of principle, to enlarge and diversify the sign capacities and resources of its members . . . Only in such a society would semiotic(s) be given a basic place in the educational process, so that the individual would be prepared to resist the exploitation of himself by other users of signs, to avoid pathic signs in his behavior, and to make his contribution to the constant correction and creation of signs upon which a healthy society depends.26

莫里斯提出一个非同寻常的看法：受过符号学教育的人，可以在行为中避免“病态符号”。这把我引到本章最后要考虑的问题，也就是符号学、因而也是结构主义在精神分析中的位置。如果说，就实践而言，符号衍义始于古希腊对医学症状的研究，那么以这门学科在当代医学中的另一种应用来结束这番简要考察，就是恰当的。有人声称，所有精神疾病或障碍，都可以描述为患者符号活动能力或表意能力的缺陷。这一说法显然过于笼统。不过，许多常见的精神状态可以用符号学术语来描述，因为它们影响患者的语言表现，并可能最终使其交际能力崩溃。如果“符号行为”本质上是参与双方共同拥有的，那么，倘若其中一方在符号活动上无能，不能以认可的方式使用共同的符号——姑且假定是语言符号——这个行为的完整性就受到威胁。丧失的是社会交往能力。

Morris’s remarkable suggestion that the semiotically educated person might avoid ‘pathic signs’ in his behaviour brings me to the final consideration of this chapter: which is the place of semiotics and hence of Structuralism within psychoanalysis. If semiosis may be said to have begun, practically speaking, in the ancient Greek study of medical symptoms, then it is fitting to end this brief survey with another, contemporary medical application of the discipline. The claim has been made that all mental illnesses or disturbances can be described as defects of the sufferer’s semic or signifying powers. This claim is surely too sweeping. But a number of familiar mental conditions can be described in semiotic terms because they affect the linguistic performance of the victim and may end in the collapse of his or her communicative capacity. If a ‘semic act’ is essentially shared between the two parties to it, then its integrity is threatened if one of those parties is semically incompetent and fails to use the common signs – of the language, let us suppose – in an approved way. The loss is one of sociability.

心理治疗师的职责，是恢复社会交往能力，使符号活动能力受损的患者重新能够充分或足够地与他人交流。履行这一职责时，治疗师面对的是一些符号；他受过训练，能够将它们辨认为症状，其形式是没有明显器质性原因的身体功能障碍，或者强迫行为、令人不安的梦、严重抑制、非理性恐惧等等行为功能障碍。这些就是期望分析师为之找出内容的表达。在患者呈现的形式中，症状要么没有内容，要么被赋予某种令精神分析师不满意的内容；分析师相信它们的“真实”内容不同于表面呈现的内容。无论如何，这就是弗洛伊德的模型，从任何符号学观点来看，它都极为重要。弗洛伊德派分析师的任务，是找回被患者成功“压抑”的内容；这一模式令人强烈地联想到巴特的工作：《神话学》主要处理的是意识形态症状，在训练有素的解释者出现以前，它们一直潜藏着。

The psychotherapist’s role is to restore sociability and restore semically incapacitated patients to a full or sufficient communicability with others. The therapist is faced, in performing this role, with signs which he has been trained to identify as symptoms, in the form of bodily malfunctions without apparent organic cause or behavioural malfunctions such as compulsions, disturbing dreams, severe inhibitions, irrational phobias and so on. These are the expressions for which the analyst is expected to locate the content. As presented by the patient, the symptoms are either without content or have been given a content with which the psychoanalyst will be dissatisfied, believing that their ‘real’ content is other than what it appears to be. This, anyway, is the Freudian model, which is of the greatest moment from any semiotic viewpoint. The task of the Freudian analyst is to recover contents that have been successfully ‘repressed’ by the patient, in a pattern strongly reminiscent of that to be found in the work of Barthes, who in Mythologies is dealing very much with ideological symptoms which remain latent until the trained interpreter of them appears.

因此，弗洛伊德派精神分析师正是符号学家的典范。弗洛伊德本人描述自己发展的方法时说，在早期，“精神分析首先是一门解释的艺术”。27它一直如此，尽管弗洛伊德曾作出转变，让解释症状的责任从医生转移到患者；分析师这样邀请患者去“体验”其症状的“真实”意义，希望由此达到治愈。这种观念也与莫里斯等乐观的符号学家所持的观念十分接近：准确的符号衍义能带来健康，首先是个体的健康，进而是整个社会的健康。

The Freudian psychoanalyst is thus the very model of a semiotician. Freud’s own description of the method which he evolved was that, in its early days, ‘Psychoanalysis was above all an art of interpretation’.27 It has remained so, despite the shift which Freud introduced whereby the responsibility for the interpretation of symptoms passed from doctor to patient; by thus inviting the patient to ‘experience’ the ‘true’ meaning of his symptoms the analyst hopes to achieve a cure. This notion, again, is very close to that held by buoyant semioticians such as Morris, that in accurate semiosis there is health, for individuals in the first instance and hence for society as a whole.

用符号学的术语来说，弗洛伊德让人们承认某些此前会被视为琐碎甚至毫无意义的现象也是符号，从而极大地扩展了人类生活中公认的意义范围。笑话、口误或笔误，以及梦，是一种众所周知的弗洛伊德式分析或“重读”的主要材料；它们全都被赋予一种新的意义，尽管这种意义常常有争议。符号学或结构主义关注的，不是弗洛伊德主义所擅长的解释的性质，而是它产生这些解释所用的方法。这些方法的一个前提是：梦或遗忘行为等“症状”，不能作为孤立的实体来解释，而应作为某一心理系统内部的事件来解释；在弗洛伊德思想中，这个系统各部分之间的关系至关重要。弗洛伊德的人类心理模型不能被指责为原子论，因为他如此经常地强调它是“地形学的”、动态的，甚至是“经济学的”；这些隐喻全都凸显他的一项根本前提，即我们每个人内部都存在心理结构。旧有的自我观念，把自我当作在自己的身体居所内绝对当家作主的统一原则或力量；在弗洛伊德把心理划分为自我、本我和超我之后，这种观念已很难恢复元气。这三个要素显然构成一个结构，因为它们不能彼此独立地被设想，而是由连接它们的关系构成。它们在任何个体内部各自的“价值”不断变动，心理“健康”被认为取决于它们能否保持某种平衡。另一方面，这个特定结构中的三个要素或“项”，通常被看作具有等级差异，而非平等：我们倾向于把自我看作高于另外两项的一项，因而认为我们的心理结构具有“中心”。我们还没有准备好迎接一种真正结构主义式的平等主义的涌入；那会使我们的自我彻底“去中心化”。

In semiotic terms Freud extended the area of recognized significance in human life quite dramatically, by bringing people to admit as signs phenomena which would previously have been thought trivial or even meaningless. The jokes, slips of the tongue or pen, and dreams which are the staple of a well-known kind of Freudian analysis or ‘re-reading’ have all been charged with a new, if often debatable significance. What is of concern to Semiotics, or to Structuralism, is not the nature of the interpretations which Freudianism specializes in but the methods it employs to produce them. A premise of these methods is that such ‘symptoms’ as a dream, or an act of forgetfulness, are not interpretable as isolated entities but as events within a certain psychic system, the relations between whose parts are of the essence to Freudian thought. Freud’s model of the human psyche cannot be accused of being atomistic, when he stressed so often that it was ‘topographical’, dynamic or even ‘economic’, metaphors all of them bringing out his underlying premise of a psychic structure existing in each one of us. The old idea of the Self as a unitary principle or force that is absolute master in its own bodily house has found it hard to recover from Freud’s tripartite division of the psyche into Ego, Id and Superego; these three elements would certainly seem to form a structure since they are unthinkable in independence one of the others and are constituted by the relations which join them. Their respective ‘values’ within any individual shift constantly, and psychic ‘health’ is taken to depend on their preserving a certain equilibrium. On the other hand, the three elements or ‘terms’ of this particular structure are normally seen hierarchically, not as equals: it is our inclination to look on the Ego as a term superior to the other two, and our psychic structure, therefore, as having a ‘centre’. We are not prepared for a truly Structuralist access of egalitarianism, which would ‘de-centre’ our Selves altogether.

无论在专业领域还是大众领域，弗洛伊德主义之所以兴盛，是因为它善于把出人意料的内容与貌似无辜的表达联系起来，从而产生震撼效果。压抑这个概念给符号衍义带来一种戏剧性、同时也是动态的要素。正是压抑扭曲了健康的符号功能，把平凡的符号变成谜题。精神分析师知道、或自称知道，如何为我们正确解开这些谜题，因而同时带来理解与精神健全。《梦的解析》是本世纪在符号衍义方法方面的杰作之一；在该书中，弗洛伊德用近似语言学的术语描述分析师的职责：

If Freudianism, both professional and popular, has flourished, this is because of the shock effects it is well capable of producing in associating unexpected contents with seemingly innocent expressions. A dramatic and also dynamic element is brought into semiosis by the notion of repression. Repression it is which distorts the healthy semic function and which converts banal signs into puzzles. The psychoanalyst, who knows or claims to know how correctly to solve these puzzles for us, brings both understanding and sanity in one. In The Interpretation of Dreams, which is one of this century’s masterworks in the methods of semiosis, Freud describes the analyst’s role in quasi-linguistic terms:

这样，我们面前出现了一个此前并不存在的新任务：即考察梦的显在内容与潜在梦思之间的关系，并追溯后者转化成前者的过程。

We are thus presented with a new task which had no previous existence: the task, that is, of investigating the relations between the manifest content of dreams and the latent dream-thoughts, and of tracing out the process by which the latter have been changed into the former.

梦思和梦的内容呈现在我们面前，仿佛是用两种不同语言写成的同一主题的两个版本。或者，更恰当地说，梦的内容仿佛是把梦思转写为另一种表达方式；我们的工作，就是通过比较原文和译文，发现这种方式的字符和句法法则。28

The dream-thoughts and the dream-content are presented to us like two versions of the same subject-matter in two different languages. Or, more properly, the dream-content seems like a transcript of the dream-thoughts into another mode of expression, whose characters and syntactic laws it is our business to discover by comparing the original and the translation.28

弗洛伊德主义是最具诱惑力的符号衍义，因为它为那些否则可能仍是令人痛苦的心理谜团提供治疗性的解决办法；但它同时也最具强制性，因为弗洛伊德式解释阐明的潜在内容，似乎很少允许争论。不过，从任何结构主义观点看，无论弗洛伊德主义在治疗意义上成功与否，其具体解释可信与否，它都很重要，因为它根本上依赖于语言在我们生活中的功能，尤其是任何这种符号系统都必然对完整的自我表达施加的限制。它彻头彻尾是语言性的，因为它建立在患者与医生交换话语的基础上，也就是精神分析师在较为乐观时可能称作“谈话疗法”的东西。

Freudianism is semiosis at its most seductive, inasmuch as it offers us therapeutic solutions to what might otherwise remain distressing psychic mysteries, but also at its most coercive, since the latent contents elucidated by Freudian interpretation seldom seem open to argument. But from any Structuralist point of view, Freudianism, be it successful in therapeutic terms or not, and plausible in its specific interpretations or not, is of importance because of the essential reliance which it places on the function of language in our lives, and more especially on the restrictions on complete self-expression which any such sign- system necessarily imposes. It is linguistic through and through because it is founded on the exchange of words between patient and doctor, or what in their more optimistic moments psychoanalysts may refer to as ‘the talking cure’.

最充分揭示弗洛伊德思想与方法对语言的依赖，以及弗洛伊德主义与结构主义之间汇合关系的精神分析师，是法国人雅克·拉康，一个转行做分析师的超现实主义诗人。拉康把自己看作这样的人：清除弗洛伊德主义中错误的附加物和误读，回归纯粹的学说。但不幸的是，他用来执行这个计划的散文，风格如此炫目、句法如此奇特，几乎无人能轻松读懂，许多人根本无法读懂。结果，拉康自己的形象过分强烈地横亘在弗洛伊德与那些希望从拉康那里获得弗洛伊德著作忠实解说的人之间。然而，正是通过拉康，弗洛伊德的思潮最终流入了结构主义；或者，如果愿意，也可以说，结构主义的思潮流入了弗洛伊德主义。

The psychoanalyst who has done most to bring out the dependence of Freud’s thought and method on language, and the convergence between Freudianism and Structuralism, is the Frenchman Jacques Lacan, a Surrealist poet turned analyst. Lacan saw himself as the man who would purge Freudianism of false accretions and misreadings and return to the pure doctrine, but unfortunately he carried out this programme in a prose of such flamboyance and syntactic oddity that few can follow it with ease and many cannot follow it at all. The consequence has been that the figure of Lacan himself interposes over-strongly between Freud and those looking to Lacan for a faithful account of Freud’s writings. However, it is through Lacan that the Freudian current has flowed once and for all into Structuralism or, if one prefers, that the Structuralist current has flowed into Freudianism.

拉康的著述和教学的总体效果，是比以往任何时候都更强烈地肯定精神分析的语言基础。许多人怀疑“谈话疗法”是否真能治病，但没有人——尤其在拉康之后——会怀疑它是否善于谈话。拉康既研究索绪尔，也研究弗洛伊德，因而坚持把言语行为或其他符号行为的显在平面与潜在平面看作相互连续的，并认为两者都像语言一样被结构化。例如，我们或许倾向于认为，弗洛伊德的“梦思”处在语言之外或超越语言，尽管这等于认为它们无法交流。但在拉康看来，凡进入患者与分析师之间交流的东西，都参与他所称的“象征秩序”；其符号学理由十分充分：没有象征符号（符号），就不能有交流。患者与分析师，像共享一个符号系统的任何其他两个人一样，在象征秩序内相遇，尽管他们交换的象征符号并未穷尽其心理的内容：没有交流出来的、或不可言说的东西，仍作为无法获准进入“象征秩序”的东西存在，却是个体独有的心理财产。这个不可表达的领域，不能等同于无意识，因为拉康的无意识能够得到表达；它要么是患者与分析师的交流中缺失的东西，要么是以扭曲形式进入交流的东西：“无意识是我的故事中标有空白、或被谎言占据的那一章：它是被审查的那一章。但真相能够重新被发现；它往往已经写在别处了。”29

The overall effect of Lacan’s writings and teachings has been to assert more strongly than ever before the linguistic basis of psychoanalysis. There are many who doubt that ‘the talking cure’ is really curative, but there can be none, especially since Lacan, who doubt that it can be talkative. As a student of Saussure as well as of Freud, Lacan is committed to seeing the manifest and latent planes of verbal or other sign-behaviour as continuous with each other, and both as being structured like a language. We may be tempted to think of Freud’s ‘dream-thoughts’, for instance, as lying outside or beyond language, even though that would be to deem them incommunicable. But whatever enters into the exchange between patient and analyst participates for Lacan in what he calls the ‘symbolic order’, for the good semiotic reason that without symbols (signs) there can be no communication. Patient and analyst meet, like any two other individuals who share a sign-system, within the symbolic order, even though the symbols they exchange do not exhaust the contents of their psyche: the uncommunicated or ineffable remains as what cannot find admission to the ‘symbolic order’ but is the unique psychic possession of the individual. This inexpressible realm is not to be identified with the unconscious, because the Lacanian unconscious can be brought to expression; it is that which is either missing from the exchange between patient and analyst or that which enters the exchange in a perverted form: ‘The unconscious is that chapter of my story which is marked by a blank or occupied by a falsehood: it is the censored chapter. But the truth can be rediscovered; most often it is already written elsewhere.’29

这个“别处”会是患者行为的另一个部分；拉康所谓“写”，意思是能由警觉的分析师成功地加以解释。仿佛一个人的行为构成了一个“文本”，分析师的工作便是让这个“文本”连贯起来，尽管它有种种空缺、不合逻辑之处等等。

This ‘elsewhere’ will be some other part of the patient’s behaviour and by ‘written’ Lacan means open to successful interpretation by the vigilant analyst. It is as if a person’s acts constituted a ‘text’ and the analyst’s job was to render that ‘text’ coherent, despite its various gaps, illogicalities and so on.

拉康明确使用了索绪尔的语言符号模型，以及它对能指和所指的划分。在《普通语言学教程》中，能指与所指的关系用一个图式来表示：30

Lacan makes explicit use of Saussure’s model of linguistic sign and its division into a signifier and a signified. In the Course in General Linguistics the relation of signifier to signified is given in the form of a diagram:30

这个简单却关键的图式，表示了本章中我称作“符号功能”的东西；拉康把它称作索绪尔的“算法”。但拉康故意歪曲了索绪尔的图式。索绪尔把所指放在能指之上，或者说把内容放在表达之上，拉康则反其道而行之。他把“算法”重新设计为：Ss。这里，上面较大的S代表能指，下面较小的s代表所指。因此，拉康的图式版本赋予能指比所指更高的地位，并以此指责索绪尔及其追随者所假定的唯心主义；当然，前提是我们接受这样的解释：索绪尔这样绘制图式，是想让我们认为内容在某种意义上先于其表达。（这个解释依赖于我们的假定，即分隔线上方的符号“高于”下方符号，不仅仅是在位置意义上。）相反，拉康决心消除“能指应履行表现所指的功能这一幻觉”。31他反复阐述并加以发展的弗洛伊德学说认为：能指与所指之间首先存在“滑动”，而索绪尔图式中分隔它们的“横杠”本身具有功能，因为每当符号功能出现某种“病态”障碍时，这道横杠就可以被看作代表无意识施行的审查。审查把能指与它们的“真实”所指拆开，使能指可以仿佛以私人的方式，而非以通常的公共功能发挥作用。这仿佛是表达与内容两个平面被一种离合器装置分隔开来，让前者获得相对于后者有限却极为重要的独立性。拉康从弗洛伊德以及弗洛伊德关于将“梦思”“转写”为“梦的内容”的发现中，获得这一观点的权威依据。弗洛伊德对一者如何转变为另一者的解释，极大地依赖他称作“凝缩”和“移置”的“句法”操作。当分析表明，显在的“梦的内容”中某个单一能指或表达，在潜在的“梦思”中具有多重所指或内容时，就发生了“凝缩”（这种不成比例的关系通常称作“过度决定”）；而当分析师认定为某个显在表达的“真实”内容的东西，被发现在这种表达序列中不属于它的正当位置，也就是潜伏在一个虚假的能指背后时，就发生了“移置”。拉康相信，此类发现让释梦者重新分配表达与内容，从而证明他的“字面主义”，以及他把能指提升到优先于所指的地位，都是有根据的。因为能指是分析师唯一可依凭的东西，它是实际在场的东西，形式可能是患者的话语，也可能是某项其他行为。在这些“病态”的实例中，该表达的内容，并不是由患者、分析师以及符号共同体的所有其他成员共有的代码预先决定的；不过，当然，它可能由弗洛伊德派为处理这类符号功能障碍而采用的次级代码预先决定。

This simple but crucial representation of what in this chapter I have called the ‘semiotic function’ is described by Lacan as the Saussurean ‘algorithm’. But Lacan deliberately misrepresents Saussure’s diagram. Where Saussure has placed the signified above the signifier, or the content above the expression, Lacan does the opposite. He redesigns the ‘algorithm’ thus: Ss. Here the upper and larger of the two Ss stands for the signifier and the lower and smaller for the signified. Lacan’s version of the diagram, therefore, sets a higher store by the signifier than by the signified, and in so doing reproaches Saussure and his followers for their presumed idealism; if we accept, that is, that by drawing his diagram as he did Saussure intended us to think that the content somehow preceded its expression. (This interpretation depends on our assuming that the symbol which appears above the dividing-line is ‘superior’ to the lower one in more than merely topological terms). Lacan, on the contrary, is set on removing ‘the illusion that the signifier answers to the function of representing the signified’.31 The Freudian doctrine, as he repeats and elaborates it, is that there is above all ‘slippage’ between signifiers and signifieds, and that the ‘bar’ which separates them in Saussure’s diagram is itself functional, since whenever there is some ‘pathic’ disturbance of the semiotic function the bar can be taken as representing the censorship exercised by the unconscious. The censorship divorces signifiers from their ‘true’ signifieds and frees them to function as it were privately rather than in their usual public function. It is as if the planes of expression and content were separated by a clutch mechanism which grants the first a limited but highly significant independence from the second. Lacan’s authority for this view he gets from Freud and from Freud’s discoveries concerning the ‘transcription’ of ‘dream-thoughts’ into ‘dream-content’. Freud’s explanation of how one is turned into the other depends greatly on the ‘syntactical’ operations that he calls ‘condensation’ and ‘displacement’. ‘Condensation’ occurs whenever a single signifer or expression in the manifest ‘dream-content’ is shown by analysis to have multiple signifieds or contents in the latent ‘dream-thoughts’ (this disproportion is often known as ‘over-determination’); and ‘displacement’ occurs whenever what the analyst believes to be the ‘true’ content of a manifest expression is found in other than its rightful place in the sequence of such expressions, lurking, that is, behind a false signifier. It is Lacan’s belief that discoveries of this sort, which involve the dream analyst in a redistribution of expressions and contents, justify him in his ‘literalism’ and in his elevating the signifier to a precedence over the signified. For the signifier is all that the analyst has to go on, it is what is actually there, either in the form of the patient’s words or of some other item of behaviour. The content of that expression is not pre-determined, in these ‘pathic’ instances, by the code common to patient and analyst and to all other members of the sign-community; though it may, of course, be pre-determined by the subcode adopted by Freudians to deal with such semiotic malfunctions.

精神分析实践给符号学带来的重要教益是：我们所有人，不管是否属于“病态”病例，都很容易偏离作为社会成员所继承和使用的代码。精神分析试图修复造成痛苦的极端偏离；符号学则可以只说明偏离如何起作用，以及它最终如何依赖语言内容平面的“百科全书”性质。符号学非但不应具有强制性，反而应当主张自由，指出至少语言符号所能容许的解释具有无限丰富性；同时也指出拉康那富有其典型悖论色彩的真理：“恰恰因为我的语言为我和其他主体所共有……我才有可能用它来意指与它所说的完全不同的东西。”32也就是说，我们能够撒谎、虚构和欺骗（既欺骗别人，也欺骗自己），因为我们语言的表达平面极为节约，因而含混。这一阴暗的想法，十分适合放在这里，作为通往下一章的过渡；下一章将讨论结构主义和符号学在文学研究中的应用。

The important lesson for Semiotics to come from the practice of psycho-analysis is of the ease with which we can all, ‘pathic’ cases or not, deviate from the codes we inherit and use as members of our society. Psychoanalysis attempts to repair extreme cases of such deviance, where it has caused unhappiness; semiotics can limit itself to showing how deviance works and how it depends ultimately on the ‘encyclopedic’ nature of the content plane of language. Far from being coercive, Semiotics should be libertarian, in pointing to the infinite richness of interpretation which linguistic signs at least are open to; and pointing also to Lacan’s typically paradoxical truth of: ‘the possibility I have, precisely to the extent that my language is common to me and to other subjects . . . to use it to signify something quite other from what it says.’32 That is, we can lie, romance and deceive (ourselves as well as others) because the expression plane of our language is so very economical and hence ambiguous. Which dark thought is entirely suited to stand here, as the transition to a chapter on the applications of Structuralism and Semiotics to the study of Literature.

4 文学4 Literature

文学结构主义是结构主义最具挑衅性、最不受欢迎的形式。结构语言学家、人类学家和符号学家，或许不能让所有同行都同意他们，但他们遇到的分歧很少尖锐；然而，采用结构主义分析方法的文学专家，却常常遭遇艰难的处境。这是因为，许多关心文学创作和教学的人相信，结构主义理解文学文本的方式狭隘而机械，足以造成损害；它把文学降格为一种在很大程度上非个人的技巧，而不是我们通常认为的那种高级自我表达形式。结构主义确实动摇了那种天真却根深蒂固的观念，即文学像其他艺术形式一样，本质上是自我表达；因为它坚持认为，一个“自我”只有借助共同的、客观的符号系统，才能传达给另一个自我。就文学而言，作家必然求助的客观系统有两重：一是其母语本身的系统，二是叠加在其上的、带有鲜明“文学”特征的惯例系统；要写出能够被认作文学的作品，作家也必须接受后一个系统。结构主义把注意力投向位于作者与读者之间的这些文学的客观方面，纠正它所认为的文学批评思想中一种古老、根深蒂固的不平衡，即过分强调文本和作者的独特性。每个文本和作者都是独特的，但要确定这种独特性是什么，就需要理解它们所存在于其中的文学“系统”。不同文本和作者的共同之处，远比人们可能设想的多，结构主义试图揭示这一点。它寻求的是语言系统，而每一部个别的文学作品都是这一系统的言语。

Literary Structuralism is Structuralism in its most provocative and unpopular form. Structural linguists and anthropologists, and semioticians, may not carry all their colleagues with them but the disagreements they meet with are seldom bitter; but those specialists in literature who have adopted Structuralist ways of analysis have often had a hard time of it. This is because there are many concerned with the making and teaching of literature who believe that Structuralism is a damagingly narrow and mechanistic way of understanding literary texts, that it reduces literature to a largely impersonal technique instead of the superior form of self-expression we ordinarily hold it to be. Certainly Structuralism undermines the naïve but endemic view that literature, like other art-forms, is essentially self-expression, because of its insistence that one ‘self’ may be mediated to another only by means of a common, objective system of signs. In the case of literature, the objective system which authors necessarily have recourse to is two-fold: there is the system of their native language itself and the system, overlaid on that, of characteristically ‘litrary’ conventions to which they must also accede if they are to achieve recognizably literary works. By giving its attention to these objective aspects of literature, situated between author and reader, Structuralism corrects what it sees as an old, deep-rooted imbalance in literary-critical thought which emphasizes the uniqueness of both texts and authors. Each text and author is unique, but in order to decide what this uniqueness is one needs to understand the literary ‘system’ within which they exist. Different texts and authors have much more in common than may be supposed, and Structuralism tries to bring this out. It is in search of the langue of which each individual literary work is the parole.

这似乎是一个温和的计划，并无冒犯任何人的意图。结构主义尤其应当能够与邻居和平共处，因为在真正的结构主义者看来，所有不同的文学批评方式构成一个单一系统，其中任何一种方式的“价值”，都由它与其他方式的关系决定。文学领域对结构主义的敌意，一部分源于某些结构主义者试图论证其立场时那种咄咄逼人、晦涩故弄玄虚的方式，另一部分源于许多非结构主义者的恐惧：他们认为结构主义的胜利将意味着文学中“人文主义”的死亡。这种恐惧毫无根据。结构主义打算做的，仅仅是确立主体性必须在其中发挥作用的界限。

This would seem a modest programme, not calculated to give offence to anybody. Structuralism above all should be able to live in peace with its neighbours because to a true Structuralist all the alternative modes of conducting literary criticism form a single system and the ‘value’ of any one of them is determined by its relations with the others. That there has been antagonism towards Structuralism in the literary field has been due partly to the aggressive and obscurantist way in which some Structuralists have tried to make their case, and partly to the fear felt by many who are not Structuralists that the triumph of Structuralism would be the death of ‘humanism’ in literature. This fear is unfounded. All that Structuralism proposes to do is to establish the limits within which subjectivity must work.

文学结构主义并不像反对者声称的那样新奇。毋宁说，它是自亚里士多德以来就存在的一种文学批评形式所达到的最新、格外精密的阶段。正如亚里士多德在《诗学》中着手把古希腊实践中的悲剧体裁加以规范化，今天的任何文学结构主义者也希望辨明塑造整个文学实践的众多规则与惯例。他寻找的是系统，而个别文本是这个系统的“事件”。这个过程是抽象的过程，也是文本之间比较的过程，因为只有比较那些被认为属于同一系统的文本，才能确定其不变的要素。事实上，如果“诗学”一词没有仅适用于诗歌的暗示，结构主义完全可以自称诗学。当代结构主义超越了亚里士多德式的诗学，但它的出发点是想知道文学如何制作而成。作为读者，我们若了解一些文学遵循（或打破）的规则，并认识到写作者要有意义地写作，就必须在何种程度上服从文学系统，都会从中受益。任何文学教育中，都有、或应当有一定程度的结构主义。

Literary Structuralism is not the novelty which its opponents claim it to be. It is, rather, the latest, unusually sophisticated stage reached by a form of literary criticism that has existed since Aristotle. Just as Aristotle, in his Poetics, set out to codify the literary genre of tragedy as it was practised in Ancient Greece, so any literary Structuralist today wants to identify the very many rules and conventions informing the practice of literature in general. He seeks the system of which individual texts are the ‘events’. The process is one of abstraction and of comparison between texts, since it is only by comparing texts perceived as belonging to the one system that their invariant elements can be determined. Structuralism might well call itself Poetics, indeed, if it were not for the implication that that term applies only to poetry. Contemporary Structuralism goes beyond the sort of Poetics one finds in Aristotle, but it starts from the desire to know how literature is made. We are all better off, as readers, for knowing something of the rules which literature follows (or breaks) and for recognizing to what extent those who write must yield to the literary system if they are to do so meaningfully. A certain amount of Structuralism is, or should be, a part of any literary education.

即使诗学有悠久的历史，它也是在本世纪才作为一种文学研究走到前台；要恰当地说明文学结构主义，就需要从首次协调一致地把注意力重新引向文学结构方面的努力说起。大约1915年至1930年间，俄国形式主义者进行了这番努力。1917年俄国革命爆发以前，形式主义者已经开始阐发自己的思想；不过，他们尤其兴盛于20年代的革命氛围中，当时人们认为，无论在文学思想还是其他思想中，推翻所有旧的正统观念都是正确而令人兴奋的。后来，形式主义失去了支配俄国思想生活的强硬马克思主义者的支持，陷入危险，几乎从公众视野中消失。然而，其追随者在斯大林的清洗中活了下来；20世纪60年代及其后，俄国形式主义以一种更明确的符号学形式重新出现。它与西方结构主义的联系并不密切，但两者的思想存在明显的汇合。

Even if Poetics has a long history, it is in the present century that it has come to the fore as a literary study, and a proper account of literary Structuralism needs to begin with the first concerted effort at redirecting attention to the structural aspects of literature. This was made between about 1915 and 1930 by the Russian Formalists. The Formalists had begun to elaborate their ideas before the Russian Revolution broke out in 1917, but they flourished especially in the revolutionary atmosphere of the 1920s, when it was thought right and exciting to overturn all the old orthodoxies, in literary as in other thought. Later, Formalism went dangerously out of favour with the hardline Marxists who dominated intellectual life in Russia and it more or less disappeared from public view. Its followers, however, survived Stalin’s purges, and Russian Formalism reappeared, in a more explicitly semiotic form, in the 1960s and after; its links with Western Structuralism have not been close but there is a strong convergence of ideas between the two.

形式主义，像从它衍生的结构主义一样，并非文学研究者偶尔可选的立场，而是一种恒久的选择：可以说，它是文学意识的理论一极。俄国形式主义者专注于文学的技巧和惯例，是因为该国先前的批评家忽略了这些东西。形式主义者所取代的“象征主义者”和“唯美主义者”，关心的是文学的“信息”或“美”。在形式主义看来，这些都是死去的术语，它因而极其有力地加以反抗。形式主义者年轻而反传统，正如许多结构主义者一样；他们的主张，是摆脱长辈中盛行的温文尔雅、过于个人化的批评风格，代之以强硬得多、客观得多的东西。他们相信，这会让自己与时代合拍。走到极端，形式主义就可能显得像一种文学“科学”，提出不容争论的公理。但这种科学主义在珍视解释自由的文学界从来不受欢迎——而文学界珍视这种自由是正确的。形式主义常常走得太远，因为它具有论战性；倘若没有走得太远，它也许就走得不够远。对结构主义也可以作同样的判断。

Formalism, like the Structuralism that descends from it, represents not an occasional but a permanent option for the student of literature: it is the theoretical pole of the literary consciousness, one might say. The Formalists in Russia were preoccupied with the techniques and conventions of literature because their critical predecessors in that country had ignored them. The ‘Symbolists’ and ‘Aesthetes’ from whom the Formalists took over had worried about literature’s ‘message’ or about its ‘beauty’. To Formalism these were dead terms, against which it reacted with great vigour. The Formalists were young and iconoclastic, as many Structuralists have been, and what they proposed was to break away from the genteel, over-personal styles of criticism prevalent among their elders and to replace these with something much tougher and more objective. This, they believed, would put them in tune with the times. Taken to extremes, Formalism can come to look like a ‘science’ of literature, propounding axioms that are beyond argument. But such scientism is never welcome in literary circles which prize – and rightly – freedom of interpretation. Formalism often went too far, because it was polemical; if it hadn’t gone too far, it might not have gone far enough. And the same can be said of Structuralism.

形式主义的出发点，是坚持把文学当作文学来研究，而不是当作其他某门学科的分支——例如心理学或社会学的分支。作家可能而且确实会作心理学或社会学的探讨，但那是旁枝问题，并非文学的本质。形式主义极其重视文学的“特殊性”，也就是文学文本的独有特征；发现这些特征并将其分类，是批评家的工作。用重要的形式主义者B. M. 艾亨鲍姆的话说：“我们的特点，是努力依据文学材料的特殊性质，建立一门自主的文学研究学科。”1把文学看作一个具有自身法则的自主领域，这一观念与索绪尔的语言观极为接近（尽管形式主义者至少在早期全然不了解索绪尔的工作）。

The starting-point for Formalism is the insistence that literature be studied as literature and not as a branch of some other discipline – of psychology, say, or sociology. Authors may and do psychologize and sociologize, but that is a side-issue: it is not of the essence of literature. Formalism lays immense store by the ‘specificity’ of literature, or those peculiar features of literary texts which it was the critic’s job to discover and to classify. In the words of a leading Formalist, B. M. Eichenbaum: ‘What does characterise us is the endeavour to create an autonomous discipline of literary studies based on the specific properties of literary material.’1 This notion, of literature forming an autonomous field, with its own laws, echoes closely the Saussurean view of language (though Saussure’s work was quite unknown to the Formalists, at least in the early days).

俄国形式主义从一开始就把文学研究与语言研究紧密联系起来。一些形式主义者，尤其是罗曼·雅各布森，研究语文学，在语言学方面已经极为博学。他们把文学看作语言的一种特定运用，谴责先前那些批评家：后者谈论文学问题时，仿佛语言本身是透明而无足轻重的，因为只有超越语言的“信息”才重要。一些形式主义者与当时俄国在技巧上最前卫的作家友好交往，即未来主义者以及所谓“超意义”或“超理性”诗人。“超意义”诗歌是诗人只关注诗句的声音与其他形式特征的诗歌，结果，其意义要么晦涩，要么在最坏（或最好？）的情况下根本不存在。雅各布森推崇的“超意义”诗人赫列勃尼科夫，在他眼中胜过本世纪所有其他俄国诗人；赫列勃尼科夫认为自己的职责，是操心能指，让所指自行料理自己。这种技巧的理想产物，是悦耳的无意义之言，或者，用更好听的称呼，是“语言音乐”。诗人十分明显地成了其工作媒介的仆人，而不是显得让媒介服从他的命令。再往前一步，这种技巧就变成超现实主义者的“自动写作”：他们放弃通常意义上的一切创作特权，让自己的写作由潜意识“口授”。

From its beginnings Russian Formalism brought the study of literature close together with the study of language. Some Formalists, Roman Jakobson notably, were students of philology and already extremely erudite in linguistic terms. They saw literature as a particular use of language and condemned earlier critics who had written of literary matters as if the language itself were transparent and negligible, because only the transcendent ‘message’ counted. Some of the Formalists were friendly with the most technically progressive writers of the day in Russia, with the Futurists and the so-called ‘trans-sense’ or ‘transrational’ poets. ‘Trans-sense’ poetry was poetry in which the poet attended solely to the sounds and the other formal features of his verses, with the result that their meanings were either obscure or, at worst (or best?) non-existent. Khlebnikov, the ‘trans-sense’ poet whom Jakobson esteemed above all other Russian poets of this century, saw his role as being to worry himself about the signifiers and let the signifieds take care of themselves. Euphonious nonsense is the ideal product of such a technique, or ‘verbal music’ as it can more flatteringly be called. The poet becomes very conspicuously the servant of the medium in which he is working rather than appearing to make it do his bidding. Taken a step further, the technique turns into the ‘automatic writing’ of the Surrealists, who abandon all creative prerogatives, as normally understood, and allow their writing to be ‘dictated’ by their subconscious.

赫列勃尼科夫这样的诗歌，为另一位重要形式主义者维克托·什克洛夫斯基兴奋地称作“词语的复活”的东西作出了重大贡献。从前，人们要求读者“透过”词语看过去，现在则要求他们停下来，看着词语。如果像赫列勃尼科夫那样，通过从现有词汇中推演来创造词语，就会把读者的注意力吸引到单个词项上，并提出其意义的问题。这种技巧既是词语的“复活”，也揭示词语的“物质性”；这种物质性首先以其不透明性使我们感到震动。

Poetry such as Khlebnikov’s was a major contribution to what another prominent Formalist, Viktor Shklovsky, referred to excitedly as the ‘resurrection of the word’. Previously, readers had been expected to see ‘through’ the word, now they would have to pause and look at it. If you invent words, as Khlebnikov did, by extrapolation from the existing stock, you attract a reader’s attention to the individual term and pose the problem of its meaning. As much as a ‘resurrection’ of the word, such a technique reveals a ‘materialism’ of the word, which strikes us first of all by its opacity.

这种做法不只是承认文学相对于现实的自主性，而是炫示这种自主性，正如英语文学中最非凡的“超意义”成就——詹姆斯·乔伊斯的《芬尼根的守灵夜》——所炫示的那样。但作家注意力的明显焦点从所指转向能指，可能会使读者感到焦虑和怀疑。乔伊斯、赫列勃尼科夫这样的作家，被谴责为同我们玩“游戏”；他们留给我们的文本包含太多谜题，而这源于作者在语言本身的力量面前应受责备的退位。“超意义”的东西绝非没有意义；它可能像《芬尼根的守灵夜》一样，意义满得溢出来，以至批评家能够用一辈子不断从中提取更多意义。于是意义成了问题：在结构主义看来，是文学的那个问题。必须以某种方式对它加以限制。

The procedure does more than just recognize the autonomy of literature in respect of reality: it flaunts it, as it is flaunted in English literature’s most extraordinary ‘trans-sense’ achievement, James Joyce’s Finnegans Wake. But the shift from the signified to the signifier as the apparent focus of the author’s attention may lead to anxiety and suspicion on the part of the reader. Authors like Joyce and Khlebnikov are deplored for playing ‘games’ with us; the texts which they leave to us contain all too many puzzles, stemming from a culpable authorial abdication before the forces of language itself. What is ‘trans-sense’ is never without sense; like Finnegans Wake it may be full to overflowing with sense, to the point where critics can spend a lifetime extracting more and more meanings from it. Meaning thus becomes a problem: for Structuralism the problem of literature. It has somehow to be contained.

形式主义和结构主义都从文学作品意义的“内在”观点出发。也就是说，它们试图把所研究的文本之外的一切排除在考虑范围之外。然而，任何两位文学研究者都不会完全同意什么属于文本“外部”、什么不属于，因为我们乐于认为“在”文本中的许多东西，经仔细考察，原来是我们自己带入文本的。在批评或解释中，不可能有纯粹的“内在性”；但这并不妨碍这个原则有其价值。它有价值，是因为它警告我们，文本解释不要回到从前的心理学、社会学模式；对作品的“内在”解读，尽可能排除关于作者、或者作者生活与写作的社会和时代的信息，当然，除非这些信息包含在文本中。文本的意义要在文本本身之内，通过各部分之间的相互关联来确定：我强调，这些是文本的意义，不应视作我或任何读者在阅读中可以从中获得的全部意义；后者有许多是我独有的。“内在性”的要点，是说明每个文本如何形成自身的语义系统，而仔细阅读这一系统，是读者的首要责任。“内在论”批评家克制自己，不从文本事实推论独立于文本的心理学或社会学事实；那些用作者的某项传记资料来“解释”作品中某个要素的批评家，却极其普遍地这样做。文本事实的“价值”，必须依据它在特定系统中的位置，以文本的方式确定。“内在”批评要求的，不是永远取消对文本的一切外部解释的资格，而是让我们把它们看作次要的；文学事实首先是文学的，然后才是心理学的、历史学的，等等。

Both Formalism and Structuralism start from an ‘immanent’ view of the meaning of literary works. That is, they attempt to exclude from consideration whatever is external to the text or texts they are studying. No two students of literature, however, will agree on what is or is not ‘external’ to the text, since a lot of what we like to think is ‘in’ the text turns out, on close inspection, to have been brought to the text by ourselves. There can be no pure ‘immanence’ in criticism or interpretation; but this does not stop the principle being a valuable one. It is valuable because it warns us against reverting in textual interpretation to the psychological and sociological modes of old; the ‘immanent’ reading of a work seeks to exclude as far as possible information about the author or about the society and age in which he or she lived and wrote, unless of course it is contained in the text. The meanings of the text are to be determined within the text itself, by the correlation of its parts: this, I stress, are the meanings of the text, and are not to be taken as the full sum of meanings I or any reader may derive from it in reading, many of which will be unique to myself. The point of ‘immanence’ is to show how any text forms a semantic system of its own, a careful reading of which is a reader’s first responsibility. The ‘immanentist’ critic forbears to argue from textual facts to psychological or sociological ones independent of the text, as is so commonly done by those critics who ‘explain’ an element in a work by some biographical datum of its author. The ‘value’ of a textual fact has to be determined textually, according to the place it occupies within its particular system. What an ‘immanent’ criticism asks is not that we should disqualify all external interpretation of a text for ever and ever but that we should see it as secondary; literary facts are literary before they are psychological, historical, and so on.

因此，俄国形式主义的着力点，是发现文学所独有的东西：即共同构成雅各布森所称的“文学性”的那些特征。如果这种“文学性”必然是语言的一种功能，那么，就只能通过差异来发现它。如果存在专门的文学性语言运用，也就一定存在非文学性的运用，文学性运用可以同它区别开来。形式主义逐渐建立在一种二元模式上，据此，语言被分为“诗的”或文学的语言，以及“日常”语言。这个区别我们大多数人都能认出，因为我们的语言中包含一些词语和措辞，倘若在非文学语境中遇到它们，我们会很快认定它们是文学的或“书面味”的。不过，还有更多词语和措辞同样属于文学语言和日常语言；而那些文学味特别浓的词语，恰恰是讲究的作家写作时竭力避免的。这个区别绝不能是词汇或语法上的区别。

The thrust of Russian Formalism, then, was to discover what was peculiar to literature: those features which together constitute ‘literariness’ as Jakobson calls it. If this ‘literariness’ is, as it has to be, a function of language, it can be found only differentially. If there is a specifically literary use of language, there must be a non-literary use of it also, from which the literary use can be distinguished. Formalism came to be based on a binary scheme whereby language was divided into the ‘poetic’ or literary and the ‘everyday’. This is a distinction we can most of us recognize, in so far as our own language contains words and turns of phrase which we characterize promptly enough as literary or ‘bookish’ if we meet with them outside a literary context. However, there are a great many more words and turns of phrase which belong equally to the literary and the everyday language, and those which are conspicuously literary are the very ones which a fastidious author is anxious to avoid when writing. The distinction can never be one of vocabulary or grammar.

相反，必须像雅各布森所做的那样，用更宽泛的功能性术语来表述它。他认为存在六种不同的语言功能，在特定情境中，其中任何一种都可能居于优先地位。出于这里的目的，我只挑出雅各布森称作“诗性”的功能。它与其他五种功能的区别在于，它把交际行为双方——说者与听者、作者与读者——的注意力，固定在实际使用的词语上。焦点落在信息本身，为信息自身而关注它。雅各布森并不认为诗性功能可以单独运作，只是认为，在任何文学性语言运用中，它都是“主导的”（这是形式主义者的一个关键术语，也预示了结构主义，因为它意味着一个功能系统的存在，其中任何一种功能都可能暂时取得对其他功能的支配地位）。

Rather, it must be stated in wider, functional terms, as it was by Jakobson. He argues that there are six distinct linguistic functions, any one of which may have precedence in a given situation. For my purposes here I shall single out only the function which Jakobson calls the ‘poetic’. This is to be distinguished from the other five functions by the fact that it fixes the attention of the two parties to the communicative act – speaker and hearer; writer and reader – on the actual words which are being used. The focus is on the message, for its own sake. Jakobson does not suggest that the poetic function can operate alone, only that in any literary use of language it is ‘dominant’ (a key term with the Formalists and one which looks forward to Structuralism, since it entails the existence of a system of functions, any one of which may achieve temporary dominance over the rest).

语言诗性功能的效果是：我们阅读文学时，比面对语言的其他功能时，更能意识到语言本身。再引入一个形式主义者喜爱的术语，文学中的语言被“前景化”了。正如雅各布森强调的，这是文学的倾向，在诗歌中比在散文中得到更充分的认识。在日常语言运用中，把语言“前景化”很少实用，甚至可能被认为不礼貌。日常语言通常是传递信息的、工具性的；说者／作者或听者／读者，都没有必要停留在所说／所写的形式上，因为只要某条信息成功传递，或某项行动成功促成，实现这一切所用的词语就可以算是“透明的”。诗性功能带来某种不透明性，因为作者不再是在传递信息，也不在寻求促成行动。它还可能带来有意的含混；在出于日常目的使用语言时，这也是我们通常尽可能避免的，因为它很可能挫败这些目的，或者使之落空。（诗性功能也完全可能在讲话中居于主导地位，尽管听众可能判定它矫揉造作。）

The poetic function of language has as its effect that when we read literature we become more aware of language than we are when we are confronted by language in its other functions. To introduce another term dear to the Formalists, in literature language is ‘foregrounded’. This, as Jakobson stresses, is the tendency of literature, much more fully recognized in poetry than it is in prose. In the everyday use of language it will seldom be practical and may even be found impolite to ‘foreground’ language. Everyday language is usually informative and instrumental; there is no call for either speaker/writer or hearer/reader to dwell on the form of what is said/ written since if a piece of information has been successfully passed or some action successfully instigated, the words by which this has been managed can count as ‘transparent’. With the poetic function comes a certain opacity, for the writer is no longer passing information nor seeking to instigate action. There may also come an intentional ambiguity, which is something else we customarily avoid if we can in using language for our everyday purposes since it may very well defeat those purposes or cause them to miscarry. (It is quite possible for the poetic function to be dominant in speech also, though it may be adjudged pretentious by listeners.)

因此，并非只有诗歌才是“诗性的”，任何一段可以认定为文学的语言，无论长短，都是如此。散文也是“诗性的”，可以接受与诗歌中完全熟悉的那种同类结构分析：事实上，俄国形式主义的一个长处，就是十分热切地研究叙事散文，确定其构造法则——用一篇著名形式主义论文那带有典型傲慢的标题来说，就是发现《果戈理的〈外套〉是怎样写成的》，这里的“外套”是果戈理一篇小说的篇名。用形式主义的术语来说，诗歌与散文相似，只是程度更甚：两者都依赖语言的独特运用，描述这种运用是批评家的任务。然而，有时被忽略的是，具有文学性的是语言的运用，而不是语言本身。巴赫金与P. M. 梅德韦杰夫在一篇早期（1929年）而深刻的形式主义批评中，明确指出了这一点：“语言只有在具体的诗性构造中，才获得诗的特征。这些特征不属于语言作为语言的能力，而属于构造，不论其形式如何。最简单的日常话语或贴切的表达，在某些情况下也可能得到艺术性的感知。”2

It is not only poetry which is ‘poetic’, then, but any piece of language, long or short, specifiable as literary. Prose too is ‘poetic’ and open to the same kind of structural analysis that is perfectly familiar in the case of verse: it was one of the strengths of Russian Formalism, indeed, that it was so eager to investigate narrative prose and determine the laws of its construction – to find, in the typically insolent title of a famous Formalist essay, ‘How Gogol’s ‘‘Overcoat’’ is made’, the ‘Overcoat’ in question being the title of a story by Gogol. In Formalist terms poetry is like prose only more so: both rely on a distinctive use of language which it is the critic’s task to describe. It is sometimes overlooked, however, that it is the use of the language and not the language itself which is literary, a point decisively made by Bakhtin and P. M. Medvedev in an early (1929) and profound critique of Formalism when they wrote that: ‘Language acquires poetic characteristics only in the concrete poetic construction. These characteristics do not belong to language in its linguistic capacity, but to the construction, whatever its form may be. The most elementary everyday utterance or apt expression may be perceived artistically in certain circumstances.’2

这是“整体论”的表述。我希望，结构主义者比形式主义者较少会陷入巴赫金与梅德韦杰夫在此纠正的错误。是整体决定部分，而不是部分决定整体；只有在一个“诗性的”或文学的整体中，各部分才能被把握为“诗性的”。形式主义者善于辨认文学所依赖的“手法”，也就是作家能够为其审美目的而采用的技法。“手法”是文学语言运用偏离日常语言运用的手段，因为它们存在于前者而不在后者中。情节、人物塑造、对话、韵脚、节奏、隐喻：这些都是散文或诗歌中“手法”的例子，在文学史的不同时期有不同运用，或者在文学史的不同时期、或同一时期的不同作家那里有不同运用。早期形式主义一般满足于分离出“手法”，而不是考察它们在特定文本中的用途。永远热衷论战的什克洛夫斯基曾作出一项声名狼藉的宣言：“文学作品是其中运用的一切文体手法的总和。”3这番热情洋溢的表述，缺少任何这样的意识：文学作品有某种统一性，并不只是作家从现成库存中取出的一项项“手法”的堆积。

This is a statement of ‘holism’. Structuralists, I hope, are less likely than the Formalists were to fall into the error Bakhtin and Medvedev are here correcting. It is the whole which determines the parts, and not the parts the whole; only in a ‘poetic’ or literary whole can the parts be grasped as ‘poetic’. The Formalists were adept at identifying the ‘devices’ on which literature depends, the artifices, that is, able to be employed by a writer for his or her aesthetic purposes. The ‘devices’ are the means by which the literary use of language departs from the everyday, since they are found in the one and not in the other. Plot, characterization, dialogue, rhyme, rhythm, metaphor: all are examples of ‘devices’ in either prose or verse, differently used in different periods of literary history or by differently used in different periods of literary history or by different authors within the same period. In its early days Formalism was generally content to isolate ‘devices’, rather than to consider the uses they were put to in particular texts. The ever-polemical Shklovsky declared, notoriously, that: ‘A work of literature is the sum-total of all stylistic devices employed in it.’3 What was missing from this exuberant formulation was any sense that the work of literature had a certain unity, and that it was more than a mere accumulation of specific ‘devices’ taken by the author from stock.

形式主义多年来满足于这样收集文学“事实”，是可以理解的，因为以前没有人系统地做过这件事，而要刻画文学的“特殊性”，就需要这样的收集。这是扎实的基础工作。但它还不够，因为它不能解释我们作为读者的一种直觉：一部作品，无论是叙事的还是诗的，都有统一性；再次借用巴赫金与梅德韦杰夫的论证，它不仅是“制作”出来的，也是“创造”出来的。4把作品作为结构来把握，就能恢复它否则可能丧失的一体性。作为整体，它很容易与其他整体比较，这些整体可以是同一作家的不同作品、同一体裁内不同作家的作品，等等。对单一文本的结构性看法，为结构主义更广泛的应用开辟了道路，必要时可以扩展到整个文化。由此可能进行的比较，是结构与结构之间的比较，而不是一个结构的某个要素与另一个结构的某个要素之间的比较。结构主义的立场是：例如，把小说中的这个或那个要素联系到生活，没有意义；只能把该要素所属的整体联系到生活。这一立场可以用F. W. 加兰（论述布拉格结构主义时）的话概括：“总之，在每件艺术作品中，构成支配着再现……”5

It is understandable that for a number of years Formalism was content to collect literary ‘facts’ in this way, because it had not been systematically done before, and such a collection was needed if the ‘specificity’ of literature was to be characterized. This was sound spadework. But it was not enough because it fails to account for our intuition as readers that a given work, be it narrative or poetic, has unity, that, to go for a second time to the arguments of Bakhtin and Medvedev, it is not only ‘made’ but also ‘created’.4 To grasp the work as a structure restores to it the oneness it might otherwise lose. As a whole, it becomes readily comparable with other wholes, which may be different works by the same author, works by different authors within the same genre, and so on. A structural view of a single text opens the way for wider applications of Structuralism, extensible if need be to whole cultures. The comparisons made possible are between structure and structure, not between one element from one structure and one element from another. The Structuralist position is that it is no good relating this element or that of a fiction, say, to life, but only the whole to which the element belongs, a position that may be generalized in the words of F. W. Galan (writing about Prague Structuralism) that ‘In every work of art, in sum, composition holds sway over representation . . .’5

如果没有形式主义者先期的努力，揭示文学艺术完全的人为性，文学结构主义就不可能以目前的形式存在。顾名思义，形式主义淡化了内容在文学中的重要性，因为它认为先前批评家给了内容过高的地位。许多形式主义批评走得过头，主张文学内容只是为形式而存在，而非相反；主张作家的目标，是以前所未有的方式运用“手法”，在形式上革新文学，而不是“说”出此前未曾说过的东西。形式主义者是否完全认真地提出这些观点，是可疑的；但只要他们继续持有这些观点，就可以忽略文学艺术作品的统一性问题，也可以忽略文学与其他同时代文化实践之间的关系问题。

Literary Structuralism could not exist in its present form without the preliminary endeavours of the Formalists to bring out the full artificiality of literary art. Formalism, as its name indicates, played down the importance of content in literature, because it thought that earlier critics had accorded it too great a place. Much Formalist criticism is excessive in arguing that the content of literature is only there for the sake of the form, and not the other way about; that a writer’s object is to renovate literature formally, by some unprecedented use of ‘devices’, rather than to ‘say’ something that has not been said before. It is doubtful whether the Formalists advanced such views quite seriously, but for as long as they continued to hold them it was possible to ignore any question of the unity of works of literary art or of the relation between literature and other contemporary cultural practices.

随着形式主义发展成结构主义，这些问题重新流行起来。可以说，这一发展是在20世纪20年代末至30年代逐渐发生的，很大程度上受到雅各布森，以及在布拉格围绕他形成的一批志同道合的语言学家、批评家及其他人的新群体的影响。正是他们提出并在一定程度上普及了系统的概念；这个概念很容易从语言领域扩展到审美领域：“随着形式主义进一步发展，出现了这样一种准确的观念：诗的作品是一个结构化的系统，是一个具有规则秩序的、由艺术手法组成的等级集合。”6这里包含、而在早期形式主义中大体缺失的两个关键新观念，是整合与动态性；两者都是结构主义的根本。如果诗的作品——请记住，“诗的”在这里用的是广义，指任何文学文本——现在被看作系统，那么系统各部分就必须被理解为按照有待确定的规则彼此作用；系统某一部分的改变，会在整个系统中引起连锁反应。而且，诗的作品的这个新定义，可以远远扩展到单一文本之外，涵盖一个作家的全部作品、一种体裁、一国的整个文学；它们都完全能够作为类似的动态整体来研究。从形式主义转向结构主义，使文学得以重新置入生产和阅读它的社会，并重新进入历史。鉴于人们广泛相信结构主义极力反对历史，这似乎有些奇怪；但着手将文学与其他艺术及一般文化实践整合起来，正是布拉格结构主义的重要标志。雅各布森反对索绪尔在他看来极端的共时主义，理由是纯粹共时的系统缺少动态性。他指出语言变化的动态过程：相互竞争的形式争夺语义地盘，直到其中一者让步或被转化。而布拉格结构主义逐渐把一种文化的共时系统看作以同样方式运动的动态系统；它的能量首先来自辩证性的较量，即系统中通行的公认规范，与使用系统的人想打破这些规范的愿望之间的较量。

These were questions which came back into fashion as Formalism evolved into Structuralism, a development which may be said to have happened gradually during the late 1920s and 1930s, very much under the influence of Jakobson and the new group of like-minded linguists, critics and others which had formed around him in Prague. They it was who propounded and to some extent popularized the notion of system, which was easily extended from the linguistic sphere to the aesthetic: ‘With the further development of Formalism, there arose the accurate conception of a poetic work as a structured system, a regularly ordered hierarchical set of artistic devices.’6 The two crucial new ideas included here and which were largely missing in the earlier Formalism are those of integration and dynamism, both of which are fundamental to Structuralism. If the poetic work – and ‘poetic’, remember, is being used in its wider sense to mean any literary text – is now seen as a system, then the parts of the system have to be understood as acting one upon the other, according to rules to be determined; and an alteration in one part of the system will have repercussions throughout it. Moreover, this new definition of the poetic work can be extended way beyond a single text, to include an oeuvre, a genre, a whole literature, all of which are quite able to be studied as similarly dynamic wholes. The passage from Formalism to Structuralism enables literature to be reinserted into the societies in which it is produced and read, and to re-enter history. Given the widespread belief that Structuralism is furiously opposed to history, this may seem strange; but it was very much a mark of Prague Structuralism that it set out to integrate literature with other arts and with cultural practices generally. Jakobson contested the extreme synchronism (as he saw it) of Saussure, on the grounds that a purely synchronic system lacked dynamism. He pointed to the dynamics of linguistic change, with rival forms competing for semantic territory until such time as one gives way or is transformed. And Prague Structuralism came to see the synchronic system of a culture as dynamic in the same way, its energy being provided first and foremost by a dialectical contest between the acknowledged norms prevailing in the system and the wish of those using the system to break with them.

对于雅各布森这样的语言学家，文学作品的整合或许必然仍是形式上的，并回溯到形式主义者的“手法”。在他论述文学而非语言学问题的文章中，雅各布森专长于对诗歌作详尽得令人望而生畏的语法分析，仿佛可以而且有益于根据个别诗人如何利用可供选择的语法形式，来刻画他们的作品：“关于诗篇、诗人和诗歌流派的个性及比较特征，这个根本的文学批评问题，可以而且应当在语法领域提出。”7这不是写于人们可能猜想的1921年，而是1961年。在漫长的一生中，雅各布森始终把诗歌的语义方面——他对散文即使有所论述，也寥寥无几——看作次于形式的；事实上，是从形式派生的。1958年，在一次美国大学的会议上，他作了自己观点最响亮的一次公开宣示，极为热情地赞同杰拉德·曼利·霍普金斯一个世纪前的话：诗歌的根本问题是“平行”。平行是这样一种结构原则：一首诗的某个要素被感知为与另一个要素相关，例如押韵、头韵、明喻，等等。关于这些形式上的复现，霍普金斯断言，它们的力量在于“在词语或思想中生成与之对应的复现或平行；粗略地说，与其说是必然结果，不如说是倾向：结构中无论铺陈还是强调，越显著的平行，就会在词语和意义中生成越显著的平行”。8

With a linguist like Jakobson it was perhaps inevitable that the integration of the literary work should remain a formal one, and to hark back to the ‘devices’ of the Formalists. In his own essays on literary as opposed to linguistic subjects, Jakobson has specialized in the most dauntingly thorough grammatical analyses of poetry, as if it were possible and fruitful to characterize the work of particular poets according to the use they have made of the grammatical choices open to them: ‘The essential literary-critical question of the individuality and comparative characteristics of poems, poets and poetic schools can and should be posed in the realm of grammar.’7 This was written not in 1921, as one might think, but in 1961. Jakobson continued all his long life to look on the semantic aspects of verse – he had little if anything to say about prose – as coming second to the formal; indeed, as derivative from the formal. In the most resonant public statement he ever made of his views, at an American university conference in 1958, he endorsed with great enthusiasm remarks made by Gerard Manley Hopkins a century earlier to the effect that ‘parallelism’ was the fundamental problem of poetry, parallelism being that structural principle whereby one element in a piece of verse is perceived as related to another, as with rhyme, alliteration, simile, and so on. Of these formal recurrences Hopkins asserts that their force is ‘to beget a recurrence or parallelism answering to it in the words or thought and, speaking roughly and rather for the tendency than the invariable result, the more marked parallelism in structure whether of elaboration or of emphasis begets more marked parallelism in the words and sense.’8

在分析具体诗篇时，雅各布森从未放弃“手法”优先于意义的立场。他对“语法修辞格”（这也是霍普金斯的说法）加以计数、分类和对照，人们因而认为这些分析穷尽无遗。但如果关心的是把握诗的语义结构，那么从语法分析中得出的结论却单薄而可疑。雅各布森作为结构语言学家，其方法是在语法类别内部建立对立的配对；不过，即使对立是语义的，而非纯粹形式的——例如他列举并对照一首诗中的“有生命”与“无生命”名词时——结果也不特别有说服力。对许多结构主义者而言，它们最吸引人的特点，是雅各布森处理的大部分模式化安排属于语言或系统，而不属于其所分析作品的作者。也就是说，这些模式完全可能是作者没有意识到的。可以肯定，作者大概不会意识到这样造成的结构具有对立性质。雅各布森揭示诗歌结构意想不到的严密性与规则性，令人印象深刻，有力强化了结构主义的观点：当我们作为个体服从符号系统时，必然放弃我们个性的某些部分。诗歌的悖论一直在于，它同时是最个人化、也是限制最多的文学形式；雅各布森这样的结构主义，正是围绕这个悖论展开。

This precedence of the ‘device’ over the sense is one never abandoned by Jakobson in his analyses of particular poems. These, one concludes, are exhaustive, as he counts, classifies and contrasts ‘figures of grammar’ (also Hopkins’s phrase). But the conclusions to be drawn from the grammatical analysis are thin and open to question, if one’s concern is to grasp the semantic structure of the poem. Jakobson’s method is to establish, like the structural linguist he is, opposed pairings within his grammatical classes, but even where the opposition is semantic rather than purely formal – as when he enumerates and contrasts ‘animate’ and ‘inanimate’ nouns in a poem – the results are not especially telling. Their most appealing feature for many Structuralists is that much of the patterning in which Jakobson deals belongs to language, or the system, rather than to the author whose work he is analysing. That is to say, they are patternings of which the author may well have been unconscious. Certainly, he or she is unlikely to have been conscious of the oppositional nature of the structure so created. Jakobson’s demonstrations of the unsuspected rigour and regularity of verse structures are impressive and serve to reinforce powerfully the Structuralist view that when we submit, as individuals, to sign-systems we necessarily abdicate something of our individuality. The paradox of poetry has always been that it is at once the most personal and the most constraining of literary forms, and Structuralism such as Jakobson’s centres on that paradox.

在文学散文中，这个悖论不那么明显，但仍同样存在。没有人怀疑，散文比诗歌限制少，作者的选择自由相应更大，因而散文结构比诗歌结构更松散。对于结构主义者，散文比诗歌提供更新颖、更困难的挑战；若能成功证明它究竟如何具有高度结构性，就会更加引人注目。结构主义者主要考察叙事散文，理由很充分：叙事的结构显而易见，而比如一篇文学随笔的结构，就并非如此。对柯南·道尔那些几乎全是叙事的故事进行结构研究，其前景远比研究查尔斯·兰姆那些基本没有叙事的随笔更令人鼓舞。迄今，结构主义对西方小说除叙事之外的另一主要组成部分——人物——研究甚少。文学结构主义可能被指责为只处理了最容易的东西，只关注那些在其他结构中格外醒目的结构。然而，将来有一天，虚构“人物”的语法与语义，一定会得到有效的规范化，增进我们对“人物”如何制作而成的理解，正如情节的语法与语义已经开始得到规范化一样。

The paradox is less obvious with literary prose, though it is present just the same. No one questions that prose is less constraining than verse, that the author’s freedom of choice is correspondingly greater and that prose structures are thus looser than verse ones. To a Structuralist, prose offers a fresher and harder challenge than poetry, and any successful demonstration of just how highly structured it is will be all the more dramatic. What Structuralists have examined is mainly narrative prose, for the good reason that the structure of a narrative is apparent, whereas that, say, of a literary essay is not. A structural study of the stories of Conan Doyle, which are nearly all narrative, is an altogether more encouraging prospect than one of the essays of Charles Lamb, from which narrative is largely absent. Structuralist study of the other major component of Western fiction (apart from narrative), which is character, has to date been skimpy. Literary Structuralism is open to the charge that it has tackled only what is easiest, and engaged with those structures that stand out from the rest. It must be the case, however, that the grammar and semantics of fictional ‘characters’ will one day be effectively codified, to the improvement of our understanding of how ‘characters’ are made, as those of plots have already begun to be.

当代叙事散文研究的模式，也是20世纪20年代在俄国由弗拉基米尔·普罗普确立的。1928年，他出版了一部短篇研究《俄国民间故事形态学》。他的目标既是科学的，也是结构性的：“形态学一词意指对形式的研究。在植物学中，形态学包括对植物组成部分、及其彼此之间和与整体之间关系的研究；换句话说，就是对植物结构的研究。”9

The pattern for the contemporary study of narrative prose was also set in Russia, in the 1920s, by Vladimir Propp. In 1928 he published a short study called The Morphology of the Russian Folk-tale. His aims were both scientific and structural: ‘The word morphology signifies the study of forms. In botany, morphology comprises the study of the component parts of plants, and of their relations one to another and to the whole; in other words, the study of the structure of a plant.’9

普罗普选择研究的材料，是一百个俄国民间故事，每个都包含某种“神奇”因素。故事的分类不是他自己提出的，而是民俗学家确立的，因此，不能责备他按自己的需要挑选故事。另一方面，对于普罗普进行的那种分析，民间故事确有简单易析的优势：叙事要素最为重要，事件与人物显然模式化，衔接笨拙，因为它们是“制作”而非“创造”出来的，而且是匿名的。它们在各方面都可以与列维-斯特劳斯分析的神话相比。

The material with which Propp chose to work was 100 Russian folktales all of which contained some element of the ‘marvellous’. The classification of the tales was not his own but one established by folklorists, so he cannot be faulted for choosing his stories to suit himself. Folktales, on the other hand, do have advantages of simplicity for the kind of analysis on which Propp was engaged: the narrative element is all-important, they are patently stereotyped in terms of events and characters, they are clumsily articulated, having been ‘made’ rather than ‘created’, and they are anonymous. They are in every respect comparable to the myths analysed by Lévi-Strauss.

划定研究领域，是结构主义的第一项要求；普罗普的一百个可比较的故事构成一个整体。第二项要求，是界定整体的构成单位。人们可能认为，在这里，每个民间故事代表一个单位；在一个格外乏味的分析层次上，确实如此。停留在这一层次，就忽略了每个故事极其明显的复合性质。可以用结构的术语，把这些故事作为整体彼此联系起来；但一开始更有启发的做法，是界定每个故事由哪些要素构成。

To delimit the field of study is the first requirement of Structuralism; Propp’s 100 comparable tales form a whole. The second requirement is to define the constituent units of the whole. It might be thought that in this case each folktale represents one unit, and so, at a peculiarly uninteresting level of analysis, it does. To remain at this level is to leave out of account the very strikingly composite nature of each tale. It would be possible to relate the tales one to another as wholes, in structural terms, but more instructive at the outset to define the elements of which each tale is constructed.

普罗普的方法，是把故事分割为他所称的“功能”。一般读者最可能把这些叫作“行动”，但普罗普将行动重新命名为“功能”，就已经开始了结构主义所必需的抽象与概括的工作。“功能”是被看作能够与故事集其他地方的可比较行动归为一类的行动。辨认这种相似性，有时困难，却是任何结构主义文学研究方法必不可少的一部分。结构主义依靠每个文本中大量存在的重复而兴盛；在这一意义上，雅各布森关于诗歌“平行”的原则，也可以视为散文的核心。为了说明普罗普的归类采取什么形式，我引用他对不同故事中行动的四项描述，每项都包含两个不同的“功能”：

Propp’s method is to divide the tales up into what he terms ‘functions’. These are what an ordinary reader of them would most likely refer to as ‘actions’, but by redesignating actions as ‘functions’ Propp has already begun on the necessary Structuralist task of abstraction and generalization. A ‘function’ is an action seen as lending itself to assimilation to other, comparable actions elsewhere in the corpus of tales. The spotting of such similarities is a sometimes difficult but essential part of any Structuralist approach to literature. Structuralism thrives on the repetitions in which any text abounds, and to this extent Jakobson’s principle of ‘parallelism’ in poetry may be taken as central to prose also. To make clear what form Propp’s assimilations take, I will cite four of his own descriptions of actions in different tales, each of which contains two distinct ‘functions’:

  1. 1 国王把一只鹰给了一位勇士。鹰把勇士带到另一个王国。

    1 The king gives an eagle to a brave man. The eagle carries the brave man off into another kingdom.

  2. 2 祖父把一匹马给了苏钦科。马把苏钦科带到另一个王国。

    2 The grandfather gives a horse to Soutchenko. The horse carries Soutchenko off into another kingdom.

  3. 3 魔法师把一条船给了伊万。船把伊万带到另一个王国。

    3 A magician gives a boat to Ivan. The boat carries Ivan off into another kingdom.

  4. 4 王后把一枚戒指给了伊万。壮汉从戒指里出来，把伊万带到另一个王国。10

    4 The queen gives a ring to Ivan. Strong men come out of the ring and carry Ivan off into another kingdom.10

这里，动词形式“给了”和“带到”的重复，使归类变得简单。每个编号的例子都可以辨认出普罗普的两个“功能”，一对句子中每句一个。四个第一句共有的功能，被普罗普描述为“获得魔法物品”；四个第二句共有的功能，则为“两王国之间的空间位移，在引导者陪同下旅行”。这些描述从每组四种变化中挑出不变的要素。这两个功能，实际上是包含三十一个功能的完整清单中的第十四与第十五个。凭借这些功能，可以构造所选故事集中的全部故事。这是结构主义节约原则的又一个实例，这个原则在其语言哲学中尤为突出；有限的“功能”能够生成多种多样的故事，正如语言中相对少量的音位可以构成大量词语，有限的词汇可以构成无限的句子。

The assimilations here are made simple by the repetition of the verb-forms ‘gives’ and ‘carries off’. In each of the numbered cases two of Propp’s ‘functions’ are to be identified, one in each sentence of the pair. The function common to the four first sentences is described by Propp as ‘Receipt of the magic object’, and the function common to the four second sentences as ‘Displacement in space between two kingdoms, journey with a guide’. These descriptions pick out the invariant element in each set of four variations. The two functions themselves are in fact the fourteenth and fifteenth in a complete list of thirty-one. From these it is possible to construct all of the tales of the chosen corpus. This is a further instance of the Structuralist principle of economy, so prominent in its philosophy of language; from a limited number of ‘functions’ an enormous variety of tales can be generated, just as in language a great many words can be made from relatively few phonemes, and an infinite number of sentences from a limited vocabulary.

然而，在民间故事中，“功能”的组合能力受到其他类型约束的限制。必须考虑它们的句法。在一种特定语言中，有些音位序列不会出现，使用该语言的人会认为它们不可能存在、或无法发音，即使并没有任何先天的生理原因使它们必然如此。同样，在普罗普的叙事“功能”中，许多组合会基于逻辑理由被排除。在我引用的第十四与第十五“功能”的实例中，有一个明显的逻辑限制：在实现“空间位移”的魔法手段被提供以前，不能发生这种位移。如果鹰根本没有到来，主人公就不能被带走。从逻辑上说，“功能十五”必须在“功能十四”之后。不过，在比普罗普所研究的更复杂的叙事形式中，可能偏离这种逻辑顺序；叙述者可以先介绍结果，再介绍原因，侦探故事的叙述者惯常这样做。先是被带走，然后，作为补叙，才出现鹰。这种可能性在叙事中得到极其广泛的运用，利用了叙述者摆脱真实逻辑顺序专制的自由；它在结构分析中引入一项重要区别，即叙事中事件的“实际”顺序，与提供给我们阅读的顺序之间的区别。对于大多数现代叙事，如果读者愿意，就必须重建“实际”顺序；毫无疑问，多数读者只是顺着给出的顺序读下去，不知道自己的期待正如何受到操纵。

In the case of the folktales, however, the powers of combination of the ‘functions’ are limited by other kinds of constraint. There is their syntax to be considered. In a given language some sequences of phonemes do not occur, and would be held by speakers of the language to be impossible or unpronounceable, even if there is no innate physiological reason why that should be so. Similarly, in the case of Propp’s narrative ‘functions’ many combinations will be disallowed on logical grounds. In the instance I have cited, of the fourteenth and fifteenth ‘functions’, there is an obvious logical constraint inasmuch as no ‘displacement in space’ can be effected before the magic means of it has been provided. The hero cannot be carried off if his eagle never arrives. Logically, ‘function fifteen’ must follow ‘function fourteen’. But in more sophisticated forms of narrative than the one with which Propp was concerned there may be departures from this logical sequence; a narrator may introduce effects before their causes, as the narrators of detective stories habitually do. First the carrying off, then, as an afterthought, the eagle. This possibility, which is extremely widely used in narrative, exploiting as it does a narrator’s freedom from the tyranny of real, logical sequence, introduces an important distinction in structural analysis, between the ‘actual’ sequence of events in a narrative and the sequence we are given to read. With most modern narratives the ‘actual’ sequence will have to be reconstructed, if he or she feels so inclined, by the reader; most readers no doubt follow the sequence as given without being aware of how their expectations are being manipulated.

在普罗普的民间故事中，功能出现的顺序显得格外恒定。正如人们可以预料的，没有一个故事包含全部三十一个“功能”；但所考察的一百个故事，在可能存在的故事中只占很小一部分。普罗普式分析使研究者能够自己编出民间故事，把“功能”恰当地排列成尚未实现的模式。这是我先前提过的结构主义的又一个特点：如果它成功，就具有一定的预测能力。

With Propp’s folktales the order in which functions occur proves remarkably constant. There is, as one would expect, no one tale which incorporates all thirty-one ‘functions’, but the corpus examined, of 100 tales, is a very small proportion of those which might have existed. Analysis of Propp’s kind gives the student the possibility of making up folktales of his or her own, by ordering ‘functions’ appropriately in patterns as yet unrealized. This is another feature of Structuralism to which I have alluded earlier: that it has, if it is successful, a certain predictive power.

普罗普所完成的那种结构分析，其危险也是所有形式主义的危险：分析会越来越接近代数，用希腊字母取代故事实际的构成行动，结果在寻找不变量时，看不到各种变体之间的一切区别。在这方面，普罗普更像形式主义者，而非结构主义者。他认为没有必要通过建立三十一个“功能”之间的对立，尝试将它们安排进某个系统。列维-斯特劳斯后来就此批评他；列维-斯特劳斯既受到普罗普方法的影响，也意识到其缺陷。他指出，如果普罗普的一个“功能”可以描述为“违反命令”，另一个为“禁止”，那么这两个“功能”显然是相关的，前者是后者的矛盾项；同样，描述为“禁止”的“功能”，是另一个描述为“命令”的功能的否定转换。这就是普罗普从未掌握的转换逻辑。

The danger of a structural analysis of the kind achieved by Propp is the danger of all Formalism: that the analysis will approximate more and more to an algebra, replacing the actual constituent actions of the tales with letters of the Greek alphabet, so that all distinctions between variants are lost sight of in the search for invariants. In this respect Propp is more Formalist than Structuralist. He saw no need to try and arrange his thirty-one ‘functions’ in any system, by establishing oppositions between them. For this he was eventually criticized by Lévi-Strauss, who was both influenced by Propp’s methods and aware of their shortcomings. He points out that if one of Propp’s ‘functions’ can be described as ‘violation of an injunction’ and another as ‘prohibition’, these two ‘functions’ are surely related, the first being the contradictory of the second; just as a ‘function’ described as ‘prohibition’ is the negative transformation of another described as ‘injunction’. This is the logic of transformations which Propp never grasped.

列维-斯特劳斯对美洲印第安神话的分析，与普罗普对民间故事的分析走的是类似的路线；但形式主义者满足于记录“功能”的分布，结构主义者则把它们彼此对立起来，整合进一个系统。列维-斯特劳斯认识到，神话中的这些对立，比民间故事中的强得多：前者处理宇宙论的、形而上学的或自然的问题，后者则通常限于地方性的、社会的和道德的范围。因此，列维-斯特劳斯认为民间故事是介于神话与文学之间的形式，因为文学中可以发现的对立更弱。他本人十分注重节约，因此责怪普罗普最终得出三十一个组成性的“功能”，有些浪费；通过确定成对“功能”之间的对立模式，完全可以减少总数。他把神话分析为构成性的“神话素”，其分析的节约程度远远超过普罗普，体现了结构主义者整合资料的追求。11

The analyses which Lévi-Strauss has made of American-Indian myths follow a similar course to Propp’s analysis of folktales, but where the Formalist is content to register the distribution of ‘functions’, the Structuralist integrates them into a system, by opposing them one to another. Lévi-Strauss recognizes that these oppositions are much stronger in myths than they are in folktales, that where the first treat of matters that are cosmological, metaphysical or natural, the second are usually limited to the local, the social and the moral. For this reason, Lévi-Strauss suggests that folktales are an intermediate form between myth and literature, since the oppositions to be found in the latter are weaker still. His own sense of economy is such that he faults Propp for a certain profligacy in ending up with thirty-one component ‘functions’ when, by determining the pattern of oppositions between pairs of ‘functions’, the total could be reduced.
