The economy of his own analysis of myths, into their constituent ‘mythemes’, goes well beyond Propp in the Structuralist’s quest for integration of his data.11

值得注意的是，列维-斯特劳斯与普罗普所实践的叙事结构分析，分别涉及对“神话素”和“功能”的某些“描述”。我已经举过普罗普“描述”的例子，比如“两王国之间的空间位移”。由分析者引入的这种措辞，可能会得到任何其他受命辨认功能不变量的分析者的同意；然而，在“描述”较为主观的情况下，两名分析者也可能对某个“功能”或“神话素”的刻画意见不一。有一种危险：所选“描述”可能有利于分析者自己的立场，源于他想建立相似或对立模式的愿望。列维-斯特劳斯在神话分析中提出的“描述”，其恰当性已经引起严重的怀疑。例如，在著名的俄狄浦斯故事分析中，他根据自己断定的某种共同属性，给各种行动（“神话素”）分类。在列维-斯特劳斯那里，归于同一个标题之下的三个“神话素”是：

It is noticeable that the structural analysis of narrative as practised by both Lévi-Strauss and Propp involves certain ‘descriptions’ of, in the one case, ‘mythemes’, and, in the other, ‘functions’. I have already given examples of Propp’s ‘descriptions’, e.g. ‘displacement in space between two kingdoms’. It may be that such a form of words, introduced by the analyst, would command agreement from any other analyst called on to identify functional invariants; yet it is also possible that two analysts might not agree on the characterization of a ‘function’ or a ‘mytheme’, in cases where the ‘descriptions’ are more subjective. There is a danger that the ‘description’ chosen may be loaded in the analyst’s favour and stem from his desire to establish patterns of similarity or opposition. The ‘descriptions’ found by Lévi-Strauss in his analyses of myths have raised serious doubts as to their adequacy. In his celebrated analysis of the Oedipus story, for example, he classifies the various actions (‘mythemes’) according to what he asserts to be a common property. Three such ‘mythemes’ which fall under the one heading for Lévi-Strauss are:

  1. 1 卡德摩斯寻找被宙斯劫走的妹妹欧罗巴。

    1 Cadmos seeks his sister Europa, ravished by Zeus.

  2. 2 俄狄浦斯娶了自己的母亲伊俄卡斯忒。

    2 Oedipus marries his mother, Jocasta.

  3. 3 安提戈涅不顾禁令，埋葬哥哥波吕尼刻斯。

    3 Antigone buries her brother, Polynices, despite prohibition.

按照列维-斯特劳斯的说法，这三个行动的共同属性是“过高评价血缘关系”；在他的分析中，与它们对应的是另一组行动，共有相反的属性：“过低评价血缘关系”。这个对立模式建立在分析者为神话中的行动选定的“描述”上；它是否是从资料中能够得出的唯一模式，远不能确定。其他分析者选择对相同行动作出其他“描述”，可能会提出其他类似模式。

The property common to these three actions, according to Lévi-Strauss, is ‘the overrating of blood relations’, and to them, in his analysis, there corresponds another set of actions with the opposed property in common of ‘underrating blood relations’. It is far from certain that this scheme of oppositions, founded on the analyst’s chosen ‘descriptions’ of actions in the myth, is the only one to be derived from the data. Other such schemes might be advanced by other analysts, who chose to give other ‘descriptions’ of the same actions.

这里值得提出这个问题，因为它指出了任何结构主义文学分析的一个关键时刻：分析从所研究文本的形式层面转向语义层面，或者从“形式”转向“内容”。俄国形式主义者曾受到责难，因为他们主张形式在先，内容只是为了证明形式合理才存在。结构主义无需犯这种过度的错误。诚然，它寻求制定一种文学“语法”，也就是一套规则：文本要被归为文学，就必须通过遵守或违反这些规则来体现它们；这只能通过从实际文本中抽象来实现。这是作为诗学的结构主义，致力于从形式角度区分它所处理的文学实例。但也存在研究个别文本的结构主义，寻求从结构上分析它们：也就是让各部分彼此关联，并说明这一特定文本如何“运作”。

This issue is worth raising here because it points to a crucial moment of any Structuralist analysis of literature, when it passes from the formal level of the texts it is concerned with to the semantic, or from ‘form’ to ‘content’. The Formalists in Russia were taken to task for arguing that form came first and that content was only present in order to justify the form. Structuralism need be guilty of no such excess. It seeks, agreed, to work out a ‘grammar’ of literature, or set of rules which texts must exemplify either in the observance or the violation if they are to be classed as literary; and this can be achieved only by abstraction from actual texts. This is Structuralism as Poetics, set on differentiating the literary examples with which it works from a formal point of view. But there is also a Structuralism of individual texts, which seeks to analyse them structurally: that is, to relate their parts to each other and to show how this particular text ‘works’.

不考虑文本的语义方面，就无法做到这一点；恰恰相反。后期形式主义者自己也认识到，如果文学作品是一个统一体，那么这种统一性或整合原则就与作品“意指”什么密切相关。例如托马舍夫斯基说：

This is not to be done without taking account of the semantic aspects of the text; on the contrary. The later Formalists themselves recognized that if a work of literature was a unity, then this unity or integrative principle was intimately connected with what the work ‘meant’. Tomashevsky, for example:

在艺术过程中，个别句子按照意义彼此结合，实现某种构造；在这一构造中，它们被一个观念或共同主题统一起来。作品各个要素的意义构成一个统一体，这就是主题（我们所谈论的东西）。我们既可以谈论整部作品的主题，也同样可以谈论其各部分的主题。每部用具有意义的语言写成的作品，都有一个主题……当文学作品从一个单一主题构造而成，并在作品展开过程中揭示这一主题时，它就具有统一性。12

In the course of the artistic process, particular sentences combine with one another according to their meaning and realise a certain construction in which they find themselves united by an idea or a common theme. The meanings of the particular elements of the work constitute a unity which is the theme (what we talk about). We can talk equally well about the theme of the work as a whole as of the theme of its parts. Every work written in a language provided with meaning possesses a theme . . . The literary work is endowed with a unity when it is constructed from a single theme which is unveiled in the course of the work.12

这里同样可以看出，分析像列维-斯特劳斯那里一样，取决于分析者为作品各部分、甚至为整部作品找出的“描述”。正如托马舍夫斯基在括号中作出的关键说明，主题是“我们所谈论的东西”。这里诉诸读者的“直觉”，或者说经由教育和实践而获得的文本解释才能；仿照乔姆斯基假定的语法“能力”，或许用乔纳森·卡勒的术语“文学能力”来称呼它更好。13作为读者，我们必须以某种方式获得文学理解，正如从前获得语言理解一样，才能达到那种把我们引向作品“主题”的读者直觉水平。可以合理地把文学理解看作依赖结构的：文学结构决定的不是我们阅读时理解什么，而是我们如何理解。我们从阅读中得到的具体意义是“事件”，我们的“能力”是这些事件所依赖的系统。这个系统必须通过学习获得；不存在全然未经训练就能理解文学的方式。

Here, again, one can see that the analysis hinges, as it does for Lévi-Strauss, on the ‘description’ found by the analyst for the parts of the work or even for the work as a whole. The theme is, as Tomashevsky parenthetically but crucially remarks, ‘what we talk about’. The appeal here is to the ‘intuition’ of the reader, or that gift for textual interpretation which comes with instruction and with practice and which might better be called by Jonathan Culler’s term of ‘literary competence’, by analogy with the grammatical ‘competence’ posited by Chomsky.13 As readers we have somehow to acquire literary understanding as we once acquired linguistic understanding in order to achieve the level of readerly intuition that will lead us to a work’s ‘themes’. And literary understanding can reasonably be seen as structure-dependent: the structures of literature determine not what we understand when we read but how we understand it. The particular meanings we derive from our reading are the ‘events’, our ‘competence’ is the system on which those events depend. That system must be learned; there is no wholly untutored way of making sense of literature.

如果这种文学理解有一项赖以建立的文本原则，那就是重复原则，或者，用法国文学理论家A. J. 格雷马斯所偏好的、略令人不安的称呼，是冗余原则。“冗余”概念来自信息论；信息论把一条信息中对接收者而言并非新信息的部分，都规定为冗余。从狭义的信息角度看，文学的确以过度冗余为特征，因为任何文学文本都具有一整系列的重复。若非如此，我们就完全无法理解它。一部作品的“主题”不能只传达一次、此后再不出现，因为那样我们就无法把它们识别为主题。“主题”涉及读者心中的汇合：读者把从文本不同部分、从阅读不同时刻获得的信息保持在一起。它们涉及记忆，也涉及文本本身反复强调的模式。这个过程再一次是整合的过程，“……使我们能够为理解那些不连续、相邻、异质的要素确定方向（这些要素在组合段中给定，而组合段只知道一个维度，即相继性）”。14

If there is one textual principle on which such literary understanding rests it is the principle of repetition or, as the French literary theorist A. J. Greimas prefers a little alarmingly to name it, the principle of redundancy. The notion of ‘redundancy’ is drawn from Information Theory, which declares as redundant whatever in a message is not new information to the person receiving it. In narrowly informational terms literature is indeed characterized by an excessive redundancy, since any literary text is marked by whole series of repetitions. If it were not so we would be quite unable to make sense of it. The ‘themes’ of a particular work cannot be conveyed once and never again for if they were we would fail to identify them as themes. ‘Themes’ involve a convergence in the mind of the reader who holds together information drawn from different parts of the text and from different moments of his reading. They involve the memory, as well as patterns of insistence in the text itself. The process, yet once more, is one of integration ‘ . . . which enables us to orientate our understanding of elements which are discontinuous, contiguous, heterogeneous (as given in the syntagm which knows of only a single dimension, that of succession).’14

一切文学理解，无论是否结构主义，都有一个特点：它致力于消解文本本身的相继性。索绪尔珍视的线性原则，让位于同时性原则。我们理解时所依赖的主题重复，可能在文本中相隔很远出现，也可能贯穿文本反复出现。例如，小说中“人物”这一根本手法，只能由作者通过重复逐步建立；每一次接续的出现，都确认有关该人物性质、因而也有关其在叙事中功能的指示。用符号学术语来说，为此提供给我们的每项证据，都可以归为指示符号。没有重复，任何“人物”都根本无法在我们心中形成。（也就是说，我们将不能“谈论”这个人物，或用自己的话表述这个人物。）如果用“人物”一词替换“神话”一词，就可以同意列维-斯特劳斯的说法：“重复的功能，是使神话的结构显现出来。”15

It is a characteristic of all literary understanding, Structuralist or not, that it works to undo the successiveness of the text itself. The principle of linearity, dear to Saussure, gives way to a principle of simultaneity. The thematic repetitions we depend on in understanding may occur at widely spaced intervals in a text, or they may occur insistently throughout. If one takes, for example, the fundamental device in fiction of ‘character’, this can be built up only repetitiously by an author, with each successive appearance serving to confirm indications as to the nature, and hence the function within the narrative, of the character in question. In semiotic terms, each item of evidence that we are given for this purpose may be classified as an index. Without repetition no ‘character’ could form in our minds at all. (We would be incapable, that is, of ‘talking about’ the character, or putting the character into our own words.) If one substitutes the word ‘character’ for the word ‘myth’ one can agree with Lévi-Strauss that ‘The function of repetition is to render the structure of the myth apparent.’15

文学结构主义作为文本解释方式的独特之处，是对意义采取“功能性”的看法。它提出的问题是：文本的这个或那个要素，作为这一文本（或一个文本）的要素，意味着什么？这是以索绪尔研究语言符号的同样方式来研究文本的组成部分，即区分符号的“价值”与其“意指”。符号的“价值”，由它在自身所属的符号系统中的位置决定；构成文学的符号也是如此。它们首先是文学符号，然后才是任何其他类型的符号，必须用文学的术语从功能上理解。在形式主义发展成结构主义的那些年里，雅各布森与尤里·梯尼亚诺夫提出的关键公理之一就是：“文学中使用的文学材料与非文学材料，只有从功能观点考虑，才能被纳入科学研究的范围。”16

Where literary Structuralism is distinctive as a mode of interpretation of texts is in taking a ‘functional’ view of meaning. It asks the question: What does this or that element of the text mean as an element of the (or else of a) text? This is to approach the constituent parts of the text in the same fashion as Saussure approached the signs of a language, by discriminating between the signs’ ‘value’ and their ‘signification’. Their ‘value’ is determined by the place which they occupy within the sign-system of which they are part; and similarly with the signs of which literature is made. They are literary signs before they are any other kind of sign, to be understood functionally in literary terms. This was one of the key axioms laid down by Jakobson and Juri Tynianov in the years when Formalism was evolving into Structuralism: ‘The literary and extra-literary material used in literature may be introduced into the orbit of scientific investigation only when it is considered from a functional point of view.’16

这似乎是一个多余的限定条件，直到我们认识到，文本解释中有多大比例根本不是文学性的，而是心理学的、社会学的，等等。结构主义无权试图压制这些类型的解释，因为它们能告诉我们许多关于文学的东西；但结构主义可以主张优先于它们，因为结构解释应当先行。也就是说，再以叙事中的“人物”为例，对每个人物“意味着”什么的第一次解释，应当依据我们所见的该人物在整个叙事布局中占据的位置来作出。只有这样，才能逐步确定人物的“价值”。多数小说读者大概本来就这样做；他们把人物彼此比较，得出某些伦理或其他结论：他们讨人喜欢或不讨人喜欢，是好是坏，等等。这些比较之所以可能，是因为我们作为读者的经验，也就是我们的“能力”；但比较是在包容它们的结构的界限内进行的。《傲慢与偏见》的读者，不需要极其熟悉叙事的方式，就能把班纳特家的五个女儿看作一个同质群体——她们是姐妹，这个事实使理解更容易——并首先注意到她们之间可见的差异。她们彼此不同，因为她们必须如此，人物塑造就意味着这一点。她们在小说本身的较大系统中，形成一个小系统；把小说中的每个人物与每个其他人物比较，没有意义，因为他们并非全都能够这样关联起来。当然，没有什么能够或会阻止我们，把小说中遇到的“人物”，与我们关于小说设定时代或地点的知识、关于人类心理的知识，或者关于周围有血有肉之人的经验联系起来（我们之中较具文学修养的人，忘了这种“经验”在何种程度上涉及我们用阅读中学来的词语对他人作出的描述）。但是应当记住，小说中的“人物”首先是作为该特定小说的要素共同属于它的；无论我们随后给予他们什么评价，第一次评价都应当是功能性的。

This might seem an unnecessary proviso until one realizes what a large proportion of textual interpretation is not literary at all, but psychological, sociological and so on. Structuralism has no business trying to suppress interpretations of these kinds, which have much to tell us about literature; but it can claim precedence over them, in so far as a structural interpretation should come first. That is to say, if we again take the example of the ‘characters’ in a narrative, a first interpretation of what each character ‘means’ should be made in terms of the place which that character is seen to occupy within the scheme of the narrative as a whole. Only in that way can we work towards the ‘value’ of the character. Most readers of fiction probably do this anyway; they compare the characters one with another and reach certain ethical or other conclusions about them: that they are likeable or unlikeable, good or bad, and so on. Such comparisons are made possible by our experience as readers, by our ‘competence’ that is, but they function within the perimeter of the structure that encloses them. Readers of Pride and Prejudice do not need to be enormously practised in the ways of narrative to grasp the five daughters of the Bennet family as a homogeneous group – the way is eased by the fact that they are siblings – and notable first for the differences to be observed between them. They are different from each other because they have to be, since that is what characterization entails. They form a small system within the larger system of the novel itself; it will not make sense to compare every character with every other character in a fiction, because not all of them can be thus correlated. Nothing, of course, can or will stop us relating the ‘characters’ we meet with in fictions to our knowledge of the period or place in which the fiction is set, our knowledge of human psychology, or our experience of flesh-and-blood people around us (we forget, the more literary among us, to what point that ‘experience’ involves our own descriptions of other people in terms we have picked up from our reading). But we ought to remember that in the first instance the ‘characters’ of a fiction belong together, as elements in that particular fiction, and that whatever evaluations we submit them to subsequently, the first evaluation is a functional one.

巴特出版的一部研究17世纪法国悲剧家拉辛的著作，开篇第一句话就极其鲜明地表达了同一点：“拉辛那里有三个地中海……”17这种措辞即使不是为了震惊，也意在使人惊讶，尤其因为人们可能认为，没有哪位作家比拉辛更不关心地点。然而，地图册告诉我们，名叫地中海的海只有一个，怎么会有三个？巴特的意思是：如果地中海是拉辛悲剧中的一个要素，那么它在那里是出于拉辛的理由，而不只是地理的理由；这个特定地点一旦被置入文本，就获得文本内涵或文本功能。我们或许还会带入自己的内涵，也或许不会，因为许多人有自己私人的“地中海”；但那些不是文本内涵，而是外来的输入。

This same point is most trenchantly made in the very first words of a study published by Barthes of the seventeenth-century French tragedian Racine: ‘There are three Mediterraneans in Racine . . . ’17 The formulation is intended to surprise if not to shock, not least because no writer might be thought to have been more indifferent to locale than Racine. But three Mediterraneans when the atlas tells us that there is only one sea of that name? Barthes’s point is that if the Mediterranean is an element in a tragedy by Racine it is so for reasons of Racine’s and not for reasons just of geography, and the moment this particular locale is inserted into a text it receives textual connotations or a textual function. We may or may not bring to it also connotations of our own, since many of us have our private ‘Mediterranean’ sea; those, however, are not textual connotations, but imports.

对于“地中海”这样的词项，在某一特定文本中可能确立的内涵，并不必然是该文本独有的，因为它们可能为一个作者的全部作品、一整代作者的作品，或文学史的整个时期所共有。拉辛剧作中地中海被赋予的内涵，既可能是个人的，也同样可能是传统的。重要的不是确定它们的独特程度，而是通过一个有说服力的实例，展示结构主义方法能够使何种富于启发的单篇文本或一组文本的阅读成为可能：这种方法把文本中众多的重复关联起来，整合成语义单位。随后，这些语义单位本身又可以整合成更大的单位，直到——某些结构主义者会这样说，但肯定并非全体——最后把整体整合为一个单一的、统摄一切的意义。

The connotations which it may be possible to establish for a given term such as ‘Mediterranean’ in a given text are not necessarily peculiar to that text, because they may be common to an author’s entire oeuvre, or to the work of a whole generation of authors, or to a whole period of literary history. The connotations which the Mediterranean is invested with in a Racine play could as well be traditional as private ones. The important consideration is not to determine their degree of idiosyncrasy but to demonstrate, by a telling instance, the sort of instructive reading of a text or body of texts which is made possible by a Structuralist method, of correlating the very many repetitions those texts contain and integrating them into semantic units. These semantic units themselves may then be integrated into larger units still until, some Structuralists but certainly not all would say, one ends with the final integration of the whole into a single, over-arching meaning.

因此，结构主义既注意文学作品的“语法”方面，也注意其语义方面：即使不可避免地试图把两者分开，也不会忽略任何一方。我认为这种分离并无害处；它是不变与可变之间，或者所有可比较文本共有的东西与每个文本独有的东西之间的分离。如果继续以“人物”作为叙事手法的例子，这种分离可以理解为（虽有些困难）格雷马斯所称的行动元与行动者之间的分离。行动元是叙事中纯粹形式的要素，相当于语法学家的主语与宾语；它们是任何类型行动的参与项，无论是个人的还是非个人的，但它们没有姓名、没有性质，除了语法角色之外没有意义。简言之，它们是结构分析的产物，是所能设想的最抽象的叙事实体。相反，行动者是叙事的可见组成部分，是所发生行动的实际主体与客体；它们可以有姓名，具有独特的外貌、性情等等。用乔姆斯基的术语来说，它们构成叙事的表层结构，而行动元构成深层结构。格雷马斯能够为这种划分辩护，因为他可以证明行动元与行动者之间并非一一对应。一个“行动元”角色可以由几个行动者扮演，或者一个“行动者”角色可以由几个行动元扮演。格雷马斯的分析十分复杂，正如他为进行分析所发明的过多新术语一样；我不想在这里详谈，但它很有助于揭示文学结构主义的双重性质：一方面，它趋向某种叙事代数，把实际叙事还原为符号串或故事的形式逻辑；另一方面，它趋向“主题”的分析，而主题是我们多数人对任何叙事所感兴趣的东西。这两种分析在文本表面相遇：

Structuralism, then, is aware both of the ‘grammatical’ aspects of literary works and of their semantic aspects: it neglects neither even if it trics, inescapably, to separate them. I see no harm in such separation, which is that between the invariant and the variable, or what is common to all comparable texts and what is peculiar to each. If we remain with ‘character’ as the example of a narrative device, the separation can be understood (with some difficulty) as being between what Greimas calls an actant and an actor. Actants are purely formal elements in a narrative, equivalent to the subject and object of a grammarian; they are parties to any action of whatever kind, personal or otherwise, but they have no names, no qualities and no meaning beyond their grammatical role. They are, in short, the product of a structural analysis, the most abstract narrative entities conceivable. Actors, on the other hand, are visible components of the narrative, they are the actual subjects and objects of the actions which occur; they may bear names and possess distinctive appearances, natures, etc. They constitute, in Chomskyan terms, the surface structure of the narrative, whereas the actants constitute the deep structure. Greimas can justify this division because he can show that there is no one-to-one relation between actants and actors. One ‘actantial’ role may be played by several actors or one ‘actorial’ role by several actants. Greimas’s analysis, like the rather too many new terms he has invented with which to conduct it, is complicated, and I would not wish to dwell on it here, but it helps greatly to bring out the double nature of literary Structuralism as it moves on the one side towards the sort of narrative algebra by which actual narratives can be reduced to strings of symbols or a formal logic of stories, and on the other side towards an analysis of the ‘themes’ which are what interest most of us in any narrative. It is at the surface of the text that the two analyses meet:

因此，行动者是叙事结构与话语结构、语法成分与语义成分相遇并结合的地方，因为他同时承担至少一个行动元角色，以及至少一个主题角色；这些角色规定他的能力，以及他所做或所是的界限。18

An actor is thus the place where narrative structures and the structures of discourse, the grammatical and the semantic components, meet and are conjoined, because he is responsible simultaneously for at least one actantial and at least one thematic role which define his competence and the limits of what he does or is.18

在格雷马斯自己的工作中，语法成分的分析比语义成分的分析有说服力得多，因为他的抽象冲动格外强烈（看看他的信念便知：普罗普的三十一个叙事“功能”，实际上可以还原为一个单一的叙事“功能”，即一个范式性的行动，所有其他行动都可以通过转换逻辑由它派生！）。对格雷马斯而言，任何特定叙事都代表语义结构对叙事结构（用他的话说是“程序”）的充实；而所有只做叙事读者的人，自然会反过来看，把叙事结构看作能够从语义结构中提取出来的东西。格雷马斯的结构主义的雄心，是像他规范化“语法”方面那样，彻底而节约地规范化叙事的语义方面。行动者是两个领域的交点，因此语义分析可以从他开始。例如，某个故事中的行动者如果是渔夫，就立刻引入某些语义约束，制约叙事的发展。这个行动者的许多行动，会从其渔夫的“主题角色”中产生；被赋予的某些心理特征也可能如此，因为我们的文化认为渔夫冷静、耐心，等等。格雷马斯采取传统的语义观点，认为“渔夫”这个所指包含一个或多或少稳定的语义核心或“形象”。越向这个核心之外移动，词项的内涵就越可有可无，甚至越可争论。在“渔夫”出现的叙事过程中，这个“形象”会实现越来越多的潜能，与其他“形象”建立关系，形成格雷马斯所称的“形象星座”。每个文本都可以被认为构成一个语义“微观宇宙”（格雷马斯颇偏爱天文学隐喻），它本身从一对相反的词项逻辑地展开；格雷马斯举的例子，是生命与死亡这一对，作为法国小说家乔治·贝尔纳诺斯全部作品的基本逻辑与语义成分。

In Greimas’s own work the analysis of the grammatical component is far more convincing than that of the semantic component, since the urge to abstraction in him is unusually strong (witness his belief that Propp’s thirty-one narrative ‘functions’ can actually be reduced to a single narrative ‘function’, a paradigmatic action from which all other actions can be derived by a logic of transformations!). For Greimas any particular narrative represents the investment of a narrative structure (a ‘programme’ in his parlance) by a semantic one, whereas all mere readers of narrative naturally look at things the other way round, and see the narrative structure as extractable from the semantic one. The ambition of Greimas’s Structuralism is to codify the semantic aspects of narrative as thoroughly and economically as he has codified the ‘grammatical’. The actor being the point where the two realms meet, it is from him that the semantic analysis can start. If, for example, the actor in a given story is a fisherman, certain semantic constraints are at once introduced which will condition the development of the narrative. Many of this actor’s actions will follow from his ‘thematic role’ of fisherman, and so may some of the psychological characteristics with which he is endowed, fisherman being supposed by our culture to be phlegmatic, patient, etc. Greimas takes the conventional semantic view that the signified fisherman contains a more or less stable semantic nucleus or ‘figure’. As one moves outwards from this nucleus the connotations of the term become more and more optional, even arguable. In the course of the narrative in which the ‘fisherman’ occurs the ‘figure’ will realize more and more of its potentialities, entering into relations with other ‘figures’ to create what Greimas calls ‘figurative constellations’. Each text can be thought to constitute a semantic ‘micro-universe’ (Greimas rather favours astronomical metaphors) itself articulated logically from a single pair of contrary terms: an example Greimas offers is the pair of Life and Death as the fundamental logical and semantic components of the oeuvre of the French novelist Georges Bernanos.

如此还原性的语义分析，尤其像格雷马斯那样大多用自己强加的术语进行时，会激起许多文学研究者的反感。格雷马斯的结构主义高度精密、数学化；但只要能够跟上他的论证，它也很有趣、富有成效。如果他建立一种兼具语法性和语义性的叙事逻辑，最终证明走在正确的道路上，这并不会损害文学本身。结构主义目标的一部分，就是说明文学作品的结构远比我们否则可能想到的复杂而合乎逻辑；如果这个目标得以实现，很难明白为什么有人要为此不安。

A semantic analysis thus reductive, especially when it is carried out, as by Greimas, in a terminology largely imposed by himself, raises many hackles among students of literature. Greimas’s Structuralism is highly elaborate and mathematical; but it is also, to the extent that one can follow his arguments, interesting and productive. If he proves to be on the right track in establishing a logic of narrative at once grammatical and semantic it will not do any harm to literature as such. It is a part of Structuralism’s aim to show that works of literature are far more intricate and logical as structured than we might otherwise suspect; it is hard to see why anyone should be disturbed if this aim is made good.

把结构主义原则应用于文学作品的一个主要效果，是在语义上丰富作品。因为结构主义通过发现关联、以及关联之间的关联而工作，所以它绝不能把文本中的任何要素，看作至少不能潜在地与另一个要素关联，无论是情节、人物，还是其他方面的要素。在这一点上，结构主义可以正当地声称，比其他类型的批评更开放；后者阅读文本时往往有所偏重，忽略不合自身兴趣的东西。对小说的精神分析式阅读，在选择解释什么时，可能确实十分片面；马克思主义的阅读也可能如此，因为两者通常都只试图把文本的孤立要素，而非文本整体，联系到文本之外的现实。在结构主义看来，文本包含的一切，都必须被视作可能具有意义的东西；鉴于许多文学文本极长而复杂，这个想法令人忧虑。事实上，一种可能的反结构主义论据是：如果它要彻底，就永远找不到足够的时间实践自己的原则；许多结构主义文学分析确实是对相对短小或简单的文本进行的。对这一论据的回答是：结构主义的观点远不那么个人主义，因此完全可以成为一种协作式批评。期待一位批评家对哪怕单一文本作出完整的结构分析，也是不合理的。

A major effect of applying Structuralist principles to literary works is to enrich them semantically. Because Structuralism works by discovering correlations, and correlations between correlations, it can never treat any element of a text as if it were not at least potentially able to be correlated with some other element, of plot, or character, or whatever. In this respect Structuralism may legitimately claim to be more open-minded than other kinds of criticism, which tend to be partial in their reading of texts and to leave out of account whatever does not consort with their interests. A psychoanalytical reading of a novel may be very partial indeed, in what it chooses to interpret, and so may a Marxist reading, because both will normally seek to refer only isolated elements of the text to an extra-textual reality, rather than the text as a whole. For Structuralism everything that the text contains has to be seen as of potential consequence, which is a worrying thought, given the great length and complexity of many literary texts. Indeed, a possible argument against Structuralism is that if it was to be thorough it could never find time to live up to its own principles; it is a fact that much Structuralist analysis of literature has been done on relatively short or simple texts. The answer to that argument is that Structuralism, being so much less individualistic in its outlook, might very well become a collaborative form of criticism. It is unreasonable to expect a complete structural analysis of even a single text from one critic.

可以说，在理论上，文本中没有任何东西应被忽略，尽管实践中总会忽略。罗兰·巴特拟定的结构主义格言是：从文本上说，而且程度不同（这是重要的限定），“一切都表意”：

In theory, let us say, nothing in the text should go disregarded even if in practice it always does. The Structuralist motto, devised by Roland Barthes, is that textually and (an important qualification) to different degrees, ‘everything signifies’:

这不是（叙述者的）艺术问题，而是结构问题：在话语的秩序中，凡被记下的，按定义都值得注意；即使一个细节似乎不可化约地无关紧要，与一切功能无涉，到头来它仍会意指荒谬或无用本身；要么一切都有意义，要么什么都没有意义……19

This is not a question of art (on the part of the narrator), it is a question of structure: in the order of discourse what is noted is, by definition, notable: even when a detail may seem irreducibly insignificant, and alien to all function, it will still in the end mean absurdity or uselessness itself; either everything has a meaning or nothing does . . . 19

这等于说，文本中的一切都是符号，或者都具有内涵。或者，换一种说法，关于它总有话可说。文本本身可能生成无限多其他文本；当我们认识到它充满了不可穷尽的意义，就可能对它作出无限多的阅读，而这些阅读就是那些其他文本。

This is equivalent to saying that everything in a text is a sign, or is connotative. Or, alternatively, that there is something to be said about it. The text itself might generate infinitely many other texts, which are the infinitely many readings of it made possible by the realization that it is inexhaustibly full of meanings.

迄今为止，最出色地展示这意味着什么的，是巴特专门研究巴尔扎克一部中篇小说的书《S/Z》。（应当注意，书名来自音系学，其中S和Z构成不同的音位，由“浊音性”这一特征彼此区分：Z是浊音，S是清音。）在这个实例中，文本生成评论的力量，由巴尔扎克篇幅不大的故事与巴特长得多的分析之间的悬殊差别显示出来。反对巴特与结构主义的人，认为这种不成比例太过分了。毋宁说，它是典范。恰好，法国有一种叫作explication de texte（文本细读）的学校练习，其中这种不成比例是规定的，因为它要求对仅仅几行或几段文本写出好几页评论。巴特所做的，是把一种用于诗歌时更为熟悉、更可接受的评论风格，转用于散文的细读；诗歌这种写作形式，被认为在形式上更复杂，在语义上更密集。正如我先前指出的，让散文受到此前罕见的细致考察，是结构主义的一项成就。

The most remarkable demonstration we have yet had of what this implies is the book called S/Z which Barthes devoted to a novella by Balzac. (The title of the book derives, be it noted, from phonology, in which S and Z constitute separate phonemes distinguished from each other by the feature of ‘voicedness’: Z is voiced, S unvoiced.) The power of a text to generate commentary is shown in this case by the glaring disparity between the dimensions of Balzac’s story, which are modest, and those of Barthes’s analysis of it, which is considerably longer. This disproportion has been found excessive by those opposed to Barthes and to Structuralism. Rather, it is exemplary. There is, as it happens, a scholastic exercise in France known as the explication de texte where such a disproportion is statutory, since it involves writing several pages of commentary on only a few lines or paragraphs of text. What Barthes has done is to transfer to the explication of prose a style of commentary more familiar and acceptable when applied to verse – a form of writing presumed to be both formally more intricate and semantically more dense. As I have earlier suggested, this is an accomplishment of Structuralism, to have caused prose to be examined with a degree of care it has seldom known before.

在《S/Z》中，像在其他批评著作中一样，巴特既自信断言，又谦逊。他对巴尔扎克所做的事情，他很清楚会冒犯那些持有不同批评立场的人；但同时，他又试图不让自己的方法或结果拥有过于持久的权威。这预示了本书最后一章关于后结构主义的讨论；在那一章中，这类悖论将大显身手。事实上，巴特在《S/Z》中引入的分析方法，没有找到任何门徒；这表明他具有一定程度的个人独特性。他的结构主义比格雷马斯这样的代数学家的结构主义更个人化、更自由驰骋，但这不意味着巴特应被完全排除在结构主义概览之外。他并不严密或一贯地遵循结构主义，却十分鼓舞人心地展示：一位有他这样高超智力和洞察力的批评家，如何能够在基本的结构主义方法上进一步构建。

In S/Z, as in his other critical writings, Barthes is both assertive and modest. He does things to Balzac which he knows very well will be found offensive by those of other critical persuasions than his own, but at the same time seeks to deny too lasting an authority to his own methods or results. In this he looks ahead to the final chapter of this book, on post-Structuralism, in which such paradoxes will come into their own. It is a fact that the method of analysis which Barthes introduces in S/Z has found no disciples, which suggests a certain degree of idiosyncrasy on his part. His Structuralism is of a more personal and free-ranging kind than that of an algebraist like Greimas, but that does not mean Barthes should be excluded from a survey of Structuralism altogether. He is not rigorously or consistently Structuralist but he shows, very hearteningly, how a basically Structuralist method may be built on to by a critic of his own very high intelligence and insight.

像任何文学作品一样，巴特作为研究对象的巴尔扎克中篇小说——题名是《萨拉辛》——具有他所称的“多元性”：它是多种意义的所在，因而能够生成不同却正当的阅读。巴特把自己的结构主义，与任何坚持文学作品只有一个“真实”意义的批评学派对立起来；这些学派认为，发现这个意义，就是最终“理解”了作品。这个“真实”意义一般被等同于作者意图赋予作品的意义，尽管通常我们作为读者所拥有的作者意图的唯一证据，就是文本本身。过去五十年间，人们花费大量笔墨讨论文学批评中的“意图谬误”问题，以及在决定文本意义时，是否需要关心作者被假定的意图。结构主义非常坚决地认为，意图问题往往毫无意义，或者充其量是循环论证，因为我们用来确定意图的证据，通常取自面前的文本。

Like any work of literature, the Balzac novella which Barthes takes as his subject – its title is Sarrasine – is what he calls ‘plural’: it is the locus of more than one meaning and hence generative of different but legitimate readings. Barthes opposes his own Structuralism to any school of critical thought which holds to the principle that a literary work has a single ‘true’ meaning, to discover which is definitively to ‘comprehend’ the work. This ‘true’ meaning is generally identified with the meaning intended by the author of the work, even though normally speaking the only evidence we have as readers of authorial intentions is the text itself. Much ink has been spilt in the past fifty years over the question of the ‘Intentional fallacy’ in literary criticism, and whether we need concern ourselves at all with an author’s presumed intentions when we decide what a text’s meanings are; Structuralism believes very strongly that questions of intention tend to be pointless or, at best, circular, since the evidence we use to determine them is usually drawn from the text before us.

在巴特看来，对文本的“意图性”阅读，错在声称自身是正确的，而其他阅读必然不是错误就是次要的：

So far as Barthes is concerned, ‘intentional’ readings of a text err by claiming that they are right and other readings necessarily either wrong or secondary:

有些人——姑且称之为语文学家——断定每个文本都是单义的，承载一种真实的、权威的意义，把第二种、同时存在的意义打入批评家苦思冥想的遗忘之地。与他们相对，另一些人——姑且称之为符号学家——反对外延与内涵之间的等级关系；他们说，作为外延原材料的语言，连同其词典和句法，只是诸多系统中的一个；没有理由给予这个系统特权。20

Some – let us say, the philologists – decree that every text is unequivocal and the bearer of a true, canonical meaning, and they dismiss second, simultaneous meanings to the oblivion of critical elucubration. Confronting these, others – let us say, the semiologists – contest the hierarchy of the denoted and the connoted; language, they say, the raw material of denotation, with its dictionary and its syntax, is one system among others; there is no reason to privilege that system.20

这番有些曲折的宣言可以改述为：如果外延指的是直接指涉语言之外的世界，那么文学中就不可能有语言的外延性运用。如果文学中存在外延性语言，它就自动显示自身是外延性的；也就是说，它带有外延性的内涵，因为这是它在文学结构内被分配的功能。俄国文学理论家巴赫金在许多方面与巴特极为接近（他也是一位伟大的“多元主义者”）；他说“未经折射、仿佛处于原始状态的现实，不能进入文学的内容”21时，用略为不同的术语提出了同一论证。巴特说文本中的一切都具有“内涵”，巴赫金则要求我们把它理解为“意识形态的”：它进入文本时就已经承载文化价值。（正因为如此，巴赫金反对早期形式主义关于词语“复活”的观念；这一观念把词语设想为纯净原初的要素，作者可以使用它，而不受历史或用法所造成的这一切污染。）

This somewhat tortuous declaration might be paraphrased as that there can be no denotative use of language in literature, if by denotation we mean a direct reference to the world outside language. If there is denotative language in literature it automatically displays itself to be so: that is, it carries a connotation of denotation because such is the function it has been allotted within the literary structure. Bakhtin, the Russian theorist of literature who comes extremely close to Barthes at many points (and was also a great ‘pluralist’), makes the same argument in slightly different terms when he says that ‘Reality that is unrefracted and, as it were, raw is not able to enter into the content of literature.’21 Where Barthes writes of everything in the text being ‘connotative’, Bakhtin asks that we should understand it as ‘ideological’: it enters the text already bearing cultural values. (It was because this is the case that Bakhtin opposed the early Formalist notion of the ‘resurrection’ of the word as a supposedly pristine element which the author might use free from all such contamination by history or by usage.)

因此，巴特作为文本的符号学家（semiologist或semiotician），为分析《萨拉辛》发明了五种“代码”，使他能够在语法和语义两方面，将故事中的一定数量要素关联起来。他暗示，代码是文本本身提供给他的，而非他自己强加的：他狡黠地试图免除自己发现和运用一种非同寻常的批评方法的责任，或许是向真正结构主义的严谨致敬；真正的结构主义会声称，自己依据可从资料中推导出的原则来整理资料。对于巴特，我们不必全当真。同样，巴特竭力不在五种代码之间强加任何等级，而认为它们全都平等；正如马上将看到的，这也是挑衅性的。

Operating, then, as a semiologist or semiotician of the text, Barthes invents for the purpose of analysing Sarrasine five ‘codes’ which will enable him to correlate, both grammatically and semantically, a certain number of elements of the story. He suggests that the text itself supplies him with the codes, that they are not an imposition of his own: this sly attempt to absolve himself of responsibility for the discovery and exploitation of an unusual critical method is a tribute perhaps to the austerity of a true Structuralism, which would claim to order its data by principles derivable from the data; in Barthes’s case we need not take it quite seriously. Barthes is anxious, likewise, not to impose any hierarchy on the five codes, all of which are held to be equal; this too, as we shall see in a moment, is provocative.

简言之，五种代码是：

The five codes are, briefly:

  1. 1 “阐释代码”，把文本中先提出、后解决“谜题”的各种序列归在一起，由此在读者心中制造悬念。

    1 The ‘Hermeneutic code’, which groups together the various sequences of the text that first pose and then resolve ‘enigmas’, so creating suspense in the reader.

  2. 2 “义素代码”，把文本中各种反复出现的内涵或“主题”归在一起。

    2 The ‘Semic code’, which groups together various recurrent connotations or ‘themes’ of the text.

  3. 3 “象征代码”，最难定义，把文本包含的某些对立模式归在一起。

    3 The ‘Symbolic code’, the hardest to define, which groups together certain patterns of antithesis contained in the text.

  4. 4 “行动代码”，把构成不言自明整体的行动序列归在一起，不论它们在文本中如何散布。

    4 The ‘Action code’, which groups sequences of actions forming self-evident wholes, however scattered they may be textually.

  5. 5 “指涉代码”，把文本中对巴尔扎克时代的文化、社会及其他知识的众多指涉全部归在一起。

    5 The ‘Referential code’, which groups all the many references made in the text to the cultural, social and other knowledge of Balzac’s day.

巴特沿着巴尔扎克的文本前进，任意把它划分为每段几行的“阅读单位”，并将这些单位分配到各种“代码”中。“阅读单位”与“代码”之间并非一一对应，因为一个“单位”可能归于几种“代码”。有些“单位”得到长篇解释，有些则极简短，以此展示解释者在决定走多远时必然具有的任意性。

As Barthes proceeds through Balzac’s text, which he divides arbitrarily up into ‘units of reading’ of a few lines each, he is able to distribute these units among the ‘codes’. There is no one-to-one relation between the ‘units of reading’ and the ‘codes’, because one ‘unit’ may be referrable to several ‘codes’. Some ‘units’ are interpreted at length, others very briefly, as a demonstration of the necessary arbitrariness of the interpreter in deciding how far to go.

这种阅读巧妙而富于启发，确实揭示了巴尔扎克中篇小说中否则永远无法显现的组织层次。但巴特的文本结构主义实践，也因他对现实主义意识形态怀有强烈敌意而带有偏见。巴尔扎克是经常被选来体现19世纪小说现实主义的法国作家，被看作其时代的一种社会史家，从当时社会中周围可见的事物获得线索。他是那种作家的首要例证：对这类作家，“再现”似乎先于“构成”，忠实于文本外的事实先于艺术。展示这种天真假定的错误，是巴特目标的重要部分；他要说明，《萨拉辛》这样的文本，其结构是文学的，它是一件精心制作的文学艺术作品，而不是小说化的社会观察。这种展示很有价值，即使它走得过远。巴特故意挑衅那些坚持传统现实主义观的人，在自己的方案中淡化第五种“指涉”代码的重要性；现实主义者则会把它放在所有其他代码之上，认为它是巴尔扎克这样作家的目的的核心。他们会认为，巴尔扎克的文本充满了对当时法国社会直接而个人化的指涉；巴特则相反，主张它完全没有直接指涉该社会，因为它只指涉当时公认、因而非个人的见解。也就是说，其中没有观察，只有大量二手意见和信念。而巴特拒绝在五种代码之间建立任何等级，使“指涉”代码与比如“阐释”代码获得相等的权重，试图由此改变我们阅读所谓“现实主义”文本的整体重心，转向它的高度人为性。

Such a reading is ingenious and very instructive, and it does lay bare levels of organization in Balzac’s novella which could never otherwise have appeared. But Barthes’s exercise in textual Structuralism is also biased, by the strong animus which he felt towards the ideology of Realism. Balzac is a French writer frequently chosen to exemplify nineteenth-century Realism in fiction, and thought of as a kind of social historian of his times who took his cue from what he found around him in the society of the day. He is the prime example of a writer for whom ‘represenation’ seems to come before ‘composition’ and truth to extra-textual facts before art. It is very much part of Barthes’s purpose to show the error of any such naïve supposition, to show that the structures of a text like Sarrasine are literary, that it is an elaborate work of literary art, not fictionalized social observation. The demonstration is valuable, even if it goes too far. Barthes deliberately taunts those who hold to the traditional view of Realism by downplaying the importance in his scheme of the fifth, ‘referential’ code, which is the one Realists would set above all the others as being central to the purposes of a writer such as Balzac. They would hold that Balzac’s text is full of direct and personal references to French society of the period; Barthes argues, to the contrary, that it contains no direct references to it at all, because it refers only to the accepted and hence impersonal wisdom of the day. That is, there is no observation in it, merely a large collection of second-hand opinions and beliefs. And by refusing, as he does, to establish any hierarchy among his five codes, so that the ‘Referential’ code gets equal weighting with, say, the ‘Hermeneutic’, Barthes attempts to shift the whole balance of the way in which we read a supposedly ‘Realist’ text towards its high degree of artifice.

最后，巴特在《S/Z》中的结构主义反常之处，在于他采用一种让巴尔扎克文本瓦解的分析方法。他拒绝使任何代码从属于其他代码，不让其中一种成为俄国形式主义者所说的“主导”；更重要的是，他拒绝整理“义素”代码揭示的一切“主题”，而宁可让它们保持为散落、貌似随机的“意义的微光”22。这些都显露出巴特游戏的一面。他教给我们的是意义的多重性，以及文本中许多要素的“过度决定”；这一教益尤其有效，因为它取自一个我们过去可能认为相对“透明”的文本。

What, finally, is perverse in Barthes’s Structuralism in S/Z is that he adopts a method of analysis which serves to disintegrate Balzac’s text. His refusal to subordinate any one code to the others, to make one of them the ‘dominant’, as the Russian Formalists would have put it, and even more his refusal to bring order to all the ‘themes’ uncovered by the ‘Semic’ code, which he prefers to leave as a scattered and seemingly random ‘shimmer of meaning’,22 reveal the playful side of Barthes. The lesson he teaches is of the multiplicity of meanings and of the ‘over-determination’ of many elements in the text; and it is all the more effective for being taught from a text we might once have thought of as being relatively ‘transparent’.

巴特明确揭示了解释事实上无止境：这是皮尔士的认识，即符号只能通过使用其他符号来解释。巴特的“代码”实现了有限的整合，是通过为若干组要素命名来实现的；这些要素虽然散落在文本中，却被认为以某种方式共同属于一组。作为读者，我们习惯根据专名识别小说家的“人物”；专名把人物在叙事中的各种出场联系起来。我们较少习惯于认识另一种类似的过程：通过它，我们理解叙事本身，并识别其中反复出现的主题。这个过程是命名；巴特告诉我们，它

Barthes brings out explicitly the virtual endlessness of interpretation: the Peircean realization that signs can be interpreted only by the use of other signs. The limited degree of integration achieved by Barthes’s ‘codes’ is achieved by putting a name to groups of elements perceived as belonging together in some way even though they are scattered in the text itself. We are used as readers to recognizing a fiction-writer’s ‘characters’ by their proper names, which bind together their various appearances in the narrative; we are less used to the similar process by which we make sense of the narrative itself and recognize its recurrent themes. The process is one of nomination; which, Barthes tells us,

正是读者的活动本身：阅读就是努力命名，就是使文本的句子经受语义转换……因此，阅读被吸入某种转喻性的滑行，每个同义词都给相邻者增添某种新特征、某种新出发点。23

is the very activity of the reader: to read is to struggle to name, it is to subject the sentences of the text to a semantic transformation . . . reading is thus absorbed into a sort of metonymic glissade, with each synonym adding some new characteristic, some new departure to its neighbour.23

对于许多读者，这个过程是否曾明确展开，是个问题，因为除非他们被要求谈论或写下所读的东西，否则或许没有必要给其中任何东西“命名”。巴特的结构主义趋向于无定论：他警告不要封闭符号衍义的过程，也不要接受一种常见假定，即文学文本只能有一种解释，或只旨在传达一种单一的“最终”意义。这些警告使他与后结构主义的教导密切相连；现在，我将转向后结构主义。

With many readers it is questionable whether this process ever gets explicitly under way, since there may be no call for them to ‘name’ anything of what they read, unless they find themselves asked to talk or to write about it. The thrust of Barthes’s Structuralism is towards the inconclusive: he warns against foreclosing on the process of semiosis and the common assumption that literary texts are susceptible of only a single interpretation or directed to conveying only a single, ‘final’ meaning. These are warnings that connect him closely with the teachings of post-Structuralism, to which I now turn.

5 后结构主义5 Post-Structuralism

巴赫金与梅德韦杰夫在对俄国形式主义者富有预见、强有力的批评中（出版于1928年）指出，形式主义者把词语作为自我封闭的物质实体加以“复活”，是贫瘠而反社会的：“对意义的恐惧决定了形式主义者的诗歌语音学；意义凭借其‘不在此地’和‘不在此刻’，能够破坏作品的物质性质，以及作品在此时此地的充分在场，因而令人恐惧。”1

In their prescient and formidable critique of the Russian Formalists (published in 1928) Bakhtin and Medvedev make the point that the Formalists’ ‘resurrection of the word’ as a self-enclosed material entity is sterile and anti-social: ‘The fear of meaning, which, with its ‘‘not here’’ and ‘‘not now’’ is able to destroy the material nature of the work and the fullness of its presence in the here and now, is the fear which determines the poetic phonetics of the formalists.’1

巴赫金与梅德韦杰夫在这里不自觉地、而且远远提前地开启了后结构主义时代。形式主义者如此陶醉于自己的大胆，却被指责为恐惧，这是巧妙的论战招数；但它又不止于此，它关乎每个阅读文学的人，而不只是目光短浅的形式主义者。当意义可能把读者的注意力从眼前的词语上转移开时，许多读者就会害怕意义。巴赫金与梅德韦杰夫在此提供的深刻洞察，是关于某一文本的词语在我们阅读时，如何在心中生成更多、不同的词语。文学文本非但没有给我们提供语言稳定的立足点，反而使我们在语义空间中漂流。

Without knowing it, and well ahead of time, Bakhtin and Medvedev here inaugurate the age of post-Structuralism. To accuse the Formalists of fear, when they so revelled in their audacity, is a clever debating-point; but it is also something more, of relevance to everyone who reads literature, and not simply myopic Formalists. Many readers are afraid of meaning when it threatens to distract them from the words they have in front of them. The deep insight which Bakhtin and Medvedev are here offering is into the way in which the words of a given text generate more, and different, words in our minds as we read it. Far from affording us a point of verbal stability the literary text casts us adrift in semantic space.

这个论证惊人地预示了三十多年后法国哲学家雅克·德里达提出的论证；德里达几乎单枪匹马地创造了后结构主义。2德里达在20世纪60年代末崭露头角，当时结构主义在法国正是一股思想时尚，而且已有一些值得称道的成就。他无疑是结构主义迄今最有分量、最敏锐的批评者，同时也是一位思想有力、富于原创性的哲学家。在这里，我选择把德里达看作后结构主义者，而不采用另一种称呼“解构主义者”；他和追随者往往被如此称呼。这是因为，“后结构主义者”这一描述保留了与结构主义的密切关系，而德里达的许多效果正有赖于这种关系。后结构主义的“后”，不是已经消灭结构主义的意思；它只是后来出现，并试图沿着结构主义应当发展的方向扩展结构主义的意思。

The argument is remarkably prophetic of those put forward more than thirty years later by the French philosopher who has created post-Structuralism almost single-handed: Jacques Derrida.2 Derrida came to prominence in the late 1960s, when Structuralism was very much an intellectual fashion in France and had some reputable achievements behind it. He is quite certainly the weightiest and most acute critic Structuralism has had, as well as being a philosopher of power and originality. I choose here to consider Derrida as a post-Structuralist rather than by the alternative name of ‘deconstructionist’, by which both he and his followers have tended to be called, because the description of ‘post-Structuralist’ preserves within it the close relation to Structuralism on which Derrida depends for many of his effects. Post-Structuralism is not ‘post’ in the sense of having killed Structuralism off, it is ‘post’ only in the sense of coming after and of seeking to extend Structuralism in its rightful direction.

后结构主义是从内部对结构主义进行的批评：也就是说，它把结构主义的某些论证转过来针对结构主义本身，指出结构主义者忽视的、其方法中的某些根本不一致。它比结构主义更彻底地把语言视为结构系统的模型，并从语言的运作方式中得出出人意料的根本结论。最终，德里达的雄心远远超出仅仅指出通常实践中的结构主义的弱点：他要拆解自柏拉图时代以来的整个西方思想体系，因为他认为，这个体系未能把握语言与意义的性质，这种应受责备的失败使它走入歧途。在德里达的工作中，索绪尔制定的原则服务于一种全面而怀疑性的目的，即动摇一种占主导地位、且通常不自觉的“唯心主义”；这种唯心主义断言，语言不是创造意义，而是揭示意义，从而暗示意义先于其表达而存在。对德里达来说，这毫无道理。在他看来，不存在未被表述的意义：我们无法越出语言。

Post-Structuralism is a critique of Structuralism conducted from within: that is, it turns certain of Structuralism’s arguments against itself and points to certain fundamental inconsistencies in their method which Structuralists have ignored. It takes language to be the model of a structural system more whole-heartedly even than Structuralism does, and draws unexpectedly fundamental conclusions from the way in which language works. Ultimately, Derrida’s ambitions are much larger than merely to point to the weaknesses of Structuralism as commonly practised: they are to dismantle the whole system of Western thought since the time of Plato, because he believes it has been led astray by its reprehensible failure to grasp the nature of language and of meaning. In the work of Derrida the principles laid down by Saussure serve the sweeping and sceptical purpose of undermining a prevailing and generally unconscious ‘idealism’, which asserts that language does not create meanings but reveals them, thereby implying that meanings pre-exist their expression. This for Derrida is a nonsense. For him there can be no meaning which is not formulated: we cannot reach outside language.

就此处的目的而言，理解德里达思想的最佳途径，或许是考察他关于索绪尔的论述。他关于索绪尔的论证，是他针对其他思想家与作家提出的论证的典型：它们极为出色地展示索绪尔在哪里未能把握自身理论的全部意义。这是德里达“解构”方法的专长：把即使不普遍存在于所有创作性写作、也普遍存在于所有哲学写作中的“盲点”揭示出来（我知道，把两者等同起来是可疑的）。“盲点”之所以存在，是因为任何写作者都不是自己所用语言的完全主人，也不是语言在读者或听者心中生成的意义的完全主人。

The best access to Derrida’s ideas for present purposes may be had by considering what he has written about Saussure, his arguments in respect of whom are typical of those he has advanced against other thinkers and writers in that they show very brilliantly where Saussure failed to grasp the full significance of his own theories. This is the speciality of Derrida’s ‘deconstructive’ method, which brings into the open the ‘blind spots’ endemic in all philosophical writing if not in all creative writing (the assimilation of the two is, I am aware, dubious). The ‘blind spots’ are there because no one who writes is wholly master of the language which they use or of the meanings which it generates in the minds of those who read or hear it.

在索绪尔看来，语言符号的性质是差异性的：它凭借它不是什么，才成为它是什么；除了作为系统成员所获得的价值之外，它没有价值。德里达接受这项公理的真实性，但比索绪尔扩展得更广。在他那里，差异性成为关键概念，对我们理解世界与自身的方式具有巨大的影响。正确理解差异性，就会发现它与德里达所称的“在场形而上学”相矛盾；他声称，后者支配西方思想已超过两千年。“在场形而上学”假定，凡向我们在场的东西，都是完整而直接地在场，被不借助符号的纯粹直觉行为所把握。在场先于表意。然而，若真如此，就很难看出我们如何能够意识到在场，因为意识确实要借助符号。因此，按照德里达的说法，在场绝不能是直接的，只能由语言中介。我们意识到向我们在场的东西，是把它意识为在场；而在场概念像所有概念一样，不是自我封闭的统一体，而是有其反面栖居其中的概念。如果某物不可能“不在场”，那么断言它“在场”就没有意义：在场概念蕴含非在场或缺席的概念，因为语言的根本性质就是如此差异性的。例如，“当下时刻”被假定的完整性，结果是一种幻觉：“这样，我们很快就认识到，当下时刻的在场，只有在不断与非在场和非知觉复合的限度内，才能作为在场显现；也就是说，与原初记忆和期待（滞留与前摄）复合。”3（斜体为德里达所加。）

For Saussure the linguistic sign is differential in nature: it is what it is by virute of what it isn’t, having no value apart from that which it derives from membership of its system. Derrida accepts the truth of this axiom but extends it more widely than Saussure. With him differentiality becomes a key concept, with enormous implications for the way in which we understand both the world and ourselves. Properly understood, it contradicts what Derrida terms the ‘metaphysics of presence’, which he claims has dominated Western thought for more than two thousand years. The ‘metaphysics of presence’ presumes that whatever is present to us is wholly and immediately so, grasped in an act of pure intuition which has no recourse to signs. Presence precedes signification. If this were the case, however, it is hard to see how we could be conscious of it, since consciousness does have recourse to signs. Presence, according to Derrida, can never be immediate therefore, only mediated by language. We are conscious of what is present to us as being present; and the concept of presence, like all concepts, is not a self-enclosed unity, but one inhabited by its contrary. There can be no meaning to asserting that something is ‘present’ if there is no possibility of its being ‘not-present’: the concept of presence entails that of non-presence or absence, for such is the fundamentally differential nature of language. The presumed integrity of ‘the present moment’, for example, turns out to be an illusion: ‘One thus very quickly realizes that the presence of the present moment can only appear as such to the extent that it is continuously compounded with a non-presence and a non-perception, to wit primary memory and expectation (retention and protention)’3 (Derrida’s italics).

这里的词汇来自胡塞尔，德里达正在批评他的现象学。但德里达提出的论证切入很深：不可能有绝对、未经中介的“在场”这样的概念，因为一旦我们意识到某物向我们在场，它就已经进入符号与意义的回路。“在场”的概念由于与“缺席”概念对立而获得价值，在场仿佛被缺席污染了：就当下时刻而言，是被它与过去和未来的结构关系污染了。当下时刻之所以是它所是的，是因为它属于一个时间序列。

The vocabulary here is that of Husserl, a critique of whose phenomenology Derrida is conducting. But the argument he advances cuts deep: there can be no such concept as absolute, unmediated ‘presence’ because as soon as we are aware of something being present to us it has entered into the circuit of signs and meanings. The notion of ‘presence’ acquires its value because of its opposition to the notion of ‘absence’, and the present is as it were contaminated by the absent: in the case of the present moment by its structural relation to the past and the future. The present moment is what it is by virtue of its membership of a temporal series.

我们知道，借助语言或符号，就意味着丧失一切独一性或直接性。符号不是事物本身。德里达强调符号的“可迭代性”，符号按定义是可以重复的：“如果事件意味着不可替代、不可逆转的经验独一性，那么符号就从来不是事件。一个‘只发生一次’的符号，不会是符号。一个纯粹个人独有的符号，不会是符号。”4符号的同一性在于其重复的可能：每次出现时，我们都把某个特定符号认作与此前出现时相同的符号。它是一个“理念的”对象，不能等同于其实际出现的实例——或者，用皮尔士那套有帮助的术语，它是类型，而每次出现都是该类型的一个实例。

The resort to language or signs entails, we know, the loss of all uniqueness or immediacy. The sign is not the thing itself. Derrida stresses the ‘iterativity’ of signs, which are by definition repeatable: ‘A sign is never an event if event means an irreplaceable and irreversible empirical uniqueness. A sign which took place ‘‘only once’’ would not be a sign. A purely idiomatic sign would not be a sign.’4 In the possibility of their repetition lies the identity of signs: each time it occurs we recognize a particular sign as the same sign as on previous occurrences. It is an ‘ideal’ object, not to be identified with its actual occurrences – or, in the helpful terminology of Peirce, it is the type of which each occurrence is a token.

对德里达而言，时间的结构与语言的结构相同。我们可能认为，“当下时刻”是一个绝对简单、无法分析的点；然而，“当下”一词显而易见的相对性，打消了这种想法。“当下时刻”不是点，而是结构，其存在依赖于它与过去和未来的关系。像任何语言符号一样，它有其他符号“栖居”其中；我们可以说，“没有符号是一座孤岛”。每个符号都包含德里达所称的其他符号的“踪迹”。这是一个难以把握的概念，因为“踪迹”并不真实，它并非“真的在那里”。毋宁说，它是“符号性”内在的一种潜能，也是语言这类差异系统背后的原则。正因为有“踪迹”，我们才能说，一个符号总会引向另一个符号，处在严格说来无穷无尽的解释过程中。因此，它是语言作为系统的“根基”，而要从语言系统本身内部给它下定义，大概是不可能的。事实上，“踪迹”的难以捉摸，证明了它的重要性，因为它说明符号绝不能在自身之内完整，而是无休止地把我们引向其他符号。（“踪迹”这个符号本身，也不能免于这一过程。）

The structure of time and of language is identical for Derrida. Any idea we might have that the ‘present moment’ is an absolutely simple point incapable of analysis is scotched by the patent relativism of the term ‘present’. The ‘present moment’ is not a point but a structure, depending for its existence on its relations with past and future. Like any linguistic sign it is ‘inhabited’ by other signs; ‘No sign is an island’ we might say. Every sign contains what Derrida calls a ‘trace’ of signs other than itself. This is a difficult concept to catch hold of inasmuch as the ‘trace’ it not real, it is not ‘really there’. Rather, it is a potentiality inherent in ‘signhood’ and the principle underlying any system of differentiation such as language. It is because of the ‘trace’ that we can say that one sign leads always to another, in a process of interpretation that is strictly interminable. It is thus the ‘ground’ of language as a system, and to be able to define it from within the language-system itself is presumably impossible. Indeed, the elusiveness of the ‘trace’ is a proof of its significance, since it demonstrates that signs can never be complete in themselves but refer us endlessly to other signs. (The sign trace itself cannot be exempted from the process.)

德里达也把这种无穷指涉的过程称作“延异”（différance）。这是他自造的词，由意为“推迟”的动词 différer 构成一个新的法语名词。这个新词在法国人听来又与 différence（差异）同音，因此我们应当把它理解为兼有“产生差异”和“延宕”之意，因为在德里达看来，这两者是同一过程。（différance 与 différence 在法语中发音完全相同，却能在印刷文字中凭视觉区分，这一点也很重要；我还会回到这个问题。）从一个符号指向另一个符号的过程，在理论上永远不必停止，因为我们不可能最终逃出符号系统。德里达无情地提醒我们，每一个新的所指也都是能指，如此以至无穷。美国哲学家 W. V. 奎因的著作中也有同样的提醒，他写道：“所谓给出一个话语的意义，无非是说出一个同义表达，通常用比原话更清楚的语言来表述。”5 唯心主义者要我们相信、而德里达及其追随者称为“超验所指”的那种东西——完全处于语言之外的意义——根本不存在。这样的意义将绝对无法传达，甚至无法向我们自己传达。

This process of endless referral is also termed by Derrida ‘différance’, a coinage of his own which forms a new French noun from the verb différer meaning to ‘defer’. This new form is also to the French ear a homophone of the word différence, so that we should understand it as meaning both ‘differing’ and ‘deferring’, since for Derrida these are the same process. (The fact that différance and différence sound exactly the same in French but can be distinguished by the eye when they appear in print also has its importance: to this I shall return.) The process of referral from sign to sign need never in theory be arrested because there can be no final escape from the sign-system. Every new signified is, Derrida cruelly reminds us, also a signifier, and so on ad infinitum. One meets with the same reminder in the work of the American philosopher W. V. Quine, when he writes: ‘What is called giving the meaning of an utterance is simply the uttering of a synonym, couched, ordinarily, in clearer language than the original.’5 There is no such thing as what Derrida and Derridans choose to label the ‘transcendental signified’, which the idealists ask us to believe in: a meaning outside language altogether. That would be perfectly incommunicable, even to ourselves.

延异不可逃避，因而在意义生成过程中也不可能逃避时间。结构主义倾向于用空间的方式处理其研究的系统，仿佛构成系统的所有先后相继的要素其实同时存在。索绪尔对“共存”的强调，使人可以按需要自由重新排列事物。但是，这种空间性或无时间性的模型，是德里达急于揭露的一种幻觉。结构主义者未能承认自己的研究具有时间性——研究是先后进行的，而不是同时进行的——便助长了这种幻觉。

The inescapability of différance ensures that there can be no escape from time in the process of signification. Structuralism has tended to treat the systems it investigates in spatial terms, as if all the successive elements of which they are made were in fact present simultaneously. The Saussurean emphasis on the ‘coexistent’ sets one free to reorder things as one requires. But such spatial or timeless models are an illusion which Derrida is eager to expose. It is fostered by the Structuralist’s failure to acknowledge the temporality of his own investigations, which are successive and not simultaneous.

口语和书面语之间的明显区别也助长了这种幻觉，而这一区别对德里达至关重要。我们不大可能误以为口头说出的词语序列可以从时间中抽离，因为它们不会被保存下来（如果被录音，它们的功能就不再仅仅是口头话语）；当词语序列被写下、因而获得持久性时，我们才容易认为它们以某种方式同时存在。一本书的文本——或者通常所说的一“卷”（volume），这个称呼已经相当清楚地表明，我们的语言如何把时间转化为空间——未必让我们觉得它延展于时间之中，尽管我们知道，读完它会花去我们自己的好几个小时；而且，如果我们是结构主义者，我们也不会忘记索绪尔强调过一切组合段都具有严格的线性。

It is fostered also by the obvious difference between the spoken and the written forms of language, which is of central importance to Derrida. We are not likely to make the mistake of thinking spoken sequences of words to be capable of abstraction from time, because they are not preserved (if they are recorded, they no longer function as merely spoken words); it is when sequences of words are written and thus made permanent that we are easily able to think of them as somehow existing simultaneously. The text of a book – or of what we commonly call a ‘volume’, which is a clear enough indication of how our language transforms the temporal into the spatial – does not necessarily strike us as being extended in time, even though we know that it will take us several hours of our own time to read it, and even though, if we are Structuralists, we will not have forgotten the stress laid by Saussure on the strict linearity of all syntagma.

在德里达看来，对文学或其他文本的“结构性阅读”如果忽略了文本在时间中的实际延展，就伴随一种特别的危险：它也会成为目的论式的阅读。如果能够证明文本具有某种结构，批评家就太容易进一步证明，这一结构正是文本及其作者的目标。这样，文本便被歪曲为实现一种先于它存在的结构；它成了预先确定的东西。许多文本在一定程度上确实预先确定了，因为作者会依照计划写作。但这只能说到一定程度；计划不能与最终文本混为一谈。尤其不能把计划看作本身是非文本的东西，因为那是不可能的。计划也是一组措辞，因而也是文本或结构；它绝不可能被提出，作为另一个文本的非文本性“起源”。

The ‘structural reading’ of a literary or other text which ignores its real extension in time carries with it for Derrida the particular danger that it will also be a teleological reading. If the text can be shown to have a structure, it is all too easy for the critic then to show that this structure is the object of the text and of its author. The text is thus perverted into the realization of a structure which pre-existed it; it is pre-determined. Up to a point many texts are pre-determined, since their authors will be writing to a plan. But only up to a point; the plan is not to be confused with the final text. Nor, above all, is the plan to be thought of as something in itself non-textual, because that is impossible. The plan too is a form of words and as such a text or structure; it can never be offered as the a-textual ‘origin’ of another text.

为了对抗助长这类幻觉的有害唯心主义，德里达提出了自己强有力的创造观。文本之前，什么也没有：

Against the pernicious idealism which promotes such illusions, Derrida pits his own strong notion of creativity. Before the text there is nothing:

写作，就是知道：尚未在文字中产生的东西没有别的栖身之所，并没有作为一种预先规定，存在于某个天上境域（topos ouranios）或神的理智中等待我们。意义必须等到被说出或写出，才能居于自身之中，才能通过与自身相异而成为其所是：意义。6

To write is to know that what is not yet produced in the letter has no other dwelling-place, is not waiting for us as a prescription in some topos ouranios or divine understanding. The meaning must wait to be said or written in order to inhabit itself and to become what, by differing from itself, it is: meaning.6

德里达这里是在反驳瑞士形式主义批评家让·鲁塞。他指责鲁塞强调文本的形式，却损害了文本的力量，即德里达认为在文本中发挥作用的创造性能量。有时，他对创造性的理解确实使人觉得他带有鲜明的浪漫主义色彩——这里用的是文学史意义上的“浪漫主义”——尽管他把创造意义的力量归于语言，而不是使用语言的个人。

Derrida is arguing here against a Swiss Formalist critic, Jean Rousset, whom he accuses of emphasizing the form of a text to the detriment of its force, which is that creative energy Derrida sees to be at work in it. There are times indeed when he strikes one as decidedly Romantic, in the literary historical sense of that term, in his understanding of creativity, even though he attributes the power to create meanings to language, not to the individuals who employ it.

德里达希望把“在场的形而上学”从我们的思想中驱除，这使他作为作家把注意力专门集中在书面语言而非口头语言上；实际上，他用书面形式向我们说明，我们通常如何误解口头形式的性质。他相信自己由此在反对思考和撰写语言问题的人长期怀有的一种偏见，而这一判断是对的：这种偏见认为书面语言从属于口头语言，是对被视为第一性的口头语言的再现。如果回顾几个世纪的语言思想，人们就会同意，德里达有理由认为书写或者遭到忽视，或者被看作派生物。例如，萨丕尔这样敏锐而进步的语言学家，也给自己的《语言》（Language）加上副标题“言语研究导论”；布龙菲尔德在同样题为《语言》的书中，则在开头不久作出惊人的断言：“书写不是语言，而仅仅是借助可见标记记录语言的一种方式。”7

Derrida’s wish to exorcise the ‘metaphysics of presence’ from our thinking has led him to concentrate his attention as a writer exclusively on the written form of language as against the spoken; indeed, he uses the written form to show us how we commonly misunderstand the nature of the spoken one. In this he believes, rightly, that he is countering a long-standing prejudice among those who have thought and written about language: that the written language is subordinate to the spoken, that it is the representation of the spoken language, itself seen as primary. If one casts an eye over the centuries of linguistic thought, one will agree that Derrida is justified in supposing that writing has been either neglected or seen as derivative. A linguist as alert and progressive as Sapir, for example, subtitles his book on Language ‘An introduction to the study of speech’, while Bloomfield, in his book also called Language, makes early on the remarkable statement that ‘Writing is not language, but merely a way of recording language by means of visible marks’.7

如此赋予口语以高于书写的优先地位，无疑是语言思想漫长历史中的正统观点。它在索绪尔的《教程》中十分突出，书中抱怨书写享有不应有的威望。8 德里达要取消任何这样的优先地位。这不是像有人想象的那样，因为他试图将其颠倒，把作为语言形式的书写置于口语之上，而是为了通过表明口语和书写实际上都是同一个系统的形式，将这种优先关系消除。德里达声称，传统上赋予口语的优先地位，来自西方思想根深蒂固的“语音中心主义”。我们对人的声音怀有感情，又受它迷惑，以至忘记：即使在说话时，或在内心与自己说话、也就是思考时，我们仍然诉诸符号。由于我们必然要在一个并非只属于我们、而是一种社会制度的符号系统中与自己交流，我们甚至不能完全向自己“在场”。德里达认为，“听自己说话”是一种亲密性和直接性的虚幻模型，它使我们可以方便地忽略符号的真实性质。这个模型把交流回路缩得如此之短，以至它可能不再显得是一个回路：

Giving the priority to speech over writing in this way is undoubtedly the orthodoxy in the long history of linguistic thought. It is very much to the fore in Saussure’s Course, where the complaint is made that writing enjoys an undeserved prestige.8 Derrida would abolish any such priority. Not, as some have imagined, because he is seeking to reverse it, and to set writing above speech as a form of language, but in order to remove it, by showing how speech and writing are both in fact forms of the one system. The traditional priority accorded to speech comes about, Derrida claims, because of the deep ‘phonocentrism’ of Western thought. We are sentimental towards the human voice and deluded by it into forgetting that even when we speak, or speak inwardly with ourselves, i.e. think, we still have recourse to signs. We cannot be fully ‘present’ even to ourselves in so far as we must of necessity commune with ourselves in a system of signs that is not ours alone but a social institution. ‘Hearing ourselves speak’ is the illusory model of intimacy and immediacy which Derrida suggests has enabled us conveniently to ignore the true nature of signs. It is a model which shortens the circuit of communication so much that it may no longer appear as a circuit:

当我说话时，我不仅意识到自己向所思考的东西在场，而且意识到自己使一个能指尽可能紧密地贴近我的思想或“概念”；这个能指没有落入世界，我一说出它便理解了它，它似乎依赖于我纯粹而自由的自发性，似乎无需使用任何工具、任何辅助物、任何取自世界的力量。9

When I speak, not only am I conscious of being present to what I am thinking, but also of preserving in the greatest possible closeness to my thought or to the ‘concept’ a signifier which does not fall into the world, which I understand as soon as I utter it, which seems to depend on my pure and free spontaneity, to require the use of no instrument, no accessory, no force taken from the world.9

也就是说，符号真正的非个人性被遮蔽了：我们与听到自己说出的东西合而为一。如果这种“语音中心主义”幻觉真像德里达所坚持的那样普遍而顽固——它之所以得到支持，部分是因为我们依赖语音字母表，而语言学家通常把这种字母表看作人类在语音分析方面的一项非凡成就——那么他就必须解释为什么会这样。难道除了他自己，所有人都错了或受了蒙蔽吗？他的解释是，如此根本的矛盾只能是“欲望”的结果。这是德里达思想中的弗洛伊德成分。我们知道，即使与自己交流，也不能完全向自己“在场”，但我们却设法相信相反的情况。如果我正确领会了德里达，这就是压抑的结果：我们无法面对事情的真相，即语言甚至使我们与自我疏离。我们珍爱“语音中心主义”的亲密幻觉，因为承认它是幻觉，就等于承认我们在自己所说的话中缺席。德里达喜欢强调，从结构上理解的语言系统，是一种死亡警示（memento mori），因为它提醒我们：我们可以使用它，却不能拥有它。从结构上说，我们使用的词语并不需要我们在场才能有所意味；我们阅读并理解那么多已故作者的印刷文字，就知道这一点。语言的结构性质，即一种持续而无穷指涉的系统，也提醒我们无法逃避时间的运动，人的生命本身具有严格的线性。

That is, the true impersonality of signs is obscured from us: we are at one with what we hear ourselves say. If this ‘phonocentric’ illusion is as pervasive and as tenacious as Derrida maintains that it is – it has been sustained, in part, by our reliance on a phonetic alphabet, customarily seen by linguists as an extraordinary human achievement in the phonological analysis of speech sounds – then he is faced by the problem of explaining why this should be so. Can everyone be wrong or deluded but himself? His explanation is that such a fundamental contradiction can only be the effect of ‘desire’. This is the Freudian component of Derrida’s thought. We know that even when we commune with ourselves we cannot be fully ‘present’ to ourselves, but we manage to believe the opposite. This, if I follow Derrida properly, is the result of repression: we cannot face the truth of the matter, which is that we are alienated even from our selves by language. We cherish the ‘phonocentric’ illusion of intimacy because to acknowledge that it is an illusion is to acknowledge our own absence from the words we speak. Derrida likes to stress that the language-system, structurally understood, is a memento mori, because it reminds us that we may employ it but that we cannot posses it. The words which we use do not, structurally speaking, need us there in order to mean something, as we know from reading and understanding the printed words of so many dead authors. The structural nature of language, a system of constant and endless referral, also reminds us that no escape is to be got from the movement of time, that human life itself is strictly linear.

通过书面语言，让我们把符号看作死亡的提醒，对德里达来说比较容易，因为书面符号更加明显地处于提供符号者之外。但在这一点上，它们与口头符号并无不同，后者也同样外在于我们。口头符号与书面符号都是德里达所谓“原书写”（archi-writing）的两种形式，即作为两者基础的系统。这是一个纯粹差异性的系统：索绪尔由于对书写的偏见，以及在意义问题上残存的唯心主义，未能恰当地认识它。“原书写”是绝对根本而无所不包的：“尽管‘符号的任意性’和差异这两个主题要求提出这种原书写的概念，它却不能、也永远不可能被认作一门科学的对象。它是那种不容自己被还原为在场形式的东西。”10 因而，德里达的“原书写”“踪迹”和“延异”都服务于同一个目的：使我们始终不安地意识到符号的不稳定性和差异性，以及它们的外在性或客观性。“听自己说话”这一“语音中心主义”模型，在“看自己写字”中有其对应物；在后一种情况下，我们不太容易受幻觉欺骗，因为我们使用的符号显然如德里达所说，正“落入世界”，而且显然是“辅助物”。（录音形式的“听自己说话”，以一种非常有利于德里达论点的方式，颇为无情地使说话接近书写：它使我们与自己之间那种舒适的亲密感发生疏离。）

It is simpler for Derrida to make us think of signs as reminders of mortality by dealing with written language, because written signs are more self-evidently external to those who proffer them. But they are no different in this from spoken signs, which are equally external to us. Spoken and written signs are two forms of what Derrida calls ‘archi-writing’, the system that underlies them both. This is a system of pure differentiality: the system that Saussure failed properly to recognize because of his prejudice against writing and his residual idealism in respect of meanings. ‘Archi-writing’ is absolutely fundamental and all-embracing: ‘This archi-writing, although its concept is called for by the themes of the ‘‘arbitrariness of the sign’’ and of difference, cannot and can never be recognized as the object of a science. It is what does not allow itself to be reduced to the form of presence.’10 Thus ‘archi-writing’, ‘trace’ and ‘différance’ all function to one end in Derrida: to keep us disturbingly aware of the unstable and differential nature of signs, and of their externality or objectivity. The ‘phonocentric’ model of the ‘hearing ourselves speak’ finds its equivalent in the ‘watching ourselves write’; in this instance we are less prone to fall victim to illusion because the signs we use are so very obviously ‘falling into the world’, as Derrida puts it, and are so very obviously ‘accessory’. (The ‘hearing ourselves speak’ in the form of a tape-recording approximates speaking rather brutally to writing in a manner highly supportive of Derrida’s thesis: it alienates us from our comfortable intimacy with ourselves.)

德里达本人惊人的解构才能，大多施展于哲学家的文本。这有充分理由：哲学家提出论证，因此比不作论证的作家更容易接受解构。然而，文学批评也十分迷恋德里达的方法，这相当合乎逻辑，因为不承认哲学写作与其他写作之间的任何区别，正是这些方法不可分割的一部分。在德里达看来，由于语言，或者更确切地说“原书写”的自主性，所有写作都不可避免地具有隐喻性。后结构主义在文学领域兴盛，是因为文学批评过去一直很容易陷入“语音中心主义”的幻觉。我们太经常把读到的东西等同于听到的东西。事实上，只要稍稍反省讨论书面文本时使用的词汇，就能领会德里达论点的有效性：我们多么经常说作家“说”了这个或那个，而不是“写”了它；我们多么经常读到——还是说“听到”？——作家为某种“听众”而不是读者写作；还有许多文学批评家谈论作者的“声音”，以及读者对这个声音的“倾听”。批评词汇不断偏向语音，仿佛文学作品是说话者对听者说话，而不是写作者面向读者。尽管心理学家如今已经告诉我们，我们完全能够在阅读时不向自己默默“读出”沿途的词语，情况仍是如此。看来，无论在作者一方还是读者一方，“声音”都已经不再参与其中。

Derrida’s own astonishing deconstructive gifts have been exercised for the most part on the texts of philosophers, for the good reason that philosophers offer arguments and are more readily open to deconstruction than writers who do not. Nevertheless, literary criticism has been much taken with Derrida’s methods, and this is logical enough, because it is part and parcel of those methods not to recognize any distinction between philosophical and other kinds of writing. All, for Derrida, are inescapably metaphorical, because of the autonomy of language or, better, of the ‘archi-writing’. Post-Structuralism has flourished in the literary field because literary criticism has in the past been much given to the illusion of ‘phonocentrism’. We all too often assimilate what we read to what we hear. Indeed, one need only reflect for an instant on the kind of vocabulary we resort to in the discussion of written texts to appreciate the validity of Derrida’s case: how often are writers not held to ‘say’ this or that, rather than to ‘write’ it; how often do we not read (or do I mean ‘hear’?) of a writer writing for a particular ‘audience’, instead of a readership; and there are many literary critics who write of an author’s ‘voice’ and of readers ‘listening’ to that voice. The critical vocabulary strays constantly towards the phonetic, as if the literary work were addressed by a speaker to a hearer, not by a writer to a reader. And this despite the fact that psychologists now assure us that we are perfectly well able to read without ‘pronouncing’ the words to ourselves silently as we go. ‘Voice’, it seems, no longer comes into it, either on the author’s side or on the reader’s.

把文学文本视为某种“说出”的东西，这种广泛流行却误导人的观点，一旦想到我们多么急切地希望文学具有“表现性”，便不难理解了：也就是以高度个人化的方式，把作者最内在的思想和情感传达给我们。这是关于作者身份的抒情式观点，其推论是：作者必须使用的符号本身也具有“表现性”。符号具有表现功能，这一点毋庸置疑；它们是某种主体性的载体。但它们也具有“指示性”，这是德里达用来与“表现性”相对照的术语：当我们的感受是私人的、不可言说的，而我们的话语是约定俗成的、公共的，感受与话语之间就存在一道只能勉力跨越的鸿沟。纯粹的表现性无法传达，因为它处于共同符号系统的范围之外：

This widespread but misleading view of the literary text as something ‘spoken’ becomes understandable when one remembers how eager we are for literature to be ‘expressive’; to communicate to us, that is, the inmost thoughts and sentiments of the author in a highly individualized way. This is the lyrical view of authorship, and its corollary is that the signs which authors must use are also themselves ‘expressive’. Signs have an expressive function, that is not in doubt; they are the vehicle of a certain subjectivity. But they are also ‘indicative’, a term which Derrida opposes to ‘expressive’: there is a gap which we can only struggle to bridge between what we feel and what we utter, when the first is private and ineffable and the second conventional and public. Pure expressivity is incommunicable because it lies outside the scope of our communal systems of signs:

在我的话语中，凡是意在向别人呈现某种体验的东西，都必须经过物理层面的中介。这种不可消除的中介，使每一种表现都承担一种指示操作。呈现的功能就是指示功能。这里，我们接近了指示的根源：每当赋予意义的行为、赋予生命的意向、“意欲说”（vouloir-dire）的活生生的精神性并不完全在场时，就有指示。11

Everything in my discourse which is destined to manifest an experience to someone else, must pass through the mediation of the physical aspect. This irreducible mediation commits every expression to an indicative operation. The function of manifestation is an indicative function. Here we come close to the root of indication: there is indication each time the act which confers meaning, the animating intention, the living spirituality of the vouloir-dire, is not fully present.11

德里达所要求的，不是让我们放弃文学有所“表现”的一切观念，而是以恰当的意识来调节这种观念：文学也必须有所“指示”。在上面的引文中，我保留了未译的法语词 vouloir-dire，因为它非常巧妙地凸显了德里达所倚重的意欲与言说之间的分离。这是言说者与所说之物之间的分离；正如我们已经看到的，它是整个结构主义事业的根本。语言使用的 vouloir-dire 模型包含两个时刻或要素，一个是表现性的，一个是指示性的；对那些发现指示符号系统不能满足自身表现需要的人来说，这两个时刻之间的间隙，很可能成为极度挫折感的来源。

What Derrida asks is not that we abandon all notion that literature ‘expresses’ but that we temper it with a proper awareness that it must also ‘indicate’. In the quotation above I have left the French term vouloir-dire untranslated because it brings out very neatly the disjunction on which Derrida relies between willing and saying. The disjunction is between the sayer and the said, and it is fundamental, as we have seen, to the whole Structuralist enterprise. The vouloir-dire model of language-use involves two moments or elements, an expressive and an indicative one, and the gap between these two moments may well be a source of extreme frustration for those who find that a system of indicative signs fails to fulfil their expressive needs.

作为读者，我们接触到的是发挥指示功能的语言，但在阅读中，我们很可能希望恢复它的表现功能：“重新激活书写，始终是在指示中重新唤醒一种表现，在字母的躯体中重新唤醒一个词；这个字母作为一种可能永远空洞的象征，本身带有危机的威胁。”12 所谓“危机”的威胁，无非是文本可能永远不被重新激活，可能永远得不到赋予它表现性的阅读过程的激活。一切文本在写成的一刻都可能已经“死去”：它们被铭写下来，因此能够存续数百年，但这种铭写切断了它们与创作情境的联系，使它们在未来漂流，成为可能受到大量、或许互相冲突的重新激活的指示，也可能根本不被激活；因为，德里达冷静地提醒我们：“为了成为其所是，一切书写都必须能够在任何由经验确定的受话者彻底缺席的情况下运作。”13

As readers we meet with language in its indicative function, but as we read we may well hope to restore it to its expressive function: ‘To reactivate writing is always to reawaken an expression in an indication, a word in the body of a letter which, as a symbol which may remain forever empty, bore within it the threat of crisis.’12 The threatened ‘crisis’ is simply the chance of the text never being reactivated, of its remaining for ever unquickened by the reading process which endows it with expressivity. All texts are potentially ‘dead’ the moment they are written: they are inscribed and therefore capable of enduring for centuries, but this inscription cuts them loose from the circumstances of their composition and casts them adrift in the future, as indications susceptible of a great many, possibly conflicting reactivations, or of none; for, as Derrida coolly reminds us: ‘In order to be what it is, all writing must be able to function in the radical absence of any empirically determined addressee in general.’13

作者不可能控制自己的文本最终将遭遇的各种“重新激活”。也就是说，人们从文本中读出的意义，可能与作者相信自己赋予文本的意义一致，也可能不一致。合理的看法是：其中许多意义会一致，具体取决于作者和读者在时间、空间及文化上的距离；但另外许多意义不会一致。因为语言有不受使用者意愿支配的意义生成力量。在德里达看来，不可能存在一种让作者始终控制自己写出（或说出）之物的“掌控”概念。文本把意义从作者“传达”给读者的观念，必然受到质疑：

An author cannot conceivably control the eventual ‘reactivations’ to which his or her text will become subject. That is, the meanings that are read into it may or may not coincide with the meanings which the author believes he or she has invested it with. A reasonable view is that a large number of these meanings will coincide, depending on how far separated author and reader are in time, space and culture; but that a large number of other meanings will not coincide. For language has powers of generating meanings irrespective of the wishes of those who use it. For Derrida no concept of ‘mastery’ is possible, by which an author may remain in control of what he or she has written (or said). The idea of a text ‘communicating’ meanings from author to reader is called necessarily into question:

因此，[书写]不能再在交流这一范畴下得到理解，至少当我们把交流限定为意义的传递时是如此。反过来，在如此界定的书写的一般领域内，语义交流的效果可以被确定为特殊的、次生的、铭写的和补充的效果。14

[Writing] can no longer be understood then under the category of communication, at least if we take this in the restricted sense of the transmission of meaning. Inversely, it is in the general field of writing so defined that the effects of semantic communication may be determined as particular, secondary, inscribed and supplementary ones.14

德里达这类陈述被判为庸俗，因为它们对作者与读者之间能否建立真正的意义交流渠道，表现出极大的怀疑。我们作为文学学生所受的教育，使我们希望为在文学中发现的意义寻找归属，而通常唯一能将其归属的来源就是作者。把意义归于我们自己似乎不妥，尽管这些意义正是发生在我们身上的。然而，我们根本不必把它们归于任何人，无论作者还是读者，因为按照德里达的安排，它们可以归于语言本身，归于语言的指示功能。语言无疑才是意义的真正所在。

Statements such as these by Derrida have been adjudged philistine, demonstrating as they do a great scepticism towards the establishment of any true channel of communication of meanings between author and reader. We are brought up as students of literature to want to attribute the meanings we find in it, and the only source we can ordinarily attribute them to is the author. It seems improper to attribute them to ourselves, even though it is us in whom they occur. But there is no need to attribute them to a person at all, either to the author or to the reader, since by Derrida’s dispensation they may be attributed to language itself, in its indicative function. Language is quite certainly the real locus of meaning.

因此，历来对“作者的意义”与“读者的意义”可能不一致的忧虑，并不切合实际。任何文学文本都可以产生一个宽广的意义光谱，一端是得到确证的“作者的意义”，另一端是显然属于“读者的意义”的意义。两端之间，许多意义可能属于任一方。为它们确定归属有帮助吗？文本阐释的活力来自分歧，而不是共识；把意义诉诸已故或缺席作者的权威，通常是试图以压倒性力量消除分歧。如果这种不确定性使我们沮丧——我们不知道凭谁的权威，这个东西意味着那个东西——我们总可以努力尽可能充分地了解文本写作的背景，以缩小“作者的意义”与“读者的意义”之间的距离；过去大量文学批评的精力确实用于这一目的。但我们不应天真地以为能够消除这个间隙；不确定性始终存在。

Thus the old anxiety about possible disparities between ‘author’s meaning’ and ‘reader’s meaning’ is unrealistic. A great spectrum of meanings can be got from any literary text, with certified ‘author’s meanings’ at one end of it and blatant ‘reader’s meanings’ at the other. In between are many meanings which could be either. Does it help to attribute them? The vitality of textual interpretation comes from disagreement, not from consensus, and the reference of meanings to the authority of a dead or absent writer is usually an attempt to eliminate disagreement by force majeure. If we find the uncertainty demoralizing, of not knowing on whose authority this means that, it is always possible to work to narrow the gap between ‘author’s meaning’ and ‘reader’s meaning’ by informing ourselves as fully as we can about the context in which the text was written – this is the end indeed to which much literary critical energy in the past has been bent. But we should not be so naïve as to imagine that we can ever close the gap; uncertainty remains.

把我们在文本中发现的每一个意义都归于作者，是荒谬的。像巴特在《S/Z》中对巴尔扎克的中篇小说所尝试的那样，将意义归入五种多少具有约定俗成性质的“代码”，也仍然不够，因为德里达教给我们的是：文本是一个令人惊恐、无法遏止的“播散”过程的发生之所。

To refer every meaning we discover in a text to its author would be absurd. To refer it, as Barthes attempts to do with Balzac’s novella in S/Z, to five more or less conventional ‘codes’, is still not adequate, because what Derrida teaches us is that the text is the locus of an alarming and unstemmable process of ‘dissemination’.

“播散”把巴特及其他结构主义者津津乐道的观念——任何文学文本都有多重意义——进一步推展开来。即使在巴特那个时期，这种语义的过度增殖，也遭到一些批评家愤怒的反对。他们宁愿相信，人们可以就每个文本的规范意义取得一致，阅读时并非“怎么都行”。巴特指责他的对手，即大学教授们，固执偏狭并患有“象征失认症”（a-symbolia），意思是他们看不到文学语言丰富的内涵性质。他，以及其后更加兴高采烈的德里达，都为认识到文本以惊人的慷慨不断生成意义而欢欣鼓舞。没有什么能阻止它们。文学权威可能以不合理、不一致、不符合历史等等各种理由排除某些意义，努力限制意义的数量；但这些仍然是已经产生、并且会继续产生的意义。

‘Dissemination’ carries further the notion relished by Barthes and by other Structuralists of the multiplicity of meanings to be found in any literary text. Even in Barthes’s day this semantic hypertrophy met with angry opposition from critics who preferred to believe that canonical meanings can be agreed on for each text, and that it is not the case, when we read, that ‘anything goes’. Barthes accused his opponents, the university professors, of bigotry and ‘a-symbolia’, meaning that they were blind to the richly connotative nature of literary language. He, and Derrida even more exuberantly after him, celebrates the knowledge that texts generate meanings with terrible liberality. There is no stopping them. The literary authorities may work to limit their number by excluding certain meanings on various grounds, as unreasonable, inconsistent, unhistorical, and so on; but these are still meanings that have arisen and that will continue to arise.

传统上约束文本意义的一种特别的手段，就是摘要或改述。人们期待对“这一段（或这个文本）是什么意思？”的回答，比该段或文本本身短得多。给出一篇文字的意义，就意味着有意无意地删去它的大量语义内容。后结构主义和结构主义一样，质疑我们是否有权心安理得地进行这种删节。德里达强烈反对能够为文本意义作出摘要的观念，因为摘要非但没有替代或略去文本中的特定要素，反而增添了要素，因为摘要本身也是新的文本要素。如果我们把“摘要”误认为另一文本的“超验所指”，它就是一种幻觉；实际上，它是一个独立的文本。因此，德里达多年来转而撰写关于序言和导论这类看似“边缘”的文学形式的文章，以反驳正统观点认为它们可以不受文本性铁律约束的印象。在一种典型的“解构”操作中，他用一个序言式章节来开启题为《播散》（Dissemination）的论文集，而这个章节却瓦解了这类序言的整个观念：仿佛序言可以预先概括尚未存在的东西。这是一种虚假的“本质主义”。这一章的法语标题具有讽刺意味，叫作“horslivre”，字面意思是“书外”，尽管它明明在书内。这种讽刺针对我们中间许许多多以本质主义方式区分一本书的“主要”内容与“附属”内容的人。

One way in particular that the meanings of a text have traditionally been kept on a leash is by the device of the summary or paraphrase. The answer to the question ‘What does this passage (or text) mean?’ is expected to be a good deal shorter than the passage or text itself. To give the meaning of a piece of writing is to eliminate, consciously or not, a great deal of its semantic content. Post-Structuralism, like Structuralism, contests our right to practise this sort of abridgement with a clear conscience. Derrida resists very strongly the idea that summaries can be given of a text’s meaning(s), because the summaries, far from replacing or eliding particular elements in the text, actually add to them, being further textual elements themselves. A ‘summary’ is an illusion if we mistake it for the ‘transcendental signified’ of another text; rather, it is a text in its own right, which is why Derrida, over the years, has turned to writing about such apparently ‘marginal’ forms of literature as Prefaces and Introductions, in order to counter the orthodox impression of them as absolved from the iron regime of textuality.
