In a typical ‘deconstructive’ manoeuvre, he opens his book of essays called Dissemination with a prefatory chapter which undermines the whole notion of such prefaces, understood as encapsulating in advance what has not yet come into existence. This is a false ‘essentialism’. Derrida’s chapter has the ironic title in French of ‘horslivre’ or, literally, ‘outside the book’, even though it is very much inside the book. The irony is at the expense of those many of us who discriminate in essentialist terms between the ‘main’ and the ‘subsidiary’ contents of a book.

德里达有效地运用文本“播散”的观念，反驳那些试图为某个结构找到“中心”、因而在他看来背叛了索绪尔关键洞见的结构主义者。对德里达来说，结构没有中心，正因为它们是结构。谈论语言或任何类似系统的“中心”，不可能有意义。这些系统始终处于“游戏”之中。由于这一认识不受许多渴望“中心”与“本质”的人欢迎，它便通过围绕某个固定点为结构虚设中心而遭到“中和”：

Derrida employs the notion of textual ‘dissemination’ to good effect against those Structuralists whom he sees as having betrayed the crucial insights of Saussure in trying to locate the ‘centre’ of a particular structure. Structures for Derrida have no centre, because they are structures. It can make no sense to speak of the ‘centre’ of a language or of any other such system. These systems are in constant ‘play’. Because this is a realization unpopular with many, who crave ‘centres’ and ‘essences’, it has been ‘neutralized’ by a false centring of structures round a fixed point:

这个中心的功能，不仅在于给[结构]确定方向、赋予它平衡、组织结构——事实上，我们无法想象一个未被组织的结构——尤其还在于确保结构的组织原则限制我们可以称为结构之游戏的东西。15

This centre has as its function not only to orientate [the structure] and give it equilibrium, to organize the structure – in point of fact we cannot conceive of an unorganized structure – but to ensure above all that the organizing principle of the structure limits what we might call its play.15

这里，某种结构主义作茧自缚了，因为它声称为其结构找到的“中心”，已不再构成结构的一部分。这个中心原先只是陷于关系游戏之中的众多要素之一，如今却被提升到结构之上的特权位置，并被认为从那里决定着整个游戏。这样一来，结构本身就不再受适用于一切结构活动的规则约束。

A certain kind of Structuralism is here hoist with its own petard since the ‘centre’ which it claims to have located for its structures no longer forms part of them. From being an element among others caught within the play of relations, it is elevated above the structure to a point of privilege from where it is held to determine the play as a whole. In this way, structures as such become exempt from the rules applying to all structural activity.

由于符号的“游戏”持续不断，而它发生的场所又是文本读者的心灵，文学研究者便与所读之物建立了一种新的关系。作为读者，无论我们有什么思想，无论是否将其向他人表达，这些思想都与文本本身形成一个连续体。用皮尔士的术语说，它们是文本的解释项，即纸面上的符号唤起的符号。根据读者的兴趣、智力、知识和气质，这种唤起有无限多种可能和层次。读者可以排列成一个序列，从极端被动者到极度活跃者：前者眼中的文本词语几乎不超出外延，后者眼中的词语却充满内涵。结构主义和后结构主义都可能使读者异常活跃，因为它们鼓励读者对文本的每一个时刻给予超乎寻常的注意；我们如今已经知道，其中一切都有意义，而且意义过量。

Because the ‘play’ of signs is constant, and because the scene of its occurrence is the mind of the readers of a text, the student of literature is placed in a new relationship to what he or she reads. Whatever thoughts we may have as readers, whether they are expressed to others or not, form a continuum with the text itself. They are, in Peirce’s term, its interpretants, the signs evoked by the signs on the page. There are infinitely many possibilities and levels of such evocation, according to the interests, the intelligence, the knowledge, the temperament, of the reader. Readers may be arranged along a scale from the extremely passive, for whom the words of a text barely rise above the denotative, to the hyperactive, for whom they teem with connotations. Structuralism and post-Structuralism can both lead to hyperactivity in the reader, by encouraging him or her to be abnormally attentive to each moment of a text, knowing as we now do that everything in it means, and means to excess.

然而，后结构主义读者的作用，不只是尽量记录更多意义和歧义；为计数而计数的语义盘点是不够的。其目的毋宁是领会所考察的文本仿佛在何处、以何种方式瓦解：在哪里可以证明其表面的逻辑与连贯性已经失效，因而在哪里可以论证作者已经失去了对自己文本的权威。这种丧失之所以发生——而且确实不可避免——是因为语言有一种背信弃义的习惯，会把作者未察觉的矛盾放进文本。主动的读者阅读文学时，心中记着英国早期结构主义者威廉·燕卜荪的提醒：“一个词或语法结构同时以若干种方式发生作用。”16 我要强调，这是文学语言的事实；写作时，即使我们想避免，也无法避免多重效果（意义）。燕卜荪凭非凡的才华、满怀欣喜地发掘出来的歧义，都是对他所读文本的有效阐释，无论能否通过诉诸作家的意图获得认可。

The role of the post-Structuralist reader is not, however, merely to register as many meanings and ambiguities as he or she can: a semantic head-count for its own sake would not do. The aim, rather, is to appreciate where and how the text under consideration falls apart, as it were: where its apparent logic and coherence can be shown to have failed, and where, in consequence, the author can be argued to have lost authority over his text. This loss occurs – indeed, is inevitable – because language has the treacherous habit of putting contradictions into texts of which their authors are unaware. The active reader comes to literature bearing in mind the proviso of the English proto-Structuralist William Empson that ‘a word or grammatical structure is effective in several ways at once’.16 This, let me stress, is a fact of literary language; we cannot, when we write, avoid a multiplicity of effects (meanings) even if we want to. The ambiguities which Empson uncovers with such extraordinary flair and delight are valid interpretations of the texts he reads, whether or not they are authorized by reference to the intentions of the writer.

“解构式”读者充当语言的代言人，履行一个已经铭写在所研究文本中的角色。德里达最有力、最富巧思的追随者之一保罗·德曼这样表述：“诗是对批评的预知。”17 这个说法具有挑衅性，因为它采取了诗的定义的形式；但德曼实际上不过是在断言，并不算离经叛道：诗是有意识地含糊而难以捉摸、专为阐释而设计的语言使用。诗人写作，首先是为了在读者身上唤起“文本”。他们不希望读者被动。许多诗人不会声称自己在语义上是所写之物的“主人”，而只会说，在作品所容许的众多阐释中，他们也许只能提供其中一种。散文作家更愿意占有自己的意义，但他们也可能承认后结构主义论点的合理性：意义也会“逃离”他们；对于别人认为他们如此写作意在表达什么，他们永远不该感到惊讶。

The ‘deconstructive’ reader functions as the mouthpiece of language, fulfilling a role already inscribed in the text under study. In the formulation of one of Derrida’s most forceful and ingenious followers, Paul de Man, ‘Poetry is the foreknowledge of criticism’.17 This is provocatively put, having the form as it does of a definition of poetry; but all that de Man is asserting, less than outrageously, is that poetry is that knowingly ambiguous and elusive use of language designed for interpretation. Poets above all write in order to evoke ‘texts’ in those who read them. They do not want passivity in their readers. Many would not claim to be ‘masters’ of what they have written semantically, but to be able to offer only one perhaps of the many interpretations it is open to. Prose writers are more possessive of their meanings, but they too might admit to the justice of the post-Structuralist case, that meanings can also ‘escape’ them, that they should never be surprised by what others take them to have meant in writing as they have.

后结构主义有意提高文本批评家的地位，否认批评家的工作与作者的工作之间有任何真正的分界：双方都写作，因此都进入语言和意义的“游戏”。批评家从作者停笔处继续，而后来阅读批评家的我们，又从他们停笔处继续。然而，承认所有语言使用者之间这种真实的连续性，并不妨碍我们根据自己对其价值的判断，对各参与者的贡献作出不同评价。并非每位作者都仅凭声称拥有“作者”的头衔，就高于每位批评家。“创造性”的等级并不能如此简单地划定。

Post-Structuralism has deliberately exalted the role of the textual critic by denying that any real divide separates what critics do from what authors do: both write and both therefore enter into the ‘play’ of language and of meanings. The critic carries on from where the author leaves off, and those of us who subsequently read the critics carry on from where they leave off. The recognition of this real continuity between all who use language need not, however, stop us evaluating the contributions of its various participants differently according to what we take to be their worth. Not every author belongs above every critic merely by virtue of claiming the title of Author. The ‘creative’ hierarchy is not so simply drawn up as that.

“作者”是批评思想的一个范畴，结构主义和后结构主义都试图将其清除；或者，如果这希望过高，就根据写作的事实对其重新作出更贴切的界定。作者不再应被视为适合掌管文本的人。我们通常受到的教导，是把作者看作处于自己文本之外、具有控制力的在场者。他有能力迫使语言执行自己的命令，而这些命令本身又神秘地处于文本之外。这是一种从外部以神一般的力量掌控文本的模型，后结构主义尤其拒绝接受它，就像它拒绝接受与之平行的“书”这一范畴一样。

The Author is a category of critical thought which both Structuralism and post-Structuralism have sought either to get rid of or, if that is too much to hope for, then to redefine in closer accordance with the facts of writing. He or she is no longer to be looked on as a fit person to be in charge of a text. The Author we are usually taught to think of as a controlling presence outside the text he is the author of. He has the power of coercing language into performing his commands, which are themselves, mysteriously, extra-textual. This is a model of external and godlike mastery of the text with which post-Structuralism especially will have nothing to do, any more than it will accept the parallel category of the Book.

本章一直采用后结构主义的“文本”一词来代替“书”。后结构主义用以反对“书”概念的论证，与反对“作者”概念的论证类似：“书”是封闭在两面封皮之间、自足完整的实体——一个可以得到穷尽阐释，或还原为一组可掌握的意义（甚至一个统摄全书的单一意义）的统一体。相反，“文本”不是封闭的，而是向语言的四面八方敞开，以最大的慷慨生成意义，并与其他文本保持密切关系。它既没有“书”的统一性，也没有单一的目的。它也不应被阐释为一个人——作者——的话语，而应被读作一个合唱队的话语。用巴赫金的术语说，文本是“复调的”（巴特更进一步，甚至把它描述成一种“杂音”）；它由许多线索组成，或者说由许多“声音”组成，如果允许语音中心主义模型继续存在的话。一个文本包含对其他文本的各种暗指或回声；一个文本与其他文本之间可以建立的诸多关系——引文、戏仿、剽窃、“影响”——统称为“互文性”。指涉过程也在这个方向上运作：我们读到的符号在我们身上唤起的，可能与其说是我们自己的文本，不如说是我们已经了解的其他文本。

All through this chapter I have adopted the post-Structuralist term, Text, to replace that of Book. Post-Structuralism deploys similar arguments against the concept of the Book as it does against that of the Author: the Book is an entity enclosed between two covers and complete in itself – a unity susceptible of an exhaustive interpretation or reducible to a manageable sum of meanings (or even a single, overall meaning). The Text, on the contrary, is not enclosed but open to the four winds of language, spawning meanings with the utmost generosity and standing in close relation to other texts. It does not have the unity of the Book, nor the singleness of purpose. And rather than being interpretable as the utterance of a single person, the Author, it is to be read as the utterance of a chorus. The text is, in Bakhtin’s term for it, ‘polyphonic’ (Barthes goes further than that, and describes it even as a ‘cacophony’); it is made up of many strands, or many ‘voices’ if the phonocentric model be permitted to survive. One text contains all manner of allusions to or echoes from other texts; and the many kinds of relations that can be established between one Text and others – quotation, parody, plagiarism, ‘influence’ – are known collectively as ‘Intertextuality’. The process of referral works in this direction too, that the signs we read may evoke in us not so much a Text of our own but other Texts of which we already have knowledge.

但是，如果“文本”已经取代了“书”，后结构主义又用什么来取代“作者”呢？答案是“主体”。主体与作者不同，它存在于文本之中，而不是文本之外。这看来是用一个语法形象代替一个有血有肉的人，在反对或不理解后结构主义的人中间引起了不少反感。巴特发表题为《作者之死》的文章时，18 这些“作者之友”并不高兴——本来也没打算让他们高兴。这篇文章与《S/Z》或巴特的其他许多文章一样，所攻击的是把作者视为作品最终解释的常见观点：

But if the Text has been substituted for the Book, what does post-Structuralism offer in place of the Author? The answer is the Subject who, unlike the Author, is present in the text rather than outside it. This is a substitution, apparently, of a flesh-and-blood person by a grammatical figure, which has caused much ill-feeling among those opposed to or uncomprehending of post-Structuralism. These Friends of the Author were not pleased – they were not meant to be – when Barthes published an essay with the title ‘The Death of the Author’.18 What is under attack in this essay, as in S/Z or in many other of Barthes’s writings, is the common view of the Author as ultimate explanation of the work:

……当代文化中的文学形象，专制地以作者、他的个人、他的经历、他的趣味、他的激情为中心；批评大体上仍然在说，波德莱尔的作品就是波德莱尔这个人的失败，梵高的作品就是他的疯狂，柴可夫斯基的作品就是他的恶习：对作品的解释总是从创作它的人身上寻找，仿佛通过虚构作品或多或少透明的寓言，最终向我们倾吐心事的，总是一个人而且仅仅一个人——作者——的声音。19

. . . the image of literature one can find in contemporary culture is tyrannically centred around the author, his person, his history, his tastes, his passions; criticism still consists for the most part in saying that Baudelaire’s oeuvre is the failure of Baudelaire the man, that Van Gogh’s oeuvre is his madness, and Tchaikovsky’s his vice: the explanation of the oeuvre is always sought on the side of the man who has produced it as if, through the more or less transparent allegory of the fiction, it was always in the end the voice of one person and one person alone, the author, who was giving us his confidences.19

巴特在这里心中所想的，也许是一种简单得令人遗憾的批评思想；然而，就作者与文本之间的关系而言，这种简单化实在太常见了。巴特以及后结构主义都要把作者逐出文本：这就是说作者“死了”所蕴含的意思。作者仍然作为文学史中的条目存在，附有传记，以及归在其名下的全部作品。但他们不再是昔日统一而具有掌控力的在场者，仿佛既在自己的写作之内，又在其外，好像他们能够把完整的自己毫无损失、毫无变形地从生活转移进文学。作者其实是一种建构或假设，由读者根据阅读的证据形成。也就是说，关于作者所知道的一切都是文本性的；对我们而言，他们没有其他存在形式。因此，建构作者的过程是循环的：我们从文本中抽象出作者，再用这个抽象物解释文本。巴特和德里达的目的，就是打破这一循环，最终使文本“去起源化”。

Barthes may have in view here a lamentably simple kind of critical thought, yet such simplicity, in respect of the relation between an Author and a Text, is all too common. Barthes, and post-Structuralism with him, would expel the Author from the Text: that is what is implied by writing of the ‘death’ of the Author. Authors remain, as items in literary history, with their biographies and the oeuvre attached to their names. But they are no longer the unified and masterful presences of old to be found at once within and without of their writing, as if they had found it possible to transfer themselves whole and without loss or distortion from life into literature. The Author is in fact a construct, or hypothesis, formed by a reader on the evidence of his or her reading. Whatever is known of an Author is textual, that is; they have no other existence for us. The process by which Authors are constructed is thus circular: we abstract them from their Texts and then use this abstraction to explain the Texts. The purpose of Barthes and of Derrida is to break this circle and to finally ‘de-originate’ the Text.

这种“去起源化”使许多人痛苦。我们喜欢文本有作者。匿名或集体创作的文学作品会引起焦虑，除非它属于歌谣这类文体，其中匿名由于某种原因被视为一种美德。我们喜欢文本有作者，尽管我们所读文本的许多作者都已去世，他们真正的“声音”早已沉寂。我们知道这些已故作者曾经存在，但如今我们拥有的只是他们的符号，而不是自我（除非我们把自我视为符号的建构，而我们完全可能这样做）。这些符号远非毫无歧义、仅属个人，尽管像巴特处理巴尔扎克时那样，把它们毫无剩余地化解为完全非个人的“代码”，又走得太远了。鉴于结构主义赖以建立的语言观，绝不可能把作者看作某个从内部调控其写作语义层面的主权统一体。我们确实应当承认，诉诸语言，就是为了社会交往而接受某种自我性的让渡。

This ‘de-origination’ proves painful to many. We like Texts to have Authors. The anonymous or collective work of literature causes anxiety, unless it is of a genre such as the ballad in which, for some reason, anonymity is held to be a virtue. We like Texts to have Authors even though many of the Authors whose Texts we read are dead, and their real ‘voices’ have long been silenced. We know that these dead Authors existed, but all that we have of them now are signs, not selves (unless we take selves to be constructs of signs, as we may well). These signs are far from being unequivocal or idiomatic, even though it is going too far to resolve them without remainder into quite impersonal ‘codes’ as Barthes does in the case of Balzac. Given the view of language on which Structuralism rests, it is quite impossible to look on the Author as some sovereign unity regulating the semantic plane of his or her writing from within. We should surely allow that to resort to language is to accept a certain surrender of selfhood in the interests of sociability.

此时回到结构主义奠基文本《普通语言学教程》的作者问题，也许有益。正如前面所说，这个文本是在索绪尔去世后，由他昔日的同事利用听过其普通语言学课程的学生的课堂笔记编成的。尽管人们已将它与索绪尔留下的手稿笔记作过大量比较，我们手中这个文本究竟在多大程度上代表索绪尔的实际观点，仍永远无法确定。我们必须承认，出版的《教程》带有某种程度的匿名性或合作性。我们希望重视的索绪尔“意图”，在其中可能被歪曲，也可能没有。索绪尔本人留下的一切都是文本性的：即他的其他出版物（极少），以及去世时留下的笔记。把这些与《教程》的出版文本比较，就是文本与文本比较；如果一个文本与另一个矛盾，是否必然意味着一个正确、另一个错误呢？他可能在写笔记和讲课之间改变了看法。诸如此类。我们必须使用现有的文本，忘掉其真实性的问题。重要的是：它包含的观念是好是坏，准确还是不准确，富有成效还是误入歧途？德里达并不是唯一认为下述努力徒劳的人：诉诸文本本身之外某种假定的最终权威，为文本作真实性认证。这种努力表明，人们普遍怀念那个整块一体的“作者”形象，或者按德里达的说法，怀念上帝这位最终的立法者。

It may be useful at this point to revert to the authorship of Structuralism’s founding Text, the Course in General Linguistics. This, as I wrote earlier, was a Text constructed after Saussure’s death by former colleagues, using the lecture-notes of those who had attended his lectures on General Linguistics. The question of how far the text as we have it represents the actual views of Saussure can never be resolved, despite extensive comparison of it with the manuscript notes Saussure left behind. We have to accept that there is a certain degree of anonymity or collaboration in the Course as published. Saussure’s ‘intentions’, on which we would like to lay store, may or may not be misrepresented in it. Whatever remains of Saussure himself is textual: to wit, his other publications (very few), and the notes he left behind at his death. To compare these with the published text of the Course is to compare text with text; if one text contradicts the other does it necessarily follow that one is right and the other wrong? He may have changed his views in between writing the notes and delivering the lectures. And so on. We must work with the Text we have and forget the question of its authenticity. What matters is: Are the ideas it contains good or bad, exact or inexact, fruitful or misguided? Derrida is not alone in looking on efforts to authenticate Texts by reference to some supposed ultimate authority absent from the Texts themselves as futile. They are a sign of the common nostalgia for the monolithic figure of the Author or, Derrida would say, for God, the ultimate law-giver.

因此，使文本“去起源化”，就是将它与作者那些无法接近、先于文本的思想和构想隔离开，并强调写作行为本身的效果，即瓦解被预设的写作者统一性：“一旦一个事实被叙述出来，其目的为不及物，而不再为了直接作用于现实，也就是说，除了实际运用符号之外没有别的功能……声音便失去其起源，作者进入自己的死亡，写作开始了。”20 巴特这里所倚重的“及物”写作与“不及物”写作的区分，与形式主义历来对文学性和非文学性语言使用的区分，即使不完全相同，也极为接近。如果我们作“及物”的写作，意在通过所写之物实现某些精确而实际的目的，就可以推定我们对所写之物保留某种权威；我们追求、也通常能够得到无歧义的信息。但是，作“不及物”的写作时，写作成为自身的目的；我们或多或少自愿地服从语言本身富于动力、制造歧义的能量。巴特用稍稍新颖的术语，扩展了一种关于写作者与语言关系的模型；在法国，这种模型可以追溯到上个世纪后半叶的诗人马拉美。正是马拉美写道，诗人的角色是“把主动权让给词语”。

To ‘de-originate’ the Text, then, isolates it from the inaccessible, pre-textual thoughts and projections of an Author and accentuates the effects of the act of writing itself, which are to break up the presupposed unity of the writer: ‘as soon as a fact is recounted, for intransitive ends, and no longer in order to act directly on reality, that is to say, with no other function than the actual exercise of symbols . . . the voice loses its origin, the author enters into his own death, writing begins.’20 The distinction which Barthes here relies on, between ‘transitive’ and ‘intransitive’ writing, is extremely close to, if not identical with the old Formalist distinction between a literary and a non-literary use of language. If we write ‘transitively’, with a view to achieving certain precise and practical ends by what we write, the presumption is that we retain a certain authority over what we write; we aspire to and usually achieve unambiguous messages. But when we write ‘intransitively’ the writing becomes an end in itself; we submit more or less willingly to the dynamic and ambiguating energies of language itself. Barthes is extending, with a slightly new terminology, a model of the relations conjoining writer and language which in France dates back to the poet Mallarmé in the second half of the last century. Mallarmé it was who wrote of the poet’s role being to ‘cede the initiative to words’.

按巴特极有影响力的用法，“写作”非但没有巩固作者的在场，反而将他拆散：他被播散了，就像巴尔扎克在《S/Z》中被播散一样。主体以大量分散片段的形式存在于文本中，是众多在场中的一个，对其他在场没有任何控制。主体只能通过读者的努力获得有限的重新整合；读者可以自由地逆着文本的播散力量，建构出某种连贯的形象，归入文本的“主题”。如果“作者”一词不是禁忌，我们最终也许可以重新使用它，将它应用于那个文本形象：我们根据阅读中发现的反复强调与特异之处——无论识别得对不对——建构出来的形象。但如果我们把这个形象称为写作者，把“作者”一词保留给存在或曾经存在于文本之外的真实个人，就可以说，写作者和作者不是一个，而是两个。我们不能正当地从自己相信已经确定的写作者特征，推论作者的特征；我们轻率地谈论作者“把自己放进书里”，却没有顾及这个说法所概括的晦暗而成问题的关系。

Far from consolidating the Author as a presence, writing, in Barthes’s very influential use of that word, undoes him: he is disseminated, as Balzac is disseminated in S/Z. The Subject is present in the Text in a great many dispersed fragments, a presence among other presences and without any hold over them. The Subject can only achieve a limited reintegration by the efforts of the reader, who is free to work against the disseminating force of the Text and construct some kind of coherent Figure who can be classed among the Text’s ‘themes’. If the word Author were not taboo we might eventually hope to recycle it, to apply to the textual Figure we have constructed from the perceived insistences and idiosyncrasies we have identified, rightly or wrongly, in reading. But if we call this Figure the writer, and retain the term Author for the real person who exists or who once existed outside the Text, we can say that writer and Author are not one but two. We cannot legitimately argue from the characteristics we believe we have established for the writer to the characteristics of the Author; we speak glibly of Authors ‘putting themselves into their books’ without allowing for the obscure and problematical relation which that phrase epitomizes.

人们可能认为，当文本的主体以第一人称出现时，就像大多数自传那样（用第三人称写的自传也有，但很少），我们最有理由重新整合分散的作者。然而，第一人称在这方面并不享有任何特殊权利：它仍是语言符号，不能免于符号性的条件。也就是说，它并不体现一种其他符号所没有的“在场”，尽管我们阅读第一人称文本时，可能偷懒地假定它如此。正如有明确属于自传、却用第三人称写的作品，也有明确属于虚构、却用第一人称写的作品。德里达有力地反驳胡塞尔，指出他声称代词“我”每被一个不同的人使用，就意味着一种新东西，这其实自相矛盾。德里达指出，即使我们对使用者一无所知，即使使用者像文学文本中的情形那样可能早已去世，我们仍然理解第一人称代词：“正如知觉陈述的价值并不依赖知觉的现实性，甚至不依赖其可能性，‘我’的表意价值也不依赖说话主体的生命……我的死亡对于说出‘我’在结构上是必要的。”21

It might be assumed that we practise the reintegration of scattered Authors with the greatest justification when the Subject of the Text appears in it in the first person, as in most autobiography (there are rare examples of autobiography being written in the third person). But the first person enjoys no peculiar privileges in this regard: it is still a linguistic sign and not exempt from the conditions of signhood. It does not, that is, embody a ‘presence’ denied to other signs, even though we may lazily assume in reading first-person texts that it does. Just as there are explicitly autobiographical writings written in the third person, so there are explicitly fictive writings written in the first. Derrida argues tellingly against Husserl that he contradicts himself by claiming that the pronoun ‘I’ means something new each time it is used by someone different. As Derrida observes, we understand the first-person pronoun even though we know nothing of the person using it and even though the person using it, as in a literary Text, may be long dead: ‘And just as the value of a statement of perception did not depend on the actuality or even the possibility of perception, so the signifying value of ‘‘I’’ does not depend on the life of the speaking subject . . . My death is structurally necessary to the uttering of ‘‘I’’.’21

由于语言符号的这种性质，第一人称的“我”否定了任何使用者“自我在场”的可能性：“我”只是主体的约定俗成的表征，仅此而已。德里达写道“我的死亡对于说出‘我’在结构上是必要的”，是在再次指出一切符号的含义：它们不需要我们在场来维持。我此时此地写出的“我”，已经尽其所能地向我在场，但它一经写出，就开始退入过去；它当然可能比我活得更久，却永远未必能在一个富有同情心的读者心中重新获得生命。德里达对“在场的形而上学”的严格论证，为我们的不安提供了很多理由。

The nature of the linguistic sign being what it is, the first-person ‘I’ negates the possibility of any ‘self-presence’ of whoever uses it: ‘I’ is the conventional representation of the Subject, nothing more. When Derrida writes: ‘My death is structurally necessary to the uttering of ‘‘I’’’, he is pointing yet again to the implication of all signs, that they do not need us there to sustain them. The ‘I’ which I write here and now is as present to me as it ever can be but it is no sooner written than it starts to recede into the past; it may of course outlive me but never achieve any subsequent reanimation in the mind of a compassionate reader. Derrida offers much for our discomfort in the rigour of his argument against the ‘metaphysics of presence’.

从本章说过（也就是写过）的一切可知，后结构主义不屑于文本阐释中的“真理”观念。原因在于这种观念不可避免地伴随排他性。如果批评家声称发现了某文本的“真理”，其他阐释就必须被假定为不真，从而失去资格。在这方面，结构主义和后结构主义具有自由主义精神：它们主张文本不可穷尽，因而始终向新的批评发现敞开。“真理”是一个不受欢迎的“神学”概念，不适用于文学研究；德里达肯定还会加上，也不适用于任何其他写作的研究。一旦被揭示，它就凭自己的威望使阐释过程停止。对德里达来说，更糟的是，真理通常被看作一种在文本“内部”等待发现的东西。这是一种隐喻性、空间性的用法，是德里达深恶痛绝的，因为在他看来，文本没有“内部”或“外部”，它完全作为在时间中延展的表面存在。所谓真理是批评家自己的文本或话语的一部分，作为所指，它并不比其他所指更超验。把阐释者的任务看作以这种方式“揭开”隐藏的真理，这一观念受到德里达热切的攻击，尤其是其精神分析版本。他对雅克·拉康极为尖锐；拉康很不明智地根据患者对分析师说的话是否真正“有所意指”，区分了“空言语”和“满言语”：分析师的任务是让患者承认自己说的话，从而承担责任，由此把“空言语”变成“满言语”。“满言语”具有、或者应当具有治疗作用。22 可是，如果两个符号序列完全相同，又凭什么标准判定其“真理”呢？拉康的区分依赖一种在德里达看来站不住脚的观点：说话者可以与自己的话语亲密地合一。“满言语”流露出一种与符号使用不相容的神秘主义或直觉态度。一切言语都必须被归为“空的”。

It follows from all that has been said (i.e. written) in this chapter that post-Structuralism is dismissive of the notion of Truth in the interpretation of Texts. It is so because of the exclusiveness which inevitably attends on that notion. If a critic claims to have discovered the ‘truth’ of a Text, interpretations other than his own must be presumed to be untrue and thereby disqualified. Structuralism and post-Structuralism are libertarian in this respect, that they assert the inexhaustibility of the Text and thus its perpetual openness to new critical discoveries. The Truth is an unwanted ‘theological’ concept, out of place in the study of literature or, Derrida would certainly add, any other kind of writing. Once revealed, it brings the process of interpretation to a stop, such is its prestige. Worse still, where Derrida is concerned, the Truth is regularly looked on as something waiting ‘within’ the Text to be uncovered. This is a metaphorical and a spatial usage that is anathema to Derrida, for whom Texts have no ‘within’ or ‘without’ because they exist entirely as a surface, extended in time. The so-called Truth is a part of the critic’s own Text or discourse, and as a signified no more transcendental than any other. The idea of the interpreter’s task as being to ‘unveil’ hidden Truths in this fashion is one which Derrida has attacked with zest, especially in its psychoanalytical version. He is extremely sharp with Jacques Lacan, who was unwise enough to draw a distinction between ‘empty speech’ and ‘full speech’ according to whether a patient’s words to his analyst were truly ‘meant’ or not: it is the analyst’s job to turn ‘empty speech’ into ‘full speech’ by getting the patient to acknowledge and thus to take responsibility for his words. ‘Full speech’ is, or should be, therapeutic.22 But by what criterion is its ‘truth’ to be decided if both sequences of signs are identical? Lacan’s distinction depends on the – to Derrida – untenable view that a speaker may be intimately identified with his or her words. ‘Full speech’ betrays a mystical or intuitive attitude incompatible with the use of signs. All speech must be classed as ‘empty’.

德里达并不声称自己的话能够通向他人的话所不能通向的真理。恰恰相反。他接受语言的条件及其隐喻性质。解构式阐释不提供持久真理，而是提供进一步的文本；如果愿意，我们也可以用解构的方式处理这些文本。作为读者，我们必须停留在保罗·德曼所谓“悬置的无知状态”，或其他人所谓的“不可判定性”中。后结构主义与结构主义不同，是一种关于生成而非存在的哲学：它无穷地运动，不让我们逃脱不断变化的意义游戏，甚至不给我们表面的喘息。它系统地要求其他人协作完成批评任务，因为其公理是：一切文学或哲学文本都包含矛盾或“盲点”，而阐明这些盲点是其后继文本的责任。德里达如此规定批评任务，是想使我们全都成为哲学家：

Derrida does not claim that his own words accede to a Truth denied to the words of others. On the contrary. He accepts the conditions of language, and its metaphorical nature. Deconstructive interpretations offer no lasting truths but rather further texts which, were we so inclined, we might attend to ourselves in deconstructive vein. As readers we must dwell in what Paul de Man calls a ‘state of suspended ignorance’ or of what others call ‘undecidability’. Post-Structuralism differs from Structuralism in being a philosophy of Becoming rather than of Being: it is endlessly dynamic, allowing us no escape or apparent respite from the shifting play of meanings. It is systematic in demanding the collaboration of others in its critical tasks, because of its axiom that all literary or philosophical texts contain within them contradictions or ‘blind spots’, which it is the responsibility of their successor texts to elucidate. Derrida would make philosophers of us all in so specifying the critical task:

阅读必须始终针对一种作家未曾察觉的关系，即他所用语言的图式中，他能够控制的东西与不能控制的东西之间的关系。这种关系并不是对阴影与光明、弱点与力量的某种数量分配，而是一种必须由批评性阅读产生的表意结构。23（斜体为德里达所加。）

The reading must always aim at a certain relation, unperceived by the writer, between what he controls and what he does not control in the schemata of the language he is using. This relation is not a certain quantitative apportioning of shadow and of light, weakness or strength, but a signifying structure that the critical reading must produce.23 (Derrida’s italics)

那些追随德里达的文学批评家，把挑出文本“盲目”时刻——作者失去对自身论证控制的时刻——的工作推得很远。他们大概全都会热烈赞同弗洛伊德式观点：这种“盲目”是某种深层“欲望”的症状，而作者未能向自己承认这种欲望。通过这类解构式阅读，文本可能显得像是语言对作者自负的报复，而解构批评家就是这种报复的执行者，因为盲目是在他或她的后继文本中变成视力的。这个角色带有攻击性；值得注意的是，较细腻的后结构主义者会费心为自己辩护，以免被指责不是在批评文本，而是在利用文本压倒作者。他们的辩护是邀请我们这些读者解构地阅读他们自己的写作。因此，保罗·德曼要求我们接受：“批评家对自己的假设最为盲目的时刻，也是他们获得最深刻洞见的时刻。”24 的确如此，前提是我们这些批评家的读者足够聪明，能看出他们在哪里出错。德曼的悖论把批评的误解变成最高形式的理解，其效果是让误解显得是批评家故意为之，仿佛他或她正在请求我们帮助。如此富有成效的盲目，看来像是有意选择的盲目。但德里达派对此指控的辩护会是：涉及文本时，意志并不参与，因为造成混乱和误解的必要工作，会由具有辉煌自主性的语言来完成。

This singling out of a Text’s ‘blind’ moments, where the Author has lost control of his argument, is carried a long way by those literary critics who follow Derrida. They would probably all of them enthusiastically endorse the Freudian view that such ‘blindness’ is a symptom of some deep ‘desire’ which the Author has failed to acknowledge to himself. By this sort of deconstructive reading, the Text may come to seem like the revenge of language on authorial presumption, and the deconstructive critic to be the agent of that revenge, since it is in his or her subsequent Text that blindness becomes sight. This is an aggressive role to play, and it is noticeable that the subtler post-Structuralists take trouble to defend themselves against accusations that rather than operating as critics of Texts they are using Texts to score off Authors. Their defence is to invite us, as their readers, to read their own writings deconstructively. Thus Paul de Man asks us to accept that ‘Critics’ moments of greatest blindness with regard to their own assumptions are also the moments at which they achieve their greatest insight.’24 They do provided we, the critics’ readers, are clever enough to see where it is they have gone wrong. De Man’s paradox, whereby critical misapprehension turns out to be the highest form of apprehension, has the effect of making misapprehension seem deliberate on the part of the critic, as if he or she were appealing for our help. Blindness so productive seems like a blindness willed. The Derridan defence against such a charge, however, would be that will does not come into it, where Texts are concerned, since the necessary work of confusion and misapprehension will be done by language, in all its glorious autonomy.

后结构主义比结构主义更可能被指责为崇拜语言，或者像万物有灵论者对待自然世界那样对待语言。语言确实有能力发展自己的动力、走自己的路，而不顾我们赋予它什么意图，也不顾我们重读自己所写之物时以为曾对它施加什么控制；后结构主义很容易夸大这种力量。后结构主义论证最薄弱之处，出现在这种论证被转而用来反对后结构主义的时候。解构主义者告诉我们，他们愿意、甚至希望自己的文本被解构，我们相信他们吗？我们有足够的智力、时间或耐心这样阅读他们吗？如果没有，而解构主义者也知道我们没有，那么他们从权威位置宣布自己没有权威，岂不就可能被指责在欺骗我们？后结构主义佯称放弃权力和责任，却明知这是不可能的，而且在坦诚的时刻甚至可能承认，这也是不可取的。它关于文本自主性的自由主义主张，是由作者们以权威方式作出的。

Post-Structuralism, even more than Structuralism, might be accused of idolizing language, or of behaving towards it as animists behave towards the natural world. It can easily exaggerate the undoubted power of language to develop its own impetus and to go its own way, irrespective of whatever intentions we have brought to it or whatever control we fancy we have exercised over it when we reread what we have written. The weakest point in the post-Structuralist argument occurs when that argument is turned against post-Structuralism. Do we believe deconstructionists when they assure us that they are ready and even hoping for their own Texts to be deconstructed? Do we have the intelligence, or the time, or the patience so to read them? And if we do not, and the deconstructionists know that we do not, are they not then open to the charge that they are tricking us, by declaring from positions of authority that they have no authority? Post-Structuralism pretends to an abdication from power and responsibility which it knows is impossible and might even admit in a candid moment is undesirable. Its libertarian assertions of the autonomy of the Text are authoritatively made, and by authors.

实际上，巴特在法兰西学院的就职演讲中，以幽默和带有自嘲的坦诚探讨了这一矛盾。演讲中，他戏谑地宣称，自己作为文学阐释者是怯懦而“边缘”的；然而他知道，听众受邀来聆听他，恰恰是把他当作一位地位居中、声名卓著的权威。25 若没有反讽——后结构主义的一个突出特点——这种立场就会显得欺诈。

This contradiction is in fact explored with humour and with a wry honesty by Barthes in his inaugural lecture at the Collège de France. In the course of it he playfully asserts his own diffidence and ‘marginality’ as an interpreter of literature to an audience he knows to have been invited to listen to him, on the contrary, as a central and renowned authority.25 Without irony, which is a strong feature of post-Structuralism, such a position would seem deceitful.

可以认为，后结构主义超越了（或“解构”了）许多结构主义理论对二元性的迷恋，尤其是雅各布森和列维-斯特劳斯所表述的理论。雅各布森在音系学中的区别特征分析，或列维-斯特劳斯对神话的阅读，所依赖的那种项与项的对立，对德里达来说过于生硬。他仿佛在这些对立“之下”或“之间”探索，以表明将它们对立起来是虚假的。“之间性”是德里达的一个关键概念，因为正如他所写，“‘之间’一词本身没有完整的意义。”26 这个词或符号如此明显地不完整，以至立刻引导心灵继续前进：它要求补全，因而非常巧妙地体现了德里达关于语言生产力的整个论点。

Post-Structuralism may be seen as having gone beyond (or as having ‘deconstructed’) the binary obsession of much Structuralism, especially as formulated by Jakobson and Lévi-Strauss. The kinds of opposition of terms on which Jakobson’s distinctive-feature analysis in phonology, or Lévi-Strauss’s readings of myth, depend, are too stark for Derrida, who searches, as it were, ‘beneath’ or ‘between’ them in order to show the falsity of opposing them. ‘Betweenness’ is a key concept in Derrida for, as he writes, ‘The word ‘‘between’’ has no full sense in itself.’26 It is a word or sign which leads the mind instantly on because it is so obviously incomplete: it calls for completion and so exemplifies very neatly Derrida’s entire case for the productivity of language.

因此，他不信任对立的项，因为它们被赋予了一种虚假的自身完整性。他的哲学容不下任何这种貌似最终的分离。他自己的哲学方法引导他消解对立，把各项恢复到它们仿佛被从中撕出的语言连续体中。正如我们所见，他一直反对的根本对立，是“在场”与“缺席”之间的对立。这一对立在索绪尔那里十分突出；索绪尔认为它是语言的组合轴与聚合轴对立的基础：前者是作为“在场”的语言，后者是作为“缺席”的语言。鉴于语言这两个方面完全相互依赖，德里达希望消除在场和缺席仿佛不可挽回地相互对立的观念，确实是正确的；在这里也可以说，他是在依据索绪尔本人的论证，为索绪尔式结构主义自身的利益而纠正它。在这个例子和其他例子中，德里达寻求的不是颠倒传统等级，让我们把缺席看作较高项、在场看作较低项，而是使我们看到，两项都带有对方的“踪迹”，因而我们的心灵不能在任何一个极端找到安息之所。

Opposed terms he distrusts, therefore, as having been endowed with a spurious completeness in themselves. There is no place in his philosophy for any such final-seeming disjunctions. His own philosophical method leads him to dissolve oppositions and restore the terms to the continuity of language from which they may appear to have been torn. The fundamental opposition against which he has campaigned, as we have seen, is that between ‘presence’ and ‘absence’. This is an opposition which occurs prominently in Saussure, who takes it to underlie his linguistic opposition between the syntagmatic and paradigmatic axes of language: the first being language as ‘presence’, the second language as ‘absence’. Given the utter interdependence of these two aspects of language, it is surely right for Derrida to want to get rid of the notion that presence and absence are somehow irremediably opposed to one another; here too, he may be said to be correcting Saussurean Structuralism for its own good in accordance with the arguments of Saussure himself. What Derrida seeks, in this instance as in others, is not to turn the traditional hierarchy upside down, so that we take absence to be the higher term and presence the lower, but to make us see both terms as bearing the ‘trace’ of the other so that our minds can find no resting-place in either extreme.

其他被认为是根本性的对立也一样，它们全都没有顾及延异原则。实际上，我们似乎恰恰因此而珍爱它们：它们否认该原则，从而安慰我们，使我们免于直面自身的有限生命与依赖性。因此，德里达把哲学家及其他人随意使用的、文本意义之“精神”与“字面”之间的古老隐喻对立，与“身体”及“灵魂”之间由来已久的对立联系起来。但大多数人会认为，字面与精神之间的语义对立，是隐喻地派生于更基本的身体与灵魂对立；德里达却反过来论证：“身体与灵魂的对立不仅处于这种意义学说的中心，而且得到它的确认；正如哲学中基本上一贯如此，它依赖于对语言的解释。”27 因此，按照德里达的说法，把人的灵魂设想为一种不能等同于个人现象性身体的精神“本质”，这个观念本身就来自西方普遍的语言误解。“灵魂”将是一个超越性实体，自身完整，不受符号永不停息、最终涉及死亡的游戏影响。因此，它必须被取消资格；“灵魂”就像“上帝”或任何其他假定的超验所指，结果都只是又一个能指，不在符号游戏之外，而在其中。德里达本人乐于这样堵死那些长期被视为逃出“语言牢笼”28 的道路。尽管他表现出种种反讽，甚至承认对自己的论证也持怀疑态度，他的写作中仍有一种强制性，以及思想家传统的权威意志。

And so with other supposedly fundamental oppositions, all of which fail to take account of the principle of différance. Indeed, we cherish them, it seems, for this very reason, that they deny that principle and so afford us comfort, against the realization of our own mortality and dependence. Thus Derrida takes the old metaphorical opposition freely resorted to by philosophers and others between the ‘spirit’ and the ‘letter’ of textual meanings and relates it to the age-old opposition between ‘body’ and ‘soul’. But where most would assume that the semantic opposition between letter and spirit derived metaphorically from the more basic opposition of body and soul, Derrida argues the other way round: ‘The opposition between body and soul is not only at the centre of this doctrine of meaning, it is confirmed by it; as it has basically always done in philosophy, it depends on an interpretation of language.’27 So that, according to Derrida, the very conception of the human soul, as a spiritual ‘essence’ not to be identified with the phenomenal body of the person, comes about through the prevalent Western misunderstanding about language. The ‘soul’ would be a transcendent entity, complete in itself, exempt from the ceaseless and ultimately mortal play of signs. It has therefore to be disqualified; the ‘soul’, just like ‘God’ or any other presumedly transcendental signified, turned out to be just one more signifier, not outside the play of signs but within it. Derrida himself takes pleasure in thus stopping up what have long been seen as avenues of escape from the ‘prison-house of language’.28 For all the irony which he displays, and his admissions of scepticism even in respect of his own arguments, there is a coerciveness in Derrida’s writings; and the thinker’s traditional will to authority.

作为思想家，他也许已经把结构主义的洞见推到了尽可能远的地方，并赋予索绪尔《教程》的思想以它原先所欠缺的连贯性，尽管也正是德里达揭示了这种欠缺。把后结构主义看作结构主义的完成，是合理的；用它自己的术语说，它是结构主义在其“盲目”中呼唤的“洞见”。后结构主义也进一步推进了结构主义的计划，向我们表明作为符号使用者，我们有多大自由，又受到多大约束。结构主义容许我们相当大的自由，可以随意组织自己的言语行为，只需遵守所用语言的规则；我们意识到使用的每个符号都得到社会而非个人的认可，却仍然掌握着所说或所写之物。后结构主义则论证，任何这样的“掌握”都是幻觉；一旦我们作为说话或写作的主体进入语言，我们服从语言的程度与语言服从我们的程度是一样的。

As a thinker he has carried the insights of Structuralism as far perhaps as they can go, and given to the thought of Saussure’s Course a coherence which it lacked, even though it took Derrida to reveal that lack. Post-Structuralism may reasonably be looked on as the completion of Structuralism: in its own terms, as the ‘insight’ which Structuralism was calling for in its ‘blindness’. Post-Structuralism has also carried further the project of Structuralism to show us how far we are free and how far constrained as users of signs. Structuralism allows us the considerable freedom to organize our acts of parole as we wish, respecting only the rules of the language we are using; it leaves us still in command of what we say or write though conscious that every sign that we employ is socially, not personally authorized. Post-Structuralism argues that any such ‘command’ is an illusion, that once we enter language, as speaking or writing subjects, we are as subservient to it as it is to us.

在文本的任何一个时刻，都无法知道谁占上风。德里达告诉我们，想要知道本身就是错的。因此，他抛弃的另一组对立，是语言的自愿使用与非自愿使用之间的对立，他轻蔑地称之为“处理与语言的关系时的一件极其粗糙的工具”。29 无论粗糙与否，这都是我们习惯运用的工具，尤其自弗洛伊德以来如此。在弗洛伊德以前，无疑有许多文本读者和批评家假定，文本中的一切都是有意的，作者的意志在任何时候都至高无上。然而后来，我们开始了解到“无意识”意义：它们由作者无法或不愿承认的欲望偷运进文本。我们得意地断定，这些才是作者“真正”的意思；在揭示问题上，有意识的意志无法与无意识竞争。我们发现自以为对作者本人隐藏的文本意义时，感到如此满足，可能不愿放弃这种弗洛伊德模型。德里达论证说，我们应当放弃它，拒绝承认有意的意义与无意或“无意识地”有意的意义之间有任何区别。作者必须有意愿，否则便没有文本；但我们没有可靠办法确定他究竟意愿了什么。

There is no knowing which is on top at any given moment of a Text. Derrida tells us we are wrong to want to know. Another opposition which he thus discards is that between the voluntary and involuntary uses of language, which he scornfully describes as ‘a very coarse tool when it comes to treating of the relation to language’.29 Coarse or not, it is a tool we are accustomed to wielding, especially since Freud. In pre-Freudian times, there were no doubt many readers and critics of Texts who assumed that everything in them was voluntary, that the Author’s will was paramount at all times. Then, however, we began to learn of ‘unconscious’ meanings, smuggled into the Text by desires the Author could not or would not acknowledge. These, we triumphantly decided, were what the Author ‘really’ meant; the conscious will could not compete with the unconscious when it came to revelations. Gratified as we feel to uncover textual meanings hidden as we suppose from their Author, we may be reluctant to abandon this Freudian model. Derrida argues that we should, by refusing to recognize any distinction at all between willed and unwilled or ‘unconsciously’ willed meanings. An Author must will, or there can be no Text, but we have no sure way of telling what he has willed.

后结构主义有助于纠正我们对写作与作者身份的看法，使之更切合实际。根据德里达或巴特来阅读文学，就是以一种更接近创作时的心态来阅读。当我们诉诸语言时，语言对心灵的影响不必成为一个令人内疚的秘密。当我们为了某种文学目的而“不及物地”使用语言时，感到它辜负我们、使我们失望的可能性，与感到它充分为我们服务的可能性一样大；我们也知道，诉诸符号就放弃了完整主体性的可能，尽管我们未必像德里达希望的那样对此带有病态的忧虑。这不妨碍我们享受语言，也不妨碍我们重新努力，试图让它做我们想让它做的事。德里达自己的著作流畅而浩繁，这就证明，后结构主义学说中没有任何东西必然使我们悲观地陷入沉默。

Post-Structuralism serves to correct our view of writing and of authorship and to make it more realistic. To read literature in the light of Derrida or Barthes is to read it in a frame of mind closer to that in which it is written. The influence which language exercises over our minds when we resort to it does not have to be a guilty secret. When we use it ‘intransitively’, for some literary purpose, we are as likely to feel that it has failed or disappointed us as that it has served us fully; and we know, without perhaps being as morbid about it as Derrida would like us to be, that in the resort to signs we abandon the possibility of an entire subjectivity. That does not stop us enjoying language nor renewing the struggle to try and get it to do what we want. The fluency and extent of Derrida’s own writings are one proof that there is nothing in the doctrine of post-Structuralism which need cause us to fall pessimistically silent.

结论Conclusion

书中的结论通常是或多或少具有简化性质的收束陈述：它们貌似概括了前面更充分展开的论证，却限制了后结构主义者极为珍视的播散过程。结论是作者借助一种既定文学惯例，试图严密控制读者心中意义游戏的手段。作者的结论是一种获准使用的方法，使他能以更少的文字重申已经申明的内容，而希望不超越此前文本划定的语义界限。用德里达的术语说，结论是一种“框定”装置，旨在通过强化把旧意义保留在内，把新意义排除在外。简言之，它完全违背后结构主义在语义方面崇尚自由的倾向。

Conclusions in books are normally closing statements of a more or less reductive kind: in seeming to epitomize the arguments advanced more fully in the preceding pages, they set limits on that disseminative process so highly prized by Post-structuralists. Conclusions are the author’s attempt to keep close control of the play of meaning in his readers’ minds by resort to an established literary convention, the authorial Conclusion being a licensed means by which he may reassert the already asserted in fewer words, without it is hoped going beyond the semantic bounds inscribed in the earlier text. In Derridan terms, a Conclusion is a ‘framing’ device, intended to keep old meanings in, through reinforcement, and new meanings out. It goes in short entirely against the semantically libertarian grain of Post-structuralism.

我在这里不会这样使用结论。我不会借此巩固自己的论证，而要探讨一个本书其他地方有所暗示、却未展开的问题：结构主义中主体与客体的关系。我按照“主体与客体”这个顺序写，是因为正统观念规定主体总在客体之前；我们会说，这是“自然”的次序。但结构主义严重质疑主体先于客体的地位，就像它严重质疑一切这样被认为“自然”的等级关系一样。

I shall not employ it in that way here. Rather than use this Conclusion to consolidate my arguments, I shall use it instead to take up a question alluded to but not developed elsewhere in the book: of the relation between subject and object in Structuralism. I write ‘subject and object’, in that order, because orthodoxy dictates that subjects invariably come before objects; this, we would say, was the ‘natural’ order. Structuralism, however, calls seriously in question the precedence of subject over object, as it calls seriously in question all such supposedly ‘natural’ hierarchies.

主体与客体的等级，也许是所有等级中最根本、影响最大的，植根于我们的直觉假设：作为人的“主体”，我们面对的是被我们感知为独立于我们存在的客体。但正如尼采最先指出的，我们给予“我”的优先地位，可能是语法强加给我们的幻觉；语法分析我们与世界的关系时，区分主语、谓语和宾语，并把主语放在句首。结构主义并不主张简单颠倒这个恭维我们的等级，用客体先于主体取代主体先于客体；它主张以不同方式表述两者关系，严格限定主体对于其所卷入系统的权威。

The hierarchy of subject and object is perhaps the most fundamental and influential of all, rooted in our intuitive assumptions as human ‘subjects’ confronted by objects we perceive as existing independently of us. But as Nietzsche first observed, the priority we accord to our ‘I’ may be an illusion foisted on us by our grammar, which analyses our relation to the world in such a way as to distinguish subjects from predicates and objects; and places the subject at the head of the sentence. Structuralism does not argue that this flattering hierarchy should simply be reversed, and the precedence of subject over object be replaced by that of object over subject; it argues that the relationship of one to the other be differently formulated so as to set strict limits on the authority of the subject in respect of those systems in which it is implicated.

结构主义以战斗姿态出现时，喜欢宣称，在从前被认为以人的主体为主宰的思想领域中，人的主体的时代已经过去。在《词与物》（The Order of Things）的最后几段，米歇尔·福柯把“人”这一历史过程的“自然”、被假定为内在的主体弃置，借此反对史学中占主导地位、并被他当时视为有害的人文主义。他转而论证：“人是一项发明，我们思想的考古学不难表明，这项发明是近代的产物。而且也许即将结束。”罗兰·巴特那篇声名狼藉的《作者之死》走的是类似的反人文主义路线，拒绝把文学文本视为至高无上的“作者—上帝”的产物、从而奉其“统一性”为神圣不可侵犯，转而支持多元论观点，例如：“文本是由来自无数文化中心的引文编织成的织物。”这些陈述刻意走向极端；拉康攻击法国许多思想根深蒂固的笛卡尔主义时也如此。这种思想坚持一种幻觉，认为我们作为人的主体拥有完全向自身透明的意识；不言而喻，这与弗洛伊德关于无意识隐秘运作的学说不相容。至于阿尔都塞，他把所谓“个人的资产阶级‘人文主义’”与未来的“社会主义”人文主义相对照，在后者中，个人“主体”与集体“主体”似乎将无法区分。因此，20世纪60年代结构主义的整体趋向极为鲜明地反人文主义，也就是说极为鲜明地反资产阶级，因为资产阶级传统上被视为缺乏根据的主观主义在阶级上的体现。

Structuralism in its crusading guise was fond of making statements to the effect that the day of the human subject was past, in fields of thought where previously that subject had been assumed to be paramount. In the final paragraphs of The Order of Things, Michel Foucault contested the dominant and, as he then believed, perverse humanism of historiography by dismissing Man as the ‘natural’ and presumedly intrinsic subject of the historical process, and arguing instead that ‘Man is an invention that the archaeology of our thought has no difficulty in showing to be of recent date. And perhaps its imminent end.’ Roland Barthes’s notorious essay on ‘The Death of the Author’ pursued a similar anti-humanist line, rejecting the hitherto sacrosanct ‘unity’ of the literary text, conceived as the product of a sovereign ‘Author-God’, in favour of such pluralistic arguments as that ‘The text is a tissue of quotations drawn from the innumerable centres of culture.’ Statements such as these were deliberately extreme, as were Lacan’s attacks on the endemic Cartesianism of much French thought, which clung to the illusion that we possess as human subjects a consciousness fully transparent to itself, a view incompatible it need hardly be said with Freud’s doctrines concerning the occult operations of the unconscious. As for Althusser, he opposed what he called the ‘bourgeois ‘‘humanism’’ of the person’ to a future ‘socialist’ humanism, in which the individual and collective ‘subjects’ would seemingly be indistinguishable. The thrust of Structuralism overall in the 1960s was thus very markedly anti-humanist, which is also to say markedly anti-bourgeois, the bourgeoisie being traditionally seen as the class embodiment of an ill-founded subjectivism.

正如我在本书导论中指出的，巴特、拉康或福柯的生涯，实在不是那种隐退自我的类型，无法佐证他们的哲学论点。他们是在行使知识权威的同时否认知识权威的思想家。1 然而，撇开过激之处，他们提出人的主体的新模型，确实提出了一个真实而重要的问题。结构主义的主体，绝不可能是其他哲学所设想的那种高高在上、自足的行动者，即本性上独立于要求其发挥作用的系统的本质。结构主义更谦抑地把行动者看作系统的一种功能。当所论系统足够庞大复杂，比如一种经济体系，我们不难承认，个人甚至集体行动者对它的权威有限。但把系统规模缩小到一种政治体制，我们立刻更愿意承认个人行动者在其中有更大的发言权；我们的政治史传统上一直从人文主义角度书写，而当代媒体的政治报道仍然极度坚持这样的信念：“人物”决定政治事件，显要个人或群体不是在政治系统之内行使权力，而是对政治系统行使权力。

As I observed in the Introduction of this book, the careers of Barthes, or Lacan, or Foucault were hardly of the self-effacing kind to bear out their philosophical case. They were thinkers who disclaimed intellectual authority in the act of exercising it.1 However, excesses apart, they raised a genuine and important question in proposing a new model of the human subject. The Structuralist subject can never be the superior, self-contained agent envisaged in other philosophies, an essence independent by its nature from the system within which it is called on to function. Structuralism conceives of the agent more modestly, as a function of the system. When the system in question is sufficiently large and intricate, an economy for example, we have little difficulty in recognising that the authority over it of an individual or even a corporate agent is limited. Reduce the size of the system, however, to that of a political system and we are at once readier to grant individual agents a greater say in it; our political history has traditionally been written from a humanist point of view, and the contemporary reporting of politics by the media remains extravagantly committed to the belief that ‘personalities’ determine political events, and that prominent individuals or groups exercise power not within but over the political system.

人们通常赋予行动者的权威，与行动者实际上能够实现的事情之间，已经失去平衡。结构主义重新界定人的主体的力量，提供了宝贵的纠正；如果我们学会它的重要教训，毫无疑问，我们都可能对周围社会的运作采取更健全的看法。这一教训以最容易理解的形式，体现在结构主义关于语言的教导中。我回到索绪尔对语言系统与言语的区分，也就是作为抽象系统的语言，与作为日常实用工具的语言之间的区分。言语是使用中的语言，需要行动者；语言系统只是潜在的，是行动者每次语言行为所调用、但只极为局部地实现的系统。这个行动者就是人的主体，其权威的限度现在开始变得清楚。语言使用者诉诸已经存在的语言及其使用规则；偏离规则太远，就会使对话者无法理解。在这个意义上，为了交流，语言行动者必须留在系统之内。但在系统之内，他或她享有相当大的自由，不是在词汇或语法层面，而是在句子层面。行动者必须按照公认的句法规则，用相关语言词典中列出的词来组成句子；除此之外，他或她可以自由地随意造句（这里我不考虑拉康派及其他精神分析学家假定在无意识中运作的那些句子生成约束）。

The balance has been lost, between the authority popularly ascribed to the agent and what that agent is in truth capable of achieving. In redefining the powers of the human subject, Structuralism offers a valuable corrective, and were its great lesson to be learnt there can be no question but that we might all take a saner view of the functioning of the society around us. That lesson is spelt out in its most accessible form in what Structuralism has to teach about language. I return to the Saussurian distinction between langue and parole, or language as abstract system and language as everyday practical instrument. Parole is language in use, it requires an agent, wheres langue is merely potential, the system that is invoked but only very partially realized in each language-act of an agent. This agent is the human subject, the limits of whose authority now begin to become clear. The language-user resorts to a language which already exists, along with the rules for its use, to depart too far from which is to fail to be understood by an interlocutor. In this sense, it is imperative for the purposes of communication for the linguistic agent to remain within the system. Within it, however, he or she enjoys a considerable freedom, not at the lexical or the grammatical level but at that of the sentence. The agent is required to form sentences according to recognized syntactical rules and out of words listed in the dictionaries of the language concerned; otherwise, he or she is free to form sentences at will (I leave out of account here those constraints on the production of sentences presumed by Lacanian and other psychoanalysts to function unconsciously).

这可以作为任何结构主义主体的模型：受相应规则约束，却能够在规则内自由地以独特方式行动的行动者。这个模型如此无可非议、如此不具限制性，人们完全可能奇怪，为什么结构主义曾因据说偏向反对主体性而遭到那样的攻击。一个原因也许在于，结构主义的奠基文本索绪尔《普通语言学教程》本身显得有偏向，因为它几乎专门关注语言系统，忽略言语。但这与其说证明索绪尔有偏见，不如说只是证明这部作品不完整；我们可以推想，他后来本会转向言语的语言学，使其与开创性的语言系统分析相配合。

This might be taken as the model for any Structuralist subject: an agent bound by the appropriate rules but free to function distinctively within them. It is a model so unexceptionable and unconstricting one might well wonder why Structuralism ever came under attack as it did for being supposedly biased against subjectivity. One reason may have been that Structuralism’s foundational text, the Course in General Linguistics of Saussure, appeared itself to be biased inasmuch as it is almost exclusively concerned with langue at the expense of parole. But rather than of bias on the part of Saussure, this surely is evidence only of that work’s incompleteness; we may suppose he would in time have turned to the linguistics of parole to accompany his pioneering analysis of langue.

第二个原因支持第一个原因：结构主义由于集中于书面语言，可能显得偏向系统、反对主体。恕我不同意德里达，书面语言与口头语言不同，它不能记录我们通过语调、节奏、音高变化、犹豫等等赋予口头话语的大部分“个性”，更不用说如今所谓“身体语言”，即可能伴随话语的有意识和无意识的身体动作。书面语言对这些交流辅助因素，要么表现不足，要么完全不表现，从而强化了系统凌驾于语言行动者之上的观念。但即使声音的转折或手势使我们说出的句子“个性化”，也必须认识到，它们本身同样具有制度性，而非个人性。它们也必须被编码，否则根本无法进入交流回路。巴赫金批评索绪尔忽视言语，也同样批评一种观念：语言中实际发生的话语仿佛是独一无二的事件，因此处于描写语言学的范围之外。他认为“语调”对结构主义至关重要：“在语调中，话语与生活进入直接接触。也正是首先在语调中，说话者与听者进入接触：语调具有突出的社会性。”2 巴赫金希望把结构语言学的注意力重新引向对话那种复杂而影响深远的结构，从而提醒结构主义，它的使命是作为一种深刻社会性的研究方法。

A second reason, supportive of the first, is that Structuralism may have appeared biased in favour of the system and against the subject because of its concentration on the written language. Pace Derrida, written language differs from spoken in its incapacity to register much of the ‘individuality’ we are able to give to our spoken utterances by the use of intonation, cadence, change of pitch, hesitation and the like, not to mention what is now called ‘body language’, or those bodily movements, conscious and unconscious, that may accompany our spoken words. Written language represents these props to communication either inadequately or not at all, thus strengthening the notion of the system as predominating over the linguistic agent. But even if inflections of the voice or gestures with the hand ‘individualize’ our sentences when we say them, it is important to realize that they are themselves also institutional, not personal. They too must be coded or they will fail to enter the circuit of communication at all. Bakhtin, who was critical of Saussure’s neglect of parole, but equally critical of any idea that actualized utterances of a language lay outside the purview of a descriptive linguistics, as being somehow unique events, took ‘intonation’ to be of prime importance for Structuralism: ‘In intonation, discourse enters into immediate contact with life. And it is in intonation first of all that the speaker enters into contact with his listeners: intonation is eminently social.’2 Bakhtin wanted to redirect the attention of structural linguistics towards the complex and far-reaching structure of dialogue, so reminding Structuralism of its vocation as a profoundly social method of inquiry.

写作中，与说话时借语调等手段达到的个性化程度相当的东西，要由“风格”来获得，即被认为使某位作家的作品与众不同的一组特异之处。对风格的衡量常常宽泛而凭印象，但不必怀疑它的真实性。事实上，如今可以通过计算机分析一位作家出版的全部作品，识别其惯用方式，即使未必能识别其“风格”。作为事实，风格确认而非反驳了结构主义的语言观与文学观，因为“风格”可以定义为被认为使一个作家有别于所有其他作家的语言特征的总和：它是衡量该作家在文学系统中位置的一个尺度。如果一种语言中只有一个作家写作，就绝不可能说他或她拥有“风格”。风格就是差异。因此，它必然内在于语言：“风格不是一个绝对物，不是要强加给语言的某种东西……而仅仅是语言本身，沿其自然轨道运行，并带有足够的个人腔调，使艺术家的个性作为一种在场而非杂技表演者被感受到。”3 萨丕尔的“个人腔调”，达成了语言系统与语言行动者之间唯一正当的平衡。

In writing, the equivalent degree of individualization achievable in speaking by means of such devices as intonation, is to be got by ‘style’, or that set of idiosyncrasies deemed to be distinctive of a certain writer’s work. Style is often only loosely and impressionistically measured, but there is no need to doubt its reality. Indeed, a writer’s mannerisms, if not his or her ‘style’, can be identified nowadays by computer analysis of a published oeuvre. As a fact, style confirms rather than contradicts the Structuralist view of language and of literature, because a ‘style’ might be defined as the sum- total of those particularities of language held to distinguish one writer from all others: it is one measure of that writer’s place within the literary system. If there were only one writer writing within a language, he or she could not possibly be said to possess a ‘style’. Style is difference. As such it is necessarily internal to language: ‘Style is not an absolute, a something that is to be imposed on the language . . . but merely the language itself, running in its natural grooves, and with enough of an individual accent to allow the artist’s personality to be felt as a presence, not as an acrobat.’3 Sapir’s ‘individual accent’ strikes the only legitimate balance between language-system and linguistic agent.

每一次言语行为都以其微小方式成为一个历史事件，是时间中的一次发生；这提出另一个紧密相关的问题：结构主义是否因为偏向反对言语，也偏向反对“事件”？简短的回答是，它没有这种偏见，只是反对任何忽视事件扎根于特定系统的阐释。采用结构主义原则的历史学家挑战所谓“事件史”（l’histoire événementielle）的主导地位，并不意味着他们认为历史事件毫无意义，而只是认为，把事件与历史结构隔离的史学歪曲了事件的性质。事件产生于系统之内，应当据此阐释。用美国人类学家马歇尔·萨林斯的话说：“事件不只是世界中的一次发生；它是某次发生与一个既定象征系统之间的关系。”4

Each act of parole is in its tiny way a historical event, an occurrence in time; and it raises the further though closely related question of whether Structuralism is also biased against ‘events’ by being biased against parole. The quick answer is that it is not biased, merely hostile to any interpretation of events which ignores their rootedness within a particular system. That historians adopting Structuralist principles should have challenged the predominance of what they called l’histoire événementielle does not mean that they look on historical events as having no significance, only that a historiography which isolates them from historical structures falsifies their nature. An event is produced from within a system, and should be interpreted accordingly. In the words of the American anthropologist Marshall Sahlins: ‘An event is not just a happening in the world; it is a relation between a certain happening and a given symbolic system.’4

结构主义可能被误认为对主体性看法失衡，最后一个原因涉及克洛德·列维-斯特劳斯的态度。这位学者既不追求公众关注，也不像福柯或拉康那样以炫目的姿态从事思想斗争。但他以自己的安静方式成为一个极端主义者，即我前面所说的结构主义纯粹主义者；同时，结构主义对他而言又是一种客观主义信条。我怀疑这是因为列维-斯特劳斯把它看作战后法国存在主义的理想解毒剂；存在主义夸大地崇拜“自由”、意志坚强的个人，认为个人有责任通过行动创造自己的价值。列维-斯特劳斯认为，这是一种堕落而戏剧化的尼采主义，荒谬地膨胀个人的重要性，却牺牲了社会的约束性在场。因此，他提出极为严苛的结构主义，作为一种贬抑自我的禁欲修行。

A final reason why Structuralism may have been wrongly seen as unbalanced in its view of subjectivity concerns the attitude of Claude Lévi-Strauss. He is a scholar who has not looked for publicity nor to militate intellectually with the flamboyance of a Foucault or a Lacan. But in his quiet way he has been an extremist, the purist of Structuralism as I described him earlier, but at the same time someone for whom Structuralism has served as an objectivist creed. It has done so I suspect because Lévi-Strauss saw it as an ideal antidote to postwar French Existentialism, with its overblown cult of the ‘free’, strong-willed individual whose responsibility it is to create his own values by his acts. This for Lévi-Strauss was a debased and histrionic form of Nietzscheanism, absurdly inflationary of the importance of the individual at the expense of the constraining presence of a society. Hence his own hugely austere Structuralism, offered as an ascesis in the belittlement of self.

列维-斯特劳斯的严苛，特别有力地提出一个令人困惑的问题：结构应当位于“哪里”——“真实”或外在的世界中，还是认识该世界的心灵中？它们已经“在那里”，还是我们通过思考把它们放在那里？这是一项古老认识论问题的略为新颖的表述：人的心灵在多大程度上塑造外在现实，而不是被动地记录它。列维-斯特劳斯断言，“结构本身是一项原初事实”，从而立刻把观察者降为从属角色。按这种安排，即使地球上没有人去发现结构并使之形式化，自然中大概仍会有结构。如此赋予系统绝对的优先地位，列维-斯特劳斯希望清除人文科学中他认为伪装成观察的内省：“结构主义在卢梭、马克思、涂尔干、索绪尔和弗洛伊德之后试图实现的，是向意识揭示一个不是意识自身的对象；从而使意识面对人的现象时，处于自然科学和物理科学所处的相同位置。”（斜体为他所加。）5

Lévi-Strauss’s austerity raises with peculiar force the perplexing question of ‘where’ structures should be located: in the ‘real’ or external world, or in the mind which cognizes that world? Are they ‘there’ already, or do we by taking thought put them there? This is a slightly new formulation of an ancient epistemological problem, of to what extent the human mind conditions as opposed to passively registering the reality external to it. ‘Structure itself is a primordial fact,’ Lévi-Strauss asserts, thereby instantly reducing the observer to a subordinate role. There would by this dispensation presumably be structures in nature, even if there were no human beings on earth to discover and formalize them. By thus according an absolute priority to the system, Lévi-Strauss is hoping to cleanse the human sciences of what he believes to be introspection masquerading as observation: ‘What structuralism tries to accomplish in the wake of Rousseau, Marx, Durkheim, Saussure and Freud, is to reveal to consciousness an object other than itself; and therefore to put it in the same position with regard to human phenomena as that of the natural and physical sciences.’ (his italics)5

这些陈述使反对结构主义者的抗议显得有理由：结构主义是唯科学主义的，太迷恋“硬”科学的方法和客观性，不应让它插手人文科学。但即使列维-斯特劳斯在非个人性的方向上走得太远，他也走得富有启发性，从不忘以有力论证支持自己的立场。这里所涉及的问题，比他作为结构人类学家独特的“风格”更为实质。在分析亲属制度、仪式或神话系统时，列维-斯特劳斯不声称拥有任何主观权威。他所描述的结构就在那里，而且似乎如此急于显露自身，以至分析者只需把自己看作“一个无实体的地点或空间，匿名的思想在其中得以发展，与自身拉开距离，发现并实现自己的真实倾向和组织，同时适应其本性所固有的约束”。6 这是对行动者在证据面前退位的非凡陈述。列维-斯特劳斯为自己设定的抱负，只是仿佛匿名地实现一个完全独立于他的逻辑系统所具有的自我分析潜能。

Such statements justify the protests of those opposed to Structuralism that it is scientistic, too much in love with the methods and objectivity of the ‘hard’ sciences to be allowed to meddle with the human sciences. But if Lévi-Strauss goes too far in the direction of impersonality, he does so suggestively, never failing to support his position by powerful arguments. There is a more substantial issue at stake here than that of his own distinctive ‘style’ as a structural anthropologist. In his function as an analyst of systems of kinship, or ritual, or myth, Lévi-Strauss claims no subjective authority whatsoever. The structures he describes are there, and so eager to reveal themselves seemingly that the analyst need account himself nothing more than ‘the insubstantial place or space where anonymous thought can develop, stand back from itself, find out and fulfil its true tendencies and organization, while coming to terms with the constraints inherent in its very nature’.6 This is a remarkable statement of the agent’s abdication before the evidence. Lévi-Strauss’s ambition for himself is simply to realize, as it were anonymously, the potentiality for self-analysis of a logical system quite independent of him.

他的观念是，受观察的系统会分泌出所谓“具体逻辑”，即数据中内在的、可以通过适当排列数据而揭示的逻辑。他把这种“具体逻辑”与“理论”逻辑相对照，后者从外部强加给数据，依照预先确定的外来图式解释数据。结构主义如果给数据添上东西，就背离了自己：

His conception is of the system under observation secreting what he calls a ‘concrete logic’, by which he means a logic inherent in the data that may be brought to light by their suitable arrangement. This ‘concrete logic’ he opposes to the ‘theoretical’ kind, which is imposed on the data from outside and interprets them in accordance with a preordained and extraneous schema. Structuralism is untrue to itself if it adds to its data:

与形式主义相反，结构主义拒绝把具体与抽象对立起来，也拒绝赋予后者优越的价值。形式是在与某种异于它的质料相对立中界定的；但结构没有与自身相区别的内容；它就是内容本身，通过一种被视为现实属性的逻辑组织而得到把握。7

Contrary to formalism, structuralism refuses to oppose the concrete to the abstract, and to accord the second a privileged value. Form is defined in opposition to a matter that is foreign to it; but structure has no distinct content; it is the content itself, apprehended by a logical organization conceived of as a property of the real.7

列维-斯特劳斯为自己分派的这种顺从角色，比如为了阐明某些北美印第安神话群的“具体逻辑”，暴露出他对主观主义的敌意。他采取这一角色时，明知自己在法国及国外的社会人类学中都处于极具权威的位置。他的谦卑是勉强的。对于列维-斯特劳斯所研究的这类系统，结构主义者的角色必然包含某种超越因素；这些系统的“逻辑”要被揭示，就需要外来“理论”的有力贡献。如果具体事物的“逻辑”如此容易纯粹根据自身而得到解释，无需外部添加，那么我们竟须等这么久，列维-斯特劳斯阐释的那些系统才找到代言人，就令人惊讶了。我们可以同情他对极端主观主义不可分割的傲慢的厌恶，却不必一路追随他，直至把人的主体消除。

This submissive role which Lévi-Strauss allots himself in order to elucidate, for example, the ‘concrete logic’ of certain groups of North American Indian myths, betrays his animus against subjectivism. It is a role which he adopted while holding as he knew a position of high authority within social anthropology, not only in France but abroad also. His humility is forced. The role of the Structuralist can but involve an element of transcendence in respect of a system such as those with which Lévi-Strauss is concerned, whose ‘logic’ requires a strong contribution of extraneous ‘theory’ if it is to be laid bare. If the ‘logic’ of the concrete is so susceptible of explication purely in its own terms, without addition from outside, it is surprising that we should have had to wait so long for systems like those expounded by Lévi-Strauss to find their spokesman. It is possible to sympathize with his distaste for the arrogance inseparable from an extreme subjectivism without following him all the way to the elimination of the human subject.

然而，列维-斯特劳斯如此严格地把结构投射到物质世界中，是在为更谨慎、更调和的皮亚杰所称的“一切结构主义的中心问题”提供一种可能的解答：“复合的整体是否从来就已组合而成，然而怎样、由谁组合？还是它们起初（并且仍然）处于组合过程中？”8 换言之：结构是否已经在那里（用皮亚杰的术语说，“预先形成”），还是由我们在认识行为中制造出来？更尖锐地说：列维-斯特劳斯是其神话“具体逻辑”的发现者，还是发明者？皮亚杰指出，把结构看作封闭、自主的整体，倾向于使人假定它必定预先形成，因此出现“逻辑或数学中柏拉图主义倾向的不断复兴，以及某种静态结构主义在喜爱绝对起点或独立于历史和心理学的立场者中间的成功”。9 这话可能是针对列维-斯特劳斯写的，因为从来没有哪一种结构主义比他的更静态、更非历史。但结构主义只是在认为有必要保持共时观点时才排除历史；其职责的重要部分也可以是研究结构因某些“历史”事件而发生的转变。它并不像马克思主义者曾愤怒抱怨的那样，在意识形态上反对历史，而是要求有权在当下的同时性中综合历时的历史。阿尔都塞也许是1945年后出现的最重要的马克思主义理论家；他辨识马克思思想从一个结构性问题框架（problématique）向另一个框架转变的相继时刻时，是作为结构主义者写作的；但与此同时，他承认这一转变的“主要推动者”，是处于时间中的“具体个人”，即历史上的卡尔·马克思。10 对一个人的思想作结构主义“阅读”，追踪它经历的转变，就是在此时此地、在当下综合这一思想，而且可以像阿尔都塞对马克思所做的那样，以思想家的成熟哲学为标准来源，评价其早期哲学。如此理解的结构主义，能够具有历史性却不成为历史主义，也就是说，不以目的论方式解释某一哲学演进的早期阶段，仿佛它们只是后来阶段的必要准备。

By projecting his structures so rigorously into the material world, however, Lévi-Strauss is offering one possible solution to what the much more cautious and conciliatory Piaget refers to as ‘the central problem of all structuralism’: ‘have composite wholes always been composed, but how or by whom, or were they first (and are they still) in the process of composition?’8 In other words: are structures already there (‘pre-formed’ in Piaget’s terminology) or are they made, by ourselves, in the act of cognition? More starkly: is Lévi-Strauss the discoverer of the ‘concrete logic’ of his myths, or its inventor? The conception of a structure as a closed, autonomous whole tends, as Piaget remarks, to the assumption that it must be pre-formed, hence ‘the perpetual renascence of platonic tendencies in logic or mathematics, and the success of a certain static structuralism among those fond of absolute beginnings or positions independent of history and psychology’.9 This might have been written with Lévi-Strauss in mind, for no Structuralism was ever more static or a-historical than his. But Structuralism excludes history when it sees the need to only so as to maintain its synchronic view; a large part of its remit may also be to study the transformations which structures undergo as a consequence of certain ‘historical’ events. It is not opposed ideologically to history, as Marxists once angrily complained, it is claiming the right to synthesize a diachronic history in the contemporaneity of the present. Althusser, perhaps the most considerable theorist of Marxism to have arisen in the years after 1945, writes as a Structuralist when he identifies the successive moments of Marx’s thought in its passage from one structural problématique to another; but at the same time Althusser allows that the ‘principal mover’ in this passage is the ‘concrete individual’ located in time, the historical Karl Marx.10 A Structuralist ‘reading’ of an individual’s thought, tracing the transformations it has passed through, synthesizes that thought here and now, in the present, and may use, as Althusser does in the case of Marx, a thinker’s mature philosophy as the source of the criteria by which to judge his earlier philosophy. A Structuralism so conceived is able to be historical without also being historicist, that is without interpreting the earlier stages of a given philosophical evolution teleologically, as if they were merely the necessary preparation for what came later.

* * *

皮亚杰和胡塞尔一样，反复思考这个问题：我们作为主体，如何理解并“内化”逻辑和数学的客观结构。我们判定这些结构是先验的、普遍有效的。但儿童不会立即认出它们，必须学习；按照皮亚杰的说法，这个过程可能长达十二年。因此，结构的获得成为他感兴趣的问题，就像儿童获得语言结构很快成为乔姆斯基感兴趣的问题一样。为了把握数学结构，皮亚杰不是明确地把结构放在柏拉图主义者的“外面”，或唯心主义者的“里面”，而是提出一条中间道路，即“建构主义”，或者以更难捉摸的说法，“操作结构主义”。在他的模型中，这类结构的“必然性”不是给定事实，而是一项成就，是学习与适应过程的最终产物。

Piaget, like Husserl, worries at the problem of how as subjects we come to comprehend and ‘internalize’ the objective structures of logic and mathematics. We adjudge these to be a priori and universally valid. But children do not immediately recognize them and have to learn them, a process which, according to Piaget, may take up to twelve years. Hence it is the acquisition of structures which becomes for him the interesting question, just as the child’s acquisition of the structures of its language soon became the interesting question for Chomsky. Rather than locate structures categorically, either ‘out there’ with the Platonists, or ‘in here’ with the idealists, Piaget proposes a middle way, of ‘constructivism’ or, more elusively, of an ‘operational structuralism’, to a grasp of the structures of mathematics. In his model, the ‘necessity’ of such structures is not a datum but an achievement, the end-product of a process of learning and adaptation.

这样理解的结构主义，应有助于消解主体与客体之间顽固的二分法。它所处理的结构，不必归入人的心灵与外部世界这种不正当分离的任何一极：它们存在于两者之间。它们是关系性的，是我们与事物联系的条件。列维-斯特劳斯在文化分析中可能依赖对立鲜明的图式，因此仿佛使可理解性依赖分割；但他也承认“心灵之所以能够理解世界，只是因为心灵本身就是这个世界的一部分及其产物”，11 从而提醒我们一种更为根本的统一。

Understood in this way Structuralism should serve to undo the stubborn dichotomy between subject and object. The structures in which it deals need not be attributed to either pole of the illicit disjunction between the human mind and the world outside it: they exist between. They are relational, the condition of our connection with things. Lévi-Strauss may rely on schemes of strongly marked oppositions in his analyses of culture, and thus appear to make intelligibility depend on division, but he also reminds us of an even more fundamental unity, by acknowledging that ‘the mind is able to understand the world only because the mind is itself part and product of this world’.11

延伸阅读Further Reading

关于结构主义和后结构主义的文献如今已经相当庞大，而且还在增长。我在这里只提供书籍而非文章的详细信息，并且仅限于以英语写成或已译成英语的书。我尽量避开过于技术化或沉迷行话的著作，希望列出一份来源易懂的核心书单。

The literature of Structuralism and Post-structuralism is by now very considerable, and growing. I give here the details only of books, not of articles, and only of books written in, or which have been translated into English. I have tried to avoid books that are too technical or given to jargon, in the hope of producing a core reading-list of readily intelligible sources.

综合论著General

综合论著中，最全面、最严谨的仍是瑞士著名心理学家让·皮亚杰的《结构主义》（Structuralism；Chaninah Maschler 译，伦敦：Routledge，1971）。这本书偏重数学和科学，并非总是容易阅读。彼得·考斯的《结构主义：可理解之物的艺术》（Structuralism: The Art of the Intelligible；纽约：Humanities Press，1988）全面探讨了哲学方面的问题。另外两部从哲学角度撰写的有价值研究是：菲利普·佩蒂特《结构主义的概念：批判性分析》（The Concept of Structuralism: A Critical Analysis；加利福尼亚大学出版社，1977）；霍华德·加德纳《心智的探寻：皮亚杰、列维-斯特劳斯与结构主义运动》（The Quest for Mind: Piaget, Lévi-Strauss and the Structuralist Movement；芝加哥大学出版社，1972）。特伦斯·霍克斯文笔优美的《结构主义与符号学》（Structuralism and Semiotics；伦敦：Methuen，1977）更关注结构主义的语言和文学维度。罗兰·A. 香槟的《法国结构主义》（French Structuralism；波士顿：Twayne，1990）是一部明白直率、值得称赞的综合介绍。《结构主义导论》（Structuralism: An Introduction；戴维·罗比编，牛津：Clarendon Press，1973）收录了著名专家在牛津所作的七篇权威讲演。《批评的语言与人的科学》（The Languages of Criticism and the Sciences of Man；理查德·麦克西和欧金尼奥·多纳托编，巴尔的摩：约翰斯·霍普金斯大学出版社，1970）收录了1966年一次会议的论文，其中包括罗兰·巴特、雅克·德里达、茨维坦·托多罗夫等人支持或反对结构主义的论述。《结构主义及其后》（Structuralism and Since；约翰·斯特罗克编，牛津大学出版社，1980）由专家撰写的五篇阐释性论文组成，分别讨论列维-斯特劳斯、巴特、福柯、拉康和德里达的思想。最后，如需阅读对结构主义运动精明而怀疑的叙述，可参看托马斯·帕维尔《语言之争：结构主义思想史》（The Feud of Language: A History of Structuralist Thought；琳达·乔丹和托马斯·帕维尔译，Blackwell，1989）。

The most comprehensive and rigorous of the general books remains that by the eminent Swiss psychologist, Jean Piaget: Structuralism (trans. Chaninah Maschler. London: Routledge, 1971). This is angled towards mathematics and the sciences, and is not always easy reading. The philosophical aspects are thoroughly gone into in Peter Caws: Structuralism: The Art of the Intelligible (New York: Humanities Press, 1988). Two other valuable studies written from a philosophical point of view are: Philip Pettit, The Concept of Structuralism: A Critical Analysis (University of California Press, 1977); and Howard Gardner, The Quest for Mind: Piaget, Lévi-Strauss and the Structuralist Movement (University of Chicago Press, 1972). More concerned with Structuralism’s linguistic and literary dimensions is Terence Hawkes’s elegantly written Structuralism and Semiotics (London: Methuen, 1977). A commendably straightforward general account is Roland A. Champagne: French Structuralism (Boston: Twayne, 1990). Structuralism: An Introduction (ed. David Robey. Oxford: Clarendon Press, 1973) contains seven magisterial lectures given in Oxford by prominent specialists. The Languages of Criticism and the Sciences of Man (ed. Richard Macksey and Eugenio Donato. Baltimore: Johns Hopkins University Press, 1970) contains the proceedings of a conference held in 1966, with contributions both for and against Structuralism by, among others, Roland Barthes, Jacques Derrida and Tzvetan Todorov. Structuralism and Since (ed. John Sturrock. Oxford University Press, 1980) consists of five explanatory essays by specialists on the thought of Lévi-Strauss, Barthes, Foucault, Lacan and Derrida. Finally, for a shrewdly sceptical account of Structuralism as a movement, see Thomas Pavel, The Feud of Language: A History of Structuralist Thought (trans. Linda Jordan and Thomas Pavel. Blackwell, 1989).

语言Language

绝对关键的文本是费尔迪南·德·索绪尔的《普通语言学教程》（Course in General Linguistics；罗伊·哈里斯译，伦敦：Duckworth，1983。这个译本优于韦德·巴斯金早先的译本）。乔纳森·卡勒在“现代大师”丛书中的《索绪尔》（Saussure；伦敦：Fontana Press，1976），是一部关于索绪尔思想的出色简论。罗伊·哈里斯的《阅读索绪尔》（Reading Saussure；伦敦：Duckworth，1987）程度更深，但极富启发性。戴维·霍尔德克罗夫特的《索绪尔：符号、系统与任意性》（Saussure: Signs, System, and Arbitrariness；剑桥大学出版社，1991），以细密分析式阅读探讨索绪尔主要语言学观念，也很有价值。弗朗索瓦丝·加代的《索绪尔与当代文化》（Saussure and Contemporary Culture；格雷戈里·埃利奥特译，伦敦：Century Hutchinson，1989）论述索绪尔式思维方式的传播。

The absolutely key text is Ferdinand de Saussure’s Course in General Linguistics (trans. Roy Harris. London: Duckworth, 1983. This translation is preferable to the earlier one made by Wade Baskin). An excellent short study of Saussure’s ideas is Jonathan Culler’s Modern Master volume, Saussure (London: Fontana Press, 1976). More advanced but highly instructive is Roy Harris’s Reading Saussure (London: Duckworth, 1987). And valuable as a close analytical reading of Saussure’s principal linguistic ideas is David Holdcroft’s Saussure: Signs, System, and Arbitrariness (Cambridge University Press, 1991). The spread of Saussurian ways of thinking is covered in Francoise Gadet: Saussure and Contemporary Culture (trans. Gregory Elliott. London: Century Hutchinson, 1989).

两部综合著作很好地体现了北美语言结构主义传统：爱德华·萨丕尔简洁而全面的《语言：言语科学导论》（Language: An Introduction to the Science of Speech；伦敦：Hart-Davis，1971）；伦纳德·布龙菲尔德的《语言》（Language；伦敦：Allen and Unwin，1935），篇幅很长，重事实，具有行为主义色彩。《伦纳德·布龙菲尔德选集》（A Leonard Bloomfield Anthology；C. F. 霍凯特编，印第安纳大学出版社，1970）收录了布龙菲尔德1923年对索绪尔《教程》的有趣评论。泽利格·S. 哈里斯的《结构语言学》（Structural Linguistics；纽约：Phoenix，1947）技术性更强，不适合初学者。了解诺姆·乔姆斯基思想的最佳入门读物，也许是带注释的选读本《乔姆斯基选读》（Chomsky: Selected Readings；J. P. B. 艾伦和保罗·范布伦编，牛津大学出版社，1971）。乔姆斯基本人有两部面向非专业读者的著作：《语言与心智》（Language and Mind；纽约：Harcourt Brace Jovanovich，1972）和《语言反思》（Reflections on Language；纽约：Pantheon，1975）。约翰·莱昂斯在“现代大师”丛书中的《乔姆斯基》（Chomsky；修订版，伦敦：Fontana Press，1992）清晰、权威而及时地综述了乔姆斯基的思想。

The North American tradition of linguistic Structuralism is well exemplified in two general books: Edward Sapir’s compact and comprehensive Language: An Introduction to the Science of Speech (London: Hart-Davis, 1971); and Leonard Bloomfield’s Language (London: Allen and Unwin, 1935), which is long, factual and behaviouristic. A Leonard Bloomfield Anthology (ed. C. F. Hockett. Indiana University Press, 1970) includes Bloomfield’s interesting 1923 review of Saussure’s Cours. Of a more technical kind and not for beginners is Zellig S. Harris: Structural Linguistics (New York: Phoenix, 1947). The best introduction to the ideas of Noam Chomsky is probably to be had from the annotated reader, Chomsky: Selected Readings (ed. J. P. B. Allen and Paul van Buren. Oxford University Press, 1971). Two of Chomsky’s own books which are aimed at non-specialist readers are Language and Mind (New York: Harcourt Brace Jovanovich, 1972) and Reflections on Language (New York: Pantheon, 1975). For a clear, authoritative and up-to-date survey of Chomsky’s ideas there is John Lyons’s Modern Master volume, Chomsky (revised edition. London: Fontana Press, 1992).

在索绪尔结构主义传统中工作的理论家，有两位特别突出。丹麦人路易·叶尔姆斯列夫的《语言理论导论》（Prolegomena to a Theory of Language）有英译本可读（弗朗西斯·J. 惠特菲尔德译，威斯康星大学出版社，1969）；法国语言学家埃米尔·本维尼斯特的《普通语言学问题》（Problems in General Linguistics）也有英译本（玛丽·伊丽莎白·米克译，迈阿密大学出版社，1971）。安德烈·马丁内的《普通语言学要素》（Elements of General Linguistics；E. 帕尔默译，伦敦：Faber，1960），是一本以结构主义原则为基础、非常清楚而有帮助的综合教材。

Of theorists working in the Structuralist tradition of Saussure, two stand out. The Dane Louis Hjelmslev’s Prolegomena to a Theory of Language can be read in an English translation (trans. Francis J. Whitfield. University of Wisconsin Press, 1969); as can the French linguist Emile Benveniste’s Problems in General Linguistics (trans. Mary Elizabeth Meek. University of Miami Press, 1971). A very clear and helpful general textbook founded on Structuralist principles is André Martinet’s Elements of General Linguistics (trans. E. Palmer. London: Faber, 1960).

罗曼·雅各布森的《声音与意义六讲》（Six Lessons on Sound and Meaning；约翰·梅法姆译，Hassocks：Harvester Press，1978）可以很好地用来追踪音系学对结构主义的影响。该书有列维-斯特劳斯写的导论，为雅各布森的音系分析方法直接影响他自己的社会人类学方法，提供了有趣的证据。雅各布森最重要的俄国合作者 N. S. 特鲁别茨柯伊的主要著作《音系学原理》（Principles of Phonology；Baltaxe 译，加利福尼亚大学出版社，1969）技术性更强。

The influence of phonology on Structuralism can be well traced in Roman Jakobson’s Six Lessons on Sound and Meaning (trans. John Mepham. Hassocks: Harvester Press, 1978). This has an introduction by Lévi-Strauss, which provides interesting evidence of the direct influence of Jakobson’s method of phonological analysis on his own method in social anthropology. More technical is the principal work of Jakobson’s foremost Russian collaborator, N. S Troubetskoy, Principles of Phonology (trans. Baltaxe. University of California Press, 1969).

英国和美国语言哲学家最近出版的许多著作，与本书的主题密切相关。约翰·帕斯莫尔《近来的哲学家》（Recent Philosophers；伦敦：Duckworth，1985）的前三章，非常清楚地把结构主义与当代英美哲学联系起来。《语言学哲学》（The Philosophy of Linguistics；杰罗尔德·J. 卡茨编，牛津大学出版社，1985）是一部有价值的选读本，包含理论语言学家和哲学家的重要论文。

Much recent work published by philosophers of language in Britain and the United States bears closely on the subject-matter of the present book. The first three chapters of John Passmore’s Recent Philosophers (London: Duckworth, 1985) link Structuralism very lucidly to contemporary Anglo-American philosophy. A valuable reader, containing major essays by both theoretical linguists and philosophers is The Philosophy of Linguistics (ed. Jerrold J. Katz. Oxford University Press, 1985).

社会科学Social Sciences

人类学。结构主义人类学一直由克洛德·列维-斯特劳斯的著作主导。他的思想在两卷《结构人类学》（Structural Anthropology；C. 雅各布森和 B. 格伦德费斯特·舍普夫译，伦敦：Allen Lane，1968）和《结构人类学第二卷》（Structural Anthropology Volume 2；纽约：Basic Books，1976）中表达得最清楚。其自传性著作《忧郁的热带》（Tristes tropiques；约翰及多琳·韦特曼译，伦敦：Cape，1973），以及《野性的思维》（The Savage Mind；1962）也非常值得阅读。他的《神话学》（Mythologiques）四卷本分析了大量北美印第安神话，现已全部译为英语。它们专业性强且多有重复，但第一卷《生的与熟的》（The Raw and the Cooked；1970）的导论，以及最后一卷《裸人》（The Naked Man；1981）的后记，作为列维-斯特劳斯方法和哲学观点的概括，极具启发性。如需清楚而常带批判的评价，可参看 E. R. 利奇在“现代大师”丛书中的《列维-斯特劳斯》（Lévi-Strauss；伦敦：Fontana，1974）。

Anthropology. Structuralist anthropology has been dominated by the work of Claude Lévi-Strauss. His ideas are to be found most clearly expressed in the two volumes of Structural Anthropology (trans. C. Jacobson and B. Grundfest Schoepf. London: Allen Lane, 1968) and Structural Anthropology Volume 2 (New York: Basic Books, 1976). Also well worth reading are his autobiographical volume Tristes tropiques (trans. John and Doreen Weightman. London: Cape, 1973); and The Savage Mind (1962). The four volumes of his Mythologiques, in which he analyses a large number of North American Indian myths, have all been translated into English. They are specialized and repetitive, but the introduction to the first volume, The Raw and the Cooked (1970), and the postscript to the last, The Naked Man (1981), are very illuminating as a summary of Lévi-Strauss’s methods and philosophical views. For a lucid and often critical assessment of his work, see E. R. Leach’s Modern Master volume, Lévi-Strauss (London: Fontana, 1974).

历史。费尔南·布罗代尔的《论历史》（On History；S. 马修斯译，伦敦：Weidenfeld and Nicolson，1980），权威地处理了结构主义方法在历史研究和写作中的应用。同样，另一位法国历史学家埃马纽埃尔·勒鲁瓦·拉迪里的论文也常常高度相关，尤其可参看《历史学家的领地》（The Territory of the Historian；本及西恩·雷诺兹译，Hassocks：Harvester Press，1979）。法国历史学家的合集《建构过去：历史方法论论文集》（Constructing the Past: Essays in Historical Methodology；雅克·勒高夫和皮埃尔·诺拉编，剑桥大学出版社，1985）包含许多有用内容。彼得·伯克的《历史与社会理论》（History and Social Theory；牛津：Polity Press，1993），也讨论了历史学家如何不理解“结构”概念的问题。

History. The application of Structuralist methods in the study and writing of history is a matter magisterially treated in Fernand Braudel’s On History (trans. S. Matthews. London: Weidenfeld and Nicolson, 1980). Similarly, the essays of another French historian, Emmanuel Le Roy Ladurie, are often highly relevant: see in particular The Territory of the Historian (trans.
